Per [storage.md](design/storage.md) one-prose-slot-per-section is preferred; multi-slot rendering is an open question and pairs with the multi-slot sub-label syntax already flagged in [storage.md](design/storage.md) and [implementation/parsing.md](implementation/parsing.md) open questions.
- **Conformance, not graded validation.** Both rules are **parser-pre-gate conformance** (binary / intrinsic — [parsing.md](implementation/parsing.md) → Conformance diagnostics), not entries in the validation severity inventory ([validation.md](design/validation.md)). Conformance-blocking is not a severity knob — a malformed file is malformed regardless of project policy. The conformance-diagnostics table in [parsing.md](implementation/parsing.md) is updated to list both new blocking cases.
- **The SPEC example in [storage.md](design/storage.md)** is updated to show the sentinel before the criterion's `maps-to-test` bullet, so the canonical form is concrete in the reading order's storage doc.
- **Why this resolves both attacks together.** The two attacks are not separate bugs — they're two faces of the same content-sniffing leak in the parser. A fix that addresses only one (e.g., field-group sentinel without the slot heading ceiling) would still let slot prose with `## Decision` headings re-introduce the structural-mis-locate path; a fix that addresses only the other would still let prose lists silent-promote. The two rules together close the leak end-to-end.

The parsing-level changes do **not** add new entries to the validation severity inventory ([validation.md](design/validation.md)); the new conformance errors propagate via the same `finding` shape the parser already produces (the "one finding shape, two producers" rule in [parsing.md](implementation/parsing.md)).

### Pass 3 #6 — cross-task forward-ref policy (committed store + same-task only; no provisional syntax)

Cross-pass theme #5 settled. Codex walked the scenario where T1 authors `supersedes: adr:b` with B planned in T2 — claiming forward-ref integrity blocks T1's finalize and the task cannot fix. Two distinct claims to disentangle:

- **Codex's specific example doesn't actually walk.** `supersedes` is a **backward** relation by ADR convention ("I replace an earlier accepted decision"). The agent authoring `supersedes: <future-thing>` is misuse of the relation, not a legitimate pattern the invariant must protect. The MVP doc-type-schema text already implied this; this batch makes it explicit (see below).
- **The general concern is real and was under-specified.** Strip the `supersedes` semantics: what if *any* ref field in T1 legitimately points at *any* doc T2 will create? The validation.md language "blocks only on integrity the task can fix" silently assumed the task can always fix (true for same-task creates, false for sibling-task creates). The invariant survived only by an undocumented policy.

**Policy locked**: forward-ref resolution covers refs whose targets sit in *exactly* two reachable surfaces — **(a) the committed store** (already on disk), or **(b) the same task's working area** (overlaid via the edge-index working overlay, [storage.md](design/storage.md) → Edge index lifecycle). A ref to a doc in *neither* is a blocking integrity error at `finalize`. **Cross-task forward-refs are not supported in MVP** (or post-MVP without an explicit provisional-ref feature). The deadlock Codex walked is rejected at its source — the agent never authors a cross-task forward-ref; if it does, finalize blocks with a precise error naming three routing options: **(i)** order the tasks so the target exists first (T2 before T1), **(ii)** move the target's creation into this task, **(iii)** drop the ref.

- **MVP scope verification**: `single-task` is the only workflow. No fan-out, no router, no multi-task planning. A task's `supersedes` value can only point at a previously committed ADR (or, hypothetically, an ADR the same task itself created). The MVP doesn't hit the deadlock at all; the policy defends the invariant for post-MVP and for the rare edge case where an agent mis-uses a ref.
- **Why not introduce `?` provisional refs now**: nothing in MVP (or near-post-MVP) needs them. Adding syntax to the address grammar — one of the most load-bearing surfaces in the system — for a feature with no real use case is the speculative over-build the project guards against. Easier to relax later than to ship grammar now. If a real cross-task pattern lands (e.g. fan-out sub-tasks coordinating creates), provisional refs get their own design including the deferred-validation state machine at store/milestone scope.
- **Doc placements**:
  - **[validation.md](design/validation.md)** §"Integrity vs completeness" — new paragraph "Forward-ref resolution — the two reachable surfaces" makes the policy explicit, names the three routing options, and explicitly rejects the cross-task forward-ref pattern. This is where the invariant defense lives.
  - **[document-type-schema.md](design/document-type-schema.md)** §"Bidirectional, but the inverse is derived" — the existing "Forward-ref integrity *is* gated at `finalize`, because the task can satisfy it by creating the target in the same task" line is sharpened to enumerate the two surfaces and cross-ref validation.md. A new bullet states the semantic rule: `ref` fields declare backward relationships to existing or same-task-created artifacts; never planning markers; `supersedes` means "replaces an earlier accepted decision," not "plans to supersede a future one."
- **Write-time stays local-adjudication-only** ([write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md) → write-time vs finalize-time checks): the locked split is unchanged. Ref-field writes check address *shape* at write-time; *existence* at finalize. A ref whose target won't exist by finalize fails at finalize, not at the write. Catching it earlier would constrain authoring order (a task creating A then B with B→A would fail when B's ref is written before A is created), which is a worse tradeoff than catching it at finalize where the full working area is visible.
- **Pass 4 "partially proven differentiator" concern is separate and deferred.** Pass 4 flagged that `supersedes` as a *self-relation* that's also *optional* doesn't fully exercise the integration advantage even with `supersedes` in MVP. That's a question about MVP differentiator *strength* (whether the MVP is convincing enough), not about the invariant Pass 3 is testing (whether the boundary holds). It rides in the Pass 4 batch.

### Pass 3 #5 + #3 — fan-out coordination-boundary defenses (cross-area refs rejected at join; adapter launch templates typed-schema validated at install)

Two paired attacks on the fan-out coordination boundary settled together. Fan-out is post-MVP, but locking the design rules now is cheap and forces the implementation to respect them when it ships.

- **#5 (outbound, sub-task → CLI)** — sub-agent B authors `supersedes: adr:cache`, guessing sub-agent A's slug; collision-suffixing rewrites only A's *local* self-refs; B's hand-typed cross-area ref can dangle or silently retarget. The previous workflow-dialect.md disclaimer "isolation guarantees the only references to a sub-agent's new doc are from its own area, so cross-area rewriting never arises" was wrong on its face: filesystem isolation prevents *writes into* a sibling's area, but not the LLM *typing* a plausible sibling slug.
- **#3 (inbound, CLI → sub-task)** — the assistant adapter's launch template decides whether the sub-agent ever sees the CLI's payload. A stale or broken template (omits `task_id`, paraphrases inline instructions, fails to invoke `jigc workflow`) lets the sub-agent write outside the working area or never reach the CLI at all; the join's assumptions collapse silently.

**Same root cause: a boundary the design treated as "obviously honored" without an enforcement mechanism. Same fix shape: explicit defense at the boundary.** Two rules:

- **Rule C (#5) — fan-out cross-area refs are rejected at join.** Each sub-task's outgoing refs are validated at join against `(committed store ∪ this sub-task's own working area)` only. A ref pointing into a *sibling* sub-task's area is a blocking integrity error at join — **same shape as the cross-task forward-ref policy** (Pass 3 #6 above). Coordination patterns that require cross-area refs belong in **sequential steps** before the fan-out splits; if a real fan-out cross-area pattern lands post-MVP, ID reservation / provisional refs get their own design (no speculative grammar now). Lands in [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → `fan-out` / `join` (replaced the old "Isolation guarantees …" bullet with sharper Slug-collisions + Cross-area-refs + Never-started bullets) and cross-refed from [validation.md](design/validation.md) → Forward-ref resolution.
- **Rule D (#3) — adapter launch templates are typed-schema validated at install.** The profile's launch template carries a schema declaring required placeholders (`{{task_id}}`, `{{workflow}}`), the required invocation pattern (must invoke `jigc workflow --task <id>`; no inline paraphrase), and forbidden patterns. Validation runs at `jigc setup` / `jigc adapter install` / regeneration; a broken template is an **install-time error**, not a runtime mystery. Lands in [assistant-adapter.md](design/assistant-adapter.md) → Bind the spawn mechanism.
- **Runtime backstop (made explicit)** — the join derives every sub-task's state from the CLI ("messages are notifications, CLI state is truth" — [VISION.md](VISION.md) → Sub-agents). A sub-task with **no recorded CLI activity** is treated as **never-started**, distinct from "started and failed." Catches the case where a template passes schema but somehow still doesn't reach the CLI (assistant API changed, user mis-wrapped, spawn primitive misfires). Lands as a new bullet in [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → `fan-out` / `join`.

Decisions (recommended → chosen):

- **Cross-area ref policy = α (forbid at join)**, not β (ID reservation) or γ (provisional refs). Mirrors the #6 cross-task policy. No new grammar. If a real fan-out coordination pattern needs cross-area refs, that's the moment to design β or γ — but the project explicitly guards against speculative grammar for surfaces that don't exist yet.
- **Adapter template validation timing = install + runtime backstop**, not install-only or runtime-only. Two complementary layers: install catches the broken template before any sub-agent runs (fast feedback to the profile author); the runtime backstop surfaces the silent "sub-agent never started" case as a distinct routing outcome rather than a deadlock.
- **Start-ack mechanism = implicit via CLI state**, not a new explicit `jigc task ack` command. The existing "CLI state is truth, messages are notifications" pattern already supports it; we just made the never-started case explicit. No new command surface.
- **Missing-activity treatment at join = block + distinct routing.** A sub-task with no CLI activity surfaces as "never-started" — separable from "started and failed" so the human knows whether to look at the adapter or the sub-agent's work.
- **Doc placement = split across three docs**, not one big section. Each rule lives in its natural home (workflow-dialect for fan-out coordination; assistant-adapter for the template schema; validation as the integrity-rule home cross-refs both). One-purpose homes; cross-reference, don't restate.

The two attacks' resolutions inherit the discipline of #6: **the boundary the design treated as "obviously honored" is now enforced; the failure mode is rejected at its source.** Pass 3 themes #5 (cross-pass) and the single-pass adapter-spawn concern are now closed against the invariants they targeted.

### Pass 3 #4 — OOB rename: path-rename is identity-change, detected and routed (MVP); `jigc doc rename` post-MVP

Codex's scenario: a human runs `git mv decisions/rate-limit.md decisions/gateway-rate-limit.md`. Because identity = path, the CLI sees `adr:rate-limit` as deleted and `gateway-rate-limit.md` as new untracked; every `supersedes: adr:rate-limit` ref in the store now dangles. The hash-on-known-path state machine ([reconciliation.md](design/reconciliation.md) → State machine, Pass 2 F5) didn't cover this case — it classifies *edits*, not *renames*; Pass 2 F5 explicitly deferred untracked-file handling to post-MVP, which silently left the rename gap uncovered.

- **Reframing**: VISION's honest-boundary note "IDs survive rename" was meant for **title rename** (H1) — the title field is mutable, the id is frozen. A **path rename** is a different operation: it's an **identity change** because id = filename slug (storage.md's "identity is the path"). The locked invariant doesn't promise path-rename survival; this batch makes the distinction explicit.
- **Path rename must be CLI-orchestrated.** Consistent with [VISION.md](VISION.md) principle #2's "splits and merges are explicit CLI ops." Auto-rewriting referrer refs to follow an OOB rename would be the "the CLI just does things" failure mode the determinism boundary exists to prevent.

**Policy locked**: rename detection runs at the existing `file-state` probe trigger points as a separate classifier. Two signals, two outcomes:

- **Strong signal** (tracked path missing + untracked path with same content hash) — **suspected rename**. Conformance error names old/new paths + the `git mv` revert one-liner + a forward pointer to the post-MVP `jigc doc rename` op.
- **Weak signal** (tracked path missing, no content-matching new file) — **deletion-or-loss**. Conformance error routes the human to restore the file (or run post-MVP `jigc doc delete`).

**MVP policy: block, route to revert. No auto-rewrite of refs, no in-MVP rename op.** Same shape as the rest of strict-MVP scope (Pass 2 F5 Auto-repair scope): the conformance error names exactly what's missing; the human resolves it explicitly. The conformance error carries enough detail (old path, new path, matching hash, revert one-liner) that the human can choose between reverting or waiting for the post-MVP op.

**Post-MVP `jigc doc rename <addr> --to <new-addr>`** is a single atomic transaction (transactional like `finalize`): re-key the `file-state` hash · rewrite every referrer ref across the committed store · commit as one logical change. Pinned in [write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md) → Post-MVP verbs with a one-line contract; not designed in detail until the verb is needed. The parallel `jigc doc delete` lands the same way.

- **Doc placements**:
  - **[reconciliation.md](design/reconciliation.md)** — new top-level §"Rename detection" placed after §"The state machine" (since rename is a separate classifier, not a state-machine pair-outcome). MVP scope vs post-MVP table extended to list rename-detection (MVP) and `jigc doc rename` / `jigc doc delete` (post-MVP). §"What reconciliation does NOT do" gains a "No silent rename" line.
  - **[storage.md](design/storage.md)** §"Identity, order, fields, slots, items" — the "Identity is the path" bullet now explicitly distinguishes title-rename (survives, id frozen) from path-rename (identity change, CLI-orchestrated).
  - **[write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md)** §"The verbs" — new "Post-MVP verbs — pinned, not designed in detail" subsection listing `jigc doc rename` and `jigc doc delete` with one-line contracts.
- **Decisions (recommended → chosen)**:
  - **Detection signal = two-tier** (strong + weak), not strong-only. The path-missing-alone case is real (accidental delete, file loss) and deserves its own routing rather than being silently ignored.
  - **MVP policy = block + route to revert**, no in-MVP `jigc doc rename`. Same shape as Pass 2 F5 strict scope; no rushing a CLI op into MVP for a case that hasn't yet caused real pain.
  - **No auto-rewrite of refs in MVP**, even on confirmation. Post-MVP `jigc doc rename` does it atomically by design; MVP is detect-and-route only.
  - **Conformance error content = rich**: old path, new path, matching content hash, copy-pasteable `git mv` revert, forward pointer to the post-MVP op. Enough detail that the human chooses between reverting now and waiting for the op.
  - **Doc placement = split across three docs** (reconciliation primary; storage sharpening; write-commands pin), one-purpose homes per the cross-reference-not-restate discipline.

The cross-pass theme this closes: **storage.md said "identity is the path" and "a retitled H1 changes the title, never the frozen filename-id" — but the disclaimer covered only H1 retitle, not path rename.** The gap is now closed by making path rename an explicit identity-change operation that the CLI orchestrates (post-MVP) and detects-and-routes (MVP).

### Pass 3 #8 — "Never positions" precisely: position-token IDs banned, physical-order serialization fine; workflow body is include-only

Codex's attack: workflow files use a body of ordered `{{include}}` lines, but invariant 3 ([VISION.md](VISION.md) principle #2) says "ordering lives in a separate ordered list of IDs, never in positions." Hand-editing a workflow body to move an include changes structure by position, apparently contradicting the invariant.

The attack is real but it's **a language gap, not a substance gap**:

- "Never positions" was meant to ban **position-token identifiers** — the GSD `01-locate.md`, `02.5-implement.md` rot pattern, where the ID itself encodes order and renumbering breaks every reference.
- Physical-order serialization of an ordered list — where the items carry their own non-positional IDs — is fine. A line move is a reorder; nothing renumbers; cross-refs to `workflow:single-task#locate` keep resolving regardless of include position.
- The pattern is used uniformly: doc instances' repeatable items ([storage.md](design/storage.md): "Order = physical order. Reordering is moving a block — a clean diff move, never a renumber") and workflow bodies' include lines ([workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md)) follow the same shape. Same principle, two surfaces.

There's also **a conformance gap**: workflow-dialect.md said "the body is the ordered `{{include}}`s" without formally requiring that includes are *all* that's allowed. A workflow body with prose interleaved between includes would be ambiguous — is that prose embedded in the workflow, instructions to the agent, both? Two rules close both gaps:

- **Rule E — Language clarification.** [VISION.md](VISION.md) principle #2 and [structural-grammar.md](design/structural-grammar.md) sharpened: "never positions" bans IDs that *encode* order (`01-`, `02.5-` prefixes; numeric positions used as references), not physical-order serialization of an ordered list whose items carry their own non-positional IDs. The clarification names both surfaces (doc instances' items, workflow bodies' includes) so the pattern is recognized as one principle.
- **Rule F — Workflow body is include-only at top level.** A workflow file's body may contain only `{{include: step:X}}` lines, blank lines, and HTML comments (`<!-- ... -->`, for human notes about why a step is included). Prose, ATX headings, other placeholders, or any other top-level content is a conformance error — `workflow-refs.body-include-only`, intrinsic blocking, same tier as the other workflow-refs checks ([validation.md](design/validation.md) → Severity inventory). Step files carry the prose; the workflow file is purely composition. The ambiguity Codex spotted ("is this prose embedded or instruction?") is eliminated by construction.
- **Decisions (recommended → chosen)**:
  - **Approach = both rules** (language clarification + conformance tightening), not language-only or conformance-only. The invariant overpromised, *and* the workflow-body parser contract was loose; both want fixing.
  - **Allowed at body top-level = `{{include}}` lines + blank lines + HTML comments**. Comments are useful (human-readable rationale notes about why a step is included) and survive diffs cleanly. Including them keeps the rule from being so strict that it pushes useful rationale into commit messages or external docs.
  - **Enforcement = extend `workflow-refs` probe**. Adds one row to the severity inventory (`body-include-only`, intrinsic blocking). No new probe; same in-process compose-time check as the other `workflow-refs` rules. MVP count goes 16 → 17 checks.
  - **"How a workflow composes" example label sharpened** — the existing example used `## locate` headings with inline content under each, mislabeled as "A definition." The actual on-disk format uses `{{include: step:X}}` lines per the new Rule F. Label updated to **"Conceptual shape"** with a clarifying note that the on-disk format uses includes; each `##` heading and its content lives in its own step file.
  - **Doc placement = split across four docs** ([VISION.md](VISION.md) principle #2 + [structural-grammar.md](design/structural-grammar.md) for the language clarification; [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) for the include-only rule and the example label; [validation.md](design/validation.md) for the severity inventory entry). One-purpose homes per cross-reference-not-restate discipline.

The substantive invariant survives: stable IDs, never renumber on insert/reorder. The language has been sharpened to defend it against the read Codex made; the conformance rule eliminates the remaining ambiguity at the surface where it would have leaked.

### Pass 3 #11 — pack-probe determinism contract (six rules + four meta-finding failure modes; locked now, implementation post-MVP)

Codex's attack: `validation.md` called pack probes "read-only and deterministic by contract," with sandboxing flagged as deferred. A subprocess pack probe behind a JSON contract could call an LLM, hit the network, read time, mutate files, or return inconsistent results — and its findings still feed `validate` / `finalize`. "By contract" is aspiration, not enforcement; the determinism boundary leaks through a surface labeled "deterministic by contract."

Pack probes are post-MVP. MVP ships none. But the rules need to be locked **before** the implementation lands — same forward-binding-rule discipline as Pass 3 #3/#5/#4. If the implementation ships without rules, the rules get retrofitted under pressure.

**Contract locked**: six rules + four meta-finding failure modes, both intrinsic-blocking when they fire.

- **The six rules** ([validation.md](design/validation.md) → Pack-probe determinism contract): (1) no network access (no sockets to external endpoints); (2) no model calls (LLM-free interface); (3) no time/random reads (deterministic randomness seeded by input is allowed); (4) read-only filesystem outside a probe-supplied scratch dir (engine cleans between invocations; no persistent state); (5) bounded resources (engine-set CPU/memory/output limits); (6) JSON-in / JSON-out only (no shelling out).
- **Failure semantics**: every misbehavior surfaces as a **meta-finding** through the standard `finding` shape; engine never crashes. Four modes: **timeout** (probe exceeded time budget), **crash** (non-zero exit, no JSON), **malformed output** (JSON parse failure), **sandbox violation** (blocked network call, fs write outside scratch, etc.). All **intrinsic blocking** — a probe that timed out, crashed, returned malformed output, or violated its sandbox can't be trusted to have validated anything, and a missing finding could mask a real integrity error.
- **Implementation deferred, contract binding.** OS-level sandboxing (seccomp, Landlock, or equivalents) is the post-MVP implementation question; the contract above is the floor it must stand on. When subprocess probes ship, the severity inventory gains a `pack-probe-integrity.*` category mirroring the four failure modes (parallel to the `doc-code.*` placeholder).
- **Decisions (recommended → chosen)**:
  - **Rule scope = six rules**, not a subset (network + model only) or a broader spec (specific syscall lists). The six cover the failure modes that break determinism in practice; specific syscalls are an implementation question for the sandbox tech chosen.
  - **Failure semantics = all four modes intrinsic-blocking meta-findings, engine never crashes**, not advisory + propagation. A probe that failed silently could mask an integrity error; the meta-finding is the audit trail.
  - **Enforcement timing = lock contract now, defer implementation**, not full-deferral. Same shape as Pass 3 #3/#5/#4 — the rules bind the implementation; the implementation lands when the verb is needed.
  - **MVP severity inventory updates = prose only**, not placeholder rows. Pack probes don't ship in MVP; cluttering the inventory with post-MVP placeholders would noise the MVP surface. Rows land when subprocess probes ship.
  - **Doc placement = split across two docs**: [validation.md](design/validation.md) as the contract home (new §"Pack-probe determinism contract" between §"Probes" and §"Severity inventory"); [implementation/module-layout.md](implementation/module-layout.md) §"Probe boundary (in `engine`)" cross-refs the contract as a binding requirement on the subprocess impl.
- **Side effects**:
  - The `validation.md` open-question entry "Pack-probe sandboxing — deferred" is replaced by "Pack-probe implementation — the OS-level sandboxing tech, the concrete enforcement mechanism for the locked determinism contract, and the JSON request/response schema; the contract itself is locked."
  - The same shape lands in `implementation/module-layout.md` open questions.

The contract closes the leak Codex spotted: "deterministic by contract" is now an enforceable spec, not aspiration. The implementation can't ship without satisfying it.

### Pass 3 #16 — bootstrap compaction resilience (routing footer always; resume hook per-assistant capability)

Codex's attack: the bootstrap pointer is the agent's *entire* a-priori knowledge ("run the front door and follow it"), but the hook only runs at session start. Mid-task compaction can drop or dilute the injection — even the static line in `CLAUDE.md` is summarizable away. The agent then resumes without the routing intent. Low severity (state isn't lost, just orientation), but the fix is cheap and lasting.

Two complementary defenses:

- **Routing footer on every CLI output** (universal baseline, no assistant capability required). Every composed workflow / `jigc start` / `jigc task validate` output in **agent-text** and **human-pretty** format ends with a one-line footer: `— jigc · run \`jigc start\` for orientation; all writes through \`jigc\`.` JSON-format output carries no footer (it's consumed by code, not the agent's reading flow). Self-reinforcing: compaction can drop one injection, but every CLI call re-shows the routing pointer. Specced in [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md#emitted-format) → Routing footer; emitted at phase 9 of the [resolution algorithm](design/overrides.md#resolution-algorithm) after step content (step bodies never author it).
- **Resume / post-compaction hook** (per-assistant capability). The adapter profile gains a `resume` event slot parallel to `SessionStart`. If the assistant exposes a "context resumed" or "compaction completed" event, the adapter re-injects the bootstrap on that event. Claude Code: ships when the API exposes such an event; the seam is designed, no implementation gated on it. Lands in [assistant-adapter.md](design/assistant-adapter.md) → Inject the bootstrap as a third bullet and as a new `Resume` hook line in the profile example.

The two defenses mirror the existing universal-floor + best-available-primary discipline that bootstrap injection itself uses: the footer is the universal floor; the resume hook is the per-assistant strengthening.

- **Decisions (recommended → chosen)**:
  - **Routing footer scope = always in agent-text + human-pretty**, not just long-running outputs. One line is cheap; consistency reinforces. JSON output excluded (it's tooling-consumed).
  - **Footer content = one-line routing reminder** (`— jigc · run \`jigc start\` for orientation; all writes through \`jigc\`.`), not multi-line or richer. Stays within "routing, not content" — names the entrypoint, embeds no rules. Cascade provenance (#15) is a separate header line; the footer is bootstrap reinforcement.
  - **Resume hook design = the seam now, ship when supported**. The profile slot is declarative; assistants without the capability simply omit it. Same shape as the rest of capability-dependent profile entries.
  - **Doc placement = split across three docs**: [bootstrap.md](design/bootstrap.md) is the strategy home (new §"Context compaction resilience" between §"Benefit detection" and §"Placement is the adapter"); [assistant-adapter.md](design/assistant-adapter.md) §"Inject the bootstrap" gains the third bullet + the profile example shows the `Resume` hook line; [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) §"Emitted format" specs the footer. One-purpose homes per the cross-reference-not-restate discipline.

Pass 3 is now complete except for **#14** (pack assets separation for non-CLI frontends), deferred to Pass 5 (implementation-fit) where the same concern was flagged from a different angle.

### Pass 4 (MVP red-team) — triage

Codex Pass 4 ([reviews/codex-2026-05-28/mvp-redteam.md](../reviews/codex-2026-05-28/mvp-redteam.md)) tested differentiator *strength* — does the thickened MVP *prove* its five claims, or merely support them. Triage: **Differentiator 5** (deterministic loop) and **Differentiator 2** (drift/OOB) are already PROVEN by Pass 1/2 work (emitted-format F7; `jigc start` four-forms; dirty-tree policy F1) — confirmed, no change. The three PARTIAL/NOT verdicts (Diff 3 context-slice, Diff 4 supersedes-strength, Diff 1 round-trip) share one root and resolve together — see S1.

### Pass 4 S1 — the superseding-decision path proves context-slice + edge-integrity + round-trip together

Diff 3 (context-slice over a persisted doc) was **NOT PROVEN**: spec-less `single-task` only embeds `{{task.intent}}`, and the ADR was output-only — nothing composed a slice of a committed doc. The fix unifies Diffs 1/3/4 because **`supersedes` is the unique logic-free handle from live task state to a committed doc** (spec-less ⇒ no input doc to slice; no-selection ⇒ can't search for related docs). A `single-task` step that composes the superseded ADR's `#decision` slice proves all three at once.

- **Unify, not three separate fixtures** — Diff 3 can't be proven independently: with no spec input and no selection, the only path from `task` to a committed doc is an agent-authored relation edge (`supersedes`). So the slice source *is* the edge Diff 4 needs, and reading the committed target *is* Diff 1's round-trip. One step converts all three from *supported* to *proven*.
- **Rejected: add a spec to the MVP.** A spec is an *input*; a spec-less MVP has no honest way to author a *structured* one — hand-authoring hits the post-MVP mint-on-import wall (criteria items need minted `{#id}`), and agent-authoring an input is semantically backwards/circular. The ADR is correctly agent-*output*, already committed through `finalize`, and read cleanly in a later task with zero new ingestion. Spec also wouldn't remove `supersedes` (Diff 4 needs a managed-doc edge regardless), so it is net-more scope. Spec lands post-MVP as a parallel `task.spec` role — the `task.decision` role paves the way, no rework.
- **Reachability = gate-bound context role, no new grammar** ([write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md) → The create-gate). The create-gate gains an object entry form `{type: adr, as: decision}`: `as:` both **declares** the `decision` role and **binds** the agent-created ADR to it on `create`. The path `{{@task.decision.supersedes#decision}}` is structurally the existing `task.spec.derived-from#goal` shape (root → role hop → relation hop → fragment) — no grammar or engine-root change. Rejected an engine-native `task.docs` facet (a collection ⇒ reintroduces selection unless `max:1`, which is post-MVP).
- **Timing = re-compose/resume.** The slice surfaces when the agent re-composes (`jigc start --task <id>`) after creating the ADR and setting `supersedes`; it serves the consequences prose (legitimately post-decision). Consistent with the re-derived progress model; no new flags. The step is always present and resolves to empty text before the edge exists — substitution, not a conditional.
- **Empty vs unresolvable, sharpened** ([workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → Leaves). A structurally valid path resolving to nothing — an **unbound optional role** or an **unset `0..1` ref** — yields **empty text, not a `workflow-refs` finding**; only a structurally invalid path (undeclared root/role/relation/section/leaf) is a finding. Required so the always-present `superseded-context` step composes cleanly in the common (non-superseding) case. The `@`-on-a-scalar conformance error is unaffected (malformed path, not absent value).
- **Proof container** — new [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow #5 (*Superseding decision*) walks both the passing edge (target committed ⇒ finalize passes) and the dangling edge (target missing ⇒ finalize blocks). [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md) MVP scope now states the differentiator proof is **mandated** by this path, not merely supported.
- **Slice scope = `#decision` only** — matches Codex's `{{adr:x#decision}}`; minimal meaningful slice.
- **Doc placements**: `write-commands.md` (gate object form + create-time bind), `workflow-dialect.md` (the `superseded-context` step + object-form `allows-create` in the on-disk example; empty-vs-unresolvable rule), `worked-examples.md` (flow #5), `CLAUDE.md` (MVP-scope mandate). No new doc; no VISION open-question change (the empty/unresolvable rule is a clarification, not a new open).

### Pass 4 S2 + S3 — resolved by S1; supersedes-strength and round-trip confirmed, no new design

S1's mandated flow #5 closes both, and `implementation/parsing.md` already owns the round-trip property — so these are confirmations + one precision fix, not fresh design.

- **S2 (Diff 4, supersedes / edge index) — resolved by S1.** The "optional `0..1` ⇒ skippable" gap is closed: flow #5 *mandates* one passing edge (target committed → finalize passes) and one dangling edge (target missing → finalize blocks). **Self-relation is sufficient — mechanism, not coverage.** The edge-index walk and forward-ref resolution are type-agnostic code paths; adr→adr exercises the identical mechanism spec→prd would, so adding a second managed-doc type purely for "cross-type" graph behavior is the scope-padding rejected for spec in S1. Inverse derivation *is* exercised (the superseded ADR's read view derives `superseded-by`). Bonus: flow #5's edge spans **committed store (target) + working overlay (source)**, exercising two of the five edge-index lifecycle sites, not a trivial same-area edge.
- **S3 (Diff 1, lossless round-trip) — owned by `parsing.md`, exercised by S1.** Codex's "add an acceptance test: commit ADR → no-op read/write or single-slot edit → byte-identical outside the target span" *is already* `parsing.md`'s load-bearing golden-tested invariant (idempotent on canonical content = the no-op case; splice-don't-regenerate = the single-slot-edit case, [parsing.md](implementation/parsing.md) → The load-bearing guarantee). Byte-stability stays a **writer property** proven by golden+fuzz fixtures — stronger than any one worked example. The MVP *loop* exercises parse/slice of a committed file (flow #5) + splice-write of in-task files (create/set-slot); the splice-write-into-committed-bytes branch ([parsing.md](implementation/parsing.md) "existing doc copied in on first touch") stays fixture-tested, not loop-exercised — **rejected** manufacturing an edit-a-committed-ADR flow, since supersede (not in-place edit) is the MVP's ADR-evolution pattern.
- **Precision fix (S3):** the S1 CLAUDE.md `(a)` clause said the loop "round-trips a committed file *on read* (retiring the #1 risk)" — that overclaimed, since read/slice exercises only the parse half. Re-attributed: byte-stability + #1-risk-retirement belong to `parsing.md`'s writer property; flow #5 is the in-loop parse/slice exercise.
- **Pass 4 status:** all five differentiators now PROVEN (Diff 2 + Diff 5 confirmed already-proven by Pass 1/2 work in the triage entry; Diff 1/3/4 via S1 + these confirmations). Remaining Pass 4 items are scope-audit observations Codex itself rated non-blocking (adapter line, team layer, ADR status lifecycle — fine to keep) and **#14** (pack assets separation for non-CLI frontends), already deferred to Pass 5. Pass 5 (implementation-fit) is the next untouched pass.

### Pass 5 (implementation-fit) — triage

Codex Pass 5 ([reviews/codex-2026-05-28/implementation-fit.md](../reviews/codex-2026-05-28/implementation-fit.md)) checked whether `implementation/` choices faithfully serve `design/` commitments — six green/yellow/red tables + six foreclosed options. Triage: two foreclosed items are **already resolved** by prior passes — **#3** (heading-backed item identity vs "id-source is a field") by Pass 1 F12 (*id-source = the title field, rendered as the heading*), **#5** (final schema-looking prose lists structurally reserved) by Pass 3 #1+#2 (the `<!-- fields -->` sentinel *is* the escape rule Codex asked for). Substantive remainder, each its own loop: **#1** (definition front-matter YAML), **#2 + #6** (frontend-neutral boundaries — the parked #14), Area-1-row-22 (absent-optional-section insertion point), **#4** (subprocess-probe JSON wire contract).

### Pass 5 #1 — definition front-matter is config-family YAML, not the flat document-instance field block

Codex (foreclosed #1, SEV high): parsing.md's flat `key: value` front-matter parser would foreclose nested workflow/step-definition front-matter (`fan-out.over`, and — post-S1 — `allows-create: [{type, as}]`). Triage found the **parsing.md side already resolved** (its Front-matter §"Scope" already routes definition front-matter to the config YAML parser — a prior-pass silent drift-fix); the residual was **`workflow-dialect.md`**, which still described definitions as reusing "the same Markdown + front-matter pattern as document instances," implying the *same parser*.

- **Rule locked**: *document instances* use the flat field-block front-matter parser; *workflow / step / schema definitions* are config-family and use the **config YAML parser** ([overrides.md](design/overrides.md)). The two families share only the **shape** (a Markdown body under a `---`-fenced front-matter block), never the front-matter **grammar**. Consistent with the existing "config family = YAML" decisions (doc-type schema, delta manifests) — this just makes the parser boundary explicit where workflow-dialect.md had blurred it.
- **S1 consistency**: the `allows-create: [{type: adr, as: decision}]` object-list landed in S1 parses cleanly as config YAML — no tension with this rule (it was the acute trigger Codex's flat-parser concern would have hit).
- **Fix = one sharpening**, no parsing.md change: [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → On-disk definition format now states the shared *shape* but config-YAML *grammar* and cross-refs [parsing.md](implementation/parsing.md) → Front-matter + [overrides.md](design/overrides.md). No storage.md change — it is instance-scoped, and a definition-parser note there would break one-home discipline.

### Pass 5 #2 + #6 — frontend-neutral boundaries (`PackSource` provider in; versioned result types out)

Two paired foreclosed options about what a non-CLI frontend (the post-MVP `mcp` bin) reuses from the engine. #2 (SEV high): the dev pack embedded only in `cli` would force a second frontend to re-embed it or depend on CLI packaging; the engine's "source abstraction" was named but unspecified. #6 (SEV low): treating result-type JSON as incidental `serde` output underspecifies external consumers. Same root — the engine-as-reusable-core boundary was asserted but not pinned — so settled together in [module-layout.md](implementation/module-layout.md).

- **`PackSource` provider trait, defined in `engine`** ([module-layout.md](implementation/module-layout.md) → The dev pack's home). The named, specified form of the prior hand-wavy "source abstraction": `pack_version` + `list(kind)` + `read(kind, id)` over resource kinds (schemas / workflows / steps / config). It is **how `cli` provides the pack-default cascade layer** — fits the existing "cli locates layers, engine resolves" boundary; pack-default is one located layer, not a special case. Two impls: `EmbeddedPack` (MVP) and `FilesystemPack` (post-MVP installable packs — the "free extension through the same abstraction" already promised). Closes the "named, not specified" gap (Codex #2).
- **Built-in bytes: embed in `cli` now, relocate when a 2nd frontend lands** (the genuine fork; chose embed-now + rule over a shared crate now). MVP keeps the one-self-contained-binary value; the moment `mcp` exists, the embedded built-in pack + its `EmbeddedPack` impl **relocate to a shared `pack-builtin` data-crate** both bins depend on — neither re-embeds, neither depends on the other's packaging, **the engine never changes** (only knows the trait). Rejected shared-crate-now (premature ceremony for one frontend, per the crate-topology decision) and per-frontend embedding as the end state (byte duplication + version-skew). Same design-rule-now / build-when-needed discipline as Pass 3 #3/#5/#11. Adapter profiles follow the same embed-now/relocate-if-shared pattern.
- **Result types are a versioned semantic API, not incidental `serde` dumps** ([module-layout.md](implementation/module-layout.md) → Renderers; Codex #6). The public result types' JSON projection is part of the contract (explicit serde attributes + a schema-version marker); changes are intentional and versioned, so the renderers *and* any external JSON / `mcp` consumer bind to a stable surface. MVP keeps it internal discipline (one frontend); locking the stance makes a non-CLI consumer zero-rework rather than silently broken by a field rename.
- **Side effects**: the dependency-graph diagram now shows `PackSource::EmbeddedPack`, the `FilesystemPack` seam, the versioned-API result types, and the post-MVP `pack-builtin` shared crate; the "Embedded-resource mechanism" open question narrows to *just* the embed mechanism (`rust-embed` vs `include_dir` vs build-script) now that the provider boundary is specified. This is the substance of the parked Pass 3 **#14** (pack assets separation for non-CLI frontends).

### Pass 5 (Area 1, row 22) — absent optional sections generate at their schema-ordered insertion point

Codex (Area 1 yellow): the offset-splice model edits existing byte spans, but a section can be *optional* and therefore **absent** — so `set-slot` into it has no span to splice and the splice model lacked a canonical insertion point for the absent structural home. MVP-relevant via the `commit` doc's optional `body` slot.

- **Rule = one unifying principle** ([parsing.md](implementation/parsing.md) → The write pipeline): *the canonical writer materializes any absent structural home at its schema-ordered position before the leaf write proceeds.* Already true for an absent `set-field` (generate-and-insert); now lifted from leaves to sections — `set-slot` on an absent optional section, and `add-item` on an absent optional repeatable container, generate the `##` home first, then write. Not an error, not a require-add-section-first: sections aren't per-instance structural variation (only repeatable *items* are, [write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md)), so an optional section's absence is just "its slot is unfilled," and first-write materializes it.
- **Insertion point = deterministic from schema document-order** ([storage.md](design/storage.md): schema order = physical order). The writer knows the full schema section list and which are present, and inserts so present sections stay in schema order. No positional ambiguity.
- **Generation scope = the structural home only** — heading + the leaf being written; field groups / sentinel / sibling slots materialize when their own writes land, incrementally. Required-but-absent fields stay a `finalize` integrity concern, not forced at this write. Validate-after-write covers the inserted region like any other edit.
- **Canonical-writer caller count 5 → 6** — `set-slot`-when-section-absent joins the enumeration (parallels the four → five bump logged in the 2026-05-27 MVP thickening for the commit string sink). `add-item` was already a generation caller; the new behavior is that it also generates an absent container.
- **Doc placement**: [parsing.md](implementation/parsing.md) only (the write-pipeline mode table + a new paragraph + the caller enumeration); cross-refs document-type-schema (optional sections) and storage (schema order). No design-doc change — this is mechanism realization.

### Pass 5 #4 — subprocess-probe wire seam sketched; `ctx`-serializable is what secures "zero engine change"

Codex (foreclosed #4, SEV med): the `Probe` trait's "zero engine change" claim for post-MVP subprocess probes was unsupported until the JSON wire contract was at least sketched. Pass 3 #11 deliberately deferred the *full* schema; this resolves the tension by sketching the **seam** (validating the claim) while keeping the concrete schema deferred.

- **The crux is `ctx`, not field names.** The in-process trait is `check(target, ctx) -> Vec<Finding>`. For a subprocess impl to satisfy the *same* trait with no engine change, **`ctx` must be effective-state the engine can serialize** — in-process and subprocess impls consume one logical input (a live graph vs its serialized projection). A `ctx` defined as a live in-memory handle would force a new engine mechanism when subprocess probes ship — exactly the foreclosure. So sketching the wire contract now *constrains the in-process trait's `ctx` design today*; that is the real value, beyond satisfying Codex.
- **Effective-state access falls out of the already-locked six rules.** No callbacks (rule 6) + no network (rule 1) ⇒ the engine **materializes a read-only effective-state snapshot** (rule 4 permits read-only fs) and the request carries a **path-ref**, not a live handle. No new constraint — a consequence of Pass 3 #11's rules.
- **Sketched, explicitly illustrative** ([validation.md](design/validation.md) → The wire contract): `request { probe_id, target, effective_state{snapshot_path}, config, schema_version }` / `response { findings[], schema_version }`. `severity?` advisory (engine owns final severity via cascade); `target` uses the one address grammar; `schema_version` present *as a field* now (the versioning *policy* stays deferred).
- **Still deferred** (honors Pass 3 #11): the concrete finding envelope, versioning policy, snapshot format, and OS sandboxing tech. Decision was **sketch-now** over keep-fully-deferred — the seam de-risks a forward-binding claim, same discipline as locking the determinism contract before implementation.
- **Doc placements**: [validation.md](design/validation.md) → Pack-probe determinism contract gains §"The wire contract" + the MVP-scope and open-question lines retargeted; [module-layout.md](implementation/module-layout.md) probe-boundary bullet + open question cross-ref the seam. The `pack-probe-integrity.*` severity category stays post-MVP (no inventory rows in MVP).

### Pass 5 complete

All six foreclosed options resolved: **#3** + **#5** by prior passes (F12; the `<!-- fields -->` sentinel); **#1** (definition front-matter YAML), **#2 + #6** (frontend-neutral boundaries — closes the parked Pass 3 **#14**), Area-1-row-22 (absent-section insertion), **#4** (wire seam) this pass. Remaining table-yellows are acknowledged opens that can't resolve pre-implementation (span-precision spike, startup-time-with-pack-discovery, subprocess batching/caching), not design gaps. **The Codex 2026-05-28 cross-model review (all five passes) is now closed.**

## 2026-05-30

### Work-unit intermediate layer named `increment` (renamed from `slice`)

Pre-planning vocabulary lock, so planning docs use a settled term. The layer between `milestone` and `task` — added as `slice` in the Pass 1 F2 work-unit-family decision (2026-05-28) but left under-specified — is now named and defined.

- **Meaning (confirmed)**: an **increment** is a *deliverable increment* — a coherent group of tasks that together deliver one vertical slice of a milestone's outcome; the unit `milestone-planning` decomposes a milestone into, and the rollup parent of its tasks. Hierarchy: `milestone > increment > task`.
- **Renamed `slice` → `increment` after a Codex cross-model naming review.** Two collisions drove the rename off the placeholder: `phase` (already finalize's 7-phase transaction + the override resolution's 9 phases + the GSD anti-pattern — triple-overloaded, never a work-unit name) and `slice` (collides with the pervasive read-path "doc-slice"/"context-slice" fragment term). `increment` is domain-neutral, collision-free, and **collapses name and meaning into one word** — it literally *is* the chosen "deliverable increment" role. Codex ranked it #1 (over segment / bundle / group / epic) and judged the `slice` collision not worth keeping since the work-unit usage was tiny vs the pervasive fragment term. Rejected: `slice` (collision), `phase` (overload), `segment`/`group` (less meaningful), `epic` (PM-jargon, implies larger scale).
- **Still planned/post-MVP** — MVP stays task-only; this is vocabulary, not new MVP scope. Whether the `increment` or the `task` is the one-level `fan-out` boundary is a deferred post-MVP question.
- **Doc placements**: [structural-grammar.md](design/structural-grammar.md) → Work-units (entry fleshed out with the meaning), [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md) (invariant list — also drops the erroneous `phases`), [write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md) (work-unit-family siblings). The 2026-05-28 Pass 1 F2 entry is annotated superseded-on-naming. Doc-fragment "slice" / "doc-slice" / "context-slice" usages are deliberately untouched — a different concept on the read path.

### `describe` self-description surface — direction locked, parked post-MVP

Direction locked: a self-contained, on-demand **prose self-description** of jigc's resolved surface (*what doc-types / workflows / commands this project has and how they're used*) — **facts-not-advice**, **usage-not-mechanism**, a **projection of the resolved definitions** assembling human-authored `description:`/`usage:` fields (no LLM call), and **non-contractual by design** (prose-shaped to stay un-dependable, which dissolves rather than negotiates the "not a public API" non-goal). Open thread: the authored description field — which definitions carry it, required vs optional, cascade replace-vs-append. Full shape in [ideas/describe.md](ideas/describe.md); indexed at [VISION.md](VISION.md#open-questions) → Cross-cutting.

**New convention (this entry establishes it):** shaped-but-unscheduled ideas park under **`ideas/`** (one descriptive file each) — the home for a direction too detailed for a one-line DECISIONS entry and too immature for a `design/` part-doc. They're indexed from VISION → Open questions → Cross-cutting, carry a terse pointer here when the direction is locked, and promote into `design/` when de-parked.

## 2026-05-31

### MVP roadmap — one milestone, six increments, risk-first

Build order locked in [implementation/roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md): the `single-task execution loop` milestone decomposes into six increments — foundations/orientation, the round-trip parser/writer (risk spike), compose, write+commit-only finalize, persisted ADR + edge index (the differentiator), adapter+ship. Ordered **risk-first** (spine → #1 risk in isolation → loop → differentiator → ship), strictly linear. Increment size deliberately uneven; the priority is a clean logical build order with focused, single-concern groupings. Tasks are cut per-increment at pickup, not enumerated up front. Pack content rides along where first needed, not as its own increment.

### Dev workflow (TDD) defined for the build

The implementation loop is captured in [implementation/dev-workflow.md](implementation/dev-workflow.md): a six-step test-first loop (scope → red → green → refactor → gate → commit) run per task within an increment, distinct from the design-collaboration loop. Written as prose-steps now; shaped as named ordered steps so it promotes into the jigc workflow dialect (`step:` files + `workflow:dev-task`) once jigc can compose. The loop deliberately mirrors jigc in miniature (gate≈validate, commit≈finalize) so dogfooding informs the real `single-task` workflow.

### CLAUDE.md flipped to implementation mode; design-collaboration loop extracted

CLAUDE.md updated for the implementation phase: "Project state" now reflects the cargo workspace + real build/lint/test commands; the **design-collaboration loop** is extracted to [implementation/design-workflow.md](implementation/design-workflow.md) (parallel to dev-workflow.md), with CLAUDE.md keeping a two-modes reference (design vs dev) plus the durable conventions verbatim. MVP scope left in place for now (removed post-MVP). The conventions stay in CLAUDE.md because they are cross-mode governance, not planning-only.

### Gating build crates picked: include_dir (embed) + serde_yaml_ng (config YAML)

Increment-1 gating decisions, both behind interfaces (embed behind the `PackSource` trait, YAML behind serde) so either is swappable.

- **Embed mechanism = `include_dir`** (closes the module-layout.md open question). Always-embedded → what you test is what ships; rejected `rust-embed` for its debug-disk/release-embed duality (dev/prod divergence, a determinism smell) despite its nicer iteration, and the build-script approach as needless boilerplate the crate already solves.
- **Config-family YAML = `serde_yaml_ng`** (the leaf-instance flat parser is separate, [parsing.md](implementation/parsing.md)). Maintained serde-native drop-in fork of the archived `serde_yaml`; rejected `serde_yaml` (unmaintained) and `serde_yml` (contested fork). Typed structs govern coercion, so YAML footguns do not bite.

Both declared in `[workspace.dependencies]`, consumed per-increment; exact versions pin at first `cargo add` in increment 1.

### Cross-cutting build crates: thiserror+anyhow, blake3, insta+proptest

Three increment-1/2 cross-cutting picks (shape many signatures, so settled up front; all reversible behind their seams). Captured forward-look for later increments in [implementation/decisions-pending.md](implementation/decisions-pending.md).

- **(I) Errors** = `thiserror` (engine, typed/exhaustive) + `anyhow` (cli, ergonomic context) — the standard split, fits the union-heavy domain.
- **(I) Hashing** = `blake3` — one fast modern algo for `file-state` + content-drift; no crypto-compat need, so chosen over `sha2`.
- **(I) Test tooling** = `insta` (snapshot/golden) + `proptest` (property/fuzz) — exactly what the [parsing.md](implementation/parsing.md) round-trip contract asks for.

Also established the **decision-backlog** convention: `implementation/decisions-pending.md` lists decisions each increment will force (tagged (D) design vs (I) impl), graduating to DECISIONS.md when made. Tasks still cut per-increment at pickup.

## 2026-05-31

### Address grammar parse/Display + id-hop newtypes (inc 1)

- **Fragment parse is structural by hop-count** — 1 hop = `unit`, 2 = `unit/leaf`, 3 = `unit/item/leaf`. `#unit/item` and `#unit/leaf` are byte-identical, so a schema-free parser yields `UnitLeaf`; the `UnitItem` variant exists in the typed model but is reachable only with schema knowledge. Both render to the same two-hop bytes, so round-trip holds either way.
- **Id hops are thin `String` newtypes** (`Type/Slug/Unit/Item/Leaf`, via a small macro) carrying raw token text verbatim — no normalization on the parse path (slug/minting normalization stays deferred to inc 3/4 per decisions-pending). `Display` writes the bytes back unchanged, giving byte-for-byte round-trip.

### Result envelope: `Orientation`/`Catalog`/`CatalogEntry` + `SCHEMA_VERSION` (inc 1)

- **`schema_version` is a root scalar marker on the result envelope** (`pub const SCHEMA_VERSION: u32 = 1`, stamped by `Orientation::new`), not a per-type or wrapper field — one bump point covers the whole projection, and a contract test pins the exact JSON shape so any rename is intentional.
- **`Catalog` is `#[serde(transparent)]` over `Vec<CatalogEntry>`** so it projects as a bare `workflows[]` array under `Orientation`; entry keys are the verbatim `id`/`when` from the bootstrap orientation example.

### `PackSource` provider trait + `PackResourceKind`/`ResourceId` (inc 1)

- **`PackSource` is a plain object-safe trait** (`pack_version`/`list`/`read`), no serde derives — it's the *input* provider boundary the engine resolves over, not a result type, so it stays off the versioned-API surface.
- **Sketch's `Version`/`Bytes`/`Result` ground out as `String`/`Vec<u8>`/`Result<_, PackError>`** — no new dep (no `semver`); `pack_version` carries the binary version verbatim and reconciliation parsing belongs to the override layer, not this seam. `ResourceId` is a thin `String` newtype (the `address` id-newtype style); `PackError::NotFound{kind,id}` is the only read failure mode.

### `EmbeddedPack` + embedded dev-pack layout (inc 1)

- **Embedded pack tree = `crates/cli/pack/`, one sub-dir per `PackResourceKind`** (`workflows/` `schemas/` `steps/` `config/`); a resource's `ResourceId` is its **file stem** (so `workflows/single-task.yaml` → id `single-task`). Embedded whole via `include_dir!("$CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR/pack")`; `list`/`read` resolve the kind→sub-dir then match/strip the stem. `pack_version` = `env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")` (built-in pack-default version = binary version).
- **Inc-1 ships a minimal `single-task.yaml` stub** (id + `when` only) so the catalog is non-empty and `read` returns non-empty bytes; the full workflow definition (steps/slots/`allows-create`) lands in inc 3. `EmbeddedPack` is `#[allow(dead_code)]` until the cascade-location task wires it into `main` (later in inc 1).

### Workflow-metadata catalog builder: parse `when` from front-matter (inc 1)

- **`engine::catalog::build_catalog(&dyn PackSource) -> Result<Catalog, CatalogError>`** derives the catalog: per `Workflows` resource it reads the bytes, slices the leading `---`-fenced front-matter, and `serde_yaml_ng`-parses *only* `when` (a `WorkflowFrontMatter { when: Option<String> }` — extra config-family keys like `allows-create`/`creates-task` are ignored, not rejected, so richer inc-3 defs don't break the catalog). Entry **id is the `ResourceId`** (filename stem), never a front-matter field — per workflow-dialect "the id is the filename". Order = the pack's `list` order.
- **Missing `when` (or absent/blank) is a hard, id-bearing `CatalogError`, not a silent skip** — a workflow earns its catalog place by declaring its hint, so a missing one is a definition bug. `CatalogError` (thiserror) also covers NotUtf8 / MissingFrontMatter / MalformedFrontMatter. Front-matter detection reads the canonical LF form (`---\n` … `\n---`), matching the writer's LF default.
- **`serde_yaml_ng` opted into `engine` deps** (pre-declared load-bearing crate; no new external dep). Note: the embedded `single-task.yaml` stub is still pure-YAML (no fences) from the inc-1 pack task; aligning it to the canonical fenced-front-matter form is left for the wire-up/inc-3 step — this task is engine-only (fake `PackSource`), so the stub mismatch is latent, not active.

### Cascade-layer location in `cli`: repo-root discovery + the three layer paths (inc 1)

- **`cli::locate::locate(&Path) -> anyhow::Result<RunContext>`** is the CLI-side locator (CLI locates, engine resolves). `RunContext { repo_root, project_config: Option<PathBuf>, team_config: PathBuf }`: repo root = first ancestor of the start dir holding a `.git` entry; **project** layer = `<repo_root>/.jigc/config/` as `Some` only when that dir exists (else `None` = absent); **team** layer = a *path only* (`~/.config/jigc/`) — presence is the engine's to check, so it is never `Option` here. `pack-default` is not a path (served in-binary by `EmbeddedPack`).
- **`cli` opted into `anyhow`** (the pre-declared cli error crate; thiserror=engine / anyhow=cli) — no typed `LocateError`; not-in-repo / `$HOME`-unset surface as `anyhow` messages. **`$HOME` env var resolves the home dir** (no `dirs`/`home` crate); `locate_from(start, home)` is the testable core (explicit home, temp-dir fixtures). `mod locate` is `#[allow(dead_code)]` until command dispatch wires it into `main` (later in inc 1).

### Cascade read-path resolution: phase-2 file shadowing + phase-3 scalar deltas (inc 1)

- **`engine::cascade::resolve(&PackDefaultLayer, Option<&OverrideLayer>, Option<&OverrideLayer>) -> Result<Resolved, CascadeError>`** is the engine resolver (CLI locates, engine resolves; feed-layers-in). Implements only phases 2 (by-id file shadowing, project > team > pack-default) + 3 (scalar deltas, base → team → project so project wins; within-layer manifest order). `PackDefaultLayer` is the **base** carrying the closed declared-key surface + base scalars + base file ids; `OverrideLayer` (team/project) carries `scalar_set` deltas + shadowed file ids + an optional `config_path`. **Closed knob surface enforced**: a `scalar-set` for an undeclared key → `CascadeError::UndeclaredScalar { layer, key }` (typed-value adjudication deferred to the field model / write path — scalar values are opaque `String` here).
- **`Resolved` exposes `scalar(key)` / `file_owner(id)` / `provenance()`; `Provenance::header()`** renders the engine-owned segments `Pack: <id>/<version>` then `· Project config: <path>` / `· Team config: <path>` only for populated external layers — the frontend appends `Branch:`/HEAD at render time. `resolve` is not yet wired into `main` (command dispatch lands later in inc 1).

### Renderers: agent-text + generic-JSON over result types (inc 1)

- **`cli::render` holds `json<T: Serialize>(&T) -> String`** (pretty, **generic** over `serde` — one fn over any engine result, no per-type code) and **`orientation_agent_text(&Orientation) -> String`** (per-type). Footer rides agent-text only; JSON carries none. **`pub const ROUTING_FOOTER`** is the verbatim `design/workflow-dialect.md` line; agent-text lists `id — when` per workflow (em-dash, matching the `bootstrap.md` orientation example) then the footer. **`cli` opted into `serde` + `serde_json`** (pre-declared crates; serde for the generic `Serialize` bound) + `insta` dev-dep for the inline snapshots. `mod render` is `#[allow(dead_code)]` until command dispatch wires it in (later in inc 1).

### `jigc` command tree skeleton + `--format` selector (inc 1)

- **`cli::cli` holds the clap command tree**: `Cli { format: Format, command: Command }` (derive `Parser`), a global `--format=agent|json|human` selector (`ValueEnum`, `default_value_t = Format::Agent` — the non-TTY agent path is the floor; TTY→human detection is presentation-time, not parse-time), and `Command::Start { intent: Option<String> }` (the front door; bare = orient, `<intent>` = compose). `Cli::dispatch(self)` matches `Start` to a **handler stub** — orient/compose body lands later. **`cli` opted into the pre-declared `clap` workspace dep** (no new external crate). `main` now `Cli::parse().dispatch()`s. Only `start` exists (no `--workflow`/`--task`/`doc`/`task` yet — minimal scope); 6 `cli_parse` tests cover the agent default, json/human selection, intent positional, unknown-value rejection, and `--help` clean.

### Bare `jigc start` orientation end-to-end: no-pack + clean-no-task (inc 1)

- **`cli::orient::orient(&Path) -> anyhow::Result<String>`** is the bare-`start` handler: locate → resolve cascade → build catalog → render, read-only (it only *reads* `.git` for repo discovery; no writes/compose/git). **State discriminator = `RunContext::project_config` presence** (the embedded pack always ships, so "unset" = no in-repo project layer): absent → the `Run: \`jigc setup\`` unset-project text; present → resolve the cascade and render the provenance header + catalog + footer.
- **The embedded dev pack's provenance id is the constant `DEV_PACK_ID = "dev"`** in `cli::orient` (→ `Pack: dev/<version>`); the pack has no `config/` layer declaring its own id until inc 6, and version comes from `EmbeddedPack::pack_version()`. The pack-default layer fed to `resolve` has empty scalars (no config dir yet) + the workflow ids as base files; the project layer carries only its `config_path` for the header.
- **Branch/HEAD + the `Recent: …` finalization line** from the `bootstrap.md` state-2 example are **deferred** — they need git-HEAD inspection and a task store, neither of which exists in inc 1. **Two render fns added** (`render::orientation_clean(header, &Orientation)` / `render::orientation_unset()`), inline-snapshot-locked; the prior leaner `orientation_agent_text` is superseded for orientation and `#[allow(dead_code)]`-retained (with `json`, awaiting `--format` wiring). `dispatch` now returns `ExitCode`; `Start { intent: Some(_) }` is a not-yet (inc 3).
- **Closed the latent `single-task.yaml` fence mismatch** (flagged since inc-1 seq 5): the embedded stub now opens with `---`-fenced front-matter carrying only `when` (id is the filename), so `build_catalog` reads it. Integration test (`crates/cli/tests/start_orientation.rs`) drives the built binary via `CARGO_BIN_EXE_jigc` over temp repos (no `assert_cmd`/`tempfile` — `std::process` + self-cleaning `TempDir`), asserting both states + read-only (no files created).

### `--format` wiring for orientation (inc 1)

- **`orient` now returns a structured `engine::result::OrientationView`, not a pre-rendered `String`** — a `#[serde(tag = "state")]` enum (`unset-project` | `clean { header, workflows }`), each stamping `SCHEMA_VERSION`. It's the versioned contract the renderer maps over (presentation stays in `cli::render`, engine stays presentation-free). **`render::orientation(Format, &OrientationView)`** is the one `Format → renderer` mapping: `agent`/`human` → the existing per-state agent-text (with `ROUTING_FOOTER`); `json` → generic `render::json` (serde, **no footer**). `run_orient(format)` threads `self.format` through; agent-text bytes for both states are byte-for-byte unchanged (insta snapshots untouched). MVP `human` renders identically to `agent` (TUI is post-MVP).

## Increment 2–6 design-gate pre-decisions (settled 2026-05-31)

Decided up front so increments 2–6 run autonomously without stopping to surface a genuine fork mid-run (the four `(D)` gates that needed a human call; the rest are doc-elaborations the executor pins+logs at pickup).

### Slug / minting normalization (cross-cutting; blocks inc 3/4 write path)

- **Frozen content-slugs are lowercase ASCII kebab-case.** Normalize the id-source: lowercase; spaces and `_` → `-`; **transliterate non-ASCII to ASCII** (e.g. `café`→`cafe`); strip to `[a-z0-9-]`; collapse repeated `-` and trim leading/trailing `-`; cap at ~50 chars truncated at a word (`-`) boundary; empty result falls back to the type name. **Collisions get a numeric suffix** `-2`, `-3`, … applied **deterministically in task-id merge order** (the only suffix site at task scope is the `fan-out`/`join` parallel case; serial collisions reject per write-commands.md). Numeric (not hash) for readability; the suffix is disambiguation, not ordering, so it doesn't violate "ids must not imply order". Closes structural-grammar.md → Open questions (minting mechanics).

### Git invocation at finalize (inc 4)

- **Shell out to the user's `git` binary** (not git2/gitoxide). Respects the user's hooks/config, matches "CLI orchestrates, git executes", keeps the pure-Rust no-C-dep cross-compile story, requires `git` on PATH (already assumed by the adapter). Closes decisions-pending inc-4 (D).

### Blocked / error payload shape (inc 4)

- **A block reuses the `finding` shape** — severity + located message + a `route` directing the agent's next action — the same envelope as validation findings and conformance diagnostics (validation.md → "one finding shape, two producers"). One channel the agent and renderers learn once; a hard block is a blocking-severity finding carrying a route, not a separate type. Closes decisions-pending inc-4 (D) and the write-commands.md / finalize.md open question on block payloads.

### Product name (inc 6)

- **Ship the MVP as `jigc`** — adopt the placeholder as the real name; no rename pass in inc 6. A later rename (if a better name emerges) is a mechanical find-replace, not a blocker. Resolves the `jigc` token placeholders across the docs for MVP purposes. Closes decisions-pending inc-6 (D, product name).

## 2026-05-31 — Increment 2 doc-elaboration pins (parser/writer)

Pinned at inc-2 pickup (the executor pins concrete forms + logs; settled gates above stay authoritative). All follow the locked `parsing.md` / `storage.md` / `document-type-schema.md`; this records the *concrete* byte/struct choices the round-trip golden tests freeze.

### Canonical byte form (frozen in golden tests)

- **Frozen by golden test, the exact serializer output** (`storage.md` → Anatomy, `parsing.md` → Field-group delineation / Round-trip guarantees): front-matter is a `---\n`-fenced flat `key: value` block (one field per line, schema order, no blank line between fences and fields), then a **single blank line**, then the `# H1` title; sections are `## <Heading>\n` followed by a blank line then slot prose; a body/item field group is `<slot prose>\n\n<!-- fields -->\n- key: value\n- key2: value2` — i.e. exactly one blank line before the sentinel, the sentinel on its own line, then a contiguous bullet list (`- ` prefix, single space), one field per line in schema order, **no** blank line between sentinel and list or between bullets. Repeatable items are `### <title>  {#id}\n` (two spaces before `{#id}`, matching the storage.md SPEC example) then a blank line then item body. Exactly one trailing newline (LF) at EOF; LF line endings for generated content. Sentinel emitted **only** when ≥1 declared field has a value. Why: one concrete form makes generated and conformant-human content converge, so the idempotent-on-canonical clause is byte-checkable.

### Round-trip canonicalization ledger (the modulo in the fuzz contract)

- **The only first-touch canonicalizations** (everything else preserved byte-for-byte per `parsing.md` → Round-trip guarantees): strip a leading BOM; normalize trailing newline to exactly one; never reflow prose or normalize interior blank lines. EOL is *preserved* (matched locally at edit sites), not normalized — so the no-op-write fuzz assertion is "byte-identical after BOM-strip + single-trailing-newline normalization," nothing else. Why: bounds the fuzz oracle precisely so "byte-identical modulo declared canonicalizations" is a decidable assertion.

### Finding shape (conformance diagnostics + block envelope)

- **`engine::finding::Finding { severity: Severity, code: String, message: String, location: Option<Location>, route: Option<String> }`** with `Severity { Blocking, Warning, Advisory }` and `Location { address: Option<String>, line: usize, col: usize }` — the one envelope used by conformance diagnostics (this increment), and later validation findings + hard blocks (the settled inc-4 block-payload gate). Inc-2 produces only conformance `Finding`s (severity `Blocking`, with `code` + located `message`; `route` typically `None` at parse scope). Why: "one finding shape, two producers" (`parsing.md` → Conformance diagnostics) — introduce it where the first producer (the parser) needs it.

### Schema model + commit/adr schema YAML

- **`commit` ships now, `adr` ships as a structural skeleton** (`document-type-schema.md` → On-disk definition format; roadmap inc-2 "commit (and skeleton adr)"). `commit` = a header section + prose slot(s), sink = git message (no `location:`/persisted). `adr` skeleton = the `status` header section (`status` enum, `date`, `supersedes` ref) + `context`/`decision`/`consequences` slots + `location: decisions/` + `id-from: title` — enough for the writer/parser to round-trip an ADR fixture; the `create`-gate + promotion wiring is inc-5. Schema YAML lives in the embedded pack under `pack/schemas/<type>.yaml`, loaded via `PackResourceKind::Schemas`. Why: inc-2 must round-trip both MVP doc-types over fixtures; the persisted ADR is what the #1-risk golden splice exercises.

### Slug normalization home

- **`engine::slug::slugify(id_source: &str) -> String`** implements the settled normalization (lowercase; spaces/`_`→`-`; transliterate non-ASCII to ASCII; strip to `[a-z0-9-]`; collapse/trim `-`; ~50-char cap at a `-` boundary; empty→type-name handled by the caller that knows the type). Collision-suffix (`-2`, `-3`) is the caller's concern at mint sites (inc 3/4); inc-2 needs the pure normalization for the generation path's `{#id}` minting and is golden+property-tested here (idempotent: `slugify(slugify(x)) == slugify(x)`; output always matches `^[a-z0-9-]*$`). Why: the writer's generation/`add-item` path mints item anchors from the id-source, so the normalization lands with its first consumer.

### Finding envelope — serde-projection picks (implements the pinned shape)

- **`engine::finding` realizes the pinned `Finding`/`Severity`/`Location` shape**, fixing the serde projection that became its stable JSON contract: `Severity` is `#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]` (`"blocking"`/`"warning"`/`"advisory"`); `Location.address` is `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]` so a positional diagnostic projects to exactly `{line,col}`; `Finding.route` is **kept** (projects as `null` when absent) so the block envelope always carries the key. `line`/`col` are 1-based. The golden pins `serde_json::to_string_pretty` (the field-*ordered* string), not a key-sorted value, so it locks field order too — `assert_json_snapshot` was rejected because it sorts keys and would misrepresent the projection order. Why: the parser (first producer, inc-2) needs the envelope, and its JSON projection is a renderer/JSON contract that must be pinned by attribute, not by derive accident.

### Slug normalization — impl picks (2026-05-31, inc-2)

- **Transliteration is a tiny hand-rolled ASCII fold, no new crate.** `engine::slug::transliterate` covers the common Latin-1 / Latin-Extended-A accented letters + ligatures (`café`→`cafe`, `Æ`→`ae`, `œ`→`oe`, `ß`→`ss`, …); any non-ASCII char outside the table is **dropped**, not romanized. Why: the slug is a legibility aid, not a faithful transliteration, so the settled `café`→`cafe` rule + ordinary Latin titles is all that's load-bearing — a transliteration dependency would be over-built (PRINCIPLES → keep it minimal; the brief's "prefer a tiny hand-rolled ASCII fold").
- **The ~50-char cap (`MAX_LEN = 50`) cuts at the last `-` ≤ cap; a single word longer than the cap is hard-cut at 50.** A trailing `-` the cut exposes is re-trimmed, so the output invariant (`^[a-z0-9-]*$`, no leading/trailing/doubled `-`) holds after capping. Pinned in the golden (`cap-at-boundary-50`, `cap-one-long-word`).
- **The golden is an *inline* `assert_snapshot!` over a labelled input→output table** (one line per clause), matching `finding`'s inline-snapshot style — no `cargo-insta` / file-snapshot dependency for accepting. Property tests (`proptest`, added as an engine dev-dep) pin idempotence (`slugify(slugify(x)) == slugify(x)`) and the charset/shape invariant over arbitrary strings.

### Schema model — representation picks (2026-05-31, inc-2)

- **`engine::schema::Schema { ty, location: Option, id_from: Option, sections: Vec<Section> }`** is the deserializable doc-type model (resolves decisions-pending inc-2 *(I) In-memory schema representation*). A `Section { id, header: bool, #[flatten] body: SectionBody }`; `SectionBody` is an **untagged** enum `Repeatable { repeatable } | Simple { slot: Option<Slot>, fields: Vec<Field> }` distinguished by which on-disk key is present (a header section is the `fields`-only `Simple` case). A repeatable item's `block` is a `Vec<Leaf>`, `Leaf` = untagged `Slot { id, slot } | Field`. A `Field { id, ty: FieldType, of, default, set, to, card, inverse, inverse_card }` carries the `ref` relation metadata inline (one field declaration, per document-type-schema.md → "Why one declaration, not two"). `FieldType` is an enum `enum|string|date|bool|int|ref|code-anchor` (`kebab-case`), so an undeclared type is a typed `SchemaError::Malformed`, never a panic. Why: the trichotomy projected onto deserializable types — the model the parser/writer consume; relation metadata travels *with* the field, not a parallel block.
- **`deny_unknown_fields` lives on the leaf structs (`Schema`/`Repeatable`/`Slot`/`Field`), not on `Section`.** serde forbids `deny_unknown_fields` alongside `#[serde(flatten)]`, so `Section` omits it (the flattened untagged `SectionBody` must consume the shape keys); the value-level guards on the leaves still reject typos/bad types. Why: catch schema mistakes as typed errors where serde permits the guard.
- **Schema model is `Serialize` and its `to_string_pretty` JSON is the pinned contract** (file-snapshot golden over the shipped `commit.yaml`/`adr.yaml`, accepted into `crates/engine/src/snapshots/`). Field-ordered (struct order), not key-sorted, so it locks the projection order — same stance as the `finding` envelope. Why: engine result types are the renderer/JSON contract.
- **MVP pack ships `commit` (transient, no `location:`, `id-from: subject`) + `adr` skeleton (`location: decisions/`, `id-from: title`, `status` enum `[proposed,accepted,superseded]` default `proposed`, `date set: on-create`, `supersedes` ref `to: adr` `card: "0..1"` `inverse: superseded-by`, three prose slots)** at `crates/cli/pack/schemas/<type>.yaml`, loaded via `engine::schema::load_schema(&[u8])`. Why: the two MVP doc-types the parser/writer round-trips over fixtures (DECISIONS 2026-05-31 → Schema model + commit/adr schema YAML).

### Flat field-block reader — representation picks (2026-05-31, inc-2)

- **`engine::field_block` is the one flat `key: value` reader for both locations** (storage.md → "One field grammar, two structural frames"; parsing.md → Front-matter / Field-group delineation). It operates on **bare** `key: value` lines: callers strip the structural frame first (front-matter strips `---` fences; a body field group strips the `<!-- fields -->` sentinel + `- ` bullet prefixes), so this module owns *only* the shared grammar. `parse(&str) -> Result<FieldBlock, FieldBlockError>` yields ordered `Field { key, value }`; `emit(&FieldBlock) -> String` is its byte-exact inverse. Why: "the field grammar is one" — a single reader, the frames are the callers' concern.
- **`Value = Scalar(String) | List(Vec<String>)` — values are opaque trimmed text, never coerced.** Scalar = the literal text after the first `:`, trimmed (so `no`/`2026-05-23`/`1.0`/`adr:single-node-cache` all stay strings — schema adjudicates; YAML's dynamic typing is forbidden). A top-level `[ … ]` value is the inline-flow `List` (cardinality > 1), elements comma-split + trimmed; `[]` → empty list. Split on the **first** `:` only (ref/code-anchor values carry `:`). Why: the determinism boundary applied to a value — the reader draws only the structural Scalar/List line; the schema types the content.
