- **`emit` canonical form: `key: value\n` (single space after colon), inline-flow `[a, b, c]` (comma-space), one line per field in physical order, trailing `\n` per line.** A line with no `:` or an unexpected blank line is a located `FieldBlockError { line, message }` (1-based, block-relative; promoted to a conformance `Finding` at the call site), never a panic. Golden (insta inline) pins the opaque-value parse + the canonical bytes; a proptest property pins `emit → parse → emit` byte-identical (the diff-clean flat-line guarantee, parsing.md → Round-trip clause 1). Why: one concrete byte form makes the round-trip byte-checkable; this is the field-block-granularity slice of the #1-risk contract.

### Block-structure read path — representation + pulldown-cmark picks (2026-05-31, inc-2)

- **`engine::parse::parse_sections(&Schema, &str) -> Result<Document, Vec<Finding>>` is the schema-driven block read path** (parsing.md → The parse model; storage.md → Schema-driven parse). It returns *either* a `Document { sections: Vec<ParsedSection> }` *or* the collected conformance `Finding`s — "conformance falls out of the parser, no second pass." `ParsedSection { id, slot: Option<Span> }`; `Span { start, end, start_line }` is a half-open byte range whose `slice(src)` re-slices to the **opaque** slot prose verbatim. Result types are `Serialize` (the renderer/JSON contract stance). Why: the read half of the #1-risk round-trip — locate structure, record slot prose as opaque bytes the parser never interprets.
- **(I) pulldown-cmark handling settled for the read path** (resolves decisions-pending inc-2 *(I) pulldown-cmark handling*): `Parser::new_ext` with `ENABLE_HEADING_ATTRIBUTES` + `ENABLE_YAML_STYLE_METADATA_BLOCKS`, walked via `into_offset_iter()` for **byte** ranges. The `---` front-matter is the `MetadataBlock` event (bounds the header section); a section is an `H2` heading event (the `# H1` is the *title* per storage.md → Identity, excluded; `####`+ are allowed slot-internal structure, also excluded from section matching); a slot span runs from a heading's end to the next `##`/`<!-- fields -->` sentinel/EOF, blank-trimmed. The empirical span-precision spike (parsing.md → Open questions) is now exercised by an ADR fixture + a proptest property — pulldown-cmark's offsets are exact enough; no `tree-sitter-markdown` fallback needed. Why: a real block parse, not a line scanner, so a fenced ` ```\n## x\n``` ` stays opaque prose, never mis-read as a heading.
- **Slot heading-depth ceiling enforced at read time over the located span**: an ATX `##`/`###` (detected by `HeadingLevel` H2/H3) or any Setext heading (a heading event whose source range does not start with `#`) inside a slot span is a located `Severity::Blocking` `Finding` (`conformance.slot-heading-depth` / `conformance.slot-setext-heading`), never an instance (parsing.md → Slot heading-depth ceiling, enforcement site 2). Why: `##`/`###` are CLI-owned structural markers; allowing them in prose re-introduces the content-sniff the determinism boundary exists to prevent.
- **Scope = simple sections (header + body slot); a `repeatable` section is an explicit `conformance.repeatable-unsupported` finding, not a mis-parse.** `###` item parsing, `{#id}` anchor reads, front-matter *field* parsing, and the surgical-splice writer are separate later inc-2 tasks; the MVP `commit`/`adr` schemas use only simple sections, so the read path is complete for them. Why: keep the task to the one concern (block structure → sections + slot spans) without building untested item machinery no fixture exercises.

### Read path: front-matter, field-groups, `{#id}` items — picks (2026-05-31, inc-2)

- **`ParsedSection` grows `fields: Vec<field_block::Field>` + `items: Vec<ParsedItem>`; a new `ParsedItem { id, title, slot: Option<Span>, fields }`** (supersedes the prior task's `repeatable-unsupported` stub — repeatable sections now parse). Front-matter fields map to the header section; a simple body section's sentinelled bullet group maps to its `fields`; a `repeatable` section maps to `items`. `field_block::{Field, Value}` gained `Serialize`/`Deserialize` so the parse result stays the renderer/JSON contract. New `serde(default, skip_serializing_if)` on the added fields keeps the existing slot-only section projection byte-stable. Why: complete the read structure (block + field + item) the writer/validation consume; one field grammar reused at both field locations.
- **Field-group detection is sentinel + *immediately following* bullet list, never content-sniffing** (parsing.md → Field-group delineation): a `<!-- fields -->` HTML-comment block followed (only blank-line whitespace between) by a top-level `List`; each `- key: value` item is de-bulleted and read through `field_block::parse`. Bullets with **no** sentinel stay inside the slot prose span (the `- status: TBD` case) — golden-pinned. A sentinel with no following list is `conformance.orphaned-sentinel`; a bullet key not declared by the section is `conformance.unknown-field` (with a cheap one-edit "did you mean"). Why: the parser identifies structure by CLI-controlled markers, closing the prose-to-field reclassification leak (Pass 3 #1+#2).
- **`{#id}` anchors are re-scanned from the raw `###` heading source and validated as canonical slugs (`slug::slugify(id) == id`), not trusted from pulldown's lenient anchor recovery.** pulldown silently truncates `{#Bad Id}` → `Bad`; re-scanning the literal `{#…}` catches `missing` (absent or empty `{#}` → `conformance.item-anchor-missing`), `malformed` (not a clean slug → `conformance.item-anchor-malformed`), and `duplicate` (per repeatable section → `conformance.item-anchor-duplicate`). The id-source field is the heading text (title), never a trailing bullet, so item field-keys exclude `repeatable.id_from`. Why: CLI owns ids; a malformed anchor must surface, not be silently reconstructed (strict-MVP no auto-repair).
- **Item body span runs heading-end → the item's field-group sentinel or the next `###`/section end; the section region ends at the next `##`.** A repeatable section's `###` items partition its region; each item's slot prose is opaque-trimmed like a section slot, and the heading-depth ceiling applies inside it. The done-criterion `cargo test -p engine parse::fields` passes: front-matter + supersedes ref + criteria-style items with `{#id}` and per-item fields, plus one located-`Finding` golden per outcomes-table blocking case. Property (proptest): N conformant items → count + frozen `{#id}`s preserved, field values verbatim (the read-side #1-risk round-trip at item granularity). Why: read structure by markers, field/slot values verbatim — the determinism boundary at item scope.

### Canonical writer — parser-inverse, frozen byte form (2026-05-31, inc-2)

- **`engine::write::render(&Schema, &Instance) -> String` is the canonical writer** (parser-inverse; parsing.md → The write pipeline / Round-trip clause 1). Input is an owned in-memory `Instance { title, sections: Vec<SectionContent> }` (the write-side mirror of `parse::Document` — slot prose is owned `Option<String>` bytes, not a `Span`, since the writer *generates*); `SectionContent { id, slot, fields, items }`, `ItemContent { id, title, slot, fields }`, both `Default` + `Serialize`/`Deserialize` (the renderer/JSON-contract stance). It walks the schema sections in document order, matching instance content by id, and emits the frozen byte form. Why: the generate half of the #1-risk round-trip lands with its first consumers (`create`/`add-item`/`--template`/the commit string-sink).
- **The frozen canonical byte form is now golden-locked exactly** (`DECISIONS.md` 2026-05-31 → Canonical byte form, resolving decisions-pending inc-2 *(D) Exact canonical byte form*): `---\n`-fenced flat front-matter (one `key: value` per line, schema order, no blank line inside the fences) → one blank line → `# <title>` → one blank line → `## <Heading>` (the section id Title-Cased per `-` word; the parser matches headings case-insensitively so the round-trip holds) → blank line → opaque slot prose verbatim → an optional `\n\n<!-- fields -->\n` + contiguous `- key: value` bullets (sentinel emitted **iff** ≥1 field) → repeatable items `### <title>  {#id}` (two-space anchor gap) blank-line-separated → exactly one trailing `\n`. Field lines reuse `field_block::emit` (the one grammar). Slot prose is emitted verbatim (`trim_end`-only, never reflowed) — the determinism boundary on the write side. Goldens pin the exact bytes for a `commit`, an `adr`, and a repeatable `spec` instance; a proptest pins `write → parse → write` byte-identical over arbitrary schema-valid ADR instances + sentinel-iff-fields. Why: one concrete byte form makes generated and conformant-human content converge, so idempotent-on-canonical is byte-checkable — the #1 risk retired on the write side.

### Surgical splice — edit-in-place for present targets (2026-05-31, inc-2)

- **The surgical-splice edit path lives in `engine::write` (test path `write::splice`), beside the canonical writer** — `write.rs` is "the write path"; the writer is its *generate* half, the splice its *edit* half. Public surface: `splice(source, span, replacement) -> String` (the pure byte primitive: prefix + replacement + suffix, nothing outside the span touched) and the three present-target verbs `set_slot` / `set_field` / `remove_item`, each `-> Result<String, SpliceError>`. `SpliceError { NotPresent { what }, NotConformant }`: a target that is **absent** is `NotPresent` (the caller routes to the *generation* path — not this module's concern); a non-conformant buffer is `NotConformant` (no span can be located). Why: the load-bearing guarantee (parsing.md → The write pipeline) is "splice to edit, generate only new" — an in-place edit must touch *only* the located target's byte span so a conformant-but-differently-spaced file stays byte-for-byte intact everywhere else.
- **Target spans are located over the parser's real CommonMark block parse, never a line scanner** — `parse::scan_blocks` + `parse::Block` are now `pub(crate)` so the splice locators reuse the same trustworthy block model. `set_slot` splices the parser's recorded opaque slot `Span`; `set_field` locates the field's **value** bytes only (after `key:` + its separating space, to line-end — key/colon/spacing preserved) in front-matter or a sentinelled body bullet; `remove_item` deletes the item's whole block (its `### …{#id}` heading through the next `###`/`##` boundary, trailing blank line included, so survivors stay canonically spaced). Why: a `###`/`key:`-looking line *inside* fenced slot prose must never be mistaken for a target — the determinism boundary applied to location.
- **Done-criterion `cargo test -p engine write::splice` passes (9 tests).** Goldens (insta): `set_field` of `status` on a canonical ADR — full-bytes snapshot + a line-diff assertion that *only* the `status:` line differs; `set_slot` of `decision`; `remove_item` of the first of two items. A non-canonically-spaced-but-conformant input (two spaces after `date:`, a double blank line) keeps every unedited byte identical when one field changes. Property (proptest): parse an arbitrary conformant ADR → splice one field/slot → the byte-diff is confined to exactly the located span (prefix + suffix byte-identical in the result). The #1-risk round-trip clause 2 (surgical-on-edit) is now retired alongside clause 1 (idempotent-on-canonical, the prior writer task). Why: byte-stability is a *writer property* proven by golden + property tests, not assertion (parsing.md → Round-trip guarantees: tested, not asserted).

### Generation-and-insert — absent structural homes at their schema-ordered position (2026-05-31, inc-2)

- **The generation-and-insert write mode lives in `engine::write` (test path `write::generate`), beside the canonical writer + surgical splice** — the third write path: when a write's structural home is **absent** there is no span to splice, so we *generate* the canonical bytes and *insert* them at the deterministic schema-document-order position (parsing.md → The write pipeline → "Absent structural homes generate at their schema-ordered position"). Public surface: `generate_section(schema, source, section_id, slot, fields)` (materialize an absent body `##` section home + write its slot/fields), `add_item(schema, source, section_id, title, slot, fields)` (mint a `{#id}` from the title via `slug::slugify`, generate the `### <title>  {#id}` item block, append after the section's present items — materializing the section's own absent `##` home first if needed), and `insert_field(schema, source, section_id, field)` (insert an absent field's bullet, materializing the `<!-- fields -->` sentinel **once** if the section has no field group yet, else appending a bullet beneath the present ones), each `-> Result<String, GenerateError>`. `GenerateError { AlreadyPresent { what }, UnknownSection { id }, WrongShape { what } }`: a target already present is `AlreadyPresent` (the caller routes back to the surgical splice path); a minted item id colliding with a present item is also `AlreadyPresent` (serial collisions reject per write-commands.md; the numeric `-2` suffix is the post-MVP fan-out/join case only). Why: "splice to edit, generate only new" (parsing.md) needs a generation path for absent homes; lifting the absent-leaf case to absent sections keeps one component.
- **The insertion point is computed from schema document-order over the *present* `##` homes, not a conformant parse** — `present_body_sections` scans `parse::scan_blocks` for `## H2` headings matching a schema body-section id (case-insensitive) without requiring a clean parse (the target section is, by construction, absent). The generated `##` home is inserted at the heading start of the nearest *following* present section (the first present section whose schema index exceeds the target's), else appended at EOF; either way the present sections stay in schema order. Generation scope is the **structural home only** — the heading + the one leaf being written; required-but-still-absent sibling sections remain a `finalize` concern, so a partial doc need not fully parse after one generation. Why: schema order = physical order (storage.md); the parser blocks on missing required sections, so the insertion-point logic reads present homes directly.
- **Done-criterion `cargo test -p engine write::generate` passes (10 tests).** Goldens (insta), each pinning the exact frozen byte form: `set-slot` into an absent middle ADR section (`## Decision` materialized between present `## Context`/`## Consequences`), an absent *trailing* section (appended at EOF), `add-item` (title `Add a rate limiter` → minted `{#add-a-rate-limiter}`, two-space anchor gap, per-item sentinel + bullet, appended after the present item), and a first `set-field` into a fieldless section (`<!-- fields -->` + first bullet emitted once). `AlreadyPresent` route tests for a present section / colliding item id / present field; an append-bullet-to-existing-group test (sentinel reused, not re-emitted). Property (proptest): for an arbitrary subset of the three ADR body sections present, generating one absent section keeps the present `##` homes in schema document-order (and the fully-generated 3/3 case re-parses conformantly). The generated output is the canonical writer's byte form, so generated + conformant-human content converge. Why: the generation half of the #1-risk round-trip is a *writer property* proven by golden + property tests.

### Validate-after-write — re-parse + only-intended-target gate (2026-05-31, inc-2)

- **The local safety gate lives in `engine::write` (test path `write::validate_after`)** — `validate_after(schema, source, edited, target) -> Result<(), Finding>` re-parses the edited buffer and asserts (a) it parses against the schema (else a blocking `write.non-reparseable` finding) and (b) the byte-diff from `source` is confined to exactly the located `target` span — prefix `source[..target.start]` + suffix `source[target.end..]` must survive intact at the head/tail of `edited` (else `write.target-escape`). On any anomaly it returns a blocking `Finding` and the caller persists **nothing** (`parsing.md` → Validate-after-write: "never persist a file we can't re-parse"). The surgical-on-edit contract is thus *re-verified* after the write, not trusted from the splice primitive. Why: the write-time local adjudication (`write-commands.md` → Two check times) is distinct from the `finalize` validation engine; abort-before-persist is the load-bearing safety property.
- **`set_field_validated(schema, source, section_id, field_key, &Value) -> Result<String, Finding>` is the gated set-field** — the full local adjudication: (c) type-check the new value *before* touching bytes via `check_value`, locate the target value span, splice, then run `validate_after` (a + b). Returns the new buffer or a blocking `Finding` (codes `write.malformed-value` / `write.unknown-field` / `write.not-present` / `write.non-reparseable` / `write.target-escape`). An absent field at this gate is an abort (the caller asked to *edit* a present field; routing absence to generation is the caller's concern). Why: the done-criterion path (`cargo test -p engine write::validate_after`, 8 tests) — a valid set-field re-parses + returns the buffer; a malformed enum/date aborts with a located finding and no buffer.
- **`check_value(field, &Value) -> Result<(), String>` is the write-time value type-check** (the local adjudication the parser deliberately skips — it reads values opaque): `enum` → declared member of `of`; `date` → ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` via a hand-rolled `is_iso_date` (month `01..=12`, day `01..=31`; no `chrono` dep — the canonical-date authority is the CLI `set: on-create` deriver, this only rejects obvious malformations); `bool` → `true`/`false`; `int` → parses as `i64`; `string`/`ref`/`code-anchor` → any non-empty opaque value (full ref resolution is a `finalize`/edge-index concern, the `code-anchor` adjudicator ships in a pack). A `List` is checked element-wise. Why: "a `set-field` rejects a malformed date or a non-member enum *now*" (`write-commands.md`) needs a typed value check; the engine-native types are checked here, pack/finalize concerns are not.

### Round-trip risk-spike capstone — the no-op-write + surgical-edit fuzz suite (2026-05-31, inc-2)

- **`engine::write::first_touch_canonicalize(source: &str) -> String` is the "no-op write" — the only first-touch canonicalization** (`parsing.md` → Round-trip guarantees; `DECISIONS.md` 2026-05-31 → canonicalization ledger): strip a single leading BOM (`\u{feff}`); normalize the trailing newline to exactly one **EOL-preserving** unit (a CRLF file ends in one `\r\n`, an LF file in one `\n`); everything else preserved byte-for-byte (EOL never globally normalized, prose never reflowed). An empty/whitespace-only body stays empty. This is the operation the CLI applies when it first copies an on-disk doc into the task working area — *not* `render` (which regenerates to LF canonical form); the no-op write leaves a conformant-but-differently-spaced human file byte-identical except for the two ledger items. Why: the no-op-write fuzz needs a real, decidable oracle — "byte-identical modulo BOM-strip + single-trailing-newline" — and the read path already proved the BOM breaks front-matter detection, so first-touch must strip it.
- **The dedicated `#[cfg(test)] mod roundtrip` in `write.rs` is the #1-risk-retiring capstone** (done-criterion `cargo test -p engine roundtrip`, 3 tests). A generator (`arb_doc`) mints arbitrary conformant `commit`/`adr` docs — canonical front-matter + opaque slot prose carrying the edge cases real prose holds (a fenced ` ``` ` block whose body is `## not a heading`, a `- x:` prose line with no sentinel, a `#### deeper` heading, extra interior blank lines), with a chosen EOL (LF or CRLF). Two proptests + one insta golden: (1) **no-op write idempotent modulo the ledger** — parse-confirm → add BOM + extra trailing newlines → `first_touch_canonicalize` reproduces the canonical-EOL doc exactly, EOL preserved, re-parses; (2) **surgical on edits** — one `set_field` of a present front-matter field changes exactly one line (the target value), every other line byte-identical, EOL preserved (no-op edits where the value already equals the target are skipped, not asserted); (3) a golden pinning one tricky CRLF+BOM fixture end to end. Why: the round-trip contract is "tested, not asserted" (`parsing.md`) — this is the artifact that converts the #1 risk from *mitigated* to *retired* over generated arbitrary input, both clauses, both MVP doc-types.

### Real single-task pack content — the canonical embedded bytes (2026-05-31, inc-3)

- **The MVP `single-task` definition now ships full** (`crates/cli/pack/workflows/single-task.yaml`), replacing the inc-1 stub: config-family front-matter `when:` (kept the spec-less inc-1 phrasing `implement one scoped change end-to-end` — the design doc's "against an existing spec" example wording contradicts CLAUDE.md → MVP scope's spec-less `single-task`, and changing it would break the inc-1 catalog/orientation tests for no scope reason), `creates-task: true`, `allows-create: [{type: adr, as: decision}]` (the create-gate binding the agent-created ADR to `task.decision`, per write-commands.md → The create-gate), and an **include-only body** of exactly the four ordered lines `{{ include: step:locate }}` / `implement` / `superseded-context` / `finalize` (workflow-dialect.md → On-disk definition format; body-include-only is a compose-time `workflow-refs` rule). The catalog parser ignores the two new front-matter fields (no `deny_unknown_fields`), so inc-1 orientation stays green.
- **Four step files ship under `crates/cli/pack/steps/*.yaml`** (`locate`, `implement`, `superseded-context`, `finalize`) — id = filename stem; body = prompt prose with `{{…}}` placeholders drawn from worked-examples.md flows #1/#5: `locate` embeds `{{ @task.intent }}` (the engine-native task data-value root, MVP spec-less "what"); `implement` carries the `{{ cli.set-commit-summary }}` command-ref, a `<<author: {{ task.commit#summary }}>>` slot-author directive, and `{{ cli.create-adr }}`; `superseded-context` carries the always-present `{{ @task.decision.supersedes#decision }}` slice (empty-text in the common case); `finalize` carries `{{ cli.finalize-task }}`. Bytes frozen by insta inline goldens in `pack.rs` (no serializer — the file *is* the contract).
- **`config/defaults.yaml` ships the `default-workflow: single-task` cascade knob** (CLAUDE.md → MVP scope: default the work-workflow directly until ≥2 work-workflows exist) and **`config/commands.yaml` ships the command catalog** verbatim from command-catalog.md → Worked catalog (`set-commit-summary`, `validate-task`, `finalize-task`, `create-adr`). Both surface via `PackSource::list(Config)` (ids `defaults` + `commands`) — `commands.yaml` rides in `config/` because `PackResourceKind` has no `Commands` variant and a catalog is config-family YAML. **Canonical pack byte form: LF line endings, one trailing newline, definitions `---`-fenced.** No new code paths — the composer/catalog-parser that consume these arrive in later inc-3 tasks.

### Workflow-definition loader — `WorkflowDef` + include-only body (2026-05-31, inc-3 seq 2)

- **`engine::compose::load_workflow_def(&[u8]) -> Result<WorkflowDef, Finding>`** is the phase-1 input-gathering read of one workflow definition file (overrides.md → Resolution algorithm, phase 1): slices the `---`-fenced config-family-YAML front-matter + body over the **same `front_matter` slicer as the catalog** (bumped to `pub(crate)`, one fence recognizer reused, not duplicated), parses `when` (empty→`None`), `creates-task` (**serde `default = true`** when the key is omitted, per workflow-dialect.md → On-disk format), and `allows-create: [{type, as}]` into `AllowsCreate { doc_type, as_role }` (serde-renamed `type`/`as`); reads the **include-only body** into an ordered `Vec<String>` of step ids. `WorkflowDef`/`AllowsCreate` are `Serialize+Deserialize` engine result types (the renderer/JSON contract stance); the inline insta golden pins the projection (`creates_task`, `allows_create[].type`/`.as`, `includes[]`).
- **Body include-only contract:** each non-blank, non-HTML-comment top-level line must be `{{ include: step:<id> }}` (whitespace-tolerant inside the braces; `<id>` non-empty, no internal whitespace); blank lines and `<!-- … -->` lines drop. Any other line — prose, an ATX heading, a non-include placeholder like `{{ task.intent }}` — is rejected as a **blocking `Finding` `code = "workflow-refs.body-include-only"`** located at the offending source line (the settled block-payload envelope — a blocking finding, not a new error type; DECISIONS.md 2026-05-31 → block payload). This is the compile-time precursor to the `workflow-refs.body-include-only` validation rule. Malformed UTF-8 / missing front-matter / malformed YAML surface as sibling `workflow-refs.*` blocking findings.
- **The single-task done-criterion test uses an in-test literal of the shipped bytes** (verified byte-identical to `crates/cli/pack/workflows/single-task.yaml`), not a read of the cli pack — the engine stays empty of domain content (same boundary the catalog tests respect).

### Step-definition loader — `StepDef` + verbatim body split (2026-05-31, inc-3 seq 3)

- **`engine::compose::load_step_def(id, &[u8]) -> Result<StepDef, Finding>`** reads one step file into `StepDef { id, body }` (`Serialize+Deserialize` engine result type). The **`id` is the caller-held resource id** (the pack's filename stem, never read from the file — workflow-dialect.md → On-disk format: "the id is the filename"); the **`body` is the verbatim prompt**, carried byte-for-byte: a file opening with a `---`-fenced front-matter block yields the **post-fence remainder** (over the same `front_matter` slicer the catalog/workflow-loader share — one fence recognizer), and a **plain step with no fence yields the whole file** unchanged (workflow-dialect.md: a plain step needs no front-matter). Only non-UTF-8 fails (blocking `workflow-refs.not-utf8` finding).
- **Body-verbatim is the property under test:** named golden `step_def_loads_body_verbatim_with_optional_front_matter` (insta inline over the shipped `locate`/`superseded-context` plain bodies + a fenced case) plus two proptests — body bytes survive load unchanged for arbitrary fence-free UTF-8, and equal the post-fence remainder when a front-matter block precedes them. The step's **front-matter is deliberately NOT parsed here** — this task's single concern is the id+body split; a step kind's config (e.g. `fan-out`) is consumed when that kind lands.

### Data-value path grammar — `data_value::Path` parser (2026-05-31, inc-3 seq 4)

- **`engine::data_value::Path::parse(&str) -> Result<Path, ParseError>`** parses the data-value path grammar (`"@"? head ( "." relation )* ( "#" fragment )?`, workflow-dialect.md → Data-values are full graph navigation) into `Path { marker: bool, head: Head, hops: Vec<Relation>, fragment: Option<Fragment> }` (`Serialize+Deserialize`). `marker` records the `@` (content-deref) vs bare (address) distinction; `head` is `Head::Root(String)` for a live-state root or `Head::Doc(Type, Slug)` for a literal `type:name`; `hops` are the ordered `.relation` edges (`Relation` newtype). **Parse-only — no live-state resolution** (scalar/address/content/collection classification is composition's job, not the parser's).
- **The `#fragment` reuses the one `address::Fragment` recognizer** — extracted `Fragment::parse(&str) -> Result<Fragment, ParseError>` from the inline body of `Address::parse` (no behaviour change; `Address::parse` now calls it) so the addressing grammar and the data-value fragment never drift. `data_value::ParseError::Fragment` wraps `address::ParseError` via `#[from]`.
- **Round-trip is the property under test:** named golden `data_value_path_parses_every_form` (insta inline, labelled input→`Debug` over the five canonical forms; asserts the `@` marker + ordered hops are recorded) + a proptest that `Display(parse(s)) == s` for generated valid paths (optional `@`, root or `type:name` head, 0..4 hops, optional 1..3-hop fragment) + negative cases (`task.`→`EmptyHop`, `@`/`#summary`→`EmptyHead`, `task#`→wrapped `EmptyFragment`, `task#a/b/c/d`→wrapped `TooManyHops`, `:billing`/`prd:`→`EmptyHeadType`/`EmptyHeadSlug`).

### Data-value resolver — `Path::resolve` against live state (2026-05-31, inc-3 seq 5)

- **`engine::data_value::Path::resolve(&self, &ComposeContext) -> Result<Resolution, Finding>`** resolves a parsed path against the live-state feed — a **pure function of `(path, ctx)`** (the determinism boundary; no I/O, no clock, no LLM). MVP carries the one engine-native root, `task`: `ComposeContext { task: TaskRoot { intent: String, roles: BTreeMap<String, Option<Address>> } }`. A role **present** in `roles` is *declared* (structurally valid); `Some(addr)` = bound, `None` = declared-but-unbound — this map is the declared/undeclared boundary that splits *absent* from *structural error* (workflow-dialect.md → Empty vs unresolvable).
- **`Resolution` is the four emitted classes** (`Serialize+Deserialize`, internally-tagged `#[serde(tag="kind", rename_all="kebab-case")]`, struct variants so internal tagging is well-formed): `Scalar{value}` / `Address{address}` / `Content{address}` / `Absent`. `task.intent`→`Scalar`; bare declared+bound role→`Address` (role binding + the path's own `#fragment`); `@`-marked→`Content`; declared+unbound role→`Absent` (empty text, short-circuiting *before* any further `.relation` hops). The byte-read behind `Content` lands when the store is wired — MVP carries the dereferenced address as the content handle.
- **Structural errors are blocking `workflow-refs.*` findings** (the one finding envelope, not a new type): `@` on the `intent` scalar → `at-marker-on-non-scalar` (validation.md → Severity inventory, intrinsic blocking); an undeclared root → `undeclared-root`; an undeclared role under `task` → `undeclared-role`; a literal `type:name` head (committed-store read, needs the unwired edge index) → `unresolved` at MVP scope.
- **Test:** named `data_value_resolution_cases` (Scalar / Address / Absent / undeclared-root-Err / @-on-scalar-Err) + the unbound-Absent-vs-undeclared-Err split + `@`-deref-to-Content + an insta golden over the four `Resolution` JSON variants + a proptest that the same `(path, ctx)` resolves identically twice (determinism). RED confirmed by mutating the unbound branch to an error and watching `data_value_resolution_cases` fail for the right reason, then reverting.
- **decisions-pending.md `(I) Data-value path resolver + command-catalog shell-quoting` left in place** — only the resolver half landed here; the command-catalog shell-quoting half is still open.

### Command catalog loader — `load_command_catalog` keyed by id (2026-05-31, inc-3 seq 6)

- **`engine::compose::load_command_catalog(&[u8]) -> Result<CommandCatalog, Finding>`** parses `commands.yaml` (config-family YAML, `commands:` list) into `CommandCatalog { commands: BTreeMap<String, CommandRef> }` — keyed by id, **`BTreeMap` for deterministic id-ordered serde** (the composition-is-deterministic invariant). `CommandRef { command, args: Vec<CommandArg>, stdin: Option, hint }`; id is the map key, not a field.
- **`CommandArg` is the three arg kinds** (`Serialize`-only, internally-tagged `#[serde(tag="kind", rename_all="kebab-case")]`, **struct variants** — `Literal{literal}` so internal tagging is well-formed, not a newtype variant): `Literal{literal}` (plain string) / `From{from}` (data-value-path carried **verbatim** — parse/resolve is composition's job, load-only here) / `Agent{agent,hint}` (hint **required, non-empty**). Load-only, no `Deserialize` derive: args are deserialized through `serde_yaml_ng::Value` and hand-routed so an **unknown map shape is rejected** (serde's untagged/derive would silently mis-route).
- **Malformed args + duplicate ids are blocking `workflow-refs.*` findings** (the one finding envelope, not a new type): `malformed-command-arg` (unknown key, `from`/`agent` not a string, missing/empty `hint`), `duplicate-command-id`, `malformed-command-catalog` (YAML), `not-utf8`.
- **Test:** named `command_catalog_loads_three_arg_kinds` (insta golden over the loaded catalog serde projection of the shipped `commands.yaml` — all four refs present, each arg kind distinguished) + negatives (`agent:` empty-hint → Err, unknown arg key → Err, both asserting the typed code) + a byte-identity check of the in-test `include_bytes!` const vs the shipped pack file. RED confirmed: golden mismatch on the real load, then a mutation of the empty-hint guard to a no-op failed the negative for the right reason; reverted.
- **decisions-pending.md `(I) … command-catalog shell-quoting` left in place** — this task is the *loader*; shell-safe rendering (command-catalog.md → Shell-safe rendering) is still open.

### Command-ref shell-safe renderer — `render_command` + `shell_quote` (2026-05-31, inc-3 seq 7)

- **`engine::compose::render_command(&CommandRef, &ComposeContext) -> Result<String, Finding>`** renders one resolved command-ref to the literal command line **without** the `Run:` wrapper (the emitter adds that — workflow-dialect.md → Emitted format). Per-arg: `Literal` → its token; `From{from}` → parse the data-value `Path` + resolve via the seq-5 resolver, render `Scalar→value` / `Address|Content→address.to_string()` / `Absent→""`; `Agent{agent}` → an uppercase `<NAME>` marker. Tokens join by single spaces. **Pure function of `(cmd, ctx)`** — same in → same line (determinism boundary).
- **`shell_quote(&str) -> String` is the per-arg POSIX rule** (command-catalog.md → Shell-safe rendering): a non-empty arg whose every char is the bare charset `[A-Za-z0-9._:#/=@+-]` stays bare; anything else (whitespace, metachar, or empty) is single-quoted with an embedded `'` closed-escaped-reopened (`'it'\''s'`). The empty string renders as `''`.
- **The `<NAME>` agent marker bypasses `shell_quote` and is emitted bare** — `<`/`>` are deliberately *not* in the bare charset (they are shell metachars), so the marker is kept bare by `render_arg` emitting it directly, matching command-catalog.md ("the `<NAME>` agent marker is bare") without polluting the address/literal quoting rule.
- **`task.id` added as an engine-native scalar** alongside `task.intent` (`TaskRoot.id`, resolver case `id → Scalar`, `@task.id` → `at-marker-on-non-scalar`): the four shipped command-refs include `finalize-task`/`validate-task` whose `from: task.id` the renderer must resolve (command-catalog.md → "`task.id` for a scalar"; worked-examples.md → "`{{task.id}}` resolves to `add-rate-limiter`"). Minimal in-scope extension of the seq-5 resolver, required by this task's four-command golden.
- **Test:** named `command_ref_renders_shell_safe` (the four shipped refs in an `add-rate-limiter` ctx render their exact lines + insta golden table) + `shell_quotes_per_arg` (bare/quoted/escape unit cases) + proptests `quoting_predicate_holds` (bare-charset⇔bare, else single-quoted) and `render_is_pure` (same in → same line). RED confirmed via empty-returning stubs failing the real line/quoting assertions; then GREEN.
- **decisions-pending.md `(I) Data-value path resolver + command-catalog shell-quoting` deleted** — both halves now landed (resolver in seq 5, shell-quoting here).

### Include expansion — `expand_includes` + `StepSource` (2026-05-31, inc-3 seq 8)

- **`engine::compose::expand_includes(&WorkflowDef, &dyn StepSource) -> Result<Composition, Finding>`** implements overrides.md phases 6 (cycle detection) + 7 (include expansion) for the workflow-only path: walks `def.includes` in order, resolves each id to a `StepDef`, expands recursively, and flattens to a `Composition { steps: Vec<ComposedStep{id, body}> }` in **pre-order**, placeholders still unresolved (phase 8's job). Pure function of `(def, source)` — same resolved cascade in → same composition out (determinism boundary).
- **`StepSource` trait (engine) carries the engine/CLI seam** — the frontend maps a step id to the highest-precedence layer's file via the already-built `cascade::Resolved::file_owner` (phase 2 by-id shadowing) and loads its `StepDef`; the engine consumes only `step(id) -> Option<StepDef>`. Keeps feed-layers-in / assert-results-out: cascade *location* is the CLI's, *resolution+expansion* is the engine's. A `None` is a dangling include.
- **Cycle detection is DFS-path-based, not seen-based** — re-entering an id already on the active include path is a cycle (`workflow-refs.include-cycle-absent`); a diamond (id reached twice but never while on-path) is *not* a cycle and expands once per inclusion. Matches overrides.md ("cycles are never broken automatically").
- **Typed errors reuse the settled block envelope** (`Finding`): dangling include → `workflow-refs.include-resolves`; cycle → `workflow-refs.include-cycle-absent` (the validation.md check names). Both blocking.
- **Nested includes in a step body are split out and flattened in pre-order**: a `{{include: step:X}}` line inside a body is removed from the emitted `ComposedStep.body` and X is expanded immediately after the parent (recognized by reusing `parse_include_line`). Every MVP step is plain (no nested includes), so its body is carried verbatim.
- **Test:** named `include_expansion_flattens_in_order` (single-task → `[locate, implement, superseded-context, finalize]` golden + verbatim plain bodies + dangling-id Err + A↔B cycle Err) plus `nested_include_in_step_body_flattens_pre_order`, `self_include_is_a_cycle`, `diamond_is_not_a_cycle`, and proptest `flattened_order_equals_include_order_for_acyclic`. RED confirmed by mutating the cycle finding's code and watching the typed-code assertions fail; reverted.

### Four-class emitter — `emit_step_body` (2026-05-31, inc-3 seq 9)

- **`engine::compose::emit_step_body(&str, &ComposeContext, &CommandCatalog) -> Result<String, Finding>`** implements overrides.md phases 8 (placeholder resolution) + 9 (line emission) for one step body, into the four-class emitted format (workflow-dialect.md → Emitted format). Pure function of `(body, ctx, catalog)` — same inputs → same text (determinism boundary).
- **Line-class dispatch is per-line, longest-match-wins**: a *lone* `{{cli.<id>}}` line → `` Run: `<rendered-cmd>` `` (via seq-7 `render_command`); a `<<author: {{<path>}}>>` line → `<<author: <address>>` with the `<<…>>` wrapper preserved and only the embedded `{{…}}` resolved (rule 3); a *lone* `{{@<path>}}` line → a `> <address>` blockquote, or an **empty line** for `Resolution::Absent` (unbound role; empty-not-finding — workflow-dialect.md → Empty vs unresolvable); everything else is bare Reason prose verbatim (rule 4, the default).
- **A "lone" placeholder is the whole trimmed line** (`parse_lone_placeholder`/`parse_cli_placeholder`) — an inline `{{…}}` inside prose is not a class line, so prose is emitted verbatim. The trailing-newline of the input body is preserved.
- **Unresolvable refs reuse the settled block envelope** (`Finding`): a `{{cli.<id>}}` absent from the catalog → `workflow-refs.command-ref-resolves`; a malformed embedded data-value path → `workflow-refs.malformed-data-value`; a failing resolve surfaces the seq-5 resolver's finding. All blocking.
- **Test:** named `emit_four_classes` (a multi-class fixture → golden over the emitted text; asserts the `Run: ` line matches `^Run: `(.+)`$` and the `<<author:>>` wrapper survives with only `{{…}}` resolved) + `emit_unbound_at_is_empty_line` (unbound `@` → empty line, isolated) + proptest `emit_is_pure` (same `(body, ctx, catalog)` → identical text). RED confirmed via a body-passthrough stub failing the real `Run: `-line assertion; then GREEN.

### Full compose — `compose` + `ComposedWorkflow` + the routing footer (2026-05-31, inc-3 seq 10)

- **`engine::compose::compose(&WorkflowDef, &dyn StepSource, &CommandCatalog, &ComposeContext) -> Result<ComposedWorkflow, Finding>`** wires phases 7 (expand, seq 8) + 8/9 (emit per step, seq 9) into one deterministic step: expand includes → emit each step body → join the per-step texts in include order with **one blank line between steps** (a step's trailing newline is trimmed before the separator so the join is exactly one blank line). Pure function of `(def, source, catalog, ctx)` — same resolved cascade in → same workflow out (the determinism boundary).
- **`ComposedWorkflow { text: String }` carries the footerless four-class text** (Serialize/Deserialize, the renderer/JSON contract). The **routing footer is a presentation concern appended in `cli::render::composed`**, never by the engine (engine stays presentation-free): `agent`/`human` append `ROUTING_FOOTER` after the text; `json` emits the generic projection with no footer (workflow-dialect.md → Routing footer; the JSON-carries-no-footer invariant).
- **Bare lone `{{<path>}}` data-value emit added to `emit_step_body`** (the phase-8 gap seq 9 left): a lone bare `{{task.intent}}` resolves to its **scalar** text inline (no `> ` marker — that is the `@`-Content class); a bare path's address resolves to the address text; an unbound role → empty line. This completes the emitter so `{{task.intent}}` resolves end-to-end.
- **Pack fix — `locate.yaml` `{{ @task.intent }}` → `{{ task.intent }}`** (the bare scalar form). The seq-1 pack used the `@` form drawn from worked-examples.md flow #1's blockquote, but that contradicts the **normative** statements: workflow-dialect.md L274 ("the MVP `single-task` … its `locate` reads `{{task.intent}}`"), L95 (`{{task.intent}}` is the scalar string), L97 (`@` on a scalar is a conformance error), and write-commands.md L54 ("`{{task.intent}}` = `<intent>`"). The seq-9 agent flagged `{{ @task.intent }}` as a deferred pack-content concern; this task (first to run the shipped pack through the resolver) fixes the orphan. worked-examples.md's `> ` blockquote for the intent is the illustrative-doc outlier (it shows the spec-driven `@task.spec#criteria` Content shape conceptually) — superseded by the spec-less MVP scalar form here. Updated the in-module `STEP_LOCATE` const + the `pack.rs` locate golden to match.
- **Test:** named `compose_single_task_emits_resolved_view` (golden over the **whole** composed single-task view for intent `add rate limiter`: intent resolved inline, four steps in order, `{{cli.…}}` → `Run:` lines, `<<author:>>` preserved, the unbound `{{@task.decision.supersedes#decision}}` line empty, **no footer** on the engine view) + proptest `compose_is_deterministic_for_fixed_cascade_and_ctx` (composing twice with the same cascade+ctx is byte-identical) + `cli` render test `render_composed_agent_text_ends_with_routing_footer` (footer on agent/human, none on JSON). RED confirmed by the golden differing on a real successful compose (all behavioral assertions passed first); then GREEN.

### `workflow-refs` probe — the seven compose-time conformance checks as a gate (2026-05-31, inc-3 seq 11)

- **`engine::compose::workflow_refs(&[u8], &dyn StepSource, &CommandCatalog, &ComposeContext) -> Vec<Finding>`** runs the seven intrinsic-blocking `workflow-refs` checks (validation.md → Severity inventory) over a workflow definition + its expanded tree, returning the blocking findings (empty ⇒ conformant). It is **the composition pipeline run as a gate**, not new detection logic: `load_workflow_def` → `body-include-only` (+ malformed/missing front-matter); `expand_includes` → `include-resolves` / `include-cycle-absent`; per expanded step body `find_run_shadow` + `emit_step_body` → `run-marker-not-shadowed`, `command-ref-resolves`, `placeholder-resolves` (undeclared root/role), `at-marker-on-non-scalar`. **Pin: returns `Vec<Finding>` (not `Result`)** so multiple step breaks each surface; load/expand failures short-circuit (one structural finding — no tree to walk). Pure function of its inputs (the determinism boundary).
- **New check `find_run_shadow` (`run-marker-not-shadowed`)** — a step body line whose left margin (after leading whitespace) literally starts `Run: ` shadows the composer-reserved marker (workflow-dialect.md → Compose-time conformance). At probe time the body still carries unresolved `{{ cli.… }}`, so a literal `Run: ` line is always authored prose, never a resolved command-ref. Located at the (step-body-relative) line; the first shadow is reported.
- **Test:** named `workflow_refs_flags_each_break` (clean single-task → zero findings; six minimal negative fixtures each trip exactly one check — dangling include, dangling `{{cli.unknown}}`, a `Run: ` step line, a workflow prose line, `@`-on-scalar, an include cycle — golden over the `code @ line:col` list per fixture; every finding asserted `Severity::Blocking`) + `workflow_refs_flags_undeclared_placeholder` (the `placeholder-resolves` undeclared-root path). RED confirmed via a `Vec::new()` stub failing the negative-fixture assertions for the right reason (expected 1 finding, got 0); then GREEN.

### Task minting — `state::mint_task` + the working-area / base-pin layout (2026-05-31, inc-3 seq 12)

- **`engine::state::mint_task(jigc_root: &Path, intent: &str, type_name: &str, base: BasePin) -> Result<MintedTask, Finding>`** is task origination (`write-commands.md` → Task origination; `storage.md` → The per-task working area / base pinning): slug the intent via `slug::slugify` (empty/stripped → `slugify(type_name)` fallback, the mint site's concern since only the caller knows the type), reject a **serial collision** when `<jigc_root>/tasks/<id>/` already exists, else create that dir + write the base pin. The base SHA is **supplied by the caller** (the engine shells out to nothing — "CLI orchestrates, git executes"), so minting is a pure function of (jigc-root, intent, type, base) → on-disk effect; golden-testable. The numeric `-2`/`-3` collision suffix is *not* used here — serial collisions reject; the suffix is reserved for the post-MVP `fan-out`/`join` parallel case.
- **Working-area layout pin** (resolves decisions-pending inc-3 *(D) Task working-area on-disk layout*): `<repo_root>/.jigc/tasks/<id>/` holds **`base.json`** = pretty-JSON `{ "sha": "<full>", "short": "<short>" }` (struct field order `sha` then `short`, one trailing `\n`), the frozen byte form golden-locked in `mint_creates_task_area_and_base_pin`. Bound context roles + staged docs are added by later write-path steps; the base pin is the one file minting writes. `BasePin`/`MintedTask` are `Serialize`+`Deserialize` (the pin is read back on resume).
- **Serial-collision block reuses the `finding` envelope** (DECISIONS 2026-05-31 → Block payload): a `Severity::Blocking` `Finding { code: "task.serial-collision" }` naming the existing task, located at `task:<id>`, carrying a `route` (`resume with jigc start --task <id>` / `discard`). Not a new error type. A working-area I/O failure surfaces as `task.working-area-io` (route-less).
- **Git invocation lives CLI-side** (`cli::start::mint_in_repo`): shell out to `git rev-parse HEAD` + `--short HEAD` (DECISIONS 2026-05-31 → Git invocation: the `git` binary, not git2/gitoxide), then call `mint_task` with `<repo_root>/.jigc` as the root. **`engine` opted into `serde_json`** (pre-declared crate; promoted dev-dep → dep) for the frozen pin byte form. `mod start` is `#[allow(dead_code)]` until the `Start { intent: Some(_) }` compose dispatch wires it in (a later inc-3 step) — the established pattern.
- **Test:** engine `mint_creates_task_area_and_base_pin` (golden over `base.json` for a fixed SHA; re-mint same slug → routed blocking finding, no second dir) + empty/stripped-intent fallback cases + a proptest `mint_id == slugify(intent)` for non-colliding inputs; CLI `mint_opens_working_dir_and_base_pin_under_a_temp_git_repo` (real `git init`+commit, asserts the dir + pin record the repo's actual HEAD, serial re-mint surfaces the route). RED confirmed by mutating the collision guard to a no-op (engine) and the HEAD read to a wrong SHA (CLI), each failing for the right reason; reverted.

### `jigc start "<intent>"` wired end-to-end — mint + compose + gate + render (2026-05-31, inc-3 seq 13)

- **`cli::start::compose_in_repo(start: &Path, intent: &str) -> Result<ComposedWorkflow>`** replaces the inc-1 "composition not implemented" stub in `cli.rs`: locate the cascade (absent `.jigc/config/` → route to `jigc setup`), read `default-workflow` from the pack's `config/defaults` (MVP = `single-task`), load the workflow def + command catalog, **mint after the def loads** (so a malformed pack never strands a task dir), build the `ComposeContext`, run the `workflow_refs` gate, and on a clean gate `compose` the four-class view. The `Start { intent: Some }` arm now calls it and renders through `render::composed` (footer on agent/human, none on JSON). Deterministic, no LLM call.
- **`ComposeContext` build pin** — `task.id`/`task.intent` bound to the minted id + the human intent; the **`commit` role bound to `commit:<id>`** (the always-present sink of a `creates-task` work-workflow, so the `implement` step's `<<author: {{task.commit#summary}}>>` resolves to `commit:<id>#summary`); each `allows-create` role (here `decision`) declared **unbound** so `{{@task.decision.supersedes#decision}}` resolves to Absent (empty line) at compose-time, binding only when the ADR is created in-task.
- **`PackStepSource`** — a `StepSource` reading steps from the embedded pack; MVP has no project step override, so pack-default owns every step (the CLI-locates / engine-resolves seam, trivially one-layer for now).
- **(I)** `cli` opted into the pre-declared `serde_yaml_ng` workspace crate to read the one `default-workflow` scalar from `config/defaults` (no cascade-base scalar read wired yet — that is a broader inc-6 config-layer task).
- **Test:** integration `crates/cli/tests/start_compose.rs` (real temp git repo): `jigc start "add rate limiter"` exits 0, mints `.jigc/tasks/add-rate-limiter/base.json`, stdout carries the resolved intent + a `Run:` line + `<<author:` + the routing footer; `--format json` exits 0 with no footer and a `text` field carrying the composed view; a serial re-run exits non-zero naming the task. RED confirmed: the stub printed "not yet implemented" and exited FAILURE, then GREEN.

## 2026-05-31 — inc-4 planning pins (write path + commit-only finalize)

Doc-elaboration pins made by the inc-4 planning cut (`roadmap.md` → Increment 4 = worked-examples flows #1 + #4). These follow the locked design docs; each is a concrete form pinned for the executors, logged here per the inc-4 emphasis ("Follow the locked design doc, pin the concrete form, log it").

- **Commit schema brought to the finalize.md rendering contract (pack rides along).** The shipped minimal `commit.yaml` (`header#subject` + `body` slot) is replaced by the schema `finalize.md` → Commit-doc rendering + `worked-examples.md` flow #1 actually exercise: `header` fields `type` (enum, conventional-commits set) + `scope` (string, optional), a `summary` slot, a `body` slot (optional). Trailers (repeatable) are pinned in the schema but the rendering of the repeatable footer is the same string-sink path. The `implement.yaml` step's `commit:…#summary` / `#type` refs and the `set-commit-summary` command-ref now resolve against real leaves. Without this the write loop's first verb (`set-field commit#type`) addresses a nonexistent leaf. Settled by the locked docs; not a new fork.
- **`file-state` hashing = blake3 over raw bytes, recorded under `.jigc/state/`.** `blake3` (already workspace-declared) is wired into `engine`. The record is one JSON map `path → hex-hash` at `.jigc/state/file-state.json` (`storage.md` → `.jigc/state/` hash records; `reconciliation.md` → recorded `file-state` hash). Raw-byte hash per `parsing.md` → Drift hash. MVP inc-4 surface = baseline-adopt + hash-matches over the *task working area + the commit-only case*; the full OOB classifier (absorb/conflict/rename) is inc-5.
- **`schema-conformance` is the synthetic validate-engine category inc-4 ships:** `required-slot-present`, `required-field-present`, `field-value-conformant` — over the parsed instance, reusing `write::check_value` for field values. **`ref-resolves` (forward-ref / edge index) is explicitly inc-5**, not built here (`validation.md` → the four checks; inc-4 has no edge index). `file-state.hash-matches` is the second probe inc-4 runs at task scope.
- **`finalize` promote phase is a structural no-op in the commit-only case.** The commit doc's sink is the git message (rendered in phase 3), never promoted; inc-4 has no persisted managed doc to promote (ADR create + promotion is inc-5). Phase-7 post-commit inc-4 scope = update `file-state` hashes + remove the working area; **edge-index stamp invalidation is inc-5** (no index exists yet).
- **Task working-area on-disk layout (extends the inc-3 base-pin pin).** A staged doc instance lives at `.jigc/tasks/<id>/docs/<type>:<slug>.md` (the `:`-joined address slug); the commit doc is `commit:<id>.md`. `task diff` reads these vs base; `task discard` removes `.jigc/tasks/<id>/`. Workflow-provisioned `commit:<id>` is materialized empty (canonical template via `write::render`) at first write, copy-in-on-first-touch for any pre-existing doc.
- **Write verbs gate through one validated path.** `set_field` already has `set_field_validated` (inc-2). Inc-4 adds the sibling gated wrappers `set_slot_validated` (write-time heading-depth-ceiling enforcement + validate-after) and the `create` provisioning verb; all reuse the inc-2 `validate_after` + the one `Finding` envelope. A hard block is a blocking-severity `Finding` carrying a route (the settled payload shape).
- **Git invocation shells out to `git`** (`DECISIONS.md` 2026-05-31 → Git invocation), CLI-side only (`cli::finalize`), never in the engine: `rev-parse HEAD` (base-pin preflight), `add`, `commit -F <tmp>` (never `--no-verify`). The engine `finalize` is a pure transaction planner returning the ordered phase plan + the rendered message; the CLI executes the git steps, keeping the engine LLM-free *and* shell-free.

### Commit schema brought to the rendering contract (2026-05-31, inc-4 seq 1)

- **`commit.yaml` now ships the `finalize.md` → Commit-doc rendering schema:** `header` fields `type` (enum over the Conventional-Commits set `[feat, fix, docs, style, refactor, perf, test, build, ci, chore, revert]`) + `scope` (string, optional), a `summary` slot, an optional `body` slot, and a repeatable `trailers` section (`id-from: key`, block `key`/`value` strings). **No `id-from:`** — the commit's identity is task-derived (`commit:<task-id>`, workflow-provisioned), and the only id-source candidate (`summary`) is a slot, never a field; `id_from` is optional in the model. Why: the write loop's first verbs (`set-field commit#type`, `set-slot commit#summary`) and the existing `set-commit-summary` command-ref / `implement.yaml` `<<author: {{task.commit#summary}}>>` already address `summary`/`type`; the minimal `header#subject` + `body` schema they were authored against was the missing half. Steps + command-refs needed no edit — only the schema and its goldens (`schema_commit`, `sections_commit_fixture`, `commit_instance`) re-pinned; the commit/proptest fixtures track the new shape.

### `file-state` hash store wired (2026-05-31, inc-4 seq 2)

- **`engine::file_state` ships the raw-byte `blake3` drift hash + the `.jigc/state/file-state.json` record reader/writer** (concretizing the inc-4 planning pin above). `hash_bytes(&[u8]) -> String` is the 64-char lowercase hex of `blake3::hash` over **raw bytes** (`parsing.md` → Drift hash — no canonicalization, so a conformant no-op read/write never drifts); the golden pins the hex of a fixed non-ASCII, newline-bearing input. `FileStateRecord { hashes: BTreeMap<String, String> }` is the `path → hex-hash` map; a `BTreeMap` makes the serialized JSON **path-sorted and deterministic**, so the byte form is golden-stable. On-disk form reuses the `base.json` convention — `serde_json::to_string_pretty` + one trailing `\n` at `<jigc_root>/state/file-state.json`; `save` creates `state/`, `load` returns an **empty record on a missing file** (`reconciliation.md` → Absent-hash is not drift, never an error). Store layer only — no classifier/probe/drift logic (that consumes this and is inc-5). `blake3.workspace = true` newly opted into `engine`.

### `file-state` probe — baseline-adopt + hash-matches (2026-05-31, inc-4 seq 3)

- **`engine::file_state::file_state(&mut FileStateRecord, &[(&str, &[u8])]) -> Vec<Finding>`** is the engine-native probe over the seq-2 store, MVP surface only (`validation.md` → Probes (`file-state`); `reconciliation.md` → Baseline adoption). Three states: `UNKNOWN` (no recorded hash) → records the current hash + emits an **advisory** `file-state.baseline-adopt` finding (no route; absent-hash is not drift); recorded-and-matching → **zero** findings (`IN_SYNC`); recorded-and-differing → one **blocking** `file-state.hash-matches` finding carrying a `route: Some("reconcile <path>")` (the `reconcile` route encoded as the `Option<String>` prose the `Finding` envelope already uses, mirroring `state.rs` collision routes — no tagged-union type added). Drift does **not** advance the recorded hash — re-baselining is only the three named sites (adopt/absorb/commit), and drift is none. Pure over its inputs apart from the `UNKNOWN→baseline` record mutation; the caller hashes/reads bytes and persists the advanced record. No OOB absorb/conflict/rename — inc-5. Severity is the inventory default (`baseline-adopt` advisory, `hash-matches` blocking-tunable); cascade severity tuning is not wired into engine probes yet (parallels `workflow-refs`).

### `schema-conformance` probe — required-slot/field present + field-value-conformant (2026-05-31, inc-4 seq 4)

- **`engine::validate::schema_conformance(&Schema, source: &str, &Document) -> Vec<Finding>`** is the synthetic `schema-conformance` category over a single parsed instance (`validation.md` → MVP check inventory + Synthetic categories; `DECISIONS.md` inc-4 planning pin). Three checks, each violation one **blocking** `schema-conformance.*` finding (inventory default; no cascade tuning yet, parallels `workflow-refs`/`file-state`): `required-slot-present` — a declared body slot whose recorded span slices to empty/whitespace (the parser already blocks a *missing heading* as `conformance.section-missing`, so this is the present-but-unfilled slot, `finalize.md` phase 2 "validate blocks on required-slot presence"); `required-field-present` — an **author-required** declared field absent from the instance; `field-value-conformant` — a present field whose value fails `write::check_value` (reused, the same write-time adjudicator). **`ref-resolves` (forward-ref / edge index) is explicitly NOT built — inc-5** (no edge index yet). A present field is not also reported absent; an absent field is not value-checked; a conformant instance yields zero. **Doc-elaboration pin — "required" semantics** (the schema model carries no explicit `required` flag): a declared body slot is required (the meaningful content; design says required-but-empty blocks at finalize); a field is **author-required iff it carries neither `default:` nor `set:`** (`set`/`default` are CLI-derived/defaulted per `document-type-schema.md` → Field provenance, not the author's obligation; `ref` `card`-based optionality is the inc-5 `ref-resolves` concern, not adjudicated here). No schema-model change, so the inc-2 goldens are untouched.

### `validate(task)` engine — scope sweep aggregating the two probes (2026-05-31, inc-4 seq 5)

- **`engine::validate::validate_task(dir: &Path, schemas: &BTreeMap<String, Schema>, record: &mut FileStateRecord) -> std::io::Result<ValidationReport>`** is the single task-scope entry point both `task validate` and `finalize` phase 2 call — one engine, two entry points, so what `validate` reports and what `finalize` blocks on can never diverge (`validation.md` → How it gates `finalize`; `write-commands.md` → `finalize ≡ validate + commit`; `finalize.md` → 2-validate). No git, no commit. It resolves the working area's staged doc instances (`<dir>/docs/*.md`, path-sorted), and over each runs `file_state` (drift/baseline) then `schema_conformance`, aggregating into `ValidationReport`. `ref-resolves` (forward-ref/edge index) is **not** run — inc-5.
- **`ValidationReport { schema_version, findings: Vec<Finding> }`** is the new public, versioned result type (lives in `result.rs`, `SCHEMA_VERSION`-stamped, Serialize/Deserialize — the renderer/JSON contract). `has_blocking()` is the gate predicate: true iff any finding is `Severity::Blocking` (advisory surfaced, never blocks). Presentation-free; `cli::render` formats.
- **Doc-elaboration pin — working-area → instance resolution.** Staged instances are `<dir>/docs/<type>:<slug>.md` (extends the inc-4 layout pin); the type is the filename prefix before `:`; the `file-state` record is keyed by the docs-relative path `docs/<filename>`. The engine stays **domain-empty** — schemas are fed in by the caller (CLI resolves the cascade), keyed type→`Schema`. A staged type with no schema raises a blocking `schema-conformance.unknown-type` finding (route-less). A parse-level conformance failure surfaces the parser's findings directly; a clean parse runs the seq-4 `schema_conformance` checks. Sweep order is stable: docs path-sorted, `file-state` before `schema-conformance` within each doc.

### Task doc working-area — provision + copy-in + atomic persist (2026-05-31, inc-4 seq 6)

- **`engine::state` ships the working-area doc plumbing** (`implementation/parsing.md` → The write pipeline: "writes land in the task working area", atomic temp+rename, copy-in on first touch; `design/write-commands.md` → Instance provisioning → Workflow-provisioned; `DECISIONS.md` 2026-05-31 → inc-4 working-area layout). `provision_doc(task_dir, &Schema, slug) -> io::Result<PathBuf>` materializes a workflow-provisioned **empty** instance at `<task_dir>/docs/<type>:<slug>.md` — `write::render` of `empty_instance` (title = the task-derived slug, every schema section content-free), so the agent only fills slots. `copy_in(task_dir, type_name, slug, source) -> io::Result<PathBuf>` is copy-in-on-first-touch for a pre-existing managed doc: `write::first_touch_canonicalize` (BOM strip + single trailing newline, EOL-preserving, everything else byte-for-byte) persisted to the same path; the committed source is untouched. `persist(path, bytes)` is the atomic edited-buffer write. All three route through one `write_atomic` primitive (sibling `<filename>.tmp` → `rename`, intra-filesystem/atomic, temp removed on failure, never left behind; parent dir created on demand). Pure working-area filesystem effect — **no verbs, no git**.
- **Doc-elaboration pin — empty-commit canonical byte form (golden-locked).** The provisioned empty `commit:<id>` is `---\n---\n\n# <id>\n\n## Summary\n\n\n\n## Body\n\n\n\n## Trailers\n` — empty front-matter fences (no field values yet), the H1 = the task slug, each slot section's heading with an empty slot, the empty repeatable `## Trailers`, one trailing `\n`. Pinned by the `provisioned_empty_commit` insta golden over the bytes `write::render(empty_instance)` produces; the test also asserts the atomic persist replaces the file byte-for-byte with no `.tmp` residue. Why: the done-criterion needs a frozen template the write loop's first verbs (`set-field commit#type`, `set-slot commit#summary`) splice into.

### Gated `set-slot` write verb — heading-ceiling enforcement + validate-after (2026-05-31, inc-4 seq 7)

- **`engine::write::set_slot_validated(&Schema, source, section_id, new_prose) -> Result<String, Finding>`** is the slot sibling to inc-2's `set_field_validated` — the one validated path for a slot write (`DECISIONS.md` inc-4 planning pin → "write verbs gate through one validated path"; `parsing.md` → Slot heading-depth ceiling, enforcement site 1 + Validate-after-write; `write-commands.md` → write-time local adjudication). It (1) enforces the **heading-depth ceiling** on the agent's `new_prose` *before* touching bytes — a `##`/`###` ATX heading or any Setext underline is rejected with a located blocking `Finding` naming the offending line (1-based, **relative to the prose**) so the agent retries with non-conflicting prose; (2) **splices** the present section's recorded slot span, or **generates** the section's structural home when the section is absent; (3) runs the inc-2 `validate_after` (re-parse + only-the-target-changed) on the splice path. Persists nothing on any block.
- **Doc-elaboration pin — write-path ceiling finding codes.** The write-time ceiling emits `write.slot-heading-depth` (ATX `##`/`###`) and `write.slot-setext-heading` (Setext at any depth) — the `write.*` envelope, *distinct from* the parser's `conformance.slot-heading-depth`/`conformance.slot-setext-heading` read-time producer (same rule, two enforcement sites; "one finding shape, two producers"). The scan reuses `parse::scan_blocks` over the **standalone prose** (a real block parse, so a fenced `## …` is correctly not a heading; line numbers are prose-relative), and surfaces only the **first** violation as the block. Golden-pinned (`set_slot_ceiling_finding`). Generation-path post-write gate is re-parse only (the surgical-span confinement check does not apply to a multi-region insert); a generation failure surfaces as a `write.{already-present,unknown-section,wrong-shape}` or `write.non-reparseable` blocking finding.

### `create` provisioning verb + the create-gate (2026-05-31, inc-4 seq 8)

- **`engine::state::create(task_dir, schemas: &BTreeMap<String, Schema>, type_name, id_source) -> Result<CreatedDoc, Finding>`** is the CLI-owned provisioning verb — the workflow-provisioned path that mints `commit:<id>` (`write-commands.md` → Instance provisioning; `structural-grammar.md` → IDs: provenance and minting). Order: (1) **unknown doctype** (`type_name ∉ schemas`) → blocking `create.unknown-doctype` (`"unknown doctype \`<type>\`"`), *before* any mint/placement (`write-commands.md` step 3); (2) mint the frozen content-slug from `id_source` (empty → the type-name fallback, the same `mint_id` discipline as a task); (3) **serial collision** (instance file already exists) → blocking `create.serial-collision`, never silently suffixed (`structural-grammar.md`: the numeric suffix is the post-MVP `fan-out`/`join` case only); (4) `provision_doc` the empty instance, returning `CreatedDoc { address: "<type>:<slug>", path }`. New public result type `CreatedDoc` (Serialize/Deserialize — the renderer/JSON contract).
- **`engine::state::create_gated(task_dir, schemas, gate: &[AllowsCreate], type_name, id_source)`** is the agent-initiated wrapper consulting the workflow's resolved `allows-create` (`write-commands.md` → The create-gate, enforcement steps 3 then 5): unknown-doctype rejects *before* the gate (step 3); a known-but-disallowed type → blocking `create.gate-blocked` naming the disallowed type + the allowed set, carrying the loosen route (`"add \`<type>\` to allows-create in project config"`); an admitted type → `create`. The `as:` role bind of an admitted instance to `task.<role>` is **inc-5** (commit-only scope drives only the workflow-provisioned `create` path here). All blocks reuse the one `Finding` envelope (settled block-payload shape), route-bearing.

### Commit-doc rendering — schema-driven string sink (2026-05-31, inc-4 seq 9)

- **`engine::write::render_commit_message(&Schema, &Instance) -> String`** is the canonical writer's sixth caller — the **string sink** (`parsing.md` → The write pipeline; `finalize.md` → Commit-doc rendering + § 3 Render the commit message): a `commit` instance → a git-message string, not a file. Mapping (the pack-default Conventional-Commits shape): header `type` field → subject `<type>`; header `scope` field → `(<scope>)` **omitted entirely when empty** (no empty parens); the `summary` slot → subject text after `: `; the `body` slot → body after one blank line, skipped when empty; the `trailers` repeatable section → footer `key: value` lines after one blank line, skipped when empty. Returns no trailing whitespace/newline (consumed via `git commit -F`). Pure function of `(schema, instance)` — deterministic, no disk side effects; the line-limit advisories ride with `validate` later, not here.
- **Schema-driven via well-known section ids.** The renderer reads section roles by the commit schema's declared ids (`header`/`summary`/`body`/`trailers`), reading only sections the schema declares — a project shipping its own commit schema (the cascade-overridable case) keeps these role ids. The header section is found by its `header: true` flag, not a hardcoded id. Doc-elaboration pin: the role↔id binding is the pack-default contract, not a new schema field.

### `finalize` transaction planner — preflight→validate→render plan, shell-free (2026-05-31, inc-4 seq 10)

- **`engine::finalize::plan_finalize(task_dir, &BasePin, head_sha, &ValidationReport, has_diff, &Schema, commit_slug) -> Result<FinalizePlan, Vec<Finding>>`** is the engine half of the commit boundary — a **pure transaction planner**, no git, no commit, never shells out (`DECISIONS.md` 2026-05-31 → inc-4 planning pins: engine `finalize` is shell-free; `finalize.md` → The seven phases). The CLI reads HEAD / runs `git diff` / commits; the engine decides. Phases, each aborting *before* any plan is produced (`finalize.md` → Rollback discipline: nothing to roll back before phase 3): **1 preflight** — `task_dir` exists (`finalize.no-task`) and `base.sha == head_sha` else the divergence-routing `finalize.base-mismatch` blocking finding (the CLI never operates a task off its pinned base; the "git committable" sub-check is a CLI git-state query, not a pure decision, so it is not the planner's concern); **2 validate** — `report.has_blocking()` aborts surfacing **exactly the blocking findings** (advisory dropped; one engine, two entry points — no private check path); **empty-commit guard** — `!has_diff` aborts `finalize.empty-commit` ("produced no diff"), placed *after* validate per `finalize.md` → Empty commit ("if validate passes but the staged diff … is empty"); **3 render** — reads + parses the staged `commit:<slug>` instance and renders it via `write::render_commit_message`. Promote (phase 4) is a **structural no-op** in the commit-only case (the commit doc's sink is the git message, never a repo file). All aborts reuse the one `Finding` envelope, route-bearing (the settled block payload).
- **New public result type `FinalizePlan { schema_version, message, hash_updates: BTreeMap<String,String> }`** (Serialize/Deserialize, `SCHEMA_VERSION`-stamped — the renderer/JSON contract). `message` is the rendered git-commit string (phase 3, no trailing newline); `hash_updates` is the phase-7 post-commit set `committed-doc-path → blake3 hex`. **Doc-elaboration pin — commit-only hash set is empty:** phase 7 updates `file-state` hashes "for every committed managed doc" (`finalize.md` → 7. Post-commit), i.e. promoted repo files; in the commit-only inc-4 case promote is a no-op (the commit doc is not promoted), so the set is empty. Edge-index stamp invalidation + working-area removal are CLI-side effects of the committed plan; edge-index invalidation is inc-5 (no index yet).
- **`engine::write::instance_from_source(&Schema, source) -> Result<Instance, Vec<Finding>>`** promoted from the inc-2 test helper to a public **parse-to-instance** (the inverse of `render`) so the planner recovers the writer's input from the staged commit doc on disk: `parse_sections` + H1 recovery + slot-span re-slice, surfacing parse-level findings. Genuine reuse (the planner's render path needs it); the round-trip pre-image `render(schema, &instance_from_source(schema, src)?)` reproduces canonical bytes.

### CLI `doc` subcommand — create / set-field / set-slot with stdin handoff (2026-05-31, inc-4 seq 11)

- **`cli::doc` is the `jigc doc <verb> <addr>` write surface — CLI wiring only over the engine's built verbs** (`write-commands.md` → The verbs / Content handoff / Worked example). `set-field <addr> --value <v>` (inline) → `write::set_field_validated`; `set-slot <addr> --from-file <path|->` (stdin/file) → `write::set_slot_validated`; `create <type> --title <…>` (inline id-source) → `state::create_gated`. Each resolves the active task from cwd, loads the addressed schema from the embedded pack, maps the address fragment to a `(section, leaf)`, hands bytes to the engine verb, persists the returned buffer via `state::persist`, and on a blocking `Finding` exits non-zero with the message + route on stderr.
- **Doc-elaboration pin — active-task resolution from cwd (MVP):** `.jigc/tasks/` under the `.git`-ancestor repo root; **exactly one** task dir is the active task; **zero** rejects (`no active task — start one with \`jigc start\``); **more than one** rejects asking for `--task <id>` (the explicit selector lands with `jigc task`). The single-task MVP never hits the multi case.
- **Doc-elaboration pin — fragment→leaf mapping:** a single-hop `#<id>` (the worked-example surface) resolves for `set-field` by searching every simple section for a field of that id, and for `set-slot` by matching the simple section whose id is the hop and which declares a `slot`; the two-hop `#<section>/<field>` form is also accepted.
- **Doc-elaboration pin — block route fallback (CLI presentation):** a hard block surfaces as `<message>\n  route: <route>`; where a write-time adjudication finding carries no route (the engine's `write.malformed-value` is route-less), the CLI supplies `retry \`jigc doc <verb> <addr>\` with a conforming value`. Routes are CLI presentation (agent-text), so the determinism boundary is unaffected; this keeps "every hard block carries a route" true at the surface without touching the engine.
- **Doc-elaboration pin — `jigc start` provisions the commit doc as a *fillable form*:** a `creates-task` work-workflow provisions its commit doc at mint time (`write-commands.md` → Instance provisioning → Workflow-provisioned), so a started task is immediately writable. The CLI renders the skeleton with every **header** field pre-stamped as an empty `key:` line, because the engine's `set-field` is a *surgical splice* of a present value line — front-matter has no generate-a-new-line path (the header is not a generatable body section), so the form must carry the line for the agent to fill (body slots provision empty; `set-slot` generates the section's home on demand). Pure CLI provisioning over `write::render` — no engine change, the inc-6 `state::provision_doc` golden untouched.

### CLI `task` subcommand — diff / validate / discard (2026-05-31, inc-4 seq 12)

- **`cli::task` is the `jigc task <verb> <id>` lifecycle surface — CLI wiring only over the engine's built sweep** (`write-commands.md` → Lifecycle: `diff`·`validate`·`discard`; `finalize` lands next). Unlike `jigc doc` (which resolves the *single* active task from cwd), the lifecycle verbs take `<id>` **explicitly** — the explicit selector `doc` deferred. `validate <id>` → `engine::validate::validate_task`, rendered through a new `render::validation`, **exit code = `report.has_blocking()`** (`validation.md` → How it gates `finalize`: validate previews what finalize blocks on). `discard <id>` removes `.jigc/tasks/<id>/` (idempotent on an already-absent area). `diff <id>` prints the working changeset vs base.
- **Doc-elaboration pin — `task diff` = git-code-diff + staged docs:** the working changeset is `git diff <base.sha>` (working tree vs the task's pinned base — "CLI orchestrates, git executes") **plus** the staged managed-doc instances under `docs/` (which live in the working area, *outside* the git tree, so git never sees them). Minimal read-only surface; always exit 0. The staged-doc content *is* what an OOB reader would see.
- **Doc-elaboration pin — `task validate` loads but does NOT persist the `file-state` record.** The committed-state hash record is loaded (so drift of a copied-in *committed* doc against its real baseline is detected) but **not saved back** after a working-area sweep: staged working-area docs are not committed state, and "task writes do not update the committed-state hash" (`reconciliation.md` → Hash re-baselining — the record advances only at adopt/absorb/commit, none of which is a `validate` of a working-area edit). Consequence: a working-area instance with no committed baseline baseline-adopts fresh (advisory) on every `validate` rather than drifting against a stale staged hash — so clean validate stays clean as the agent fills the doc across calls. Absorb of clean OOB drift on a committed doc is inc-5.
- **`render::validation` is the per-type finding renderer** (`module-layout.md` → Renderers): agent/human emit one `severity · code — message` line per finding (indented `route:` line where present) + the routing footer; a clean report emits a positive `no findings` line + footer; JSON is the generic projection, no footer (the established footer-on-agent/human-only stance).

### CLI `task finalize` — execute the plan: validate → render → stage → git commit → post-commit (2026-05-31, inc-4 seq 13)

- **`cli::task finalize <id>` is the CLI executor over the engine's pure `plan_finalize`** (`finalize.md` → 5. Stage / 6. Commit / 7. Post-commit; `worked-examples.md` → flow #4). The CLI reads HEAD (`git rev-parse HEAD`), runs `validate(task)`, computes `has_diff` (`git diff <base>` non-empty OR any staged doc), loads the `commit` schema, and asks the planner for the ordered plan. A blocking branch (base-mismatch / validate block / empty diff) returns findings → rendered via `render::validation` on stderr, non-zero exit, **no** commit. A clean plan → write the rendered message to `<task_dir>/finalize-message.tmp`, `git add --all`, `git commit -F <tmp>` (**never** `--no-verify`), then post-commit.
- **Doc-elaboration pin — finalize ensures `.jigc/.gitignore` before staging.** The stage phase writes `.jigc/.gitignore` (`tasks/`/`index/`/`state/`) when absent, so `git add --all` commits only `config/` + the code changes and never the transient working area or rebuildable caches (`storage.md` → repository layout: `.jigc/` is one home, `config/` committed, the rest gitignored). Idempotent — written only when missing.
- **Doc-elaboration pin — post-commit is best-effort and advances the file-state record over the committed working set.** The plan's managed-doc hash set is empty in the commit-only case (promote is a no-op), so post-commit additionally hashes the files the just-landed `HEAD` commit touched (`git show --name-only` → `git show HEAD:<path>` → `blake3`) and saves the record, then removes `.jigc/tasks/<id>/`. A failure in either step is logged to stderr, not raised — the commit is already truth and both self-heal (`finalize.md` → 7. Post-commit; rebuildable caches per `storage.md`).
- **Doc-elaboration pin — git invocation is CLI-side, message via temp file.** `git rev-parse`/`diff`/`add`/`commit -F`/`show` all shell out to the user's `git` (no git2/gitoxide — `DECISIONS.md` 2026-05-31 → Git invocation). The commit message is handed via a temp file (`git commit -F`), deleted after the attempt whether it succeeds or the hook rejects; a hook/git rejection surfaces git's stdout+stderr verbatim as the correction signal, with no commit and the working area untouched.

## 2026-05-31 — inc-5 planning pins (persisted ADR + edge index, the differentiator)

Doc-elaboration pins made by the inc-5 planning cut (`roadmap.md` → Increment 5 = worked-examples flows #5 + #2).
