These follow the locked design docs (`storage.md` → Edge-index lifecycle, `validation.md` → Forward-ref resolution, `reconciliation.md`, `finalize.md` → Promote, `write-commands.md` → The create-gate, `worked-examples.md` → Superseding decision); each is a concrete form pinned for the executors per the inc-5 emphasis ("Follow the locked design doc, pin the concrete form, log it"). None is a new fork.

- **Committed-store reader — `engine::store`.** A read-only accessor over the committed `.md`s at type `location:`s: given a cascade-resolved schema set + repo root, resolve `<type>:<slug>` → `<location>/<slug>.md` (`storage.md` → Identity is the path), read+parse against the schema, and slice a `#fragment` (a section's slot span via `Span::slice`). This is the byte-read the inc-3 `Resolution::Content` handle deferred and the parse-the-committed-file round-trip flow #5 mandates (`parsing.md` → Round-trip guarantees). Engine stays domain-empty — the CLI feeds the schema set + repo root in. A missing/unparseable committed target surfaces a blocking `Finding` (the one envelope).
- **Edge-index on-disk format + stamp pin** (resolves the inc-5 emphasis "Pin the edge-index on-disk format + stamp per storage.md"). The committed index persists at `.jigc/index/edges.json` as `{ "stamp": "<HEAD-sha>", "edges": [ { "from": "<type:slug>", "relation": "<name>", "to": "<type:slug>" } ] }` — `serde_json::to_string_pretty` + one trailing `\n`, edges in a deterministic sort order (`from`, then `relation`, then `to`) so the byte form is golden-stable. **Stamp = the full HEAD sha** (`storage.md` → Derived caches: "stamped with the HEAD it was built against"); a stamp ≠ current HEAD triggers a committed rebuild (`storage.md` → Edge index lifecycle, site 1). Gitignored under `.jigc/index/` (already in `.jigc/.gitignore`). Edges are **forward only** (the inverse is derived on read, never stored — `document-type-schema.md` → Bidirectional; `DECISIONS.md` 2026-05-23).
- **Edge extraction = schema `ref` fields.** The rebuild walks every committed managed doc, parses it, and emits one `(from, relation, to)` edge per present `ref`-typed field value (`relation` = the field id, e.g. `supersedes`; `to` = the field's `<type>:<slug>` value). MVP carries the one adr→adr `supersedes` relation; the extraction is type-agnostic (any `ref` field), so a second relation needs no code change.
- **Working overlay is read-side, never persisted** (`storage.md` → Edge index lifecycle: "the index is never persisted with task deltas in it"). `validate(task)`/the context-slice resolve over `committed edges ∪ this task's working-area edges` derived in-memory for the scope of the call; only `finalize` writes through indirectly (stamp invalidation → next read rebuilds against the new HEAD). The overlay derivation parses the task's `docs/*.md` and emits the same edge shape the committed rebuild emits.
- **`ref-resolves` (forward-ref integrity) is the inc-5 `schema-conformance` check** deferred from inc-4. At `validate(task)` it walks the **overlaid** graph: for every edge whose `from` is a task-touched doc, the `to` target must exist in **(a) the committed store or (b) this task's working area** — the two reachable surfaces (`validation.md` → Forward-ref resolution). A target in neither → a **blocking** `schema-conformance.ref-resolves` finding naming the three routing options (fix the ref / create the target in this task / drop the field — `worked-examples.md` flow #5 dangling variant). Cross-task forward-refs are unsupported (same finding). Intrinsic blocking (severity inventory); no cascade tuning wired yet (parallels the other intrinsic checks).
- **`as:` role binding at create** (deferred from inc-4 seq 8). `create_gated` admitting an entry carrying `as: <role>` records the minted `<type>:<slug>` as the task's bound role in the working area, so `task.<role>` resolves to it (`write-commands.md` → The create-gate, step 4: "bind it to the entry's `as:` role"). On-disk: the bound roles live in `.jigc/tasks/<id>/roles.json` (`serde_json` pretty, sorted keys, one trailing `\n` — same convention as `base.json`), read back on resume so a re-composed `jigc start --task <id>` sees `task.decision` bound. The inc-3 unbound-role declaration (`start.rs` declares each `allows-create` role `None`) flips to `Some(address)` once created.
- **Context-slice resolution wires `Resolution::Content` to the committed reader + edge hop.** `{{@task.decision.supersedes#decision}}` resolves: `task.decision` → the bound ADR address (above), `.supersedes` → the edge hop (the bound ADR's `supersedes` field value, read from the working-area instance or the committed store), `#decision` → the slice. The composer dereferences the final `Resolution::Content` address through `engine::store` to the section prose and emits it as a `> ` Content blockquote (`workflow-dialect.md` → Emitted format). An unbound `task.decision` or an unset `supersedes` short-circuits to `Resolution::Absent` → empty text, no finding (`worked-examples.md` flow #1: the non-superseding task emits nothing) — the inc-3 absent-not-finding contract extends to a bound-but-no-edge role.
- **Promote phase at finalize (no longer a no-op).** Phase 4 copies each staged managed doc in the working area's `docs/` (other than the transient `commit:<id>`) from `<task>/docs/<type>:<slug>.md` to its canonical `<location>/<slug>.md` (`finalize.md` → 4. Promote: **copy, not move**, so rollback is removal of the copies). The planner's `hash_updates` set gains the promoted docs' canonical paths → blake3 (phase 7 re-baselines them). Phase 5 stages the promoted paths; phase 7 invalidates the edge-index stamp (next read rebuilds against the new HEAD — `finalize.md` → 7; `storage.md` lifecycle site 5). Promote stays engine-planned where it is a pure decision (which docs, which paths); the copy + stamp-invalidation filesystem effects are CLI-side, consistent with the shell-free engine planner.
- **Reconciliation OOB classifier extends `file-state` to the full state machine** (`reconciliation.md` → State machine; inc-4 shipped only baseline-adopt + hash-matches). The classifier reads the `(committed-state, task-state)` pair over a **committed** managed doc: `UNKNOWN`→baseline-adopt (already shipped), `DRIFTED+UNTOUCHED`→parse-classify (parse+schema-validate clean → **absorb**: re-hash + incrementally update the committed edge index for the doc's edges + surface "external edit absorbed"; parse/schema fail → **conformance-block**: a blocking finding naming file+line+expected shape, no auto-repair), `DRIFTED+TOUCHED`→**conflict-block** (a blocking finding, file granularity, explicit-discard route, no silent merge). Each outcome reuses the one `Finding` envelope; absorb is the only edge-index incremental-update site (`storage.md` lifecycle site 3).
- **Rename detection is a separate classifier at the same trigger points** (`reconciliation.md` → Rename detection). Strong signal (a tracked path missing + an untracked path with the same recorded content hash) → a conformance-block finding naming the suspected `git mv` and the revert route; weak signal (tracked path missing, no content match) → a conformance-block finding routing to restore. MVP **blocks + routes to revert in git**, never auto-rewrites referrer refs (`jigc doc rename` is post-MVP). Reuses the one `Finding` envelope.
- **Headline acceptance path is one CLI integration test** (`worked-examples.md` → Superseding decision = flows #5 + #2): task 1 creates+finalizes `adr:single-node-cache` (committed to `decisions/`); task 2 creates `adr:shared-redis-session-cache`, sets `supersedes: adr:single-node-cache`, re-composes (the `superseded-context` step's `{{@task.decision.supersedes#decision}}` slices the committed ADR's `#decision` prose into a `> ` blockquote), and `finalize` passes the `ref-resolves` walk (target in the committed store). The **dangling variant** (`supersedes: adr:typo-nonexistent`) blocks finalize with the three-option `ref-resolves` finding. This single path converts the differentiators from supported to proven.

### Committed-store reader — `engine::store::read_slice` + `canonical_path` (2026-05-31, inc-5 seq 1)

- **`engine::store::read_slice(repo_root, schemas, &Address) -> Result<String, Finding>`** is the byte-read the inc-3 `Resolution::Content` handle deferred: resolve the address's type → its `Schema` in the cascade-fed set, compute the canonical path, read+parse the committed `.md` against the schema, slice the `#unit` fragment to its section slot span via `Span::slice` (byte-exact over the committed source). **`canonical_path(repo_root, schema, slug) -> Option<PathBuf>`** is the path resolver — `<repo_root>/<location>/<slug>.md`, `None` for a transient (location-less) type (`storage.md` → Identity is the path). Engine stays domain-empty (CLI feeds schema set + repo root). Why: flow #5's `{{@task.decision.supersedes#decision}}` must re-read `decisions/single-node-cache.md` and slice `#decision` losslessly (`parsing.md` → Round-trip guarantees).
- **MVP store-read target is a `#unit` (section slot) fragment** (flow #5's `#decision`); deeper shapes (`#unit/leaf`, `#unit/item[/leaf]`) surface a located `store.unsliceable-fragment` block rather than a panic — they are not store-read targets at this scope (the edge index walk + `.relation` hop wiring is later seqs this increment). Why: scope to exactly the slot-slice flow #5 needs; the fragment recognizer already types every depth, so widening later is additive.
- **Every failure is a blocking `store.*` `Finding` carrying a located message + route** (the one block-payload envelope, not a new type — `DECISIONS.md` 2026-05-31 → block payload): `store.unknown-type`, `store.no-fragment`, `store.transient-type`, `store.not-found` (missing file), `store.unparseable` (conformance fail — surfaces the first parse finding's message), `store.no-such-section`, `store.section-has-no-slot`. Why: the reader is called on the read path where a dangling/garbled committed target must route the agent, never panic.

### Edge-index committed rebuild — `engine::index` (2026-05-31, inc-5 seq 2)

- **`engine::index::rebuild_committed(repo_root, schemas, head) -> EdgeIndex`** is the committed-rebuild (lifecycle site 1, `storage.md` → Edge index lifecycle): for every schema with a `location:`, walk `<location>/*.md`, parse each against the schema, and emit one forward `Edge { from: "<type>:<slug>", relation: "<field-id>", to: "<value>" }` per present `ref`-typed field (a `Value::List` ref → one edge per element). `from` is identity-is-the-path; edges are `sort`+`dedup`ed by `(from, relation, to)`. An unparseable committed file is **skipped** (best-effort rebuild — per-doc conformance is the parse/finalize gate's concern, not the index's). Forward-only: the inverse (`superseded-by`) is derived on read, never stored (`document-type-schema.md` → Bidirectional; `DECISIONS.md` 2026-05-23). Pins the settled inc-5 planning format (this file → "Edge-index on-disk format + stamp pin" / "Edge extraction = schema `ref` fields"); resolves decisions-pending inc-5 "(D) Edge-index storage format + stamp/rebuild".
- **On-disk form pinned exactly + golden-locked**: `{ "stamp": "<HEAD-sha>", "edges": [ { "from", "relation", "to" } ] }` at `.jigc/index/edges.json` (`EdgeIndex::path_in`), `serde_json::to_string_pretty` + one trailing `\n` (the `file-state.json`/`base.json` convention), saved atomically via `state::persist` (temp+rename). **No `schema_version`** — the index is a rebuildable cache, re-derivable from the committed `.md`s (same stance as `FileStateRecord`). Stamp = the opaque HEAD text the **caller** supplies (CLI reads it via `git`, engine stays shell-free — inc-4 I/O split).
- **`load_committed(repo_root, jigc_root, schemas, head) -> EdgeIndex`** is the read entrypoint: a present `edges.json` whose `stamp == head` loads as-is; a missing/corrupt file or a stamp mismatch (`storage.md` → Derived caches: branch switch / pull / rebase) triggers a `rebuild_committed` + atomic re-save with the new stamp. Save failure is best-effort (the rebuilt index is returned regardless; the cache-stamp self-heals on the next read). The working overlay + `ref-resolves` walk + finalize stamp-invalidation are later inc-5 seqs (this seq is the committed-rebuild + stamped persistence only).

### Working overlay + `ref-resolves` forward-ref integrity — `engine::index` (2026-05-31, inc-5 seq 3)

- **`engine::index::overlay_working(committed: &EdgeIndex, task_dir, schemas) -> WorkingOverlay`** is the working overlay (lifecycle site 2, `storage.md` → Edge index lifecycle: "the index is never persisted with task deltas in it"): parse the active task's `<task_dir>/docs/*.md`, emit the same forward `(from, relation, to)` edges the committed rebuild emits, and layer them over `committed` **in-memory, never persisted**. A staged instance's `from` identity *is* its filename stem (already `<type>:<slug>`, the `:`-joined working-area slug — `DECISIONS.md` 2026-05-31 → working-area layout), its type prefix (before the first `:`) resolves the schema. Unknown-type / unparseable staged files contribute no edges (best-effort, mirroring the committed rebuild). **New public type `WorkingOverlay { committed, task_edges, task_froms }`** (not `Serialize` — a per-call in-memory derivation, not a contract surface): `task_froms` records each touched `from` identity so `ref_resolves` walks only **task-touched** edges (a committed-only edge is not this task's to fix); `committed` carries surface-a's edges for the overlaid graph (read by the later context-slice / inverse-derivation seqs).
- **`engine::index::ref_resolves(overlay, repo_root, task_dir, schemas) -> Vec<Finding>`** is the synthetic `schema-conformance.ref-resolves` check (the inc-5 forward-ref-integrity check deferred from inc-4): for every task-touched edge, the `to` target must resolve in one of the **two reachable surfaces** (`validation.md` → Forward-ref resolution) — **(a)** the committed store (`store::canonical_path(repo_root, schema, slug)` exists) or **(b)** this task's working area (`<task_dir>/docs/<to>.md` exists). A target in neither → one **blocking** `schema-conformance.ref-resolves` `Finding` located at the source doc, whose message names the **three routing options** (fix the ref / create the target in this task / drop the field — `worked-examples.md` → Superseding decision, the dangling variant), plus a `route`. Cross-task forward-refs are unsupported (same finding). Resolvable edge → empty; intrinsic blocking (no cascade tuning wired yet, parallels the other intrinsic checks). The overlay is read-side only — `ref_resolves` writes nothing; the finalize stamp-invalidation + `validate_task`/context-slice wiring are later inc-5 seqs.

### `as:` role binding at create + `jigc start --task <id>` resume (2026-05-31, inc-5 seq 4)

- **`engine::state::create_gated` now binds an admitted object-form (`as: <role>`) instance to the task's context role** (`write-commands.md` → The create-gate, step 4; resolves the inc-5 planning pin "`as:` role binding at create", deferred from inc-4 seq 8). On admit it mints+provisions via `create`, then records `role → <type>:<slug>` in `<task_dir>/roles.json` and saves. A **bare-form** entry — represented as an empty `as_role` — grants create permission but binds nothing (no `roles.json` written), matching the design's "bare form grants create permission without declaring a role." The struct-level bare form (omittable `as:` in YAML) is not parsed here — out of scope; the empty-`as_role` predicate is the bind switch. The CLI never infers a binding; the gate entry declares it.
- **New public type `engine::state::RolesRecord { roles: BTreeMap<String,String> }`** (Serialize/Deserialize, `Default`) — the task's bound context roles. On-disk `.jigc/tasks/<id>/roles.json`: `serde_json::to_string_pretty` + one trailing `\n`, **key-sorted** (the `base.json`/`file-state.json` convention) so the byte form is golden-stable (golden `roles_one_bound_role`). Atomic save (temp+rename via `write_atomic`); `load` returns an **empty record on a missing file** (no roles bound yet — mirrors `FileStateRecord::load`). No `schema_version` — task-local disposable working-area state, not a committed contract. Read back on resume so `task.<role>` resolves to the created doc.
- **Task **intent** is now persisted at mint** (`<task_dir>/intent`, plain text, byte-verbatim) and read back by resume via `state::read_intent`. The task id is a *lossy* slug of the intent, so the intent itself must be stored to re-compose `{{task.intent}}` on a `--task <id>` resume (`storage.md` → "it persists on disk across sessions, so work resumes"). Additive — the frozen `base.json` golden is untouched. `state::read_base_pin` reads the pin back for the resume base-mismatch check.
- **`jigc start --task <id>` resume wired (`cli::start::resume_in_repo`)** — the fourth `jigc start` form (`DECISIONS.md` 2026-05-31 → Four `jigc start` forms). It **mints nothing**: resolves the existing `.jigc/tasks/<id>/`, reads the base pin + persisted intent + `roles.json`, verifies the task's base still matches the current checkout (the CLI never operates a task off its pinned base — `storage.md` → A task is pinned to its base; a mismatch blocks with the switch-back/discard route), then re-composes with the bound roles in scope. A nonexistent id → `no task \`<id>\``. `build_context` now takes a `&RolesRecord`: each `allows-create` role declares bound iff `roles.json` recorded it (the inc-3 unbound `None` flips to `Some(address)` once created), so the `superseded-context` step's `{{@task.decision.supersedes#decision}}` resolves to `> adr:<slug>#decision` on resume vs an empty line before the create. `--task` carries `conflicts_with = "intent"` (clap rejects `<intent> --task` together).
- **Test:** engine `create_gate_binds_admitted_adr_to_task_role` (object-form `[{adr, as: decision}]` create writes `decision → adr:<slug>` to `roles.json`, read back; golden over the byte form; a bare-form empty-`as_role` entry writes nothing) + CLI integration `resume_re_composes_with_the_created_adr_bound_to_task_decision` (mint→compose shows no `> adr:` slice; `jigc doc create adr` writes `roles.json`; `jigc start --task <id>` re-composes with the slice resolved — the full bind round-trip). RED confirmed for both (the bind absent → `None`; `--task` unparsed). The store-byte-read behind the `Content` handle (the `.supersedes` edge hop + `#decision` slice) is the next inc-5 context-slice seq; this seq carries the bound-address handle (the inc-3 `Resolution::Content` stance).

### Context-slice resolution — `Resolution::Content` dereferenced through the edge hop + store (2026-05-31, inc-5 seq 5)

- **The composer dereferences `{{@task.decision.supersedes#decision}}` to the superseded ADR's `#decision` prose** (resolves the inc-5 planning pin "Context-slice resolution wires `Resolution::Content` to the committed reader + edge hop"; `worked-examples.md` → Superseding decision; `workflow-dialect.md` → Emitted format). The pure `data_value::Path::resolve` is unchanged (no I/O — the determinism boundary); the **edge-hop walk + store-byte-read** live behind a new **`engine::compose::ContentStore` trait** (`walk_edge(from, relation) -> Option<String>` over the overlaid graph; `read_slice(&Address) -> Result<String, Finding>` over the committed store). `Resolution::Content { address }` carries the bound role's doc; the composer (`deref_content`) walks the path's hops **past the role** (`hops[1..]`, e.g. `supersedes`) one edge each, attaches the path's own `#fragment` to the landing doc, and slices via the store. Emitted as a **multi-line `> ` blockquote** (`blockquote()` prefixes every prose line; a blank line → bare `>`), byte-exact over the committed slice.
- **Reference impl `engine::compose::StoreContext<'a> { repo_root, schemas, overlay: &WorkingOverlay }`** wires `walk_edge` → `WorkingOverlay::walk_edge` (new method: task edges shadow committed; first match wins; `None` = unset relation) and `read_slice` → `engine::store::read_slice`. The CLI builds one per compose and feeds it in (CLI locates layers + builds the overlay, engine resolves — `VISION.md` #4); the engine stays domain-empty + shell-free.
- **Empty-vs-unresolvable extended to the bound-but-no-edge role** (`workflow-dialect.md` → Empty vs unresolvable; `worked-examples.md` flow #1): an **unbound** `task.decision` (`Resolution::Absent`) OR a **bound** role with an **unset** `supersedes` (`walk_edge` → `None`) short-circuits to an **empty line** — no `> `, zero findings. A missing/unparseable/unsliceable dereference target still surfaces the store's one routed block envelope.
- **`emit_step_body` / `compose` keep their pure (store-less) signatures** (the structural `workflow_refs` gate + the existing emitter/compose goldens are I/O-free and unchanged — they carry the inc-3 address handle for a `Content` line); the store path is the new **`emit_step_body_with` / `compose_with_store`** siblings taking `Option<&dyn ContentStore>`. So the compose-time gate never touches the filesystem; only the live read path dereferences.
- **Test:** engine `superseded_context_slices_the_prior_decision` (committed `adr:single-node-cache` + a staged `adr:shared-redis-session-cache` with `supersedes: adr:single-node-cache`, `task.decision` bound → the step emits the committed `#decision` prose as a multi-line `> ` blockquote, snapshot-pinned byte-exact) + `superseded_context_empty_when_no_edge` (bound decision but unset `supersedes` → empty; unbound decision → empty, no `>`). RED confirmed by stubbing `deref_content` to the address handle — both failed on real assertions (the slice showed `> adr:…#decision`, not the prose), then restored. The CLI compose-call wiring of `compose_with_store` + the headline end-to-end integration is the inc-5 headline-acceptance seq (this seq is the engine resolution).

### Finalize promote phase — staged managed docs → canonical paths + `hash_updates` (2026-05-31, inc-5 seq 6)

- **`engine::finalize::plan_finalize` now plans phase 4 (promote)** (`finalize.md` → 4. Promote / 7. Post-commit; `worked-examples.md` flow #5 `[4 promote]`; resolves the inc-5 pin "Promote phase at finalize"): it takes the full cascade `schemas: &BTreeMap<String, Schema>` and walks `<task_dir>/docs/*.md`; for every staged instance whose type declares a `location:`, it names a **`Promotion { source: PathBuf, destination: String }`** (`source` = the staged path, `destination` = the canonical repo-relative `<location>/<slug>.md`) and records `destination → blake3(staged bytes)` into `hash_updates`. The filename stem *is* the `<type>:<slug>` address; the type prefix resolves the schema. A **location-less type (the commit doc) is excluded** — its sink is the rendered git message, never a repo file — so a commit-only task still promotes nothing (the inc-4 behaviour, now expressed by the location predicate rather than a hard-coded no-op). Promotions sort by destination (deterministic). The hash is over the staged bytes because the copy is byte-stable (the inc-2 round-trip property), so the engine plan and the CLI's post-commit `git show`-derived hash agree.
- **New public `engine::finalize::Promotion`** (Serialize/Deserialize, part of the `FinalizePlan` contract) + a new **`FinalizePlan::promotions: Vec<Promotion>`** field. The plan is **copy-not-move**: the engine decides *what lands where*, the CLI performs the copy (a private `PromotePlan` struct carries the planner's `(promotions, hash_updates)` to keep the return type clippy-clean; `plan_finalize` carries an explicit-input `#[allow(too_many_arguments)]` — the determinism contract feeds every layer in rather than re-deriving it).
- **CLI executes promote before staging** (`cli::task`): `Task::promote` copies each `Promotion` to `<repo_root>/<destination>` (creating `decisions/` when absent) **before** `git add --all`, so the promoted ADR is staged and committed; `Task::rollback_promotions` (on a hook/git rejection) `git restore --staged --worktree`s each destination and deletes any **newly-promoted** copy (a path absent at HEAD per `git cat-file -e HEAD:<path>`), leaving the working area intact. `advance_file_state` already records the plan's `hash_updates`, so the promoted doc's `file-state` baseline lands at phase 7.
- **`is_author_required` now exempts an optional `ref`** (forward `card:` min 0, e.g. the ADR `supersedes` `0..1`) from `required-field-present` (`validation.md` → the synthetic `ref-resolves` check owns ref *resolution*, not *presence*; the inc-5 gap the prior comment flagged). Without this the headline flow-#5 setup ADR (which supersedes nothing) could not validate clean, so promotion could never be exercised end-to-end. A ref with no `card:` (defaults `0..1`) or a `card:` starting `0` is optional; presence stays required for non-ref author fields.
- **Test:** engine golden `finalize_plan_promotes_a_staged_adr` (a task staging a commit doc + `adr:single-node-cache` → the plan promotes exactly `decisions/single-node-cache.md` with its blake3 in `hash_updates`; the commit doc is absent from both; snapshot over the promote set + hash) + CLI integration `finalize_promotes_a_created_adr_to_decisions` (create an ADR via the create-gate, fill its slots, finalize → `git show HEAD:decisions/single-node-cache.md` equals the staged bytes byte-for-byte and `file-state.json` records the path). RED confirmed: the prior no-op produced an empty promote set / empty `hash_updates`, and `git show` of the canonical path failed.

### Ref-resolves wired into `validate_task` + edge-index stamp invalidated at post-commit (2026-05-31, inc-5 seq 7)

- **`engine::validate::validate_task` now runs `schema-conformance.ref-resolves`** as the final, once-per-task cross-doc sweep, so `finalize` (which gates on exactly what `validate` reports) blocks on a dangling `supersedes` and passes on a resolvable one (`validation.md` → Forward-ref resolution / How it gates `finalize`; `finalize.md` → 2. Validate; `worked-examples.md` flow #5 `[2 validate]` + the dangling variant). The signature grew **`repo_root: &Path, jigc_root: &Path, head: &str`** (the committed-store root, the `.jigc/` home where the index caches, and the opaque HEAD stamp the CLI reads via `git` — engine stays shell-free): it `index::load_committed`s the committed edge index against `head`, `index::overlay_working`s this task's staged edges over it, and extends the findings with `index::ref_resolves` (every task-touched forward edge must resolve in the committed store or this task's working area). The three existing per-instance `schema-conformance` checks are unchanged.
- **`engine::index::invalidate(jigc_root)`** (new pub fn) — lifecycle site 5 (`storage.md` → Edge index lifecycle: "finalize commit → phase 7 invalidates the stamp; next read rebuilds against the new HEAD"). It **removes `<jigc_root>/index/edges.json`** outright: an absent file can never match a stamp, so the next `load_committed` rebuilds + re-stamps against the current HEAD — the strongest invalidation. Best-effort by contract: a not-found is success; any other error is returned for the CLI to log, never raise (the commit is already truth, and the stale on-disk stamp ≠ new HEAD self-heals regardless). Pin: **removal (not a stale-stamp rewrite)** is the invalidation form — simplest correct realization of the doc's "invalidate the stamp," and consistent with the rebuildable-cache stance (delete `.jigc/` caches, rebuild from `.md`s, nothing lost).
- **CLI post-commit (`cli::task::post_commit`) now invalidates the edge index** between advancing the `file-state` hashes and removing the working area, via `engine::index::invalidate(&self.jigc_root)` — best-effort (a failure is logged, not raised). `Task::validate` reads HEAD (`git_head`) and feeds the three new args in, so `task validate` previews the same forward-ref gate `finalize` enforces.
- **Test:** engine `validate_task_blocks_on_dangling_supersedes` (a staged ADR `supersedes: adr:typo-nonexistent` over a committed store → exactly one blocking `schema-conformance.ref-resolves` finding naming the target; `has_blocking()`), `validate_task_passes_on_resolvable_supersedes` (supersede target committed → no ref-resolves finding, not blocking), and `post_commit_invalidates_edge_stamp` (after `invalidate`, the persisted index is gone and the next `load_committed` against the new HEAD re-stamps — never serves the pre-commit stamp; invalidation is idempotent). CLI integration `finalize_blocks_on_a_dangling_supersedes_ref` (create an ADR, inject `supersedes: adr:typo-nonexistent` into the staged front-matter, finalize → exit non-zero surfacing the three routing options, no commit, no promotion — matches worked-examples flow #5's dangling variant). RED confirmed by stubbing the ref-resolves block out of `validate_task` — the dangling test failed showing only the baseline-adopt advisory (no ref-resolves finding), then restored.

### Reconciliation OOB classifier — `file_state::reconcile_committed`, the full state machine (2026-05-31, inc-5 seq 8)

- **`engine::file_state::reconcile_committed(record, index, schema, path, from, bytes, task_touched) -> Vec<Finding>`** extends the inc-4 `file_state` path (baseline-adopt + hash-matches) into the **full OOB reconciliation state machine over a *committed* managed doc** (resolves decisions-pending `(I) Reconciliation state machine`; `reconciliation.md` → The state machine / OOB parse classifier / Conflict / Hash re-baselining; `storage.md` → Edge index lifecycle, site 3; `worked-examples.md` flow #2; `DECISIONS.md` 2026-05-31 → inc-5 Reconciliation OOB classifier). It reads the `(committed-state, task-state)` pair and routes deterministically: `UNKNOWN` → **baseline-adopt** (record the hash, advisory `file-state.baseline-adopt`); `IN_SYNC` → no finding; `DRIFTED + TOUCHED` → **conflict-block**; `DRIFTED + UNTOUCHED` → the **parse classifier**.
- **Absorb** (DRIFTED+UNTOUCHED, clean reparse): `parse_sections` + `validate::schema_conformance` both clean → **re-hash** the recorded baseline forward (re-baseline site 2 of three) **and** `index.absorb_doc` the edited doc's edges into the committed index in place (the only edge-index incremental-update site — `storage.md` lifecycle site 3), surfacing an **advisory** `reconciliation.absorb` "external edit absorbed: `<doc>`" (no route — a clean edit is honored, not repaired).
- **Conformance-block** (DRIFTED+UNTOUCHED, parse/schema fail): a **blocking** `reconciliation.conformance-block` finding naming the file + the **first** parse/schema cause (its message + line re-located onto the file) + a fix-or-revert route; the recorded hash is **not** advanced and the edge index is **not** touched — **no auto-repair** (`reconciliation.md` → Auto-repair scope).
- **Conflict-block** (DRIFTED+TOUCHED): a **blocking** `reconciliation.conflict-block` finding, file granularity, carrying the **explicit-discard** route (`jigc task discard-write <path>` / revert on disk); no silent merge (three-way merge deferred), hash + index untouched.
- **New pin — `EdgeIndex::absorb_doc(&mut self, schema, from, doc)`** (the absorb-site incremental update): drop every edge whose `from == from`, re-insert the freshly-parsed forward edges, keep the set sorted+deduped; the `stamp` is left untouched (absorb shifts one doc's edges in place, it does not re-baseline the whole index against a new HEAD — that is the finalize-commit site). Realizes `storage.md` lifecycle site 3 "atomic, in-place."
- **Pin — each outcome reuses the one `Finding` envelope** (the settled block payload: severity + located message + route); absorb/baseline-adopt are advisory/route-less (informational), conformance-block/conflict-block are blocking + route-bearing. The classifier is pure of I/O — the caller (the CLI, a later seq) supplies the on-disk bytes + the task-touched bit and persists the advanced record/index. CLI wiring of `reconcile_committed` into the read/start/validate triggers is out of this single-concern engine task's scope.
- **Test:** engine `oob_clean_edit_absorbs_and_updates_index` (a committed ADR with a recorded baseline, edited on disk to add `supersedes: adr:single-node-cache` → one advisory absorb finding, the recorded hash advances to the edited content, the committed index gains exactly the new forward edge), `oob_nonconformant_edit_blocks` (an edit malforming `date` → blocking conformance-block naming the file, hash unadvanced, index untouched — no auto-repair), and `oob_conflict_blocks` (the same drift with `task_touched` → one blocking conflict-block carrying the discard/revert route, no silent merge). RED confirmed first via a stub returning no findings — all three failed on the "emits exactly one finding" / "conformance-blocks" real assertions — then the classifier landed. 175 engine lib tests + all CLI suites green.

### Rename detection — `file_state::detect_rename`, the separate missing-path classifier (2026-05-31, inc-5 seq 9)

- **`engine::file_state::detect_rename(path, from, recorded_hash, untracked) -> Vec<Finding>`** is the separate rename classifier for a tracked managed-doc path gone *missing* on disk — the case the `reconcile_committed` state machine cannot catch (it sees the old path as absent and a `git mv` target as a fresh untracked file). Resolves the last open inc-5 reconciliation item (`reconciliation.md` → Rename detection / No silent rename; `storage.md` → Identity: a path change is an identity change; `DECISIONS.md` 2026-05-31 → Rename detection pin). Strong signal (some untracked path carries the same `recorded_hash` → suspected `git mv`): one **blocking** `reconciliation.rename` finding naming both paths, routed to `git mv <suspect> <tracked>` (revert). Weak signal (no content match): one **blocking** `reconciliation.rename` finding routed to **restore**. The first hash-matching candidate in `untracked` order is named.
- **Pin — no auto-rewrite, no edge/referrer mutation.** A path rename is an identity change; the MVP blocks and routes to human-side revert, and the detector is pure of I/O and of any edge/referrer mutation by construction (it reads inputs, returns findings). `jigc doc rename` (atomic re-key + ref rewrite) is post-MVP. Reuses the one `Finding` envelope (the settled block payload: blocking severity + located message + route).
- **Pin — pure-of-I/O signature mirrors `reconcile_committed`.** The caller (CLI, a later seq) collects the missing tracked path + its recorded hash + the on-disk untracked candidates as `(path, raw-byte hash)` pairs and feeds them in; the engine stays shell-free. CLI wiring of the detector into the trigger points is out of this single-concern engine task's scope.
- **Test:** engine `rename_strong_signal_routes_to_git_mv_revert` (`decisions/rate-limit.md` missing, `decisions/gateway-rate-limit.md` carries the same recorded hash → one blocking `reconciliation.rename` naming both paths + a `git mv … revert` route) and `rename_weak_signal_routes_to_restore` (the tracked path missing, an unrelated untracked file with a different hash → one blocking restore-routed finding). RED confirmed first via a stub returning no findings — both failed on the "emits exactly one finding" real assertion — then the detector landed. 177 engine lib tests + all CLI suites green.

### Headline acceptance — the superseding-decision flow end-to-end (2026-05-31, inc-5 seq 10)

- **`crates/cli/tests/superseding_decision.rs`** (new) proves flows #5 + #2 as one CLI integration test over a real temp git repo — the **mandated differentiator proof** (`CLAUDE.md` → MVP scope; `worked-examples.md` → 5. Superseding decision; `roadmap.md` → Increment 5 Proves). Two tests: (1) `superseding_decision_slices_the_prior_committed_adr_and_passes_the_edge_walk` — task 1 creates+finalizes `adr:single-node-cache` (asserts `decisions/single-node-cache.md` committed), task 2 creates `adr:shared-redis-session-cache` + `supersedes: adr:single-node-cache`, re-composes (asserts the `superseded-context` step slices the **prior committed decision prose** as a `> ` blockquote, NOT the bare address handle), and finalize lands exactly one commit promoting the new ADR (the forward edge survives in the committed file); (2) `dangling_supersedes_blocks_finalize_with_the_three_routing_options` — a `supersedes: adr:typo-nonexistent` makes finalize exit non-zero naming the target + the three routes (fix / create-in-task / drop), no commit, nothing promoted.
- **Production wiring (`cli::start::resume_in_repo`) — the context-slice over a persisted ADR is now live on the read path.** Resume switched from the pure `compose::compose` to `compose::compose_with_store` with a `StoreContext` built from `index::load_committed` (committed edge rebuild + stamp) + `index::overlay_working` (this task's staged edges) over the cascade schema set (`all_schemas`, mirroring `cli::task`). This is the CLI wiring inc-5 seqs 1/2/3/5 explicitly deferred to the headline-acceptance seq: the engine resolution existed but no front-door call reached it. Pin: only **resume** wires the store (the mint-time `compose_in_repo` binds no roles yet, so a store would resolve nothing — kept on the pure path to avoid a needless committed-index rebuild at mint).
- **Corrected semantics (`start_resume.rs` updated, not regressed).** With the store wired, a bound-but-non-superseding `task.decision` resolves the `{{@task.decision.supersedes#decision}}` placeholder to **empty** (the `.supersedes` edge is unset → absent value → empty text, the `worked-examples.md` Task 2 contract), rather than the old store-less behavior of emitting the bound role's own `> adr:<slug>#decision` address handle. `start_resume.rs`'s final assertion flipped to assert the now-correct empty slice; its true single concern (the `as:` role bind round-trips via `roles.json`) is unchanged and still asserted. The full slice-of-a-committed-ADR path is the new headline test's job.

### Increment 6 seq 1 — pack declares its own id (2026-05-31)

- **Pack identity is cascade-sourced, not a CLI constant.** The embedded dev pack's `config/defaults.yaml` now declares `pack-id: dev` (alongside `default-workflow`); `cli::orient::orient_with` reads it via `pack_id_from_config` and feeds it to `PackDefaultLayer::new`, so the `Pack: <id>/<version>` provenance segment comes from the pack-default layer (`overrides.md` → the pack names itself). The `DEV_PACK_ID` hardcode + its `// increment 6` comment are gone. *Pin:* the id rides in the existing `defaults` config resource (not a new `config/pack.yaml`) — one config resource per layer; a second file for one scalar is needless. Test: `orient::pack_segment_is_sourced_from_pack_config` (a `FakePack` declaring `pack-id: xyz` renders `Pack: xyz/…`) + `pack::embedded_pack_config_declares_pack_id`.

### Increment 6 seq 2 — embedded Claude Code adapter profile + typed model/loader (2026-05-31)

- **Adapter profiles are embedded config-family data, pack-parallel.** New `crates/cli/adapters/claude-code.yaml` embedded via `include_dir!("$CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR/adapters")` (mirroring `pack::PACK`); `cli::adapter::load_profile(assistant)` resolves `adapters/<assistant>.yaml`, UTF-8-decodes, and `serde_yaml_ng`-parses into a typed `AdapterProfile { assistant, inject, allowlist }` (`assistant-adapter.md` → The adapter profile; `module-layout.md` → Adapter (in `cli`)). *Pins:* the model carries **only the MVP surfaces** — the inject **line** floor (`InjectTarget { line: LineTarget { file, scope } }`) + the **allowlist** (`{ file, permit }`); the post-MVP **hook** / **spawn** profile fields are absent from both the model and the shipped bytes, so the golden over the file *is* the canonical contract (what you test is what ships). `ProfileError` is a **hand-rolled** `Display`/`Error` enum (`NotFound` / `NotUtf8` / `Malformed`), **no `thiserror` added to `cli`** — the engine/CLI error split holds (thiserror=engine, anyhow=cli); the typed `NotFound` exists only because `jigc setup` must route unknown-assistant to a clear install error and the loader test matches on it. `mod adapter` is `#[allow(dead_code)]` until the `jigc setup` host-file injection wires it in (a later inc-6 task — the established pre-wire pattern). Tests: `claude_code_profile_loads_with_inject_and_allowlist_targets` + a byte golden + `unknown_assistant_yields_not_found`.

### Increment 6 seq 3 — marker-fenced bootstrap block (pure) (2026-05-31)

- **`cli::adapter::bootstrap_block() -> String` is the pure block generator** — the canonical routing sentence (`bootstrap.md` → The sentence, verbatim) wrapped in idempotency markers, no filesystem (`assistant-adapter.md` → Inject the bootstrap: the static line is the universal floor; MVP = line only). *Pin — marker tokens:* `<!-- jigc:bootstrap:start -->` … `<!-- jigc:bootstrap:end -->` (HTML comments so they're invisible in rendered markdown; the `jigc:` namespace disambiguates against any other tool's markers). The markers make `jigc setup` injection idempotent — a re-run locates and replaces the span between them instead of appending a duplicate. *Pin — sentence fidelity:* the backticks around `jigc` / `jigc start` are content (they fence command tokens, as the routing footer does); the doc's surrounding `**bold**` is blockquote presentation and is dropped. Block bytes = `start\n` + sentence + `\nend\n` (one trailing newline), golden-locked byte-exact by `bootstrap_block_is_the_marker_fenced_sentence`. The host-file read/splice (CLAUDE.md + the `.claude/settings.json` allowlist) stays in the later `jigc setup` task — the decisions-pending `(D) jigc setup injection mechanism` item covers that broader work and remains.

### Increment 6 seq 4 — idempotent injection of the bootstrap block into CLAUDE.md (2026-05-31)

- **`cli::adapter::inject_line(repo_root) -> io::Result<()>` is the static-line floor's host-file write** — splices [`bootstrap_block`] into `<repo_root>/CLAUDE.md`, idempotent by the marker fence (`assistant-adapter.md` → Generated, minimal, regenerated: regenerated on upgrade, can't rot; `bootstrap.md` → Placement is the adapter). *Pins:* (1) **three cases** — missing file ⇒ created with just the block; no marker present ⇒ block **appended** after the existing content with a single blank-line separator (an empty file degenerates to just the block); markers present ⇒ only the fenced region replaced. (2) **Splice form** — replace `[start-of-start-marker .. end-of-end-marker)`, inserting the block *sans* its trailing `\n` (the newline after the end marker lives in the preserved suffix), so re-injecting the current block is byte-identical and every byte outside the fence is untouched. (3) **First-marker located** — `marker_span` takes the first start/end pair; a conformant file (only jigc ever wrote the fence) has exactly one. Tests: `inject_creates_then_is_idempotent`, `inject_replaces_only_between_markers`, `inject_appends_when_no_marker_present` (run-twice ⇒ byte-identical; surrounding user prose preserved; goldens over the resulting file). The `.claude/settings.json` allowlist write is the remaining half of the decisions-pending `(D) jigc setup injection mechanism` item, so that item stays.

### Increment 6 seq 5 — idempotent allowlist merge into .claude/settings.json (2026-05-31)

- **`cli::adapter::inject_allowlist(repo_root, &profile) -> io::Result<()>` is the allowlist step's host-file write** — a **structure-aware** `serde_json::Value` merge into `<repo_root>/<profile.allowlist.file>`, not a text splice (`assistant-adapter.md` → Make `jigc` frictionless; `module-layout.md` → Adapter (in `cli`)). Idempotency is by **structural presence**, not text markers (per DECISIONS allowlist pin): parse-or-create the settings object, navigate/create `permissions.allow`, push each profile permit only if absent, write back `to_string_pretty` + one trailing `\n`. *Pins:* (1) missing file ⇒ created (incl. the `.claude/` dir) with just the permit(s); missing `permissions`/`allow` ⇒ created. (2) Unrelated top-level keys + unrelated permissions preserved; a present permit is a no-op so a re-run is byte-identical. (3) `serde_json` ships without `preserve_order`, so object keys serialize in deterministic (BTree) order — byte-stable, the property idempotency needs. Tests: `allowlist_added_then_idempotent`, `allowlist_preserves_existing_settings` (goldens over the resulting JSON; run-twice ⇒ byte-identical). This closes the second half of the `(D) jigc setup injection mechanism` decisions-pending item (the CLAUDE.md half closed in seq 4), so the item is removed.

### Increment 6 seq 6 — `jigc setup` orchestrates the install, reports via finding (2026-05-31)

- **`jigc setup` is the adapter-install front door** — a new clap `Command::Setup` (no args; single-assistant MVP, `--assistant` is post-MVP) dispatching `cli::setup::run` which **locates** the repo root (CLI locates), loads the embedded `claude-code` profile, and runs both injectors (`adapter::inject_line` → `CLAUDE.md`, `adapter::inject_allowlist` → `.claude/settings.json`) — `assistant-adapter.md` → Generated, minimal, regenerated (items 1+2; hook/spawn out of scope). Idempotent by the injectors' own idempotency (re-run ⇒ both files byte-identical). *Pins:* (1) **Outcome rides the finding envelope** — success ⇒ `SetupSummary{line_file, allowlist_file}` (a plain summary); a write failure ⇒ a single **blocking `setup.*` finding carrying a route** (`setup.repo-root` / `setup.profile-load` / `setup.inject-line` / `setup.inject-allowlist`), mapped to a non-zero exit (`DECISIONS.md` 2026-05-31 → block-payload = a blocking finding with a route, not a new type). (2) **New `Finding::block(code, message, route)` constructor** in the engine — the route-carrying blocking complement to `Finding::blocking` (no location), the natural producer for operations that point at a human action, not a source coordinate. (3) `mod adapter`'s pre-wire `#[allow(dead_code)]` is **removed** (the injectors + loader are now reached through `setup`). Tests: `cli::cli_parse::setup_parses`, `setup::install_writes_both_targets_and_reports_paths`, `setup::install_failure_yields_blocking_finding_with_route`, and the `tests/setup.rs` integration `setup_installs_line_and_allowlist` (both files carry the markers/permit; exit 0; run-twice ⇒ byte-identical).

### Increment 6 seq 7 — release build verification + quickstart (2026-05-31)

- **The shipped artifact is verified by a release-smoke integration test + a `QUICKSTART.md`** (`roadmap.md` → Increment 6: *Release build + a quickstart*; **Proves:** the path of least resistance exists on a real machine). *Pins:* (1) **`crates/cli/tests/release_smoke.rs`** drives the *built* binary (`CARGO_BIN_EXE_jigc`) for three asserts — `jigc --version` == `jigc <CARGO_PKG_VERSION>` (the artifact names itself `jigc`, the settled name); `jigc setup` in a fresh temp repo exits 0 and leaves both adapter files; `QUICKSTART.md` exists and names the three loop commands. No new test crate — same hand-rolled self-cleaning `TempDir` + bare `.git` marker pattern as `tests/setup.rs`. (2) **`QUICKSTART.md` at the repo root** documents the MVP loop install → `jigc setup` → `jigc start "<intent>"` → `jigc task finalize <id>`, **referenced from CLAUDE.md → Project state** (the docs index). (3) **No version bump** — the workspace stays `0.0.0` (pre-release); the test asserts whatever `CARGO_PKG_VERSION` is, so a later bump needs no test edit. Closes the decisions-pending `(I) Release / cross-compile + quickstart` item (cross-compile is out of MVP scope; a clean `--release -p cli` build + the quickstart is the floor).

## 2026-05-31 — audit remediation (committed-store reconciliation wired to a command)

- **Committed-store OOB reconciliation now fires inside `engine::validate::validate_task`** — the one sweep `jigc task validate` and `finalize` (preflight) both call (`reconciliation.md` → Detection timing: the `task validate` full sweep, shared by finalize). Closes the audit gap where the inc-5 classifier (`reconcile_committed` / `detect_rename`) was unit-tested but never invoked in production. New `engine::file_state::reconcile_committed_store` walks each persisted (`location:`-bearing) schema's committed `*.md`, reconciles each against its recorded baseline — `task_touched` = the same `<type>:<slug>` is staged in this task's `docs/` (→ conflict-block), conformant drift → absorb (advisory, folds the doc's new edges into the in-memory committed index that feeds `ref-resolves`), nonconformant → conformance-block — and routes a recorded-but-missing path to `detect_rename`. **Baselines are sourced as the design already specifies, no new wiring:** first-sight `UNKNOWN` → baseline-adopt; the committed baseline is recorded at `finalize` phase 7 (`task.rs::advance_file_state` already hashes every committed file from `git show`). The record is loaded-not-persisted during `validate` (re-baselining is an adopt/absorb/commit site, not a validate). The e2e proof is `crates/cli/tests/e2e_audit.rs` `scenario_6_*` (flipped from documenting the gap to asserting the contract): a conformant committed-doc OOB edit absorbs (validate stays clean, surfaces "external edit absorbed"); a nonconformant one conformance-blocks finalize (no commit). Engine unit tests: `file_state::tests::reconcile_committed_store_{sweeps_and_routes_drift, routes_a_missing_recorded_doc_to_rename}`. Resolves the decisions-pending inc-5 reconciliation item's last open thread (the classifier existed; this wires its trigger).

## 2026-05-31 — audit remediation (medium/low review findings)

- **Stricter scalar field-value adjudication** (`engine::write::check_scalar` / `is_iso_date`): `is_iso_date` now enforces a per-month day ceiling (rejects `2026-02-31`, `2026-04-31`; February allows 29 with no leap computation — the canonical date comes from the `set: on-create` deriver), and `string`/`ref`/`code-anchor` scalars reject control chars (a newline would inject a second field line on splice). Latent in the MVP pack (no string body fields); a correctness floor for future packs.
- **Empty-commit guard counts untracked files** (`cli::task`): `git diff <base>` lists only tracked changes but finalize stages `git add --all`, so an untracked-only task could abort as falsely "empty". Added `git_untracked` (`git ls-files --others --exclude-standard`) to the diff signal.
- **`jigc doc` honors `--format`** (`cli::doc`): a blocking finding now renders through the global `--format` (JSON envelope under `--format json`, message+route otherwise) via a `DocFailure::Block(Finding)` carried to dispatch; orchestration (IO/usage) errors stay plain text. The flag was previously parsed but inert on `doc`.
- **commit-message projection coupling, made explicit** (`engine::write::render_commit_message`): the renderer is the **engine-adjacent projection of the one doctype whose sink is the VCS message** — it necessarily encodes the Conventional-Commits shape (`<type>(<scope>): <summary>` + body + trailers), so it references the commit doctype's well-known ids by name (now the centralized `COMMIT_*` constants, with an honest doc-comment replacing the over-claimed "schema-driven"). A *deliberate, documented* coupling for the single VCS-sink type — **not** a defect to "fix" by building a schema-declared message-template engine, which would be generality for a single-use thing (PRINCIPLES). Generalizing to a declared template is post-MVP if a second VCS-sink type ever lands. The header section is still located via the schema's `header` flag, never by id.

## 2026-05-31 — adapter install reworked (bootstrap by reference + project-layer init)

Three install issues surfaced by real `jigc setup` testing, fixed as one cohesive change to `cli::adapter` / `cli::setup` (design updated in [assistant-adapter.md](design/assistant-adapter.md)):

- **Bootstrap is now by reference, not inlined.** Instead of writing the marker-fenced sentence *block* into `CLAUDE.md`, `setup` writes the bootstrap sentence to a **managed `.jigc/AGENT.md`** (fully CLI-owned, regenerated on every `setup`) and injects a **bare `@.jigc/AGENT.md` import line** into `CLAUDE.md` (under a short heading), idempotent by detecting that exact reference line — **no marker comments in `CLAUDE.md`**. Keeps the always-loaded file clean and the bootstrap content rot-proof (the *structure-is-generated* discipline applied to the floor). Placement chosen 2026-05-31: `.jigc/AGENT.md` (adapter output) kept **out** of the cascade config dir `.jigc/config/` (cascade deltas) — clean separation, no risk the cascade loader trips on a markdown file.
- **`setup` initializes the project layer.** It now creates `.jigc/config/` (with a `.gitkeep`) when absent. `.jigc/.gitignore` already commits everything except `tasks/`/`index/`/`state/`, so `config/`, its `.gitkeep`, and `AGENT.md` are tracked.
- **Orientation reads "set up" after `setup`.** Root cause: `orient`'s clean/unset discriminator keys on `.jigc/config/` presence (`cli::locate` → `PROJECT_CONFIG_REL`), which `setup` never created — so a set-up project reported "isn't set up". Creating the project layer in `setup` (above) fixes it; the discriminator is unchanged.
- **`SessionStart` hook pulled into MVP** (was post-MVP). `setup` now installs the Claude Code `SessionStart` hook running bare `jigc start` (read-only orientation) into `.claude/settings.json`, alongside the `jigc *` allowlist — the *primary* bootstrap injection (advertise+demonstrate at session start), idempotent. The `Resume`/post-compaction hook (capability-gated) and the fan-out **spawn** binding stay post-MVP. Scope statements updated in [module-layout.md](implementation/module-layout.md) → Adapter and [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → Increment 6. Prompted by real testing (setup left no hook); guarded going forward by an end-to-end `setup` test asserting **every** install artifact (reference line, `AGENT.md`, `config/`, `.gitignore`, allowlist, hook) so a missing-artifact regression can't recur.

Implementation-shape picks for the hook install (the *how*, the above being the *what*):

- **`InjectTarget` is an `#[serde(untagged)]` enum of struct-variants** (`Reference { reference: ReferenceTarget }` / `Hook { hook: HookTarget }`), each carrying the YAML map key as a single named field. Untagged — *not* serde's externally-tagged enum — because each `inject:` list entry is a one-key map (`{ reference: … }` / `{ hook: … }`), which serde_yaml_ng would otherwise demand as a `!Reference` YAML tag; the two variants' inner fields are disjoint, so untagged disambiguation is unambiguous. `AdapterProfile::reference()` / `::hook()` are typed `find_map` accessors returning the first of each (setup reads them; a missing reference is a `setup.profile-incomplete` blocking finding). `HookTarget { event, run }` round-trips `{ event: SessionStart, run: "jigc start" }`. The `Resume`/spawn variants stay absent until shipped (what we test is what ships).
- **The hook merge is structure-aware, idempotent by structural presence** (mirrors the allowlist merge, `cli::adapter::inject_hook`): parse-or-create the settings object, navigate/create `hooks.<event>` (array of matcher objects), and push a `{ hooks: [ { type: command, command: <run> } ] }` matcher **only if** no existing matcher under the event already runs `<run>` — so a re-run is a byte-identical no-op and unrelated events/matchers/top-level keys are preserved. Allowlist and hook share one settings file via two sequential read-modify-write passes (`read_settings`/`write_settings` extracted as the shared open/serialize helpers); each pass is an independent structural no-op on re-run, so running both stays byte-stable.

## 2026-06-01 — milestone-planning workflow + the product-build loop

- **Added [implementation/milestone-planning-workflow.md](implementation/milestone-planning-workflow.md)** — the *opening* bookend of the milestone lifecycle (scope → detect gaps → settle → review → decompose), symmetric to [milestone-completion](implementation/milestone-completion-workflow.md). It owns turning **one** milestone (the next, never all up front) into ordered increments, with an explicit **gap-detection** pass (decisions / docs / doctypes / capabilities) before decomposition so unsettled gates surface at planning, not as mid-build halts, and an **independent review** of the settled docs — baked back before decomposition — so the increment plan isn't cut from unreviewed design. Names the product-build loop it sits in: *once* — full-product roadmap (milestones only) + a light doctype/relation map; *per milestone* — planning → increment-workflow ×N → milestone-completion → repeat. *Why now:* MVP done, planning the next milestone; we'd documented how a milestone **ends** and how an increment **runs** but not how a milestone **opens**. *Why doctypes accrete, not pre-defined:* a doctype's schema is set by the workflow that creates/reads it, so it earns its schema in the milestone that ships that workflow — defining all doctypes up front is generality for single-use things (PRINCIPLES). Cross-refs fixed in completion/increment workflow set-enumerations + CLAUDE.md.

## 2026-06-01 — full-product milestone roadmap (the spine)

- **Promoted `implementation/roadmap.md` from MVP-only to the full-product milestone spine.** Arc, risk-first: **M1** single-task ✅ → **M2** router + 2nd work-workflow → **M3** spec-driven planning → **M4** override machinery → **M5** milestone execution (fan-out/join) → **M6** project setup (new & existing). Spine is **milestones-only** for M2–M6 — each decomposed into increments by the [milestone-planning workflow](implementation/milestone-planning-workflow.md) *when picked up*, never all up front; M1 keeps its shipped six-increment decomposition as the record + worked example. *Two ordering calls (vs the first-draft arc):* **M2 is its own milestone** (a clean "selection path proven" beat, not folded into M3); **overrides (M4) precede fan-out (M5)** — customization is core to the adoption story and touches far more of the system, while fan-out, though highest architectural risk, is a bounded add-on that rides on the proven substrate. Each entry maps to its VISION "workflow to ship." *Why doctypes still aren't pre-defined:* `spec` lands in M3 from its real creator/consumer; the rest accrete per driving milestone.

## 2026-06-01 — doctype map (planning aid)

- **Added [implementation/doctype-map.md](implementation/doctype-map.md)** — step 2 of the product-build loop: the eventual doctypes (commit ✅ · adr ✅ · spec M3 · prd/arch-doc unscheduled), their cross-ref edges (adr⊸supersedes⊸adr locked; commit→spec, spec→adr, prd→spec, arch-doc→adr anticipated), and the milestone expected to drive each. **Intent-level only — no fields/slots/validation**; a doctype's schema is set by the workflow that creates/reads it, per-milestone (cross-refs VISION → Document model + design/document-type-schema.md, doesn't restate). Home is `implementation/` (sequencing aid, sibling to roadmap, consulted by milestone-planning's gap-detection) — *not* `design/`, which stays schema territory. Work-units (task/increment/milestone) noted as binding anchors, not doctypes.

## 2026-06-01 — M2 planning: settled forks (second workflow + default flip)

Settling the two genuine forks M2's gap-detection surfaced ([milestone-planning-workflow](implementation/milestone-planning-workflow.md) → settle):

- **(D) The second work-workflow is `quick-fix`** — commit-only, `allows-create: []` (no ADR create-gate), fewer steps than `single-task`. Chosen over (a) a throwaway/dummy workflow (inert scope — exactly what our own scope-honesty audit flags) and (b) a spec-driven/planning workflow (genuinely useful but M3 content — needs the `spec` doctype, which would pull M3 forward and blur the M2–M3 boundary). `quick-fix` is a *real* routing distinction ("trivial fix, just commit" vs. "implement a change, maybe record a decision"), uses **only the existing `commit` doctype**, and depends on nothing from M3 — keeping M2 small and honest.
- **(D) M2 flips the cascade `default-workflow` from `single-task` to `router`** — so `jigc start "<intent>"` routes (composes the router, no mint) instead of one-call-minting `single-task`. This is the deliberate behavior change that *is* the proof of the selection path; confirmed despite changing the default front-door behavior.
- **Confirmed no engine change** (adversarial recon): the engine already parses `creates-task`, composes any workflow, builds the catalog, and renders the four-class format. M2 is **CLI + pack** work: implement the never-built Form D (`jigc start --workflow <X>`), gate minting on `def.creates_task` in `compose_in_repo` (today it always mints), and ship the `router` + `quick-fix` pack definitions + the config flip. Latent risk to retire: the `creates-task: false` compose path is currently untested.

## 2026-06-01 — M2 planning: independent review outcomes (baked into design)

The milestone-planning **review** phase (independent adversarial agent over the settled M2 design) found the plan **not sound to decompose** as scoped — the recon's "no engine change / CLI+pack only" was wrong. Three blocking gaps, resolved with the human and folded into the design docs before decompose:

- **(B1) The router could not present the catalog** — no `catalog` data-value root existed; the catalog (`engine/catalog.rs`) was wired only into orientation, never composition. *Resolution (chosen: B, a real router over a hollow reference-router):* add a **minimal engine-native `catalog` root** exposing the live **selectable work-workflows** (`creates-task: true`) + their `when` hints to composition; deterministic per cascade; engine-native machinery (enumerates pack-supplied workflows), so "engine ships empty" holds. The router never lists itself or other `creates-task: false` workflows. Baked into [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → data-value roots + Workflow selection.
- **(B2) The `creates-task: false` compose path was broken** — `compose_in_repo` always mints/provisions/binds the `commit` role, and `{{task.intent}}` needs a minted task. *Resolution:* a `creates-task: false` workflow composes with **no task context** (no mint, no working area, no commit doc, no role binding) and **may not reference `task.*`** (a `workflow-refs` conformance error). The intent is threaded forward only by an **agent-substitution marker** in the emitted re-run (`jigc start --workflow <chosen> "<intent>"`) — *no* intent data-value feed needed (cleaner than the reviewer's fallback). Baked into [write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md) → Task origination.
- **(B3) "Recommended default" had no knob** now that `default-workflow` names the router. *Resolution:* **drop it for M2** — two `when`-hinted options need no recommendation engine; a `recommended-workflow` knob is generality for a single use. Marked post-M2 in [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md).
- **(advisory) `quick-fix` must be non-inert** — a decompose constraint: its e2e must assert it composes to *materially different* output than `single-task` (no ADR affordance, no supersedes line) with a non-overlapping `when`; minimal real diffs = `allows-create: []` + shorter step list + an `implement` variant without the `create-adr` line. Plus folded into decompose: `--workflow <unknown>` rejection, `--workflow`⊥`--task` clap wiring, orientation footer copy post-flip, backward-compat test updates for the default flip.

Roadmap M2 entry corrected: "no engine change" → minimal `catalog` root + the `creates-task: false` compose contract.

## 2026-06-01 — orchestration notes: each build-loop phase is its own agent

- **Pinned the phase→agent mapping** for the build loops with minimal "Orchestration" notes in [increment-workflow.md](implementation/increment-workflow.md) (new section) and [dev-workflow.md](implementation/dev-workflow.md) / [milestone-completion-workflow.md](implementation/milestone-completion-workflow.md) (→ Why this shape): Plan = one planning agent per increment; Execute = one agent **per task** (the whole dev-workflow → one commit, serial over the shared tree); Validate = one independent read-only agent; Fix = one agent **per blocking finding**; milestone audit = two parallel agents, fix = one agent per finding. *Why:* the docs described the logical loop (written for by-hand execution) but never the agent decomposition, so orchestrating M2 via the Workflow tool had to interpret it — and a first run mis-decomposed (Plan folded into Execute, one executor for a whole increment). *Next:* once the M2 run validates the shape, promote these loops into reusable workflow **definitions + agent roles** rather than interpreted docs.

## 2026-06-01 — M2 Increment 1 planning: decomposition

Cut [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → M2 Increment 1 ("`catalog` root + `creates-task: false` compose contract — the engine spine") into four ordered, single-concern tasks, each with an observable red step. Risk-first order: the resolver contract first (`task=None`), then the new `catalog` root, then its line emission, then the CLI no-task compose path that depends on all three. *Why this cut:* the smallest set that lands the engine spine before the front door (Increment 2) depends on it — T1–T3 are pure-engine resolver/emit work provable in unit tests, T4 is the CLI gate + the latent-bug-retirement test the review named, kept last because it consumes all three.

- **T1 (engine):** `ComposeContext.task` → `Option<TaskRoot>`. *Done:* a `data_value` unit test asserts resolving `task.intent` (and any `task.*`) against `task = None` returns a blocking `Finding` coded `workflow-refs.task-ref-in-no-task-workflow`, while `task = Some(...)` behaves as before; gate green.
- **T2 (engine):** add the `catalog` data-value root. *Done:* `data_value` unit tests show bare `catalog` (no hops / fragment / `@`) resolves to `Resolution::Catalog` carrying the fed `{id, when}` list, and `@catalog` / `catalog.x` / `catalog#y` are each a blocking conformance `Finding`; gate green.
- **T3 (engine compose):** emit a lone `{{catalog}}` placeholder. *Done:* a `compose` unit test over `emit_step_body` with a body interpolating `{{catalog}}` and a ctx carrying two entries asserts both option lines appear as `- <id> — <when>`; an empty catalog emits empty text; gate green. (Line-shape pinned below.)
- **T4 (cli):** gate `compose_in_repo` on `def.creates_task` + prove the no-task path on a fixture. *Done:* a `start` unit test composes a fixture `creates-task: false` workflow (body interpolates `{{catalog}}`) against a test `PackSource` and asserts (a) **no** `.jigc/tasks/<id>/` dir is created and (b) the output lists the selectable workflows; the existing `start_compose` integration tests still pass; gate green.

- *Elaboration pin (T2):* the engine-native `catalog` root resolves to a new `Resolution::Catalog { entries: Vec<CatalogEntry> }`, fed via a new `ComposeContext.catalog` field (reusing `engine::result::CatalogEntry`); `catalog` is the only valid form — any hop / `#fragment` / `@`-marker on it is a blocking `workflow-refs.*` conformance finding (it is a collection leaf, not a navigable root).
- *Elaboration pin (T3):* a lone `{{catalog}}` line emits one `- <id> — <when>` line per entry (em-dash `—` separator, matching orientation's catalog rendering), entries in fed order, joined by newlines; an empty catalog emits empty text (no finding) — the same empty-not-error stance as an unbound role.
- *Elaboration pin (T4):* `compose_in_repo` takes a `&dyn PackSource` (or extracts a testable core) so the `creates-task: false` arm is reachable in a unit test; when `def.creates_task` is false it does **not** mint / provision the commit doc / bind the commit role, builds a `ComposeContext` with `task = None` + the cascade catalog filtered to `creates-task: true`; the `true` arm keeps the existing path but also feeds the filtered catalog.

## 2026-06-01 — M2 Increment 1 T2: catalog-root finding codes

Landed T2. Two new blocking `workflow-refs.*` codes name the catalog misuses the pin left as `workflow-refs.*`: `catalog-not-navigable` (a hop / `#fragment` / `@`-marker on the `catalog` collection leaf, in `Path::resolve`) and `catalog-not-lone` (a `Resolution::Catalog` reaching a non-lone emitter — command arg / `> ` content / `<<author>>` / inline). *Why two:* they fail at different surfaces (resolver vs the lone-placeholder emitters T3 routes around), so a reader sees which misuse fired.

## 2026-06-01 — M2 Increment 1 T3: catalog option-line emission

Landed T3. `emit_line` resolves a lone `{{catalog}}` to `Resolution::Catalog` *before* the bare-data-value dispatch and renders it via a new `render_catalog_options` helper — one `- <id> — <when>` line per entry, fed order, joined by newlines; an empty catalog → empty string (empty-not-finding). *Why this placement:* the catalog is the only lone placeholder that resolves to a collection, so it shares the bare-placeholder line-shape but needs its own emitter — short-circuiting in `emit_line` keeps the other three emitters (`emit_bare_data_value`/`emit_content`/author) returning the `catalog-not-lone` block for every non-lone position.

## 2026-06-01 — M2 Increment 1 T4: gate `compose_in_repo` on `creates-task`

Landed T4. Extracted `compose_core(repo_root, intent, &dyn PackSource)` out of `compose_in_repo` (which now only does repo-root discovery + the config-layer check + `EmbeddedPack::new()`), so the no-task arm is reachable in a unit test over a fixture pack — the latent-bug retirement the review named (the `creates-task: false` compose path M1 never exercised). The core branches on `def.creates_task`: **true** mints / provisions the commit doc / binds the `commit` role as before; **false** builds `ComposeContext { task: None, catalog }` — no mint, no working area, no commit doc, no role bind. Both arms feed a new `selectable_workflows(&dyn PackSource)` helper (every pack workflow parsed via `load_workflow_def`, **filtered to `creates-task: true`**, paired with its `when`, in `list` order) into the context, so `{{catalog}}` resolves the selectable list and the router never lists itself. *Why the filter lives in the CLI, not engine `catalog.rs`:* `build_catalog` reads only `when` and is orientation-only; the compose-time selectable list needs `creates-task` too, and reusing the existing `load_workflow_def` parser here is smaller than threading a second read through the engine. The helper signatures (`read_pack`/`provision_commit_doc`/`all_schemas`/`PackStepSource`/`default_workflow_id`/`load_catalog`) moved from `&EmbeddedPack` to `&dyn PackSource` for the same testability.

## 2026-06-01 — M2 Increment 2 planning: decomposition

Cut [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → M2 Increment 2 ("Form D front door — `jigc start --workflow <X>`") into three ordered, single-concern tasks, each with an observable red step. Order mirrors the grouped scope (clap → compose path → dispatch), built on Increment 1's `compose_core` branch-on-`creates_task`: T1 is pure parse, T2 parameterizes the named workflow + rejects an unknown X **before** minting, T3 wires the dispatch arm that drives T2 through the public CLI surface ([write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md) → Task origination, Form D). *Why this cut:* the smallest set landing the explicit-selection front door (the target the router output names) by reusing the Increment-1 spine — Increment 1 already gates minting on `creates-task`, so Form D is "compose a named X instead of the cascade default" + the unknown-X guard, nothing more.

- **T1 (cli/clap):** add `--workflow <X>` to `Command::Start`, conflicting with `--task`, combining with the `<intent>` positional. *Done:* `cli_parse` unit tests assert `jigc start --workflow single-task "an intent"` parses to `Start { intent: Some, workflow: Some, task: None }`, and `--workflow X --task id` is rejected with `ErrorKind::ArgumentConflict`; the existing `start` parse tests still pass; gate green.
- **T2 (cli/compose):** compose an explicitly-named X, minting iff `X.creates_task`, rejecting an unknown X before any mint. *Done:* a `start` unit test over a `FixturePack` asserts (a) Form-D over a `creates-task: true` X opens `.jigc/tasks/<slug>/` and composes its body, and (b) an X **not** in the pack's workflow list rejects with a routed finding **and no** `.jigc/tasks/` dir is created (rejection precedes minting); gate green.
- **T3 (cli/dispatch):** wire the Form-D dispatch arm. *Done:* the `Command::Start { intent: Some, workflow: Some, task: None }` arm calls the T2 path and renders through `--format`; the previously-`unreachable!` intent+task arm stays unreachable; `cargo build` + `cargo test` exit clean with Form D reaching compose end-to-end through `dispatch`; gate green.

- *Elaboration pin (T2):* the named-workflow path reuses the existing `creates_task` branch — it composes `X` instead of `default_workflow_id(pack)`; an unknown X surfaces as `pack.read(Workflows, X)` → `PackError::NotFound`, mapped to a **routed** `Finding` (the same `finding_to_err` envelope minting collisions use), so rejection lands before `mint_in_repo` is ever called. No new finding-emitting machinery; membership = the pack-read miss.

## 2026-06-01 — M2 Increment 2 T2: parameterize the named workflow + unknown-X guard

Landed T2. `compose_core` gained an explicit `workflow_id: &str` parameter (the cascade-default `compose_in_repo` passes `default_workflow_id`; Form D will pass `X`), and a new `read_workflow` helper maps a missing workflow's `PackError::NotFound` to a routed block — code `workflow-refs.unknown-workflow` — fired before `mint_in_repo`, so a typo'd id strands no task dir. *Why the code lives in the `workflow-refs.*` family:* it is a compose-time reference miss (X is not a resolvable workflow ref), the same family as the catalog-misuse codes, and reusing `Finding::block` keeps "no new finding machinery." The public Form-D entry (`compose_named_in_repo`) is **deferred to T3**, where the dispatch arm consumes it — adding it in T2 would land a dead `pub fn` that fails the `clippy -D warnings` gate; the FixturePack tests drive the explicit-id behavior through `compose_core` directly.

## 2026-06-01 — M2 Increment 3 planning: decomposition

Cut [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → M2 Increment 3 ("router + quick-fix + the flip — the acceptance path") into five ordered, single-concern tasks, each with an observable red step. The engine spine (the `catalog` root, the no-task compose contract) + Form D landed in Increments 1–2, so this increment is **pure pack + config + orientation copy + e2e** — no engine/CLI machinery left to build. Order: the second selectable target first (so the router has two options to list), the router second (it lists the now-two work-workflows), the flip third (makes the router the default + fixes the M1 compose tests the flip breaks), the orientation copy + catalog filter fourth (post-flip correctness of the read-only front door), the end-to-end acceptance last (it drives all four). *Why this cut:* the smallest set landing the routing loop without engine change — each task is a pack/config/copy diff with a compose or CLI assertion, built serially so the router can list quick-fix and the flip can break-then-fix the M1 tests.

- **T1 (pack):** the `quick-fix` work-workflow — `creates-task: true`, `allows-create: []`, a shorter step list, an `implement` variant **without** the `create-adr` line. *Done:* a `start_compose` integration test runs `jigc start --workflow quick-fix "<intent>"` (Form D) and asserts it mints + composes, and that the composed text carries **no** ADR/create affordance (no `cli.create-adr`) and **no** `supersedes`/superseded-context line — materially different from `single-task`; gate green.
- **T2 (pack):** the `router` workflow — `creates-task: false`, a `when` hint + its `present-catalog` / `route-to-workflow` steps over `{{catalog}}` with the literal agent-substitution re-run `jigc start --workflow <chosen> "<intent>"`. *Done:* a `start_compose` integration test runs `jigc start --workflow router "<intent>"` and asserts the output lists both `single-task` and `quick-fix` as `- <id> — <when>` option lines and carries the literal `jigc start --workflow <chosen>` re-run prose, **without** minting (no `.jigc/tasks/` dir); gate green.
- **T3 (config + tests):** flip `pack/config/defaults.yaml` → `default-workflow: router`, and update the M1 compose tests the flip breaks. *Done:* bare `jigc start "<intent>"` now composes the **router** (no `.jigc/tasks/` dir created); the M1 `start_compose` tests that asserted bare-intent **mints** + resolves `{{task.intent}}` (`start_with_intent_mints_and_composes_the_resolved_view`, `start_with_intent_json_format_carries_no_footer`) are moved to Form D (`--workflow single-task`); gate green.
- **T4 (cli orientation):** post-flip orientation copy + catalog filter. *Done:* the clean-orientation next-step line no longer claims "the default workflow (single-task)" but routes via the router, and orientation's `build_catalog` is filtered to the **selectable** (`creates-task: true`) workflows so the router never appears in the "Available workflows" menu (the locked "router never lists itself" rule, applied to the read-only front door); a `start_orientation`/`orient` test asserts the router id is **absent** from the listed workflows while `single-task` + `quick-fix` are present; gate green.
- **T5 (e2e):** end-to-end routing acceptance in a throwaway repo. *Done:* a new integration test drives the full loop — bare `jigc start "<intent>"` composes the router (no mint) listing both work-workflows → `jigc start --workflow <chosen> "<intent>"` mints + composes — and asserts `quick-fix`'s composed output differs **materially** from `single-task`'s (no ADR/create affordance, no supersedes line) with **non-overlapping** `when` hints; gate green.

- *Elaboration pin (T2):* the router's `route-to-workflow` step body expresses the re-run as **literal prose** — `jigc start --workflow <chosen> "<intent>"` — where `<chosen>` and `<intent>` are agent-filled angle-bracket markers, **not** placeholders/slots (a `creates-task: false` workflow may not reference `task.intent` — `workflow-refs.task-ref-in-no-task-workflow`; the agent re-supplies its own intent). The markers are inert text in the step's markdown body, distinct from `<<author>>` slots and `{{…}}` placeholders by being neither delimiter.

## 2026-06-01 — M2 Increment 3 T1: the `quick-fix` work-workflow

Landed T1. Shipped `workflows/quick-fix.yaml` (`creates-task: true`, `allows-create: []`, `when: apply a small commit-only fix with no decision to record`) over a new `steps/implement-quick.yaml` — the `implement` step **without** the `cli.create-adr` line — and a body of `locate` → `implement-quick` → `finalize` (no `superseded-context` include). *Why a new step file rather than overriding `implement`:* the create-adr lines are intrinsic to `single-task`'s step, and the cascade has no "remove these lines" delta at sub-step granularity; the smallest commit-only variant is a sibling step the new workflow includes, and `include_dir` auto-embeds it with zero CLI change. Two M1 tests went stale as a direct consequence and were retargeted (not new behavior): `pack`'s step-list assertion now expects five steps incl. `implement-quick`, and `start_orientation`'s clean-JSON test now finds `single-task` by id instead of asserting it at index 0 (the now-two selectable workflows sort `quick-fix` first; the T4 orientation filter is separate).

## 2026-06-01 — M2 Increment 3 T2: the `router` workflow + selection steps

Landed T2.
