Shipped `workflows/router.yaml` (`creates-task: false`, `when: pick a work-workflow for an intent`) with a body of `present-catalog` → `route-to-workflow`: `steps/present-catalog.yaml` interpolates the lone `{{ catalog }}` (the engine renders the selectable work-workflows as `- <id> — <when>` lines), and `steps/route-to-workflow.yaml` carries the literal agent-substitution re-run `jigc start --workflow <chosen> "<intent>"` — inert markdown markers, neither `{{…}}` nor `<<…>>`, per the T2 elaboration pin (a `creates-task: false` workflow may not reference `task.*`, so the agent re-supplies its own intent). Composing it via Form D (`--workflow router`) drives the no-task path (no mint) ahead of the T3 default flip. The `pack` step-list assertion gained the two new sorted step ids (`present-catalog`, `route-to-workflow`) — the only M1-test churn, a direct consequence of `include_dir` auto-embedding the new files.

## 2026-06-01 — M2 Increment 3 re-cut: persist the resume workflow before the flip

The execute phase surfaced a genuine fork the Increment-3 plan missed, and halted (correctly). **Flipping `default-workflow` to `router` breaks `jigc start --task <id>` resume:** `cli::start::resume_in_repo` re-composes the *cascade default* (`default_workflow_id(&pack)`), not the task's **minting** workflow, and task state never persisted that id (`state::mint_task` writes only `base.json` + intent). Pre-flip this was masked (`default-workflow == single-task` was also the sole minting workflow); post-flip a resumed `single-task` task composes the no-task `router` and loses its superseded-context slice (caught by `superseding_decision` + `e2e_audit::scenario_4`). *Resolution (human-gated triage of the halt):* the direction is **design-mandated** — [write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md) → Task origination (`--task` "resumes an existing task … where it is in *its* workflow") and [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) (resume is `jigc workflow <x> --task <id>`, `<x>` = the task's own workflow) — so **persist the minting workflow id in task state at mint, and have `resume_in_repo` compose that id, not the cascade default; a missing id is a clear error** (no migration case in the MVP — tasks are ephemeral, removed at `finalize`). Inserted as a new task **before** the flip. Increment-3 cut is now: T1 `quick-fix` ✅ · T2 `router` ✅ · **T3a persist-resume-workflow (new)** · T3b flip + breaking-test updates · T3c post-flip orientation · T3d e2e acceptance.

## 2026-06-01 — M2 Increment 3 T3a: persist + resume the task's own minting workflow

Landed T3a. `state::mint_task` gained a `workflow_id: &str` parameter and persists it as a plain `<task_dir>/workflow` file (read back verbatim via the new `state::read_workflow_id`), and `cli::start::resume_in_repo` now reads that id and composes **that** workflow — replacing the `default_workflow_id(&pack)` read — erroring clearly when the file is absent. Every mint path threads the id it composes (`mint_in_repo` gained the param; `compose_core` passes its in-scope `workflow_id`, so both the bare `single-task` mint and the Form-D `--workflow X` mint record their own workflow). Lands **before** the flip so the flip stays safe. *Why this is right pre-flip:* with `default-workflow` still `single-task`, resume composes the persisted id identically to the old default read — observably unchanged for existing tasks — but a Form-D `--workflow quick-fix` task now resumes `quick-fix`, not the default, which the new `start_resume` test proves.

- *Elaboration pin (T3a):* the minting workflow id persists as **`.jigc/tasks/<id>/workflow`** — a plain UTF-8 file holding the bare id (e.g. `quick-fix`), the same diff-friendly style as the sibling `intent` file, **not** folded into `base.json`. *Why a sibling file, not a `base.json` field:* `base.json` is the base-*pin* (the commit the task started against, its own golden-locked shape); the workflow id is orthogonal task-origin state, and a separate plain file keeps each file one purpose + golden-stable on its own. `read_workflow_id` returns `Option<String>` (`None` = file absent) so the engine stays policy-free; the CLI resume site is the one that maps `None` to the routed "task `<id>` has no recorded workflow — discard and re-start" error (a missing id is a clear fault, never a silent fall-through to the cascade default).

## 2026-06-01 — M2 Increment 3 T3b: flip `default-workflow` → `router`

Landed T3b — the M2 headline flip. `pack/config/defaults.yaml` now ships `default-workflow: router`, so a bare `jigc start "<intent>"` composes the `creates-task: false` router (lists the selectable work-workflows, mints nothing); minting happens only via Form D (`--workflow <X>`). The breaking-test churn is purely the front-door swap, no behavior loss: every integration mint that bare-minted `single-task` now mints via `--workflow single-task` (the `started_repo` helpers in `task_lifecycle`/`doc_write`/`finalize_to_git`, the `superseding_decision` + `e2e_audit::scenario_4` ADR flows, `start_resume`, and the scenario-5/6 commit-only flows), and `start_compose`'s bare-intent test was retargeted to assert the router contract (no `.jigc/tasks/` dir, the catalog + agent-substitution re-run). The one golden update is the `embedded_pack_config_carries_defaults_and_commands` snapshot (`single-task` → `router`). Resume of those `single-task` tasks composes the persisted `single-task` (T3a), not the new default — so the flip is loss-free.

## 2026-06-01 — M2 Increment 3 T3c: post-flip orientation copy + selectable catalog

Landed T3c. Bare-`start` orientation now (1) filters its catalog to the **selectable** (`creates-task: true`) work-workflows by reusing the increment-1 `selectable_workflows` filter (made `pub(crate)`, called from `orient_with` in place of `engine::catalog::build_catalog`), so orientation lists `single-task` + `quick-fix` and never the `creates-task: false` router; and (2) leads its next-step copy to the **routing** flow — `Run: \`jigc start "<intent>"\`   — routes among the workflows above; pick one, then re-run with \`--workflow <chosen>\`` — replacing the pre-flip "composes the default workflow (single-task)" line, since bare `start` now composes the router rather than minting directly. *Why reuse rather than re-derive:* the front door already feeds the router's `{{catalog}}` through `selectable_workflows`, and orientation must show the same set the router selects among — one filter, one source of truth. `engine::catalog::build_catalog` is left in place (a pub engine API with its own tests; pre-existing, out of scope to remove).

## 2026-06-01 — M2 complete: milestone-completion audit + triage

M2 (router + second work-workflow) shipped — all three increments built via the [increment workflow](implementation/increment-workflow.md), then audited and remediated via the [milestone-completion workflow](implementation/milestone-completion-workflow.md) (independent code-review + e2e over the whole `e7e100e..HEAD` diff, run as two parallel agents). **Audit verdict:** deliverable holds; all six e2e routing scenarios pass (incl. resume honoring each task's own minting workflow across the default flip — the re-cut fork); no blocking findings. **Triage (human gate) → one fix:** `engine::catalog::build_catalog` was left orphaned by T3c (orientation switched to `cli::start::selectable_workflows`), so its only remaining callers were its own `#[cfg(test)]` tests — the exact "pub-but-test-only" scope defect the [increment workflow](implementation/increment-workflow.md) → Validation hardening names. *Resolution (chosen: delete the orphan, over consolidate-in-engine / keep-as-documented):* removed `build_catalog` + its private helpers (`parse_when`, `WorkflowFrontMatter`, `CatalogError`) + its 5 tests; `catalog.rs` retains only the shared `front_matter` fence recognizer (which `compose` still uses). The catalog *resolution* stays engine-native; the CLI legitimately assembles the list from the pack ("CLI locates/assembles, engine resolves"). Plus a 1-line doc alignment ([bootstrap.md](design/bootstrap.md) state-2 example → post-flip routing copy). Re-verified: full gate green (engine lib 182 tests, all CLI binaries pass), affected orientation/catalog slice clean. *Process note:* the whole milestone was run as a Workflow-tool orchestration — Plan/Execute(per-task)/Validate/Fix as discrete agents, per the [orchestration mapping](implementation/increment-workflow.md) pinned this session.

## 2026-06-01 — build harness promoted to reusable assets

Completes the "*Next*" the orchestration-notes entry named: the by-hand build loops are now **fixed orchestration**, not interpreted-per-run. Added `.claude/workflows/milestone-build.js` (drives a whole milestone: read its roadmap decomposition → per increment plan/execute(one agent per task)/validate/fix bounded to 3 rounds → the milestone-completion audit; halts surface structured) + seven agent role definitions under `.claude/agents/` (`milestone-reader`, `build-planner`, `build-executor`, `increment-validator`, `build-fixer`, `milestone-code-reviewer`, `milestone-e2e-tester`). The agent defs carry the durable role + project knowledge and **point at the canonical docs** (the workflow docs, `CLAUDE.md`, the milestone's `DECISIONS.md`/roadmap entries) for specifics — only the orchestration is frozen. Invoke: `Workflow({name: "milestone-build", args: {milestone, base}})`. Read-only roles (`milestone-reader`/`increment-validator`/`milestone-code-reviewer`) are tool-restricted (no `Edit`/`Write`) so independence is structural. Referenced from [increment-workflow.md](implementation/increment-workflow.md) → Orchestration. *Validated by:* the M2 run that exercised this exact shape by hand; the harness is the extraction of that proven structure (not yet re-run end-to-end — its first live use is M3).

## 2026-06-01 — milestone-planning runnable as a command, not a workflow

Promoted the planning loop to a runnable overlay too — but as a **`/milestone-plan` slash command**, not a Workflow-tool harness, after weighing both. *Why a command, not a workflow:* planning is **human-led** — its center of gravity is *Settle* (resolving genuine forks), which is irreducibly the human's (`CLAUDE.md` → design decisions are surfaced, never silently picked). A background workflow returns at the end and can't converse, so it would halt at *every* fork — the halt path as the main path, i.e. the wrong abstraction (plus false rigor over the least-deterministic activity in the process). A **command runs in the main session**, where the human conversation already lives: the autonomous bursts delegate to subagents (`gap-detector` ×4 parallel for *Detect gaps*; `design-reviewer` for the pre-decompose *Review*), while *Settle* + *Decompose* stay inline. *Bankable rule this clarifies:* **Workflow tool = autonomous loops, human-as-exception (build + the audit burst); slash command + agents = human-led, conversational (planning).** Added `.claude/commands/milestone-plan.md` + the `gap-detector` / `design-reviewer` roles + an Orchestration note in [milestone-planning-workflow.md](implementation/milestone-planning-workflow.md). Cross-refs the doc, doesn't restate it. First live use is M3 planning.

## 2026-06-01 — M3 planning: scope + gaps settled (spec-driven planning)

Opened M3 via the [`/milestone-plan`](.claude/commands/milestone-plan.md) command (its first live use). **Done-picture (Option A, chosen with the human over a create-gate-in-single-task / spec-supplied-externally pair):** the two-task **intent → spec → implementation** arc — a new `plan` work-workflow authors+commits a `spec`; a later `implement-from-spec` task binds that committed spec and implements it, reading `{{@task.spec#criteria}}` over the persisted file and recording `commit —implements→ spec` at finalize. Four `gap-detector` agents (decisions/docs/doctypes/capabilities) converged on one spine: **M3's only genuinely new surface is binding an existing committed doc to a role on a later task** — the read path, persisted-slice read, edge-walk, router N-enumeration, and four-class emit are all already built. Settled forks:

- **(D) Binding mechanism → a `jigc task bind <role> <addr>` verb, *not* a `start` flag** (chosen over `start --spec <id>` / generic `--bind role=id`). Keeps origination the single simple sentence the agent learns; the composed `locate` step routes the agent to `bind` via a `<NAME>` agent-substitution `Run:` line, like every other structural op. Binding happens post-compose, so the spec slice is empty on first compose and resolves on the **resume re-compose** — the exact deferred-bind-then-resume idiom flow 5 (`supersedes`) already uses. Spec'd in [write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md) → Binding a context role.
- **(D) Spec-read workflow → a separate `implement-from-spec` workflow**, leaving the spec-less `single-task` untouched (chosen over mutating single-task's `locate`, which would force optional-read-role emit semantics). Avoids touching the proven M1 workflow; cost is a fourth router entry (the `when` hints must stay non-overlapping — an e2e assertion, like M2).
- **(D) Read-role declaration → a new `reads: [{role, type, card}]` workflow front-matter key**, the dual of `allows-create`'s `as:` form (created-doc role) for a bound-from-committed-doc role. Makes `task.<role>` a declared `workflow-refs` root; declared-but-unbound resolves to empty, undeclared is a conformance error. Spec'd in [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → On-disk definition format.
- **`spec` schema (earned from its M3 creator+consumer):** sections `goal` (slot), `context` (slot), `criteria` (repeatable; `locate` reads `{{@task.spec#criteria}}`); `location: specs/`, `id-from: title`. **Cut** `status`/`date` (no M3 path reads them) and the `decided-by → adr` relation (nothing in the arc traverses it) as single-use generality — both one-line additions when a flow earns them.
- **`commit —implements→ spec` edge:** add `{id: implements, type: ref, to: spec, card: "0..1", inverse: implemented-by}` to the `commit` schema. **`0..1`, not the doctype-map's intent `1`** — spec-less tasks (`single-task`, `quick-fix`) must still produce a valid `commit`. The edge originates on the *transient* commit doc (first transient-source edge); the working-overlay already emits it and `ref-resolves` checks the target in the committed store — verified in code, build owns a golden test. doctype-map updated (location pinned, edge cardinalities/scheduling corrected).
- **`plan` finalize is code-less:** `plan` provisions a `commit` like any work-workflow (commit `type: docs`); the promoted spec is a non-empty diff, satisfying finalize's empty-commit guard. Spec born **only** in `plan` for M3 (not added to single-task's `allows-create`).

Docs elaborated this phase: [write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md) (the `bind` verb + reconciled the line-74 over-claim that binding happens "at `start`"), [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) (`reads:`), [doctype-map.md](implementation/doctype-map.md) (spec location + edges), [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) (new flow 6, the two-task arc). Next: independent design-review of these bakes, then decompose into increments.

**Independent review (design-reviewer) → bakes folded in before decompose.** The review confirmed the core sound on every load-bearing axis (determinism boundary on `bind`, transient-source `implements` edge, committed-only bind vs. the deadlock rule, the fourth workflow, the schema cuts) and surfaced seven findings; the human accepted these dispositions:

- **(#3, blocking) `step:locate` id collision** — `implement-from-spec` gets its *own* `step:locate-from-spec` (binds + reads the spec); it must **not** reuse the spec-less `step:locate` that `single-task`/`quick-fix` share (a step id resolves to one file pack-wide). Body = `[locate-from-spec, implement, finalize]`. Baked into worked-example flow 6.
- **(#1/#2) cut `card` from `reads`** — nothing consumed it and M3 ships no *required* read-role; `reads: [{role, type}]` only. The "required read-role blocks finalize when unbound" rule is deferred until one is earned (keeps the bootstrap-deadlock rule clean). Baked into workflow-dialect.md.
- **(#6) emit the re-compose** — `locate-from-spec` *emits* `Run: jigc start --task <id>` after the bind (id is `{{task.id}}`, not an agent-fill), so an agent following only the machine markers can't bind-then-forget-to-reread. Baked into flow 6.
- **(#4 → option b) spec discovery via `{{store.specs}}`** — `locate-from-spec` interpolates the `store` root enumerating committed `spec` instances as a Content menu the agent picks from before binding (chosen over (a) plan-echoes-address / (c) a `jigc doc list` verb — both deferred until needed). Lands a small capability: `store.<doctype-id>` enumerates a doctype's committed instances as a collection, and a collection in a plain step emits as a readable Content list. Baked into workflow-dialect.md (data-value roots) + flow 6.
- **(#5, advisory) unbound spec role degrades to spec-less silently** — intended for M3, no gate; the "did you mean single-task?" advisory deferred. Noted in flow 6.
- **(#7, decompose note) `implements` is the first ref on `commit`** — the build pins it as a `ref`-typed field in a section (like `adr`'s `supersedes`) and resolves the address fragment against the canonical grammar; flagged a pre-existing addressing inconsistency to settle there (flow 1 addresses commit fields flat `#type`; flow 5 addresses adr's ref section-qualified `#status/supersedes`). Carried into the decomposition, not a planning blocker.

**Decompose → four risk-first increments** (in [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → Milestone 3 decomposition): (1) `spec` schema + `commit.implements` edge on fixtures; (2) the `jigc task bind` verb + `reads:` declaration — the cross-task binding spine, M3's #1 new risk, in isolation; (3) the `plan` workflow (spec authoring via create-gate + code-less finalize); (4) `implement-from-spec` + `{{store.specs}}` discovery + the router's fourth entry + the two-task acceptance e2e. Linear, each builds on the last; the headline arc lands whole in inc 4. M3 is **planned**, ready for the [increment workflow](implementation/increment-workflow.md) / `milestone-build` harness.

## 2026-06-01 — M3 Increment 1 planning: decomposition

Cut [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → M3 Increment 1 ("`spec` doctype + `commit.implements` edge — schema foundation") into four ordered, single-concern tasks, each with an observable red step. *Grounding:* the engine schema model is already fully data-driven — repeatable sections, slots, and `ref` fields (`to`/`card`/`inverse`) all load + parse + render + edge-walk via in-test `spec`-shaped fixtures (`schema.rs`, `parse.rs`, `write.rs`, `index.rs`), and `doc_edges`/`ref_resolves` emit + walk any `Simple`-section `ref` field over the committed ∪ working overlay. So this increment is **pack bytes + tests, no engine source change** for the schema; the `implements` edge is a one-field `commit.yaml` delta. Risk-first order: ship+pin the new `spec` schema, then the round-trip (the highest-risk part — the repeatable `criteria` items), then the `commit.implements` field + canonical-address pin, then the transient-source edge walk that depends on both schemas. *Why this cut:* the smallest set that lands the schema foundation before the binding spine (Increment 2) depends on it; T1–T2 prove the new doctype on the byte-stable substrate, T3–T4 prove the first transient-source edge end-to-end at validate.

- **T1 (pack + engine test):** ship `crates/cli/pack/schemas/spec.yaml` and pin its parsed projection. *Done:* a new `schema_spec_golden` (sibling to `schema_adr_golden` in `engine/src/schema.rs`, `include_bytes!`-ing the shipped `spec.yaml`) snapshots the loaded `Schema` — `location: specs/`, `id-from: title`, the `goal`/`context` body slots, and the repeatable `criteria` section (`id-from: title`, block = `title` field + `statement` slot); a reachability assert confirms the `criteria` `Repeatable` block carries `statement` as a `Leaf::Slot`; **no** `status`/`date`/`decided-by` present; gate green.
- **T2 (engine test):** the shipped `spec` round-trips byte-stably. *Done:* a round-trip test over the shipped `spec.yaml` parses a canonical `spec` instance fixture (header `title`, `goal`+`context` slot prose, ≥2 `criteria` items each with a frozen `{#id}` + `statement` slot) and asserts (a) `render(parse(src)) == src` golden bytes and (b) `write → parse → write` is byte-identical (idempotent on canonical content), reusing the `engine/src/write.rs` canonical-writer + `engine/src/parse.rs` repeatable-item harness; gate green.
- **T3 (pack + engine test):** add the `implements` ref to `commit.yaml` + pin its canonical address. *Done:* `commit.yaml`'s `header` section gains `{ id: implements, type: ref, to: spec, card: "0..1", inverse: implemented-by }`; the `schema_commit_golden` snapshot is updated to include it; a `field_target` (cli `doc.rs`) test asserts the canonical section-qualified fragment `commit:<slug>#header/implements` resolves to `(header, implements)` and the flat alias `#implements` resolves identically; gate green. (Review #7 resolved below.)
- **T4 (engine test):** the transient-source `commit.implements` edge emits + `ref-resolves`. *Done:* an `index.rs` overlay test stages a working-area `commit` instance whose `implements` points at `spec:<slug>`, builds the working overlay, and asserts (a) `ref_resolves` **passes** when a committed `specs/<slug>.md` exists in the rebuilt committed index, and (b) **blocks** with a `schema-conformance.ref-resolves` finding when the target dangles — mirroring the existing `supersedes` overlay tests with a transient (`commit`) source; gate green.

- *Elaboration pin (T1):* `spec.yaml` ships exactly — `type: spec`, `location: specs/`, `id-from: title`; a `header: true` section carrying the `title` string field (the id-source, mirroring how `adr`'s title is its frozen-id source); `goal` and `context` as simple body slot sections (in that order); a repeatable `criteria` section (`id-from: title`, block = `{ id: title, type: string }` then `{ id: statement, slot: {hint: …} }`). No `maps-to-test` code-anchor (the in-test fixtures' third leaf is illustrative only — the doc↔code probe is post-M3 per doctype-map), no `status`/`date`, no `decided-by` ref.
- *Elaboration pin (T3 / review #7 — flow-1-flat vs flow-5-section-qualified):* the canonical grammar ([structural-grammar.md](design/structural-grammar.md) → Addressing) addresses a field as `type:slug#unit/leaf`, so a `commit` field's canonical address is **section-qualified** (`commit:<slug>#header/implements`, `commit:<slug>#header/type`), exactly as `adr`'s ref reads `adr:<slug>#status/supersedes`. The flat single-hop `#<field>` form stays an accepted MVP convenience — `field_target` already resolves it by searching every section — but is documented as an alias, not the canonical form. `implements` lives in the `header` section (the only field-bearing section, like `adr`'s `supersedes` in its `status` header), so `doc_edges` emits its edge from a `Simple` section unchanged. The build reconciles [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow 6's `commit:…#implements` / flow 1's `commit:…#type` to the canonical section-qualified form. This is a doc/test pin within the locked grammar, not a new fork.
- *Elaboration pin (T4):* the edge's *source* is the transient `commit` doc in the task working area (first transient-source edge); `ref_resolves` walks the committed ∪ working overlay exactly as the `supersedes` overlay tests do, so the only new surface is a `commit`-typed `from` — no engine logic change, a new test fixture over the existing `overlay_working` / `rebuild_committed` / `ref_resolves` path.

## 2026-06-01 — M3 Increment 1 T3: `commit.implements` ref + the fillable-form optional-ref omission

Landed T3. `commit.yaml`'s `header` section gained `{ id: implements, type: ref, to: spec, card: "0..1", inverse: implemented-by }` (mirroring `adr`'s `supersedes`); the `schema_commit` golden + a `doc.rs` `field_target` test (canonical `#header/implements` and flat alias `#implements` both resolve to `(header, implements)`) pin it; worked-examples flows 1/6 reconciled to the section-qualified address. **The one fork the planner's "purely additive" T3 pin missed:** `cli::start::fillable_form` pre-stamps *every* declared header field as an empty `key:` line (the surface `set-field`'s surgical splice needs present), so the new **optional** `implements` survived provisioning as an empty line that blocked a spec-less `finalize` twice — `field-value-conformant` (empty ref rejected) + `ref-resolves` (empty target dangles) — caught by `finalize_to_git`/`e2e_audit`/`superseding_decision`. *Resolution (design-mandated, not a new fork):* the fillable form now **omits an optional `ref`** (forward `card:` minimum 0), via a new `is_optional_ref` predicate mirroring `engine::validate`'s `required-field-present` optional-ref exemption — provisioning and validation agree on the same cardinality predicate. This is the locked intent (`doctype-map.md`: a spec-less `commit` finalizes cleanly without `implements`, the edge set only by the spec-driven workflow); the spec-driven workflow that *sets* `implements` materializes the line when it lands (a later M3 increment needing a front-matter generate-or-insert path for set-field on an absent optional ref). Required header fields (`type`, `scope`) stay pre-stamped.

## 2026-06-01 — M3 Increment 2 planning: decomposition

Cut [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → M3 Increment 2 ("`jigc task bind` + `reads:` declaration — the cross-task binding spine") into four ordered, single-concern tasks, each with an observable red step. *Grounding (read the code):* the binding **resolution** layer already exists end-to-end — `data_value::Path::resolve` already gives `task.<role>` the full declared/unbound/undeclared semantics (declared+bound → `Resolution::Address`; declared+unbound → `Resolution::Absent`/empty text; undeclared → blocking `workflow-refs.undeclared-role`), `RolesRecord` (`engine::state`) already persists/loads `roles.json` with `bind`/`get`/`save`, `resume_in_repo` already re-reads `roles.json` and re-composes the bound slice over the committed store (`compose_with_store(&def, …, Some(&store))` — the exact seam `superseding_decision` proves for the create-gate-sourced `decision` role), and `store::canonical_path` + a schema's `ty` already resolve a committed `<type>:<slug>` address and its doctype. **The only genuinely missing surface is the *declaration + bind input* side:** `reads:` is parsed nowhere (only `when`/`creates-task`/`allows-create` load into `WorkflowDef`), `build_context` seeds roles only from `allows_create`, and there is no `bind` verb. So this increment is **declaration-parse + role-seed + the verb + a fixture acceptance**, riding the already-built resolution/persist/resume substrate. Risk-first, linear: declare `reads` (T1), seed it so `task.<role>` validates+resolves empty (T2), the `bind` verb that records the binding (T3), then the fixture resume re-compose that proves a bound `reads` role's slice resolves (T4). *Why this cut:* the smallest set that lands M3's #1 new risk — binding an existing committed doc into a later task — in isolation on fixtures, before the pack workflows (Increments 3–4) ride on it; T1–T2 make `task.spec` a valid `workflow-refs` root, T3 the input verb, T4 the end-to-end deferred-bind-then-resume proof.

- **T1 (engine):** parse the `reads:` front-matter key into `WorkflowDef`. *Done:* a new `Reads { role, type }` struct (the dual of `AllowsCreate`, serde `type`-renamed, **no `card`** per review #1/#2) + a `reads: Vec<Reads>` field on `WorkflowDef`, loaded by `WorkflowFrontMatter` (`#[serde(default)]`); a `compose.rs` load test asserts a `reads: [{role: spec, type: spec}]`-declaring front-matter yields `WorkflowDef.reads == [Reads{role: "spec", type: "spec"}]`, and that an omitted `reads:` defaults to empty — gate green.
- **T2 (cli):** seed each declared `reads` role into the compose role map. *Done:* `build_context` (`start.rs`) gains a loop over `def.reads` mirroring the `def.allows_create` loop — each `reads` role declared in `TaskRoot.roles`, bound iff `roles.json` recorded it, else `None`; a test (compose- or `build_context`-level over a `WorkflowDef` carrying a `reads` role + an empty `RolesRecord`) asserts `task.spec` resolves to `Resolution::Absent`/empty (no `workflow-refs.undeclared-role` finding) when unbound, and that an undeclared `task.<other>` still raises `undeclared-role` — gate green.
- **T3 (cli):** the `jigc task bind <role> <addr> <id>` verb — clap `TaskCommand::Bind` + dispatch with the five-step enforcement. *Done:* a new variant `Bind { role, addr, id }` (positional `<id>`, matching every sibling verb — elaboration pin below) routed in `TaskCommand::dispatch`; a `task_bind` integration test (real binary over a temp repo, `superseding_decision`-style harness) asserts each rejection — **no active task** (`no task <id>`), **role not in the workflow's declared `reads`** (lists declared roles), **`<addr>` unresolved in the committed store** (`no such doc <addr>`), **target doctype ≠ the role's declared `type`** (the mismatch) — and the success path: a committed-spec `<addr>` binds, `roles.json` records `spec → <addr>`, and a re-bind overwrites last-write-wins — gate green.
- **T4 (cli, fixture acceptance):** the bound `reads` role's slice resolves on resume re-compose. *Done:* a fixture test (engine compose-layer with a stub pack supplying a `reads: [{role: spec, type: spec}]`-declaring workflow def + a committed-store fixture with a `specs/<slug>.md`, mirroring `compose_with_store` + the `superseding_decision` slice seam) mints/loads a task whose `roles.json` binds `spec → spec:<slug>`, re-composes, and asserts `{{@task.spec#criteria}}` resolves to the committed spec's `#criteria` prose as a `> ` content block (not the bare address handle) — and resolves to **empty** when the role is declared-but-unbound — gate green.

- *Elaboration pin (T3 — verb signature):* the shipped task-verb surface (`diff`/`validate`/`discard`/`finalize`, `task.rs`) takes the task `<id>` as an **explicit positional**, with no cwd-derived "active task" resolver anywhere in the code. `write-commands.md`'s "active task (cwd, or `--task <id>`)" describes the eventual ergonomic; the locked MVP shape is explicit-`<id>`. So `bind` takes a positional `<id>` like its siblings — `jigc task bind <role> <addr> <id>` — resolved via the existing `TaskArea::resolve(cwd, id)`. The worked-example's emitted `Run: jigc task bind spec <SPEC_ID>` line (no id) is an *illustration*; emitting the exact re-run/bind line from a `locate-from-spec` step is Increment 4's pack concern, not this verb's. This is a doc-elaboration pin within the settled explicit-`<id>` surface, not a new fork.
- *Elaboration pin (T4 — fixtures, not pack workflows):* the Deliverable mandates proving "on fixtures (a test `reads`-declaring workflow + a committed spec fixture), **not yet the pack workflows**." `resume_in_repo` hardcodes `EmbeddedPack::new()`, so a `reads:`-declaring workflow cannot reach the *real binary* without embedding a non-pack workflow into the shipped pack — which would be scope creep. T4 therefore proves the resume re-compose at the **engine `compose_with_store` layer** with a stub pack + an explicit `reads`-carrying `WorkflowDef` and a committed-store fixture — the same seam `superseding_decision` exercises, parameterized with a `reads`-sourced (rather than create-gate-sourced) role binding. The pack-workflow end-to-end lands in Increment 4 (`implement-from-spec`); proving it here would be that increment's work pulled forward.

## 2026-06-01 — M3 Increment 2 T3 landed: the `jigc task bind <role> <addr> <id>` verb

Landed T3. `TaskCommand::Bind { role, addr, id }` (positional `<id>`, matching the sibling verbs) dispatches `TaskArea::bind`, which runs the five-step enforcement: (1) active task via `TaskArea::resolve`; (2) `role ∈ def.reads` (the task's *own* minting workflow def, read back via `state::read_workflow_id` + the embedded pack — the same source the resume re-compose reads) else reject listing the declared roles; (3) the address's canonical `<location>/<slug>.md` exists at HEAD (`store::canonical_path` over the loaded schema set) else `no such doc <addr>`; (4) the address's `<type>` equals the role's declared `type` else the mismatch; (5) `RolesRecord::bind` + `save` (last-write-wins). The `task_bind` integration test (real binary, temp-repo harness) asserts all four rejections + the success + re-bind path. `cli.rs` needed **no** change — `Bind` rides the existing `Command::Task { verb }` dispatch.

- *Elaboration pin (T3 — the real-binary test needs a pack-reachable `reads` workflow):* T3's done-criterion mandates a real-binary, `superseding_decision`-style harness exercising the five-step enforcement — which requires a `reads`-declaring workflow the *binary* can mint over (`bind` loads the task's recorded workflow from the embedded pack, exactly as `resume_in_repo` does). Unlike T4 (kept at the engine `compose_with_store` layer precisely to avoid an embed), T3's real-binary mandate forces a pack-reachable fixture, so a `bind-spec.yaml` test-fixture workflow (`creates-task: true`, `reads: [{role: spec, type: spec}]`, body = the `quick-fix` step set) ships in the embedded pack with a `when:` line explicitly marked `TEST FIXTURE`. It appears in the router's selectable catalog (benign — every existing catalog/orient test uses `.contains()`, none broke). The real-pack `reads`-declaring workflow (`implement-from-spec`) lands in Increment 4; `bind-spec` is the isolation fixture, removable when that ships.

## 2026-06-01 — M3 Increment 3 planning: decomposition

Cut [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → M3 Increment 3 ("the `plan` workflow — spec authoring + code-less finalize") into three ordered, single-concern tasks, each with an observable red step. *Grounding (read the code):* every engine/CLI surface this increment needs is already built and proven — so this increment is **pack bytes + tests, zero engine/CLI source change**. Specifically: (a) the **create-gate is fully generic** — `engine::state::create_gated` admits any `allows-create` entry, mints the doc via the schema set, and binds it to the entry's `as:` role in `roles.json` (verified for `adr`/`decision`; a `plan` carrying `allows-create: [{type: spec, as: spec}]` reuses it unchanged); (b) **promotion is generic over `location:`** — `engine::finalize::plan_promotions` names a `Promotion` to `<location>/<slug>.md` for every staged instance whose schema declares a `location:`, so a created `spec` promotes to `specs/` with no code change; (c) the **empty-commit guard counts a promoted/untracked doc as a diff signal** (`task.rs` `has_diff` ORs `git_untracked`), so a code-less spec-only task is not falsely "empty"; (d) **Form D** (`jigc start --workflow plan "<intent>"`) mints iff `creates-task: true` (`compose_named_in_repo`, built M2 inc-2); (e) **`docs` is already a `commit.type` enum member**, set via the built `doc set-field`; (f) the **`spec` schema ships + round-trips** (M3 inc-1). The one non-obvious constraint: the spec's `criteria` is a **repeatable** section and there is **no `add-item` write verb** (only `create`/`set-field`/`set-slot` shipped — `add-item`/`remove-item`/`reorder` are designed in [write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md) → the write-verb table but never built, exactly as `commit`'s repeatable `trailers` are never authored via the binary). This is **not** a blocker: `criteria` is `0..n` and "Repeatable sections are not adjudicated by these MVP checks" (`engine::validate`), so a `goal`+`context`-only spec validates, promotes, and finalizes — the deliverable ("the agent authors a spec via the create-gate" + "spec-only finalize lands one commit") holds without `add-item`. Authoring `criteria` through the binary is left to Increment 4 (where `{{@task.spec#criteria}}` is actually *read* — and where, if a live e2e needs authored criteria rather than a hand-written fixture, `add-item` is the designed surface to build). Risk-first, linear: ship the `plan` pack wiring + author-spec step (T1), surface it in the router catalog with a non-overlapping `when` (T2), then the headline spec-only code-less finalize e2e through the real binary (T3). *Why this cut:* the smallest set that lands the doc-creation-beyond-`adr` differentiator + the code-less finalize, no creep into Increment 4's binding/discovery/two-task arc.

- **T1 (pack):** ship the `plan` workflow + its `author-spec` step + the `create-spec` command-ref. *Done:* `crates/cli/pack/workflows/plan.yaml` (`creates-task: true`, `allows-create: [{type: spec, as: spec}]`, a `when:` hint, body = `[author-spec, finalize]`); `crates/cli/pack/steps/author-spec.yaml` emitting `{{cli.create-spec}}` (the create-gate mint) + `set-slot` author lines for `spec:<…>#goal` / `#context`; `crates/cli/pack/config/commands.yaml` gains a `create-spec` entry (`jigc doc create spec --title {agent: title}`, mirroring `create-adr`). A `start_compose`-style test asserts `jigc start --workflow plan "<intent>"` mints a task on `workflow: plan`, the composed output's four-class emit carries the `Run: jigc doc create spec` line (the create-gate), and `{{task.intent}}` interpolates — gate green.
- **T2 (pack + test):** `plan` joins the router's selectable catalog with a non-overlapping `when`. *Done:* `plan`'s `when:` hint is written to not overlap `single-task` / `quick-fix` (the live router selectables; the `bind-spec` TEST FIXTURE stays orthogonal); a `routing_loop`/`start_orientation`-style test asserts bare `jigc start "<intent>"` composes the router and the composed catalog **lists `plan` with its `when` hint** (`.contains()`), and an assertion that `plan`'s `when` shares no salient phrase with the other selectables' `when` — gate green. (No `default-workflow` flip: the router is already the default since M2.)
- **T3 (cli, e2e acceptance):** spec-only code-less finalize lands exactly one `docs` commit. *Done:* a new `finalize_to_git`/`superseding_decision`-style integration test (real `CARGO_BIN_EXE_jigc` over a throwaway temp git repo) drives the whole `plan` loop: `jigc start --workflow plan "<intent>"` → `jigc doc create spec --title "<…>"` (create-gate mint, bound to `task.spec`) → `set-slot` the spec's `goal`/`context` → `set-field commit:<task>#type docs` + `set-slot commit:<task>#summary` → **no code change** → `jigc task finalize <id>`; asserts (a) finalize exits zero, (b) **exactly one** new commit lands on HEAD, (c) `specs/<slug>.md` is committed at its canonical path (promotion), (d) the rendered commit message carries `docs` (the type), and (e) the spec-only task is **not** rejected by the empty-commit guard (the promoted spec is the diff) — gate green.

- *Elaboration pin (T1 — author-spec step shape + `criteria` omission):* the `author-spec` step mirrors `steps/implement.yaml`'s create-gate idiom — a `{{cli.create-spec}}` Run line then `set-slot` author lines for the spec's body slots (`goal`, `context`). It does **not** emit `criteria` authoring (no `add-item` verb exists; criteria is `0..n` and unadjudicated). The worked-example flow 6's `jigc doc add-item …#criteria` / `set-slot …#criteria/<id>#statement` lines are an *illustration of the eventual full authoring*; for M3 inc-3 the step authors a criteria-less-but-valid spec. This is a doc-elaboration pin within the locked write-verb design (`add-item` is specified, just unbuilt), not a new fork.
- *Elaboration pin (T1 — `create-spec` command-ref):* the catalog entry mirrors `create-adr` exactly (`command: jigc`, `args: [doc, create, spec, --title, {agent: title, hint: …}]`), so the agent-substitution `<TITLE>` and the four-class `Run:` emit reuse the proven `create-adr` path; the create-gate enforcement is `plan`'s `allows-create`, not the catalog entry. No engine/CLI change — `commands.yaml` is loaded by the already-built catalog parser.
- *T2 landed (pack + test, zero engine/CLI source change):* `plan`'s `when:` rephrased from the T1 stub (`draft the spec for a change before any code is written` — overlapped `single-task` on the salient word `change`) to **`draft the specification for upcoming work before writing any code`**, which shares no >4-char content word with `single-task` (`implement`/`scoped`/`change`) or `quick-fix` (`apply`/`commit`/`decision`/`small`/`record`). `routing_loop.rs` gains a `.contains("- plan — <when>")` assertion in step 1 (the composed router catalog lists `plan` automatically — `creates-task: true`) and generalizes the non-overlap check to a **pairwise** sweep over all three selectables (`single-task`/`quick-fix`/`plan`). `bind-spec` stays orthogonal (catalog assertions use `.contains()`).

## 2026-06-01 — M3 Increment 3 T3 halt: two create-gate defects fixed (build-harness first live run)

The `milestone-build` harness (first live run) built M3 increments 1–2 clean and inc-3's pack, then **correctly halted** at inc-3 / T3 (the plan create→finalize e2e) on two real defects the grounding pins had assumed away. Tree was left clean; nothing half-committed. Both fixed here (human-gated: b1 + defer-③ confirmed):

- **Defect ① — `doc.rs::workflow_gate()` hardcoded `single-task`.** The create-gate read `single-task`'s `allows-create` for *every* task, so `jigc doc create spec` under a `plan` task was blocked with "allowed doctypes: [adr]". Latent since M2 (never bit: `quick-fix` allows no creates). **Fix:** `workflow_gate` now resolves the task's *bound* workflow via `state::read_workflow_id` (mirroring `start::resume_in_repo`), so a `plan` task's gate reads `plan`'s `allows-create`. This **contradicts the inc-3 T3 grounding pin's "zero engine/CLI source change"** (DECISIONS line ~1304) — the create-gate was *not* fully generic; that pin was wrong.
- **Defect ② — the spec `title` field had no author path (the real fork, settled b1).** Inc-1's T1 added a `header` section with an author-required `title` **field**, justifying it (T1 pin, DECISIONS line ~1276) as "mirroring how `adr`'s title is its frozen-id source." **That rationale is factually wrong: `adr` has NO `title` field** — `id-from: title` derives the slug from the document H1 (set by `create --title`), never a schema field. The added field was author-required but unprovisioned and un-`set-field`-able (`write.not-present`), so finalize was unsatisfiable. **Resolution (b1, chosen over b2's new provisioning capability):** removed the `header` section + `title` field so `spec` mirrors `adr` exactly (sections = `goal`/`context`/`criteria`; title = H1). Restores the original Settle design (which never had a title field). Updated `schema_spec_golden` + its snapshot, the `spec_criteria_block_carries_statement_as_a_slot` structural test, and the T2 round-trip fixture (no front-matter — a headerless schema renders none, parser accepts absent front-matter). Verified end-to-end with the real binary: `start --workflow plan` → `create spec` → set goal/context → set commit type=docs/scope/summary/body → `finalize` lands one `docs(spec):` commit + promotes `specs/<slug>.md`.
- **③ deferred (pre-existing, out of M3 scope):** `commit.scope` (string, no default) is author-required and `commit.body` is a required slot, though conventional-commits treats both optional and `commit.yaml`'s own comment calls them "optional". Making them truly optional needs an optional-field/slot capability (engine work). Every existing finalize test/workflow sets them (`single-task`'s step under-instructs identically), so M3's `plan`/`implement-from-spec` commits set them too — no schema change. Logged as a latent defect for a future increment.
- **Forward risk flagged:** inc-1's T3 pin (DECISIONS line ~1282) deferred a "front-matter generate-or-insert path for `set-field` on an absent optional ref" — exactly what inc-4's `implement-from-spec` needs to set `commit#implements` (the fillable form now *omits* the optional `implements` line, so `set-field` would hit the same `write.not-present`). The build may halt at inc-4; to be fixed with full context there.

Gate green (engine 192 + all cli suites + fmt + clippy). Resuming `milestone-build` from the halt to finish T3 + Increment 4.

## 2026-06-01 — M3 Increment 4 planning: decomposition

Cut [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → M3 Increment 4 ("`implement-from-spec` + spec discovery + the two-task acceptance") into five ordered, single-concern tasks, each with an observable red step. This increment lands M3's headline — the full **intent → spec → implementation** arc end-to-end ([worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → flow 6). *Grounding (read the code):* the read/bind/edge-walk substrate is already built and proven — `data_value::Path::resolve` gives `task.spec` its declared/bound/unbound semantics, `jigc task bind` records the binding (inc-2), `resume_in_repo` re-composes the bound slice over the committed store via `StoreContext`/`compose_with_store` (the `superseding_decision` seam), and `index::ref_resolves` walks the `commit —implements→ spec` edge over the committed ∪ working overlay (proven on fixtures inc-1 T4). **Two genuine engine/CLI gaps remain, both isolated up front:** (1) the **`store` data-value root is unbuilt** — `store.specs` currently raises `workflow-refs.undeclared-root` (`data_value.rs`); `ComposeContext` carries `task`+`catalog` but no `store` feed, and there is no Content-list emit for an enumerated collection; (2) the **front-matter field generate-or-insert path is unbuilt** — `commit.implements` is a `header` (front-matter) optional ref that the fillable form *omits* (inc-3 T3 fix), so `set-field commit#header/implements` hits `write.not-present`: `set_field_validated` does not route an absent field to generation, and `generate_section` *explicitly rejects* the header section (`GenerateError::WrongShape`, `write.rs`). This is **exactly the forward risk the inc-3 T3 halt carried into inc-4** ("to be fixed with full context there", DECISIONS line ~1321) and the worked-example flow 6 (line 331) mandates — settled scope, an elaboration within the locked write-pipeline ("absent structural homes generate at their schema-ordered position", [parsing.md](implementation/parsing.md)), not a new fork. Risk-first, linear: the two engine gaps on fixtures/units first (T1 store, T2 front-matter insert), then the pack workflow that rides on both (T3), then the router's fourth entry (T4), then the two-task arc e2e through the binary (T5). *Why this cut:* the smallest set that lands the arc whole — T1+T2 retire the only new engine surfaces in isolation, T3 wires the pack `implement-from-spec` over them, T4+T5 prove the four-workflow router and the mandated acceptance path end-to-end; no creep past M3.

- **T1 (engine + cli):** the `store.<doctype-id>` data-value root resolves to the committed instances as a Content list. *Done:* `ComposeContext` gains a `store` feed (a map `doctype → Vec<address>` or equivalent, fed by the CLI like `catalog`); `data_value.rs` resolves a bare `store.<doctype-id>` to a new collection `Resolution` variant (a `.relation`/`#fragment`/`@` past it is a structural `workflow-refs.store-not-navigable` error, mirroring `catalog`); `compose.rs` emits a lone `{{store.<doctype-id>}}` as a readable `> ` Content list (one line per committed instance), an empty store → empty text (empty-not-finding); the CLI enumerates committed `<location>/<slug>.md` (the `index::rebuild_committed` walk) into the feed. *Red:* a `data_value.rs` unit test asserts `store.specs` against a fed two-spec store resolves to the collection variant (and `store.specs.x`/`@store.specs` are `store-not-navigable` findings), plus a `compose.rs` emit test asserting the two-spec Content list renders and an empty store emits empty text; gate green.
- **T2 (engine + cli):** `set-field` on an absent optional front-matter ref materializes the line (generate-or-insert), so `commit#implements` is settable on a provisioned commit doc. *Done:* `set_field_validated` routes a `NotPresent` field to a generate-or-insert path (mirroring `set_slot_validated`'s `generate_section` fallback) that inserts a `key: value` line into the **front-matter** (header) block at its schema-ordered position; `run_set_field` (`doc.rs`) reaches it unchanged. *Red:* an engine `write.rs` test stages a `commit` instance whose fillable form *omits* `implements`, runs the set-field-validated path for `commit#header/implements --value spec:<slug>`, and asserts the `implements:` line materializes in front-matter at its schema position, the buffer re-parses, and the diff is confined to the inserted line (a present `implements` still routes to the surgical splice); gate green.
- **T3 (pack):** ship the `implement-from-spec` workflow + the `step:locate-from-spec` + the spec set-field command-refs. *Done:* `pack/workflows/implement-from-spec.yaml` (`creates-task: true`, `reads: [{role: spec, type: spec}]`, `allows-create: [{type: adr, as: decision}]`, a `when:` hint, body = `[locate-from-spec, implement, finalize]`); `pack/steps/locate-from-spec.yaml` surfacing `{{store.specs}}`, emitting the `bind` Run line (`{{cli.bind-spec}}`), the re-compose `Run: jigc start --task {{task.id}}`, and `{{@task.spec#criteria}}`; the `implement` step (or a `locate-from-spec`-adjacent line) sets `commit#header/implements`; `pack/config/commands.yaml` gains the `bind-spec` (+ any `set-implements`) command-ref(s). *Red:* a `start_compose`-style test asserts `jigc start --workflow implement-from-spec "<intent>"` mints on `workflow: implement-from-spec`, the composed four-class output carries the `{{store.specs}}` Content list, the `Run: jigc task bind spec` line, the `Run: jigc start --task <id>` re-compose line, and `{{@task.spec#criteria}}` resolves empty on first compose (nothing bound); gate green.
- **T4 (pack + test):** `implement-from-spec` joins the router's selectable catalog with a `when` non-overlapping the other three. *Done:* the `when:` hint shares no salient phrase with `single-task`/`quick-fix`/`plan`; a `routing_loop`-style test asserts bare `jigc start "<intent>"` composes the router listing all **four** selectables (`.contains("- implement-from-spec — <when>")`) and extends the pairwise non-overlap sweep to the four `when` hints; gate green. (No `default-workflow` flip — the router is the default since M2; the `bind-spec` TEST FIXTURE workflow from inc-2 is removed now that the real `reads`-declaring `implement-from-spec` ships, or kept orthogonal if removal is out of scope — pinned at execute.)
- **T5 (cli, e2e acceptance):** the two-task arc end-to-end (plan → implement-from-spec) through the real binary, with the `implements` edge passing and the dangling variant blocking. *Done:* a `superseding_decision`/`finalize_spec_only`-style integration test (real `CARGO_BIN_EXE_jigc`, throwaway temp git repo) drives task 1 (`plan` → create+author spec → finalize → committed `specs/<slug>.md`) then task 2 (`jigc start --workflow implement-from-spec "<intent>"` → assert the first compose surfaces `{{store.specs}}` listing the committed spec → `jigc task bind spec spec:<slug>` → `jigc start --task <id>` → assert `{{@task.spec#criteria}}` now resolves to the committed spec's `#criteria` prose → set `commit#header/implements spec:<slug>` + type/summary → make a code change → finalize), asserting: (a) the spec slice resolves on resume, (b) finalize **passes** the `implements` forward-ref walk and lands one commit, (c) a **dangling** variant (`implements` → a non-committed spec) **blocks** finalize with `schema-conformance.ref-resolves`; gate green.

- *Elaboration pin (T1 — CLI-locates / engine-resolves):* the `store` collection is fed in by the CLI exactly as `catalog` is (`selectable_workflows` → `ComposeContext.catalog`); the engine resolves over the fed list, no committed-store I/O in the resolver (the determinism boundary). The CLI enumerates the committed instances via the existing `index::rebuild_committed` walk (every `location:`-declaring schema's `<location>/<slug>.md`), so no new enumeration logic. The collection element shape (bare `<type>:<slug>` address vs. address + a title hint) is a doc-elaboration pin made at execute — flow 6 shows `spec:<slug> — "Title"`, so a title hint is preferred if cheaply available from the committed H1, else the bare address; either satisfies the readable-Content-list deliverable.
- *Elaboration pin (T2 — the generate target is the existing pipeline):* the front-matter insert reuses the write pipeline's "absent structural home generates at its schema-ordered position" rule already proven for slots (`set_slot_validated` → `generate_section`) and body-bullet fields (`insert_field`); the only new surface is the *header/front-matter* insertion site, which `generate_section` currently rejects (`WrongShape`). Whether to extend `generate_section` to admit the header or add a sibling `insert_front_matter_field` is an implementation-shape pin at execute; both land the same observable: an absent optional front-matter ref becomes settable, the diff confined to the inserted line, the buffer re-parsing. This unblocks `commit#implements`; it also retroactively makes the inc-3-deferred ③ (required `commit.scope`) materializable if a later increment wants it, but ③ stays out of M3 scope (every flow still sets scope).
- *Elaboration pin (T3 — `set-implements` emit shape):* flow 6 emits `Run: jigc doc set-field commit:<task>#header/implements --value spec:<slug>` inline in the `implement` step. Whether this rides as a `{{cli.set-implements}}` catalog command-ref (mirroring `set-commit-summary`) or an inline `Run:` line with an agent-substitution `<SPEC_ID>` is a pack-shape pin at execute; the catalog command-ref is preferred for consistency with the existing emit idiom. The `bind-spec` command-ref's `<SPEC_ID>` arg is an `{agent: …}` substitution (the agent fills the chosen id from the `{{store.specs}}` menu), matching `create-adr`'s agent-fill pattern.
- *Elaboration pin (T5 — authoring `criteria` for the slice assertion):* the slice assertion needs the committed spec to carry ≥1 `criteria` item. `criteria` is a repeatable section and `add-item` is designed but unbuilt (inc-3 grounding); if the live binary cannot author a criterion in task 1, the e2e seeds the committed `specs/<slug>.md` with a hand-written conformant criteria item (an editable-channel commit, the same way `superseding_decision` seeds its committed ADR) before task 2 binds — proving the *read/bind/slice* path (this increment's deliverable) without pulling `add-item` forward. If `add-item` turns out cheap to build, authoring it in-task is the cleaner path; pinned at execute.

## 2026-06-01 — M3 Increment 4 T1 shipped: the `store` data-value root

Built the engine-native `store` collection root + its Content-list emit + the CLI feed (`data_value.rs`, `compose.rs`, `start.rs`), mirroring the `catalog` root end-to-end. Two genuine elaboration choices resolved at execute (both within the locked grammar; not new forks):

- **The `store` root is keyed by the schema `location` stem, not the type id.** The design's concrete examples (`store.specs`, `store.findings`; `workflow-dialect.md` line 149) are the **plural collection name**, but the schema is `type: spec` / `location: specs/`. The phrase "`store.<doctype-id>`" is loose; the examples settle it. So `committed_store` keys by `location.trim_matches('/')` (`specs/` → `specs`), which is exactly what flow 6's `{{store.specs}}` writes. The resolver stays **agnostic** — it does `ctx.store.get(<hop>)` over whatever the CLI feeds; the keying is wholly a CLI concern (the determinism boundary: engine resolves the fed map, CLI does the committed-store walk). A doctype with no committed files is omitted ⇒ `store.<x>` resolves to the empty collection ⇒ empty text (empty-not-finding).
- **Collection element = bare `<type>:<slug>` address; the title hint is deferred.** Flow 6 shows `spec:<slug> — "Title"`, but the title is the committed doc's H1, which would need an extra per-doc read the `rebuild_committed` walk does not do. The planner pin explicitly allows "else the bare address," and the done-criterion only requires a `> ` Content list, one line per instance. So `render_store_list` emits `> <type>:<slug>` per line; the title hint can ride later if cheap from the committed H1.

Mechanics: new `Resolution::Store { entries: Vec<Address> }`; `ComposeContext` gains `store: BTreeMap<String, Vec<Address>>`; a bare `store.<doctype>` (exactly one hop, no `#fragment`, no `@`) resolves to the collection, anything else (`store` alone, `@store.x`, `store.x.y`, `store.x#f`) is the blocking `workflow-refs.store-not-navigable` finding (mirrors `catalog-not-navigable`). The shared "collection used non-lone" finding was renamed `catalog-not-lone` → **`collection-not-lone`** (it now guards both `catalog` and `store` misuse as a command-arg / content / author / inline value). Gate green (engine 195 + all cli suites + fmt + clippy + build).

## 2026-06-01 — M3 Increment 4 T2 shipped: front-matter generate-or-insert for `set-field`

Closed the forward risk the inc-3 T3 halt carried in (DECISIONS line ~1321): `set-field` on an **absent optional front-matter ref** (`commit#header/implements`, omitted by the fillable form) now **materializes** the `implements:` line at its schema-ordered position instead of blocking with `write.not-present`. `set_field_validated` (`write.rs`) routes an absent-field `locate_field_value` miss to the generation half of the write path (mirroring `set_slot_validated`'s `NotPresent`→generate fallback); a present field still routes to the surgical splice. `run_set_field` (`doc.rs`) reaches it unchanged — no CLI change.

Implementation-shape pin (the planner flagged this as execute-time, both shapes land the same observable): **added a sibling `insert_front_matter_field`** rather than extend `generate_section` to admit the header. `generate_section`'s contract is body-section structural homes (`## Heading` + leaf, schema-document-order); the header is a single `---`-fenced flat block keyed by *field* order, a different positioning calculus (`front_matter_insertion_offset`: line-start of the nearest following present header field, else the content end before the closing `---`). Folding both into one function would have meant a header branch that shares no positioning logic — the sibling keeps each function one-shape. The validated gate routes header→`insert_front_matter_field`, body-field-group→the existing `insert_field`; generation's only post-write gate is re-parse (no single-span to confine). Gate green (engine 197 + all cli suites + fmt + clippy + build).

## 2026-06-01 — M3 Increment 4 T3 shipped: `implement-from-spec` workflow + `step:locate-from-spec`

Shipped the three pack files (previously empty/absent): `workflows/implement-from-spec.yaml` (`creates-task: true`, `reads: [{role: spec, type: spec}]`, `allows-create: [{type: adr, as: decision}]`, body `[locate-from-spec, implement, finalize]`), `steps/locate-from-spec.yaml` (distinct from the shared `step:locate` — surfaces `{{store.specs}}`, emits the bind + re-compose `Run:` lines, reads `{{@task.spec#criteria}}`), and two new `commands.yaml` entries. No engine change — the inc-4 T1 `store` root + the inc-3 `@`-content resolver already carry every placeholder. First-compose contract proven by a `start_compose` e2e: `--workflow implement-from-spec` mints recording its own id, the composed view carries the committed-spec Content list, both `Run:` lines, and `{{@task.spec#criteria}}` resolves empty (unbound role).

Resolved the planner's two execute-time pins (both within the locked grammar):
- **Both emit shapes are catalog command-refs**, not inline `Run:` lines — preferred for consistency with `create-adr`/`set-commit-summary`. `bind-spec` carries `<SPEC_ID>` as an `{agent: spec_id, hint: …}` substitution (uppercased to the `<SPEC_ID>` marker, matching `create-adr`'s agent-fill); `recompose-task` carries `{from: task.id}` so the emitted `Run: jigc start --task <id>` line interpolates the resolved task id (not an agent fill — the id is deterministic task state).
- **`locate-from-spec` opens with `{{task.intent}}`** (mirroring `step:locate`) so the spec-driven step still surfaces the intent; the shared `step:implement`/`step:finalize` are reused unchanged. The `set-implements` emit (flow 6's `jigc doc set-field …#implements`) stays the execute-time pin for a later increment's `implement`-step edit — T3's deliverable is the locate-from-spec first-compose, which does not author `implements`.

The catalog golden (`compose.rs`) was extended for the two new BTreeMap-ordered entries; `pack.rs`'s step-list pin gained `locate-from-spec`. Gate green (engine 197 + all cli suites + fmt + clippy + build).

## 2026-06-01 — M3 Increment 4 T4 shipped: `implement-from-spec` joins the router catalog

Two pack/test changes, zero engine/CLI source change. `implement-from-spec` (`creates-task: true`, shipped in T3) already joins the selectable catalog automatically (`selectable_workflows` filters on `creates-task`), so T4 is the non-overlap rephrase + the routing-loop assertions:
- **`when:` rephrased** from the T3 phrase (`implement work whose criteria a committed spec already defines` — overlapped `single-task` on the salient word `implement`) to **`build from a committed spec whose acceptance criteria already exist`**, which shares no >4-char content word with `single-task` (`implement`/`scoped`/`change`), `quick-fix` (`apply`/`small`/`commit`/`decision`/`record`), or `plan` (`draft`/`specification`/`upcoming`/`writing`).
- **`routing_loop.rs`** gains a `.contains("- implement-from-spec — <when>")` assertion in step 1 (router lists all FOUR selectables) and the pairwise non-overlap sweep is generalized from three to four selectables. Red proven: with the old `when`, the sweep fails on the `implement` collision.

Resolved the planner's one execute-time pin: **`bind-spec` TEST FIXTURE kept orthogonal, not removed.** Removal is out of this task's Files scope (`implement-from-spec.yaml` + `routing_loop.rs`) and would force migrating `task_bind.rs` (which mints over `bind-spec` to exercise the five-step bind enforcement in isolation) plus the `compose.rs` command-catalog golden. Catalog assertions use `.contains()` and the non-overlap sweep iterates only the four explicit selectables, so the fixture's continued presence in the catalog is benign — as the inc-2/inc-3 entries documented. Gate green (engine 197 + all cli suites + fmt + clippy + build).

## 2026-06-02 — M3 Increment 4 T5 halt: store-read slices a repeatable section

The `milestone-build` resume cleared inc-3 T3 and built inc-4's engine + pack (store.specs discovery `ee668c5`, the generate-or-insert set-field for absent optional refs `6983ae1` — the forward risk flagged at the T3 halt, closed proactively by the executor; the `implement-from-spec` workflow `d476ac1`), then **halted at T5** (the two-task acceptance e2e) on one real gap: `{{@task.spec#criteria}}` could not resolve because `engine::store::slice_fragment` only sliced **slot** sections, but the shipped `spec.criteria` is **repeatable**. The proven read path (`superseded-context` → `#decision`) only ever read a slot, so this was a gap **gap-detection missed** (it assumed the persisted-slice read was general). Inc-2's engine acceptance had even worked around it with a stub slot-criteria schema (comment: "NOT the shipped spec.yaml"), so the gap was known but never closed for the real pack.

**Fix (engine):** `slice_fragment` now slices a repeatable section too — when a section has no slot, it renders its items as a Content list via `render_repeatable_items`: each item's `### <title>` heading followed by its `statement` slot prose, items joined by a blank line, in on-disk order; zero items → empty content (the absent-value case, not an error). The composer wraps it as a `> ` Content blockquote, so `{{@task.spec#criteria}}` reads as the titled, prose-carrying criteria list `worked-examples.md` flow 6 depicts. **Render-format decision:** title-heading + statement-prose per item (faithful to the doc's own structure; reuses the items' recorded title + slot span, no new byte invention) — chosen over a flat `title — statement` one-liner (loses multi-line statements) and over re-emitting frozen `{#id}` anchors (read-path noise the agent doesn't need). A golden (`store_slices_a_committed_spec_repeatable_criteria_section`) pins it over the shipped `spec.yaml`. Verified e2e with the real binary: plan authors+commits the spec, `implement-from-spec` lists it via `{{store.specs}}`, `task bind spec` + resume-compose reads the criteria slice. Gate green (193 engine + all cli + fmt + clippy). Resuming the build to finish T5 + the milestone audit.

## 2026-06-02 — M3 milestone-completion audit: triage

All four increments built and **independently validated clean**; the milestone-completion audit (independent code-review + real-binary e2e) ran. e2e: all six scenarios pass (orientation/router lists four work-workflows with non-overlapping `when`; plan authors a spec → code-less `docs(spec):` finalize; `implement-from-spec` discovery via `{{store.specs}}`; cross-task bind + persisted criteria-slice read on resume; `implements` edge passes at finalize; dangling `implements` blocks). One scope-honesty bug was caught **and auto-fixed within the milestone** by the validate/fix loop (`dd64d17` — the `bind-spec` *test-fixture* workflow had leaked into the production router catalog). Code-review surfaced two findings; triage:

- **(HIGH — FIXED) The emitted `jigc task bind` Run line was not runnable.** The `bind-spec` command-ref emitted `jigc task bind spec <SPEC_ID>`, but the clap surface is `task bind <role> <addr> <id>` (three positionals — the `<id>` is the task, consistent with the `jigc task <verb> <id>` family). So an agent following the composed `Run:` markers (M3's documented path) hit `error: required <ID> not provided`; the e2e passed only because it appended the id in test code, masking the gap. **Fix:** the `bind-spec` ref now appends `{from: "task.id"}` (mirroring `recompose-task`), emitting the runnable `jigc task bind spec <SPEC_ID> <task-id>`; the masking `start_compose` assertion was tightened to require the resolved id; the catalog snapshot + the design docs (write-commands.md signature/enforcement/emitted-form, worked-examples flow 6) reconciled to the three-arg form. Verified: the emitted line runs verbatim against the real binary (exit 0). Gate green.
- **(LOW — ACCEPTED as deferred) `plan` cannot author spec `criteria` through the binary.** No `add-item` verb ships, so the repeatable `criteria` section is workflow-inert — criteria reach a spec only via the editable channel (hand-edit + git). Already honestly logged (inc-3 T1 pin); `add-item` is designed in write-commands.md but unbuilt. The differentiator the milestone proves (cross-task spec read + the `implements` edge) holds regardless. Left deferred to a future increment that needs authored criteria.

M3 (spec-driven planning) ships: the intent → spec → implementation arc works end-to-end across two tasks, with cross-task binding and `implements` edge integrity, on the proven read/write/edge substrate.

## 2026-06-03 — Split the override milestone: M4 (apply) + M5 (reconcile); spine renumbered

The M4-planning baseline audit (four `capability-auditor` subagents, exercise-don't-infer) found the old single "M4 · override machinery" entry bundles two separable halves whose combined cost is far larger than M2 or M3: (a) **override application** — wire the cascade live + build the four delta kinds + a delta-recording surface — and (b) **upgrade reconciliation** — the entirely-greenfield `override-default` probe + `jigc upgrade` + base-hash + 3-way-merge + severity-floor stack. The audit's landmine: the cascade resolver (`cascade::resolve`, phases 2–3) is **unit-proven but unwired** — its only live caller (`orient.rs:63`) uses it for the provenance header alone, feeding an empty scalar map; live compose reads from a single layer (`PackStepSource`). Phases 4 (structural deltas) and 5 (slot-fills) are absent entirely. So "M4 builds delta application on a working cascade" was false; the cascade itself must be wired first. Split (human decision):

- **M4 · override application** — principle #5's *individualize* half: record deltas against the versioned base and apply them through the 9-phase resolution so a project composes differently from the pack default without forking. Includes the not-yet-built config read path / knob declaration+typing / loader, wiring the proven phase-2/3 resolver into the live compose `StepSource`+scalar feeds, and building phases 4–5.
- **M5 · upgrade reconciliation** (new) — principle #5's *inherit-upstream* half: `override-default` probe + guarded `jigc upgrade` (clean/conflict/orphaned, stateless base-hash compare, agent-drafts-3-way-merge → human confirms) + the severity-floor/demotion-lock machinery its tunable checks need.

Old **M5 (milestone execution / fan-out-join) → M6**, old **M6 (project setup) → M7**; the milestone execution entry's "after M4" updated to "after M5" (it rides on the now-two-milestone override substrate). `doctype-map.md`'s "(M6)" idea-development-setup pointer updated to "(M7)". This commit carries only the spine restructure; M4's increment decomposition follows from the `/milestone-plan M4` run in progress.

## 2026-06-03 — M4 (override application) planning: scope + design settled

Four `gap-detector` subagents (decisions / docs / doctypes / capabilities), each spiking the exact new shape against HEAD, surfaced that M4's reuse claims are mostly `unverified-reuse`: the cascade resolver is unwired (zero production callers for `Resolved::file_owner`/`scalar`); phases 4 (structural-op) and 5 (slot-fill) are absent; no config loader reads `.jigc/config/` bytes; no typed knob is declared anywhere; the `Address` grammar is instance-scoped and does **not** fit a definition-target (spiked + refuted); the `<<author:>>` markers are write-path sinks, not config-time fill points. Human-gated scope calls (the maximal reading on each fork):

- **All four delta kinds ship in M4** — `scalar-set`, `structural-op` (insert/replace/remove), `slot-fill`, `tracked-fork`. (`tracked-fork` *records* base-hash in M4; its only *consumer*, upgrade reconciliation, is M5 — recording now is the M5 precondition, not dead plumbing.)
- **Full `jigc config` write-verb suite** — `config set <key> <value>` (scalar, typed/closed-surface adjudicated), `config insert-step --after <id> --workflow <id> <file>`, `config replace-step <addr> <file>`, `config remove-step <addr>`, `config fill <addr> --from-file <file>`, `config fork <addr>`. Hand-editing the manifest stays a co-equal path (overrides.md "delta form only"); the loader applies all four kinds regardless of authoring route.
- **Typed knobs** — a new `pack/config/knobs.yaml` declares the closed surface as `{key, type, of?, default}` reusing `schema::FieldType`; `config set` adjudicates via `check_value` (wrong-type rejected, undeclared key rejected). Knob defaults are materialized by the resolver seeding the resolved map from declared defaults — **separate** from the broken doc-side `Field.default` (which stays unfixed in M4, logged as an orthogonal write-path defect).
- **slot-fill target = an inline `{{fill:<id>}}` placeholder** in step/section bodies — a 4th placeholder kind (read-path, CLI-filled at phase 5; pack may carry a default body). A `slot-fill` delta targets `step:<id>#<fill-id>` and overrides the content. Invariant-clean: read-path = `{{…}}`, distinct from write-path `<<author:>>`.
- **structural-op target = a definition-address**, distinct from the instance-scoped content `Address`: `workflow:<id>` + `#<step-id>` / `after:` / `before:` over the include list; `schema:<id>#<section-id>` over the sections list. Applied at phase 4 *before* expansion.

**Confirmed non-forks:** team layer stays project-only in M4 (resolver is already team-ready, no signature change to add it in M5); a `scalar-set` on a `validation.*.severity` key is M4, but the **demotion-lock floor enforcement is M5** (the floor's consumer, the tunable `override-default` severities, is M5); the doc-side `default:`-not-materialized defect is orthogonal and logged, not fixed.

**Determinism guard (carries across every wiring increment):** no-override compose output must stay **byte-identical** — `resolve` hard-errors on an undeclared key, so the populated pack-default base must declare every key any path reads, proven by a no-delta golden before any wiring lands.

Docs elaborated this milestone (Settle phase): overrides.md (M4/M5 scope markers; knob-declaration format; the `{{fill:}}` slot-fill; the definition-target address; the `jigc config` verb surface; reconciled `with:`/`after:` manifest grammar), storage.md (formal `.jigc/config/` layout: `manifest.yaml` + `steps/` + `fills/`), workflow-dialect.md (`{{fill:<id>}}` as the 4th placeholder kind), worked-examples.md (flow 3 extended into the per-kind acceptance test). The overstated `cascade.rs`/`compose.rs:1004` "already wired into compose" doc-comments are flagged for correction in M4's first increment (code, not a planning artifact).

## 2026-06-03 — M4 (override application) decomposed into 6 risk-first increments

Cut into six ordered increments in [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → "Milestone 4 — override application: decomposition": (1) config loader + live scalar cascade + `config set` + the determinism trap (the #1 risk — wiring the unwired resolver, no-override byte-identical); (2) structural-op application (phase-2 step shadowing + phase-4 include-list mutation + the definition-target address — the #2 risk); (3) the structural-op verbs; (4) slot-fill end-to-end (`{{fill:}}` phase-5 + the M4 orphan/nested `workflow-refs` checks + `config fill` + the pack `{{fill: extra-guidance}}` point); (5) `tracked-fork` (`config fork` + blake3 base-hash recording — the M5 precondition); (6) `--explain` overrides + the e2e acceptance across all four kinds. Risk-first, linear, each single-concern. The design survived an independent `design-reviewer` pass whose ten findings were all accepted and baked before the cut (two genuine forks human-gated: orphaned-slot-fill detection lands in M4 `workflow-refs` not M5; the `{{fill:}}` point ships in a real pack step `implement`, not an invented `validate`). Ready for `milestone-build` (base = the planning commit).

## 2026-06-03 — M4 Increment 1 planning: decomposition

Cut [Increment 1 — Config loader + live scalar cascade](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-1--config-loader--live-scalar-cascade-the-wiring-spine--the-determinism-trap) into seven ordered, single-concern tasks. *Why this order:* the resolver (cascade phases 2–3) is built but **unwired** — `compose_in_repo` reads `default-workflow` ad-hoc via `serde_yaml_ng::get()` in `default_workflow_id` (`start.rs:577`), and the only `cascade::resolve` caller (`orient.rs:63`) feeds an **empty** scalar map; so the cut builds the two feeds (knob declaration → seeded `PackDefaultLayer`, then manifest `scalar:` → populated `OverrideLayer`) and the read-side hard-error *before* flipping the live read, with the no-delta byte-identical golden the gate on that flip — the milestone's #1 risk retired in isolation. Grounded facts the cut rests on (verified against HEAD, not assumed): `default-workflow` is the **only** scalar any compose path reads today (the declared `validation.*.severity` knobs have no compose-time reader until M5), so the read-side invariant binds exactly that one key; `check_value(&schema::Field, &Value)` (`write.rs:2282`) is reused as-is by building a `schema::Field` per knob entry — no second type system; `knobs.yaml` reads as a `PackResourceKind::Config` resource exactly like the existing `defaults.yaml`; no project-layer `manifest.yaml` writer or `scalar:`-block reader exists yet (today `.jigc/config/` is only a directory-presence check).

- **T1** — Pack ships `pack/config/knobs.yaml` declaring the closed knob surface (`default-workflow` + the `validation.*.severity` keys as `{key, type, of?, default}`); `pack-id` stays in `defaults.yaml` (non-knob identity). *Done:* a pack test reads `Config`/`knobs` and asserts the declared keys/types/defaults via snapshot.
- **T2** — Knob loader: parse `knobs.yaml` into per-key `schema::Field`s and seed `PackDefaultLayer`'s scalar surface (closed key set + materialized defaults) from it, replacing the `BTreeMap::new()` empty feed. *Done:* a test loads the embedded `knobs.yaml`, builds the layer, and asserts `resolved.scalar("default-workflow")` is the declared default and the key set is exactly the declared keys.
- **T3** — `.jigc/config/manifest.yaml` `scalar:`-block loader → a populated `OverrideLayer` (`scalar_set` per entry, manifest order); a missing/blank `scalar:` block → an empty layer. *Done:* a test feeds a hand-authored manifest fixture and asserts the resulting `OverrideLayer` applied over the base flips the resolved value.
- **T4** — Read-side determinism invariant (engine): a compose-read accessor that treats `resolved.scalar(k) == None` as a **hard error**, not a silent fallback. *Done:* a unit test asserts the accessor errors loudly for an undeclared key and returns the value for a declared one.
- **T5** — Wire the resolved cascade live into `compose_in_repo`: build `PackDefaultLayer` (T2) + `OverrideLayer` from the project manifest (T3), `cascade::resolve`, and read `default-workflow` via the T4 accessor — deleting the ad-hoc `default_workflow_id`/`serde_yaml_ng::get()` path. *Done:* a no-delta **byte-identical golden** over the existing `start_compose` fixtures (no-override compose output unchanged) **and** a project-layer `scalar-set` of `default-workflow` flips which workflow bare-`jigc start "<intent>"` composes, both asserted through the binary.
- **T6** — `jigc config set <key> <value>`: clap `config` subcommand tree + dispatch, writing the project layer's `manifest.yaml` `scalar:` block, adjudicated at write time via `check_value` against the knob's `schema::Field` (undeclared key / wrong-type rejected with a routed finding). *Done:* a binary test sets a valid knob (then composes to confirm the change), and asserts an undeclared key and a wrong-type value are each rejected non-zero with the finding message.
- **T7** — Correct the overstated `cascade.rs` module doc + `compose.rs:1003` `StepSource` doc-comment that frame phase-2 shadowing as "already built/wired into compose" (the live `PackStepSource` never consults `Resolved::file_owner`; shadowing wiring is increment 2). *Done:* `cargo doc` / `fmt --check` / `clippy -D warnings` clean and the comments no longer claim compose-time wiring that does not exist.

Each task ends in one conventional commit; serial over the shared tree. No new fork surfaced — every bullet maps to settled M4 decisions (2026-06-03) and the locked [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) / [storage.md](design/storage.md) sections.

### M4 inc-1 T1 — `knobs.yaml` declares two per-probe severity keys (not per-check)
