The done-criterion's "the `validation.*.severity` keys" is concretized as the **per-probe coarse-default** form (`validation.<probe>.severity`) — matching the [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) `knobs.yaml` example's own granularity (it declares `validation.doc-code.severity`) — for exactly the **two engine-native MVP probes** that have severity-inventory rows: `validation.workflow-refs.severity` + `validation.file-state.severity` (both `enum [blocking, warning, advisory]`, default `blocking` per the inventory). Per-*check* keys (`validation.<probe>.<check>.severity`) and the other inventory probes (`schema-conformance`, `override-default`, `commit-rendering`, `doc-code`) are out: the closed-surface principle declares a knob only where a reader exists, and no compose-time reader consumes any severity key until M5 — so the minimal surface that satisfies "the `validation.*.severity` keys" is these two, declared at the per-probe granularity, leaving the finer/locked surface to land with its M5 consumer.

### M4 inc-1 T2 — knob loader (`engine::knobs`) requires a `default`; folds each knob into a `schema::Field`

The knob loader lives in `engine` (a pure function over `config/knobs.yaml` bytes, reusing `schema::Field` + `check_value`), not `cli` — the CLI only *locates* the bytes (it reads the `knobs` pack resource and feeds them in). Each knob entry's `{type, of?, default}` folds into a `schema::Field` with `id` = the map key, so a `scalar-set` is adjudicated by the same `check_value` the doc write path uses (no second type system). **A knob with no `default` is a load error** (`KnobError::MissingDefault`): the default *is* the value the resolver seeds the closed surface with, so a defaultless knob would seed nothing and leave a compose-read returning `None` — the exact read-side determinism hole T4 closes; rejecting at load keeps the seeded base map total over the declared keys. The doc-instance `Field.default` stays unused here (set to `None` on the folded field) — the knob default is materialized by *this* loader, orthogonal to the still-unfixed write-path `Field.default` defect.

### M4 inc-1 T6 — `jigc config set` mirrors the `task`/`doc` verb-tree pattern; writes only the project `scalar:` block, round-tripped through `serde_yaml_ng::Value`

`jigc config set <key> <value>` lands as a new `cli::config` module with a `ConfigCommand` subcommand tree mirroring `Task { verb }` / `Doc { verb }` (dispatch → exit code, a routed blocking finding on stderr → non-zero). Write-time adjudication reuses the same path the loader trusts: the key must be a declared knob in the pack's `config/knobs.yaml` (`knobs.field(key)`, else `config.undeclared-key`), and the value must pass `engine::write::check_value` against that knob's folded `Field` (else `config.value-rejected`) — no second type system, the [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) Write-time-vs-resolve-time split. **The write round-trips the manifest through `serde_yaml_ng::Value`** rather than appending text, so any existing `scalar:` entries and the (later-increment) `deltas:` block survive a `set` (last-write-wins on the one key); the read path (`start::load_project_layer`) parses back exactly that `scalar:` map. Whole-cascade consequences stay deferred to resolution-time `workflow-refs` (this increment ships only `set`; the structural verbs are increments 3–5).

## 2026-06-03 — M4 Increment 2 planning: decomposition

Cut [Increment 2 — Structural-op application: step shadowing (phase 2) + include-list mutation (phase 4)](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-2--structural-op-application-step-shadowing-phase-2--include-list-mutation-phase-4) into five ordered, single-concern tasks. This increment builds the **second from-scratch resolution phase** (the milestone's #2 risk) — structural deltas mutate the composition tree deterministically *before* expansion, over a definition-target namespace that does not collide with document addressing — proven on **fixtures** (worked-examples flow 3a's compose-time application), **not yet a verb** (the `config insert-step`/`replace-step`/`remove-step` verbs are increment 3). *Why this order:* phase 2 (layer-aware step reads) and phase 4 (include-list mutation) are independent surfaces — phase 4 mutates the **id list**, phase 2 resolves an **id to bytes** — so the cut builds the definition-target address model first (the spiked-and-refuted-reuse shape both later phases name targets with), then the phase-4 mutation pass (a pure list transform, unit-testable with a fake step source), then the live phase-2 layer-aware `StepSource`, then threads both into the live compose path, then the resolution-time cycle/orphan interaction. *Grounded facts the cut rests on (verified against HEAD at `2137eff`, not assumed):*

- The live step source `PackStepSource` (`start.rs:564`) reads pack bytes **only** (`pack.read(Steps, …)`) and **never consults `Resolved::file_owner`** — which is unit-proven (`cascade.rs:198`, test `project_file_shadows_pack_default_by_id`) but has **zero production callers** (the inc-1 T7 doc-correction says exactly this). So phase 2 is genuinely unwired on the live read path.
- `compose_core` (`start.rs:235`) **builds no `Resolved`** — `resolve_default_workflow` (`start.rs:591`) constructs the layers, calls `cascade::resolve`, reads the one scalar, and **discards the `Resolved`**. Threading a `Resolved` into `compose_core` (so the step source can see `file_owner`) is net-new wiring this increment owns.
- `load_project_layer` (`start.rs:643`) reads **only** the `scalar:` block; it never populates `OverrideLayer.files` from project step files and never reads `deltas:`. The `files`/`shadow_file` surface (`cascade.rs:104`) feeds phase-2 shadowing in `resolve` but is fed by nothing live.
- The content `Address` (`engine/src/address.rs`) is instance-scoped (`type:slug#unit/item/leaf`); the spike refuted reusing it for a definition-target (DECISIONS 2026-06-03 → planning scope). `WorkflowDef.includes: Vec<String>` (`compose.rs:72`) is the ordered id list phase 4 mutates; `expand_includes(def, source)` (`compose.rs:1074`) walks `def.includes` then resolves each id via `source`, so phase 4 must transform the id list **before** `expand_includes` runs.
- No `deltas:` parsing or structural-op application exists anywhere; the verbs are explicitly increment 3 (`config.rs:5`).

- **T1** — The **definition-target address model** (engine): a `StructuralTarget` parser distinct from the content `Address`, parsing `workflow:<id>` + the `#<step-id>` (replace/remove target) / `after:<id>` / `before:<id>` (insert anchor) forms over a workflow's include list, with malformed/empty/whitespace targets rejected as located findings (the settled block envelope). Pure, no I/O. *Done:* unit tests assert each well-formed form parses to its `{workflow-id, anchor-kind, step-id}` shape and that hostile input (empty id, wrong scheme, missing anchor, non-ASCII) is a located finding, never a panic. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Delta targets — addressing a definition; `replace` vs `tracked-fork`.
- **T2** — The **phase-4 structural-delta pass** (engine): a pure `apply_structural_deltas(includes: Vec<String>, deltas: &[StructuralDelta]) -> Result<Vec<String>, Finding>` that walks `insert` (`after:`/`before:` an anchor id) / `replace` (`#<step-id>` → another step id) / `remove` (`#<step-id>`) deltas in fed order over the include-id list, returning the mutated id list — applied **before** `expand_includes`. A `replace`'s replacement is **another step id**, never inline content (overrides.md → `replace` vs `tracked-fork`). *Done:* unit tests over a `[locate, implement, …]` fixture assert insert-after/insert-before land at the right index, replace swaps the id at position, remove drops it, and within-fed-order multiple deltas compose (replace-then-remove of the same id) — golden on the resulting id vector. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Resolution algorithm phase 4, Why structural deltas precede expansion, Within-layer manifest order.
- **T3** — The **layer-aware `StepSource`** (cli): a `CascadeStepSource` consulting `Resolved::file_owner(id)` to read a step's bytes from the **highest-precedence layer** that owns it (project `.jigc/config/steps/<id>.yaml` → pack-default via the embedded pack), replacing the single-layer `PackStepSource` — phase-2 by-id shadowing wired into the read at last. A project file present for an id the pack also ships → project bytes win; an id only the pack ships → pack bytes; a `file_owner` of `Project` whose file is missing on disk is a clear located error. *Done:* a unit/integration test with a project `.jigc/config/steps/<id>.yaml` fixture asserts `source.step(id)` returns the **project** body when the project owns the id and the **pack** body otherwise. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Resolution algorithm phase 2; [storage.md](design/storage.md) → Config layout.
- **T4** — **Wire phases 2+4 live into compose** (cli): `load_project_layer` also reads the `deltas:` list (structural-ops referencing native step files) and populates `OverrideLayer.files` from the project `steps/` dir; `compose_core` builds the `Resolved` cascade (threading it down rather than discarding it in `resolve_default_workflow`), applies T2's phase-4 pass to `def.includes` over the manifest's structural deltas, and composes through T3's `CascadeStepSource`. **Determinism guard:** the no-override path stays **byte-identical** — proven by a no-delta golden over the existing `start_compose` fixtures. *Done:* a hand-authored manifest fixture with a `replace-step workflow:single-task#implement → step:project-implement` delta + a `.jigc/config/steps/project-implement.yaml` (re-including `step:implement` per flow 3a) flips the composed include list to `[locate, project-implement(→implement + house rule), …]` **through the binary**, and a no-delta fixture composes byte-identically to the pre-increment baseline — both asserted on emitted bytes. *Refs:* [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → 3a; [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Resolution algorithm.
- **T5** — **Resolution-time cycle/orphan interaction** (engine/cli): a structural delta whose **anchor a same-manifest delta removed** (e.g. `remove-step #locate` then `insert-step --after locate`), or a `replace`/`insert` naming a step id no layer provides, surfaces at resolution via `workflow-refs` (the existing `include-resolves`/`include-cycle-absent` machinery the post-phase-4 id list flows into) — a blocking located finding, never a panic or a silently-dropped delta. A `replace` whose replacement step re-includes the replaced id is **not** a cycle (different ids — flow 3a). *Done:* fixtures assert (a) an orphaned-anchor delta yields a blocking `workflow-refs` finding with its route, (b) a structural delta introducing an include cycle is caught at phase 6, and (c) flow 3a's re-include is clean (no false cycle). *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → `replace` vs `tracked-fork`, Within-layer manifest order, Resolution algorithm phases 6–7; [validation.md](design/validation.md).

Each task ends in one conventional commit; serial over the shared tree. No new fork surfaced — every bullet maps to settled M4 decisions (2026-06-03) and the locked [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) / [storage.md](design/storage.md) / [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) sections. The verb surface (`config insert-step`/`replace-step`/`remove-step`) is deliberately **out** (increment 3) — this increment proves the apply path on hand-authored fixtures only.

## 2026-06-03 — M4 Increment 2 T3 shipped: layer-aware `CascadeStepSource`

A `CascadeStepSource` (`cli/src/start.rs`) consults `cascade::Resolved::file_owner(id)`: `Some(Project)` reads `<project_config>/steps/<id>.yaml`; any other owner (pack-default / none) reads the embedded pack body — phase-2 by-id shadowing made readable at last (overrides.md → Resolution algorithm phase 2). The `StepSource` trait contract is `Option<StepDef>` (engine-owned, not this CLI task's to widen), so the **located-error half** of the done-criterion rides an interior `RefCell<Option<Finding>>` *sink*: a `file_owner` of `Project` whose on-disk file is missing/malformed records a located `Finding` and returns `None` (never a silent fall-through to the pack body), drained by the caller via `take_error()` after a `None`. Unit-proven (project-body-wins, pack-body-otherwise, missing-project-file-is-located-error); **not yet on the live compose path** — `#[allow(dead_code)]` marks the type until T4 replaces `PackStepSource` at the `compose_core`/`resume_in_repo` call sites (populating `file_owner` from the project `steps/` dir + the no-override byte-identity goldens), the planned T3/T4 seam.

## 2026-06-03 — M4 Increment 2 T4 shipped: phases 2+4 wired live into compose

`compose_in_repo` / `compose_named_in_repo` now resolve the cascade once (`resolve_cascade` → `(Resolved, Vec<StructuralDelta>)`), build a live `CascadeStepSource` over it (phase-2 by-id shadowing live; `PackStepSource` retired from the `jigc start` compose path, kept only on the resume path), and thread both into `compose_core`, which applies the phase-4 `apply_structural_deltas` pass to `def.includes` **before** include expansion (`overrides.md` → Resolution algorithm phases 2/4). `load_project_layer` now returns `(OverrideLayer, Vec<StructuralDelta>)`: it populates `OverrideLayer.files` from every `<project_config>/steps/<id>.yaml` basename (a native step file shadows the pack step by id regardless of any delta — the `steps/` dir *is* the project step layer), and parses the manifest `deltas:` block (`replace-step` / `remove-step` / `insert-step`, stripping the `step:` prefix on `with:`) into `StructuralDelta`s. **Determinism guard:** a no-`deltas` manifest composes byte-identically to the captured pre-increment single-task baseline (`start_compose.rs` golden); the `replace-step` flip is proven on the emitted bytes through the binary (flow 3a). **Scoping calls:** (a) the compose-time `workflow-refs` gate still runs over the *raw* workflow bytes (pre-phase-4 list) — aligning the gate with the post-phase-4 list (orphan/cycle a delta introduces) is explicitly T5; T4's happy path passes because the raw list is valid and `compose` over the mutated def surfaces any real dangling include. (b) The `CascadeStepSource` located-error sink is drained only after a failed compose (`compose_drained`); a delta naming a step the project ships **no file for** is a plain `include-resolves` dangling finding (the id never enters `OverrideLayer.files`, so `file_owner` is `None` → pack fall-through → engine dangling report), not a located project-file fault — the located fault is reserved for an *owned-but-unreadable/malformed* file.

## 2026-06-03 — M4 Increment 2 T5 shipped: resolution-time cycle/orphan over the post-phase-4 list

The `workflow-refs` gate now validates the **post-phase-4** include list. `compose::workflow_refs_with_deltas(bytes, deltas, source, catalog, ctx)` (the engine fn; `workflow_refs` delegates with `&[]`, so the no-override path is byte-identical) loads the def, applies `apply_structural_deltas` (an orphaned anchor short-circuits to its one located finding), then runs the existing `include-resolves`/`include-cycle-absent`/emit checks over the mutated id list. `compose_core` (`start.rs`) computes the workflow-scoped deltas once (`scoped_deltas`), threads them into **both** the phase-4 pass that builds `def.includes` (which `compose` consumes) and the gate (so the gate validates the exact list `compose` will expand) — closing the T4-flagged gap where the gate saw the raw pre-phase-4 list and a delta-introduced cycle slipped to a generic `compose` error. **Orphan now carries its route:** `apply_structural_deltas`'s `structural-anchor-resolves` block gained a `run-command`-style `route` (remove/re-target the delta via `jigc config`) per overrides.md (`orphaned` → a `run-command` route) — the done-criterion (a) demands the route. Proven both at the engine seam (`workflow_refs_with_deltas` over a `single-task` fixture: orphan-with-route, delta-introduced cycle at phase 6, flow-3a different-id re-include clean) and on the real CLI path (`compose_core` driven with deltas: orphan fails with route, cycle fails at phase 6, 3a composes clean — all located, never a panic). Write-time verb-side checks stay out (increment 3).

## 2026-06-03 — M4 Increment 3 planning: decomposition

Cut [Increment 3 — Structural-op verbs (`config insert-step` / `replace-step` / `remove-step`)](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-3--structural-op-verbs-config-insert-step--replace-step--remove-step) into four ordered, single-concern tasks. This increment is the **authoring surface** over the apply path increment 2 already built and wired live: `load_project_layer` (`start.rs:825`) already reads the manifest `deltas:` block + the `steps/` shadow dir and `compose_core` already applies them at phase 4 — so the verbs only need to **write** the manifest+native-file shape that loader reads, and the override lands end-to-end through the existing compose path (the deliverable). *Why this order:* the manifest `deltas:` **writer** is the shared substrate all three verbs append through (today `config.rs`'s `write_scalar` round-trips only the `scalar:` block — no `deltas:` writer exists), so it lands first as a tested unit; then the two write-time *checks* both verbs gate on (basename-collision, anchor/target snapshot) land as pure helpers; then the three verbs wire clap+dispatch+native-file-write over those pieces and prove the end-to-end runnable override on the binary. *Grounded facts the cut rests on (verified against HEAD at `6f25ac4`, not assumed):*

- **No structural-op verb exists** — `ConfigCommand` (`config.rs:30`) has the single `Set` variant; its doc-comment (`config.rs:5`) explicitly defers `insert-step`/`replace-step`/`remove-step` to "later increments." `ConfigCommand::dispatch` takes only `cwd` (no `--format`). So clap + dispatch for the three verbs is genuinely net-new.
- **No `deltas:` writer exists** — `write_scalar` (`config.rs:114`) round-trips the manifest through `serde_yaml_ng::Value` and mutates **only** the `scalar:` sub-map; it preserves a present `deltas:` block but never appends to it. The append-a-delta-entry writer (same round-trip-preserve discipline, so an existing `scalar:` and prior `deltas:` survive) is net-new.
- **The loader+apply path is already live** — `load_project_layer` parses `deltas:` (`replace-step`/`remove-step`/`insert-step`, stripping the `with: step:` prefix) into `StructuralDelta`s and populates `OverrideLayer.files` from every `steps/<id>.yaml` basename (`start.rs:875`/`888`); `compose_core` applies the scoped phase-4 pass (`start.rs:303`). So once a verb writes a valid manifest+native file, the override composes end-to-end **with no engine/compose change** — verified by reading the increment-2 wiring, the basis for the "lands a runnable override end-to-end" deliverable.
- **The snapshot ("resolution as of this edit") is buildable from existing `pub` engine fns** — `engine::compose::load_workflow_def(bytes)` (`compose.rs:899`) yields `def.includes`; `engine::compose::apply_structural_deltas(&includes, &scoped)` (`compose.rs:1076`) yields the post-phase-4 id list; `read_workflow` (`start.rs:1013`) reads the pack workflow bytes. A verb computes the snapshot include list by loading the pack def and applying the *existing* manifest deltas scoped to that workflow, then checks anchor/target membership — no new resolution machinery.
- **The two step-id sets a basename-collision check reads exist** — `pack.list(PackResourceKind::Steps)` (the pack's shipped step ids) and `project_step_ids(project_config)` (`start.rs:888`, the already-shadowed project basenames). The settled rule (`overrides.md:146`): a new native-file basename colliding with an **existing step id** (pack or project) is a write-time error; the replace/remove *target* id (which must already exist) is the separate snapshot check, not a collision.

- **T1** — The manifest `deltas:` **writer** (`cli::config`): a helper that appends one `StructuralDelta` entry (`kind` + `target` + `after:`/`before:`/`with: step:<id>`) into `<project_config>/manifest.yaml`'s `deltas:` sequence, round-tripped through `serde_yaml_ng::Value` so an existing `scalar:` block and any prior `deltas:` entries survive (mirroring `write_scalar`'s preserve discipline; a missing/blank `deltas:` block is created). *Done:* a unit test writes an `insert-step` then a `replace-step` into a temp manifest (one already carrying a `scalar:` entry) and asserts the round-tripped YAML carries both delta entries **and** the original `scalar:` entry, and that `start::load_project_layer` parses the result back to the two expected `StructuralDelta`s. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Delta representation, Authoring deltas; [storage.md](design/storage.md) → Config layout.
- **T2** — The two **write-time check** helpers (`cli::config`), pure over inputs: (a) `basename` collision — the new native-file basename must not already be an existing step id (`pack.list(Steps)` ∪ `project_step_ids`), else a routed `config.step-id-collision` block; (b) anchor/target **snapshot** presence — given the pack workflow bytes + the existing scoped manifest deltas, the anchor (`--after`/`--before` step-id for insert) or target (`#step-id` for replace/remove) must be present in `apply_structural_deltas(load_workflow_def(bytes).includes, scoped)`, else a routed `config.anchor-absent` block. *Done:* unit tests assert (a) a basename equal to a pack step id (`implement`) and to a project-shadowed id are each rejected, a fresh basename passes; (b) an anchor/target present in the snapshot passes, an absent one is rejected with its route, and an anchor that an *existing* manifest `remove-step` already dropped is rejected (the snapshot reflects prior deltas). *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Native-file id = filename basename, Write-time vs resolve-time split.
- **T3** — `jigc config insert-step --workflow <id> (--after|--before) <step-id> <file>` (clap variant + dispatch): copy `<file>` to `.jigc/config/steps/<basename>.yaml` (id = basename), run T2's collision check on the basename and T2's snapshot check on the anchor, append the `insert-step` delta (`target: workflow:<id>`, `after:`/`before:`, `with: step:<basename>`) via T1; a clean write exits 0, a check-failure exits non-zero with the routed finding and **no** partial write. *Done:* a binary test runs `config insert-step --workflow single-task --after implement ./extra.yaml` in a set-up repo, asserts `.jigc/config/steps/extra.yaml` + the `insert-step` delta are written, then `jigc start "<intent>"` composes `single-task` with `extra` in the include list **through the binary** (asserted on emitted bytes); a second case asserts an unknown anchor (`--after nonesuch`) and a colliding basename (`./implement.yaml`) each exit non-zero with their route and write nothing. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Authoring deltas; [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → 3a.
- **T4** — `jigc config replace-step <workflow:id#step-id> <file>` and `jigc config remove-step <workflow:id#step-id>` (clap variants + dispatch): `replace-step` copies `<file>` to `steps/<basename>.yaml`, collision-checks the basename, snapshot-checks the `#step-id` target, appends the `replace-step` delta (`with: step:<basename>`); `remove-step` snapshot-checks the `#step-id` target and appends the `remove-step` delta (no native file). *Done:* a binary test drives flow 3a verbatim — `config replace-step workflow:single-task#implement ./project-implement.yaml` (where `project-implement.yaml` re-includes `step:implement` + a house rule) — and asserts `jigc start "<intent>"` composes the include list `[locate, project-implement, superseded-context, finalize]` with the house rule present, **through the binary** on emitted bytes; a `remove-step workflow:single-task#superseded-context` case asserts that step drops from the composed output; an absent target (`#nonesuch`) on each exits non-zero with its route. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Authoring deltas; [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → 3a.

Each task ends in one conventional commit; serial over the shared tree. No new fork surfaced — every bullet maps to settled M4 decisions (2026-06-03) and the locked [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Authoring deltas / Native-file id = filename basename / Write-time vs resolve-time split, and [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow 3a. The apply path is unchanged (increment 2) — this increment is the write-verb surface only; `config fill` / `config fork` stay out (increments 4–5).

## 2026-06-03 — M4 Increment 4 planning: decomposition

Cut [Increment 4 — slot-fill end-to-end (`{{fill:}}` + the orphan check + `config fill`)](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-4--slot-fill-end-to-end-fill--the-orphan-check--config-fill) into six ordered, single-concern tasks. This increment adds the **fourth read-path placeholder** (`{{fill:<id>}}`, the doc-creation-style extension point) + its **phase-5** application + the two M4 closed-surface `workflow-refs` checks + the `config fill` verb + the pack `{{fill: extra-guidance}}` point — so a project injects content into an anticipated step point without forking the step, orphans/survivors caught now (not deferred to M5). *Why this order:* mirrors the increment 2/3 spine — engine model + pure pass first (the new resolution phase, the milestone's remaining risk), then the validation checks, then live-wire into compose, then the verb, then the pack point + the end-to-end proof. The fill **target namespace** (`step:<id>#<fill-id>`) is distinct from both the content `Address` and the structural-op `StructuralTarget` (a `step` scheme + a `#<fill-id>` body point, not a workflow include-list entry), so it needs its own parser — same lesson as inc-2's definition-target spike. *Grounded facts the cut rests on (verified against HEAD at `4b0b482`, not assumed):*

- **No fill machinery exists anywhere** — `grep` for `fill` across `crates/` finds only `validate.rs`'s unrelated "slot filled" doc-comment; the `{{fill:}}` placeholder is referenced **only** in the design (`workflow-dialect.md:86` Leaves table row, `overrides.md` → The `{{fill:}}` placeholder). So the placeholder, its delta kind, its parser, its phase-5 pass, and its checks are all net-new.
- **`StructuralDelta` (`cascade.rs:239`) is structural-op-only** (`Insert`/`Replace`/`Remove`); it carries **no** slot-fill variant. `slot-fill` is a *separate* delta kind (it targets a body extension point, not an include-list position), so it is a new type loaded alongside the structural deltas, not a fourth `StructuralDelta` arm — `load_project_layer` (`start.rs:825`) returns `(OverrideLayer, Vec<StructuralDelta>)` today and gains a fill-delta surface.
- **Phase 5 applies to step bodies *before* expansion (phase 7)** — `overrides.md` → Resolution algorithm phase 5 + The `{{fill:}}` placeholder: fill content's own `{{include:}}`/`{{cli.…}}`/`{{@…}}` must resolve in the later phases "as if the pack had written them inline." In code, step bodies are produced by `StepSource::step(id) -> StepDef` (`compose.rs:1014`) and consumed by `expand_includes` (`compose.rs:1158`); so the phase-5 fill pass transforms a step's `body` keyed by its `id`, *upstream* of `expand_includes` (the natural seam: a body transform applied as each `StepDef` is loaded / in `compose_core` before expansion).
- **A surviving lone `{{fill:}}` is not currently a distinct finding** — `emit_line` (`compose.rs:483`) recognizes `{{cli.…}}` and `{{include:}}` distinctly *before* falling through to `Path::parse`; a lone `{{ fill: extra-guidance }}` would today parse as a literal `Head::Doc(Type("fill"), Slug(" extra-guidance"))` and resolve to a generic `workflow-refs.unresolved` finding (fragile, wrong code). The design mandates `{{fill:}}` be recognized **distinctly** (like `{{cli.}}`/`{{include:}}`) and a survivor be the dedicated blocking check — so the recognizer + check are genuine net-new work, not a reuse of the data-value path.
- **The `--from-file <path|->` (stdin/file, `-`) handoff + the routed-finding→non-zero dispatch envelope already exist** — `doc.rs:273` `read_handoff` (`-` ⇒ stdin) and `config.rs`'s `finding_to_err`/`dispatch` pattern; `config fill` reuses both (no new I/O convention).
- **The fill native file is keyed by the `<fill-id>`** — `storage.md:97` / `worked-examples.md:194` (`config fill step:implement#extra-guidance` → `.jigc/config/fills/extra-guidance.md`), the same basename-is-id rule the `steps/` dir uses.

- **T1** — The **fill-target + slot-fill delta model** (engine): a `SlotFillTarget` parser for `step:<id>#<fill-id>` (a `step` scheme + a `#<fill-id>` body point — distinct from the content `Address` and the structural-op `StructuralTarget`) and a `SlotFillDelta { target, content_id }` type, with malformed/empty/whitespace/wrong-scheme targets rejected as located findings (the settled block envelope). Pure, no I/O. *Done:* unit tests assert `step:implement#extra-guidance` parses to its `{step-id, fill-id}` shape and that hostile input (empty step id, empty fill-id, missing `#`, a `workflow:`/content-address scheme, non-ASCII) is a located finding, never a panic. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → The `{{fill:}}` placeholder, Delta targets; [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → Leaves.
- **T2** — The **phase-5 fill-application pass + the `{{fill:}}` recognizer** (engine): a pure `apply_slot_fills(step_id, body, &fills) -> Result<String, Finding>` that replaces each lone `{{fill: <fill-id>}}` line in a step body with the resolved `slot-fill` content for `step:<step_id>#<fill-id>` (higher layer already won upstream) **or** the pack-default body (empty if none) when unfilled — run *before* include expansion; plus the lone-`{{fill:}}` recognizer (a `fill_id_of(line)` distinct from `parse_cli_placeholder`/`parse_include_line`). An **unfilled** point → its default (never a finding); the pass does **not** re-run, so a `{{fill:}}` *inside* applied content survives to a later phase (T3's survivor check + T5's write-time reject keep that out). *Done:* a golden asserts (a) a body with `{{fill: x}}` and a fed `x → "house rule"` emits the content inline at that line, (b) an unfilled `{{fill: y}}` emits the (empty) pack default, (c) the resolved content's own `{{include:}}`/`{{@…}}` are left intact for the later phases (the pass touches only the `{{fill:}}` lines). *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → The `{{fill:}}` placeholder, Resolution algorithm phase 5; [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → Leaves.
- **T3** — The **two M4 `workflow-refs` fill checks** (engine): (a) **orphan** — a `slot-fill` delta targeting a `<fill-id>` no resolved (post-shadow) step body declares is a blocking `workflow-refs.slot-fill-orphan` finding with a remove/re-target route (symmetric with `structural-anchor-resolves`); (b) **survivor** — a lone `{{fill:}}` surviving the phase-5 pass (a nested fill, or an undeclared point) is a blocking `workflow-refs.fill-survivor` finding located at its line. Both thread into the compose-time gate (`workflow_refs_with_deltas` / its fill-aware sibling) so they fire at resolution over the post-phase-5 bodies, the same machinery the structural orphan uses. *Done:* unit tests assert (a) a slot-fill aimed at a fill-id no body declares yields the blocking orphan finding **with its route**; (b) a step body whose applied fill content itself contains `{{fill:}}`, and a body with a `{{fill:}}` no delta filled and no point declares, each yield the blocking survivor finding located at the line; (c) a correctly-filled body + a matching declared point produce **no** finding. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → The `{{fill:}}` placeholder (Orphan detection is M4), no nested fills; [validation.md](design/validation.md) → workflow-refs.
- **T4** — **Wire phase 5 + the fill checks live into compose** (cli): `load_project_layer` (`start.rs:825`) also parses the manifest's `slot-fill` deltas and loads each `.jigc/config/fills/<id>.md` body, returning the resolved per-`step:<id>#<fill-id>` fill map alongside the structural deltas; `compose_core` (`start.rs:283`) applies T2's phase-5 pass to each step body **before** expansion and runs T3's orphan/survivor checks in the gate. **Determinism guard:** a no-fill cascade applies the empty fill map (every `{{fill:}}` point → its pack default), so the no-override path stays **byte-identical** — proven by a no-fill golden over the existing `start_compose` fixtures. *Done:* with a hand-authored manifest fixture carrying a `slot-fill step:implement#extra-guidance` + `fills/extra-guidance.md`, `jigc start "<intent>"` composes `single-task` with the fill content in `implement`'s emitted body **through the binary** (asserted on emitted bytes); and a no-fill fixture composes byte-identically to the pre-increment `single-task` baseline. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Resolution algorithm phase 5; [storage.md](design/storage.md) → Config layout.
- **T5** — `jigc config fill <step:id#fill-id> --from-file <path|->` (cli: clap variant + dispatch): parse the `step:<id>#<fill-id>` target (T1), read the content via the existing `--from-file`/`-`-stdin handoff, run the two write-time checks — **the `{{fill:<fill-id>}}` point exists** in the resolved step body (else a routed block), and **the content contains no `{{fill:}}`** (the no-nested-fills rule, else a routed block) — then write `.jigc/config/fills/<fill-id>.md` + append the `slot-fill` delta to the manifest (round-trip-preserving the `scalar:`/`deltas:` blocks, mirroring `write_scalar`/`append_delta`); a check failure exits non-zero with the routed finding and **no** partial write. *Done:* a binary test runs `config fill step:implement#extra-guidance --from-file -` (stdin) in a set-up repo, asserts `fills/extra-guidance.md` + the `slot-fill` delta are written and `jigc start "<intent>"` then composes the fill content into `implement` **through the binary**; further cases assert a fill aimed at a non-existent point and fill content containing `{{fill:}}` are each rejected non-zero with their route and write nothing. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Authoring deltas (the `config fill` row), Write-time vs resolve-time split; [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → 3c.
- **T6** — **Pack `{{fill: extra-guidance}}` point + the end-to-end proof** (pack + e2e): add the `{{fill: extra-guidance}}` extension point to `crates/cli/pack/steps/implement.yaml` (default empty, per the design's `implement`-step example), and prove the full deliverable on the binary. The point must compose **byte-identically to today when unfilled** (an unfilled `{{fill:}}` → empty default, so the inc-3/M3 `start_compose`/acceptance goldens still hold). *Done:* an e2e in a throwaway repo asserts (a) bare `jigc start "<intent>"` over the unmodified pack composes `single-task` with the unfilled point contributing nothing (the existing baseline unchanged); (b) after `jigc config fill step:implement#extra-guidance --from-file -`, the composed `implement` body carries the house rule; (c) a hand-authored `slot-fill` aimed at an undeclared `<fill-id>` makes `jigc start` fail with the blocking `slot-fill-orphan` finding; (d) a `fills/*.md` containing a nested `{{fill:}}` is rejected (write-time via the verb, and the survivor check blocks a hand-edited one). *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → The `{{fill:}}` placeholder; [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → 3c; [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → Leaves.

Each task ends in one conventional commit; serial over the shared tree. No new fork surfaced — every bullet maps to settled M4 decisions (2026-06-03) and the locked [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → The `{{fill:}}` placeholder (phase-5, no-nested, orphan-is-M4) / Delta targets / Authoring deltas, [storage.md](design/storage.md) → Config layout (`fills/<id>.md`), and [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow 3c. `tracked-fork` (`config fork`) + the `--explain` overrides + the cross-kind e2e acceptance stay out (increments 5–6).

## 2026-06-03 — M4 Increment 4 T3 shipped: the two `workflow-refs` fill checks

`compose.rs` gains `workflow_refs_with_fills` — the fill-aware sibling of `workflow_refs_with_deltas`: after phase 4 (structural deltas) + phase 7 (include expansion), each composed step body runs through phase 5 (`apply_slot_fills`) against the resolved `fills` before the per-step checks, so both new checks fire at resolution over **post-phase-5** bodies (same machinery as `structural-anchor-resolves`). (a) **orphan** — every `slot-fill` delta whose `(step_id, fill_id)` no resolved body declares a `{{fill:<id>}}` point for is a blocking `workflow-refs.slot-fill-orphan` carrying a remove/re-target `jigc config fill` route (the declared-point set is collected from the expanded bodies via `fill_id_of`). (b) **survivor** — a lone `{{fill:}}` surviving into a post-phase-5 body (a nested fill; phase 5 does not re-run) is a blocking `workflow-refs.fill-survivor` located at its line (`find_fill_survivor`), and **supersedes** the emit check for that body (a `continue`), since the emitter would otherwise mis-parse the lone `{{fill:…}}` as a `fill`-rooted data-value — the distinct recognizer + check are genuine net-new (the planner-verified gap). No fork; maps to [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → The `{{fill:}}` placeholder (Orphan detection is M4, no nested fills) and [validation.md](design/validation.md) → workflow-refs. Live wiring into compose is T4.

## 2026-06-03 — M4 Increment 4 T4 shipped: phase 5 + the fill checks wired live into compose

`start.rs` wires the slot-fill apply path into both front doors. `load_project_layer` now **partitions** the one `deltas:` list by `kind:` — `slot-fill` entries parse into `SlotFillDelta`s (`target: step:<id>#<fill-id>` + `content: fills/<id>.md` → the basename `content_id`), the structural ops unchanged — returning a 3-tuple `(OverrideLayer, Vec<StructuralDelta>, Vec<SlotFillDelta>)`. `resolve_cascade` loads each slot-fill's `fills/<id>.md` body into a `ResolvedFills` map and bundles `{deltas, slot_fills, fills}` into one `ComposeOverrides` threaded through `compose_drained` → `compose_core` (so neither front door grew a widening tuple). `compose_core` now runs the gate via `workflow_refs_with_fills` (the orphan + survivor checks over post-phase-5 bodies) and composes through a `FillStepSource` decorator that applies `apply_slot_fills` to each fetched step body **before** include expansion (phase 5 precedes phase 7, so filled content's own `{{include:}}`/`{{cli.…}}`/`{{@…}}` resolve as if inline). **Determinism guard holds:** a no-fill cascade = empty `fills` = `apply_slot_fills` identity, so the pre-increment `single-task`/router byte-identity goldens still pass. **Slot-fills thread unscoped** (they carry no workflow id; the orphan check is keyed on whether *the composed workflow's* bodies declare each `(step_id, fill_id)` point — a slot-fill targeting a step absent from the composed workflow is therefore an orphan, the closed-surface semantics). The T4 done-criterion test declares the `{{fill: extra-guidance}}` point via a project `steps/implement.yaml` shadow (the pack point lands in T6), proving the apply path end-to-end without pre-empting T6. No fork; maps to [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Resolution algorithm phase 5 and [storage.md](design/storage.md) → Config layout.

## 2026-06-03 — M4 Increment 4 T5 shipped: the `jigc config fill` verb

`config.rs` gains the `Fill { target, --from-file }` clap variant + `run_fill`: it parses the `step:<id>#<fill-id>` target (`SlotFillTarget::parse`, T1), reads the content via the shared `doc::read_handoff` (`-`-stdin / file, now `pub(crate)`), runs **two write-time checks before any write**, then writes `fills/<fill-id>.md` (id = fill-id) + appends the `slot-fill` delta (`kind: slot-fill` / `target: step:<id>#<fill-id>` / `content: fills/<id>.md`, the spelling `parse_one_delta` reads back). The append shares a new `append_delta_entry` round-trip with the structural `append_delta` (one preserve-discipline path over the `deltas:` list, every kind). **The two checks (codes are net-new, the conditions are settled):** `config.nested-fill` rejects content whose own body declares a `{{fill:}}` point (the no-nested rule — phase 5 does not re-run), and `config.fill-point-absent` rejects a target whose `{{fill:<fill-id>}}` point no **resolved** step body declares (the write-time sibling of the resolution-time `slot-fill-orphan`, closing the closed surface at write time for both authoring paths). Both reuse one new engine recognizer `engine::compose::fill_ids_in(body)` (the `fill_id_of` rule exposed line-by-line) so write-time and resolution-time agree on what counts as a fill point; the resolved-body read is a new `pub(crate) start::resolve_step_body` (the phase-2 file-owner dispatch a `CascadeStepSource` does, as a free function — project shadow included). A rejection exits non-zero with its route and writes nothing (binary-proven). No fork; maps to [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Authoring deltas (the `config fill` row) / Write-time vs resolve-time split / The `{{fill:}}` placeholder, and [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow 3c.

## 2026-06-03 — M4 Increment 4 T6 shipped: the pack `{{fill: extra-guidance}}` point + the end-to-end proof

The pack `implement` step (`crates/cli/pack/steps/implement.yaml`) now declares a lone `{{fill: extra-guidance}}` point as its last line (default empty) — the marquee M4 deliverable that makes slot-fill demonstrable on the shipped pack, no project step shadow needed. **Byte-identity held** the way the design predicts: phase 5 collapses an unfilled lone `{{fill:}}` line to nothing, and the between-step join trims the step's trailing newlines, so the unfilled point composes byte-identical to the inc-3 `single-task` baseline (proven by a new `config_fill.rs` golden equal to `start_compose.rs`'s `NO_DELTA_SINGLE_TASK_GOLDEN`). `config_fill.rs` was re-cut to exercise the **unmodified pack** (the T5 project-step-shadow seed removed): (a) unfilled-baseline byte-identity, (b) `config fill … extra-guidance` → composed house rule, (c) orphan rejected write-time *and* a hand-edited orphan blocked at compose, (d) nested-`{{fill:}}` rejected write-time *and* a hand-edited nested fill blocked at compose by `fill-survivor`. **Resume path defect fixed (the genuine elaboration):** T4 wired phase 5 into the *fresh* front doors only; `resume_in_repo` (the `jigc start --task <id>` re-compose) still used a fill-blind `PackStepSource` + plain `workflow_refs`, so the live pack `{{fill:}}` point survived resume to phase 8 unresolved (caught by `e2e_audit`/`superseding_decision`, which resume). Resume now resolves the cascade (`resolve_cascade`), runs the fill-aware `workflow_refs_with_fills` gate, and composes through a `FillStepSource` — identical fill semantics to the fresh path, empty `fills` the identity. Two engine fixtures (`single_task_source`, `flow_3a_re_include`) that `include_str!` the pack `implement` body now pre-apply empty-fill phase 5 (modeling the post-phase-5 body the live compose path feeds), and `start_compose.rs`'s orphan test retargets to an undeclared `nonesuch` fill-id (its old `extra-guidance` target is now a *declared* pack point, no longer an orphan). No new fork; maps to [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → The `{{fill:}}` placeholder and [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow 3c.

## 2026-06-03 — M4 Increment 5 planning: decomposition

Cut [Increment 5 — `tracked-fork` (`config fork` + base-hash recording)](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-5--tracked-fork-config-fork--base-hash-recording) into four ordered, single-concern tasks. This increment adds the override ladder's **last rung** (`tracked-fork`): `jigc config fork workflow:single-task#implement` copies the resolved step bytes into `.jigc/config/steps/implement.yaml` (which already shadows the pack step at phase 2) and **records** a `tracked-fork` delta carrying `base-version` (the pack version) + the **blake3** `base-hash` of those copied bytes — the pinned basis recorded in M4, **compared only in M5**. *Why this order:* the apply path is already done (a `steps/<id>.yaml` shadow is picked up by `project_step_ids` regardless of any delta), so the increment is **the verb + the recording, not a new resolution phase** — model first (the delta type the loader/verb both name), then the loader recognition that makes "the recorded delta round-trips" true, then the verb that writes it, then the binary proof. *Grounded facts the cut rests on (verified against HEAD `bcb45c3`, not assumed):*

- **No fork/hash plumbing exists anywhere** — `grep` for `tracked-fork`/`base-hash`/`base-version`/`TrackedFork` across `crates/` finds only design cross-refs in doc-comments (`cascade.rs:236`, `compose.rs:3570`) and the unrelated `file_state` raw-byte hashing. The `tracked-fork` delta type, its manifest spelling, the loader recognition, the verb, and the hash recording are all net-new.
- **blake3 is already the hashing path** — `engine::file_state::hash_bytes(&[u8]) -> String` (`file_state.rs:42`, `pub fn`, raw-byte lowercase-hex, golden-tested) is the chosen algorithm (`DECISIONS.md` 2026-05-31 → Hashing = blake3). The fork verb reuses it; no second hashing path.
- **`base-version` is the pack version** — `EmbeddedPack::pack_version()` returns `CARGO_PKG_VERSION` (`pack.rs:8`, "built-in pack-default version = binary version"); a fork against pack-default records *that* as `base-version`.
- **The round-trip is genuinely blocked today** — `parse_one_delta` (`start.rs:1148`) **bails** on any `kind` outside `insert-step`/`replace-step`/`remove-step`/`slot-fill` (`start.rs:1194`), so a `tracked-fork` entry written to the manifest would make the **next** `load_project_layer` error. "The recorded delta round-trips" therefore *requires* the loader learn the `tracked-fork` kind — this couples T2 (loader) and T3 (verb), and is why T2 precedes T3.
- **The apply path needs no new logic** — `project_step_ids` (`start.rs:1095`) makes any `steps/<id>.yaml` basename shadow the pack step at phase 2 *regardless of any delta* (a native step file *is* the project step layer). So once the verb writes `steps/implement.yaml`, compose already picks it up; the `tracked-fork` delta contributes **nothing** to the structural/slot-fill vectors — it is a recording read only by the (M5) reconciliation (`overrides.md` → The ladder rung 4; `replace` vs `tracked-fork`).
- **The resolved unit for `workflow:single-task#implement` is the *step* `implement`** (`overrides.md:148`: "A fork addressed `workflow:single-task#validate` shadows the **step file** `validate`"). At fork time the project does not own `implement`, so its resolved owner is pack-default and the bytes copied + hashed are `pack.read(Steps, "implement")` — "the exact bytes phase 2 would load" (`overrides.md:148`). `resolve_step_body` (`start.rs:1049`) already does this exact phase-2 owner dispatch (project shadow else pack body) and is reused so the hash basis is the post-shadow body, not a re-read of the pack alone.
- **The target parser + the verb scaffolding are reusable** — `StructuralTarget::parse(target, None)` (`cascade.rs:169`) already parses `workflow:<id>#<step-id>` to `Anchor::At(step_id)` with hostile-input findings (the same `at_step` extraction `run_replace_step`/`run_remove_step` use, `config.rs:745`); `ConfigCommand`/`dispatch` (`config.rs:34`), `require_project_layer`, `append_delta_entry` (the one preserve-discipline path over `deltas:`), `finding_to_err`, and `check_basename_collision` (`config.rs:631`) all carry over. `cli.rs:95` wires `Config { verb }` → `run_config`.

- **T1** — The **`tracked-fork` delta model** (engine, `cascade.rs`): a `TrackedForkDelta { target: StructuralTarget, base_version: String, base_hash: String }` type (the `#<step-id>` `Anchor::At` form names the forked unit), `Clone + Debug + PartialEq + Eq`, pure data with no I/O — the M4-recorded basis the (M5) reconciliation will read. *Done:* a unit test constructs the delta from a parsed `workflow:single-task#implement` target + a `base-version`/`base-hash` pair and asserts its fields round-trip in memory; a second asserts the forked-unit step id reads back off the `Anchor::At` target. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → The ladder (rung 4), `tracked-fork` hash basis, Delta representation.
- **T2** — **Loader recognition + round-trip of the `tracked-fork` kind** (cli, `start.rs`): `parse_one_delta` learns `kind: tracked-fork` — `target: workflow:<id>#<step-id>` + `base-version:` + `base-hash:` — so it no longer bails, returning the T1 delta on a new `ParsedDelta::TrackedFork` arm; `load_project_layer` collects these into a third returned vector (the existing `(OverrideLayer, Vec<StructuralDelta>, Vec<SlotFillDelta>)` gains a `Vec<TrackedForkDelta>` — a recording surface, not consumed by compose, since the file shadow already applies). The **determinism guard:** a `tracked-fork` delta perturbs **neither** the structural nor the slot-fill vectors, so a fork-present manifest composes byte-identically to one carrying only the equivalent bare `steps/<id>.yaml` shadow. *Done:* a unit test seeds a manifest with a hand-authored `tracked-fork` entry (alongside a `scalar:` entry and a `slot-fill` entry) and asserts `load_project_layer` parses it back to the T1 delta **without erroring**, preserving the other blocks; a malformed entry (missing `base-hash`) is a clear located error, not a panic; and the structural/slot-fill vectors are unchanged by the fork entry's presence. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Delta representation; [storage.md](design/storage.md) → Config layout (one `deltas:` list, every kind).
- **T3** — `jigc config fork <workflow:id#step-id>` (cli: clap `Fork` variant + dispatch + `run_fork`): parse the `workflow:<id>#<step-id>` target (`StructuralTarget::parse`, `Anchor::At`), resolve the named **step** body's bytes via `resolve_step_body` (the phase-2 owner dispatch — at fork time pack-default's `pack.read(Steps, <step-id>)`), reject a basename already project-owned (`check_basename_collision` — a fork of an already-shadowed unit is rejected; nothing to fork), then **before/atomic with the write** copy those exact bytes to `.jigc/config/steps/<step-id>.yaml` and append a `tracked-fork` delta with `base-version = pack.pack_version()` + `base-hash = engine::file_state::hash_bytes(<copied bytes>)` (the blake3 of the copied bytes, not a re-read). A target that resolves to no step body, or a malformed target, exits non-zero with its route and writes nothing. *Done:* a binary test runs `config fork workflow:single-task#implement` in a set-up repo and asserts (a) `.jigc/config/steps/implement.yaml` now holds the pack `implement` body **byte-for-byte**, (b) the manifest carries a `tracked-fork` delta whose `base-version` equals the pack version and whose `base-hash` equals `hash_bytes` of the written file's bytes, (c) re-running the loader round-trips that delta (the T2 path), and (d) an unknown step id / a fork of an already-forked unit each exit non-zero with a route and leave the tree untouched. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Authoring deltas (the `config fork` row), `tracked-fork` hash basis (pinned now; read in M5), Write-time vs resolve-time split.
- **T4** — **End-to-end proof on the binary** (e2e, `crates/cli/tests/`): a throwaway-repo test driving the full deliverable through `jigc` — `config fork workflow:single-task#implement`, then `jigc start "<intent>"` composes `single-task` with the **forked (now project-owned) `implement` body** unchanged from the pack default (the fork is a faithful copy, so byte-identical to the un-forked compose), proving the recorded fork is honest about its ancestor and the apply path is "just a phase-2 file shadow, no new resolution logic." The test also edits the forked `steps/implement.yaml`, re-composes, and asserts the project edit now appears (the shadow diverges from the pack) **while** the recorded `base-hash` still pins the *original* copied bytes (the M5 reconciliation precondition: a fork that knows what it forked, basis pinned). *Done:* the e2e asserts, on emitted bytes through the binary: post-fork compose equals the pre-fork `single-task` baseline; an edit to the forked step surfaces in compose; the manifest's recorded `base-hash` equals `hash_bytes` of the *original* pack `implement` bytes (unchanged by the later edit). *Refs:* [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-5--tracked-fork-config-fork--base-hash-recording) → Increment 5 Proves; [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → `tracked-fork` hash basis, `replace` vs `tracked-fork`.

Each task ends in one conventional commit; serial over the shared tree. No new fork surfaced — every bullet maps to settled M4 decisions (2026-06-03) and the locked [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → The ladder (rung 4) / `tracked-fork` hash basis / `replace` vs `tracked-fork`, [storage.md](design/storage.md) → Config layout (`steps/<id>.yaml`, one `deltas:` list every kind), and the increment-5 roadmap scope. The `base-hash` is **recorded only** here; the stateless compare, the 3-way merge, and severity-floor enforcement stay in [M5](implementation/roadmap.md#m5--upgrade-reconciliation). The `--explain` override provenance + the cross-kind e2e acceptance stay in increment 6.

## 2026-06-03 — M4 Increment 6 planning: decomposition

Cut [Increment 6 — `--explain` overrides + the e2e acceptance](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-6--explain-overrides--the-e2e-acceptance) into four ordered, single-concern tasks — the milestone's **acceptance** increment. It builds the `--explain` resolution-tree view (the cascade/override provenance debug surface, [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow 3a's tree) and the cross-kind throwaway-repo e2e that drives all four delta kinds + asserts the no-override path is byte-identical. *Why this order:* the tree is a **derived view over the resolution inputs compose already computes** (the resolved cascade + the `ComposeOverrides`), so it builds in two model-then-wire steps — the tree data model + the structural/file-owner provenance first, the scalar winning-layer provenance second (a distinct, *missing* surface — see below), then the `--explain` flag + dispatch + renderer that emit it end-to-end, then the e2e that exercises the whole M4 ladder through the binary last (it depends on `--explain` being live to assert the tree). *Grounded facts the cut rests on (verified against HEAD `f55e6be`, not assumed — the roadmap/M4-scope phrasing "**Extend** the `--explain` output" is an `unverified-reuse` claim that the code refutes):*

- **`--explain` does not exist anywhere — there is nothing to "extend."** `grep` for `explain`/`Explain` across `crates/` finds only unrelated doc-comment prose ("make your consequences explain what changes", "loader builds…", `state.rs` "replaces"). There is **no** `--explain` clap flag on `Start` (`cli.rs:51` — `Start` carries only `intent`/`workflow`/`task`), **no** dispatch arm, **no** resolution-tree data structure, **no** tree builder, and **no** tree renderer. The flag, the model, the builder, and the renderer are all net-new. T1–T3 therefore prove the tree exists *for the new shape*, not that an existing one was extended.
- **`ComposedWorkflow` carries only `text`** (`compose.rs:1359` — `pub struct ComposedWorkflow { pub text: String }`); composition surfaces **no** per-step source layer, include tree, or override count. So the tree builder must assemble provenance from the **inputs** `compose_core` already holds, not from `compose`'s output: `resolved.file_owner(<step-id>) -> Option<LayerKind>` (`cascade.rs:607`, the winning layer per step file), the scoped `Vec<StructuralDelta>` (`start.rs:399` `scoped_deltas` — the `overrides applied: N` count + each delta's `target`/replacement for the `← replaces … at position` annotation), `overrides.slot_fills`, and `resolved.provenance()` (`cascade.rs:612`, the header). This is a derived view, re-computed each call ([workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → "`--explain` re-computes the resolution tree on demand").
- **`Resolved` does NOT record which layer set each scalar** — the verified gap. `Resolved.scalars` is a `BTreeMap<String,String>` of *final values only* (`cascade.rs:582`); the winning **layer** per scalar is discarded after `resolve` (`cascade.rs:632-643` folds team-then-project into the map and keeps no provenance). The contract's "scalar-key overrides applied with their source layer" ([workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md):32 + [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md):153 `overrides applied: 1 (replace-step…)`) therefore **cannot** be read off `Resolved` today — it needs a new per-scalar-provenance surface (which layer set each non-default key), derived from the override layers' `scalar_sets`. This is T2's whole point and its red step proves it for the new shape; it is **not** assumed to already exist.
- **The override inputs the tree needs are all already assembled in `resolve_cascade`/`compose_core`** (`start.rs:193`, `446`): the `(cascade::Resolved, ComposeOverrides)` pair carries the resolved cascade + the structural deltas + slot-fills + resolved fills. The tree builder is a **read** over these — it does **not** re-resolve, so it cannot drift from what compose emits (same inputs in → same tree, the determinism boundary; [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → "the same `definition + cascade + live state` always yields the same tree").
- **Composed output already routes through a per-type renderer** — `render::composed(format, &ComposedWorkflow)` (`render.rs:111`) appends the routing footer for `agent`/`human` and emits generic JSON for `json`. The `--explain` tree is a **sibling** result type with its own `render::explain(...)` (agent-text tree + footer, generic JSON projection), keeping the engine presentation-free (the tree model is `Serialize`, the framing lives in `cli`, per `result.rs:10` "never an accident of `#[derive(Serialize)]`").
- **The e2e harness + the byte-identical baseline pattern are established** — `tests/e2e_audit.rs` drives the real `CARGO_BIN_EXE_jigc` against throwaway temp git repos (its own `TempDir`+git helpers, self-contained), and `tests/start_compose.rs:207`/`:284` already assert `no_override_…_compose_is_byte_identical_to_the_golden` for the scalar + structural paths. The e2e task reuses both verbatim — the four `config` verbs (`set`/`insert-step`·`replace-step`/`fill`/`fork`) and the rejection paths (undeclared-key, wrong-type, orphan) all already ship from increments 1–5 (`config.rs`), so the e2e **drives** them, it does not build them.
- **`--explain` attaches to the compose front doors only** — bare `jigc start "<intent>"` (`compose_in_repo`, `start.rs:178`) and `--workflow <X>` (`compose_named_in_repo`, `start.rs:209`); flow 3a shows `jigc start --explain add-rate-limiter` over the default front door. To avoid minting a task purely to inspect the tree, `--explain` composes the **default-workflow** resolution **without** the mint side-effects (the tree is the cascade/include/override resolution, task-independent per [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → "structure is fixed by definition + cascade, task-independent") — an explicit elaboration pin, not a fork (it is *within* the settled "composed output is ephemeral / `--explain` re-computes on demand" spec).

- **T1** — The **resolution-tree model + structural/file-owner provenance** (engine + cli): a `Serialize`/`Deserialize` `ResolutionTree` result type carrying the workflow id + winning layer + `overrides_applied: N`, and per included step its id, source file, source `LayerKind`, and — when a `structural-op` replaced/inserted it — the `replaces <id> at position <n>` annotation (flow 3a's `step:project-implement … ← replaces step:implement at position 2`); plus a pure builder `build_resolution_tree(def, &resolved, &scoped_deltas, …)` that assembles it from the inputs `compose_core` already holds (`resolved.file_owner` per post-phase-4 include id + the scoped structural deltas for the count/annotation), re-using `scoped_deltas` + `apply_structural_deltas`' position semantics so the tree's include order matches the composed order exactly. Pure, no I/O, no re-resolution. *Done:* unit tests assert (a) a no-override `single-task` def + an all-pack-default `Resolved` builds a tree with `overrides_applied: 0` and every step tagged `pack-default` with its pack file; (b) a `replace-step` delta over `implement` builds a tree with `overrides_applied: 1`, the replacing step tagged `project` at the same position, and the `replaces implement at position N` annotation; (c) the built tree's step order equals the post-phase-4 composed include order. *Refs:* [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → `--explain` output contract (layers 1–2); [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → 3a (the tree); [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Resolution algorithm phases 2/4.
- **T2** — **Per-scalar winning-layer provenance** (engine, `cascade.rs`): extend the resolved cascade with a `scalar_provenance(key) -> Option<LayerKind>` surface (or a `scalar_overrides() -> &BTreeMap<String, LayerKind>` of the **non-default** keys + the layer that set each), recorded during `resolve` from the team/project `scalar_sets` folds (`cascade.rs:633-643`) — the surface the tree's "scalar-key overrides applied with their source layer" needs and that `Resolved` discards today. The base values stay the pack default (no provenance entry); only a key a higher layer **set** carries a winning layer. The builder (T1) then renders the per-knob override line. **Determinism guard:** the new surface is additive — `scalar`/`scalar_required` are unchanged, so no-override compose stays byte-identical (the provenance map is read only by `--explain`, never by the compose read path). *Done:* unit tests assert (a) with no override layer, `scalar_provenance` is empty for every key (all base); (b) a project `scalar-set default-workflow single-task` records `default-workflow → Project` and the count of override keys is 1; (c) a team key the project does not re-set records `→ Team` (project wins only where it sets); (d) `scalar`/`scalar_required` return identical values before and after the surface is added (the byte-safe-read guard). *Refs:* [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → `--explain` output contract (layer 1, "scalar-key overrides applied with their source layer"); [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → 3b; [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Resolution algorithm phase 3.
- **T3** — **The `--explain` flag + dispatch + renderer, wired live** (cli: `cli.rs` + `start.rs` + `render.rs`): add `--explain` to `Start` (a `bool` flag, combinable with `<intent>`/`--workflow`, valid on the compose forms); a `compose_explain_in_repo` path that resolves the cascade + builds the T1/T2 tree over the resolved default (or `--workflow`-named) workflow **without minting a task** (the tree is task-independent); and `render::explain(format, &ResolutionTree)` emitting the agent-text tree (workflow + `overrides applied: N`, the include tree with per-step source layer + `← replaces …` annotations, the winning-layer scalar lines, and the `findings (workflow-refs): N` tail) for `agent`/`human` + the generic JSON projection for `json`. The dispatch arm routes `Start { explain: true, … }` to this path. **Inert-flag guard:** the flag must *change observable output* — a parsed-but-ignored `--explain` is the exact scope dishonesty the increment validator hunts. *Done:* a binary test asserts (a) `jigc start --explain "<intent>"` over the unmodified pack prints the resolution tree (workflow line + `overrides applied: none` + the include tree with each step's source layer) and **mints no task** (no `.jigc/tasks/<id>/` appears) — driven on emitted bytes; (b) after a `config replace-step`, `--explain` prints `overrides applied: 1` with the `← replaces … at position` annotation on the replacing step; (c) `--explain --format json` emits the structured tree (parseable, carrying the same provenance). *Refs:* [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → `--explain` output contract (all five layers; the tree is derived/ephemeral); [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → 3a.
- **T4** — **The cross-kind e2e acceptance** (e2e, `crates/cli/tests/`): a throwaway-repo test driving the **whole M4 ladder** through the real `jigc` binary, mirroring [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow 3 — `config set default-workflow single-task` (scalar-set flips bare-`start` minting); `config insert-step`/`replace-step` (structural-op changes the composed include list); `config fill step:implement#extra-guidance` (slot-fill fills the `{{fill:}}` point → content appears in composed output); `config fork workflow:single-task#implement` (tracked-fork records a `base-hash`) — asserting **each delta changes composed output** (on emitted bytes), and the rejection paths fire (`config set <undeclared-key>` → undeclared-key block; `config set default-workflow <bad-enum>` → wrong-type block; a hand-authored orphan `slot-fill` → `slot-fill-orphan` at compose). The **headline determinism assertion:** a freshly-set-up repo with **no** overrides composes `single-task`/router **byte-identically** to the pre-M4 baseline (the `start_compose` goldens are the recorded baseline; the e2e re-asserts equality through the binary). `--explain` (T3) is exercised once to assert the tree reflects an applied override (the `overrides applied: N` line tracks reality). *Done:* the e2e passes end-to-end — each of the four `config` verbs shifts the composed bytes vs the no-override compose, the three rejections exit non-zero with their routes, and the no-override compose equals the baseline golden byte-for-byte. *Refs:* [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → flow 3 (3a–3c) + the M4 acceptance framing; [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Resolution algorithm (the four delta kinds); [validation.md](design/validation.md) → workflow-refs (the orphan/undeclared/wrong-type rejections); [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-6--explain-overrides--the-e2e-acceptance) → Increment 6 Proves.

Each task ends in one conventional commit; serial over the shared tree. No new fork surfaced — every bullet maps to settled M4 decisions (2026-06-03) and the locked [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → `--explain` output contract, [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow 3, and the increment-6 roadmap scope. The one elaboration pinned by the executor (not a fork): `--explain` composes the resolution tree **without** the task-mint side-effects (the tree is task-independent per the settled "composed output is ephemeral / structure is fixed by definition + cascade" spec). This is the milestone's **acceptance** increment; the `override-default` probe, `jigc upgrade`, the stateless base-hash compare, and the 3-way merge all stay in [M5](implementation/roadmap.md#m5--upgrade-reconciliation).

## 2026-06-03 — M4 Increment 6 T1 shipped: resolution-tree model + structural/file-owner provenance

The `--explain` resolution tree's structural slice (layers 1–2) lands as a `Serialize`/`Deserialize` `ResolutionTree { schema_version, workflow, workflow_layer: LayerKind, overrides_applied, steps: Vec<ResolvedStep> }` in `engine::result` (`ResolvedStep { id, layer: LayerKind, replaces: Option<Replacement { replaced, position } } }`), plus a pure `compose::build_resolution_tree(workflow, workflow_layer, &includes, &scoped, file_owner_fn)` that replays phase 4 with the **same** position semantics as `apply_structural_deltas` (so the built step order equals the composed include order by construction), tracking which slot each `replace` swapped for the `replaces <id> at position N` annotation, then tags every post-phase-4 step by its `file_owner`. `overrides_applied` is the scoped structural-delta count. **Design choice (pin):** the engine model carries the structural fact — the winning `LayerKind` per step — **not** the displayed source-file path; path strings (`dev/steps/locate.yaml` vs `.jigc/config/steps/…`) are framing the CLI renderer (T3) derives from `(id, layer)`, keeping the engine presentation-free (the `result.rs` stance). `LayerKind` gained `Serialize`/`Deserialize` (kebab-case: `pack-default`/`team`/`project`) + a public `label()` so it projects as the stable per-step provenance label. The CLI seam `start::build_resolution_tree(pack, &resolved, &deltas, workflow_id)` reads the pack workflow's include list + threads `resolved.file_owner` as the per-step lookup (the file-owner provenance the cli half wires); it has **no live caller until T3's `--explain` dispatch**, so it carries `#[allow(dead_code)]` — the retained-seam pattern `render::orientation_agent_text` already uses. No new fork: within the settled `--explain` contract + flow 3a.

## 2026-06-03 — M4 Increment 6 T3 shipped: the `--explain` flag + dispatch + `render::explain`, wired live

`jigc start` gained a `--explain` bool (combinable with `<intent>`/`--workflow`; `conflicts_with = "task"` — resume re-composes a minted task, not a task-independent tree). The dispatch short-circuits to `start::compose_explain_in_repo(start, intent, workflow)`, which resolves the cascade exactly as `compose_in_repo` (the cascade-presence guard routes a missing project layer to `jigc setup`), picks the workflow id from the `default-workflow` knob unless `--workflow <X>` names one (mirroring `compose_named_in_repo`'s bypass), and builds the T1 tree over the manifest's `structural-op` deltas — **without minting** (the tree is task-independent; the `intent` is accepted only to mirror the compose call shape and threads nowhere). It returns `(ResolutionTree, pack_label)`, the label being `<pack-id>/v<version>` (CLI-side framing — the engine tree carries only the winning `LayerKind`). `render::explain` is the sibling of `render::composed`: agent-text emits the `workflow:<id>    (<layer> · <pack-label>)` line, `overrides applied: none|N`, and one `step:<id>    (<layer>)  ← replaces step:<id> at position N` line per resolved include, + the routing footer; JSON is the generic `ResolutionTree` projection, no footer. The `build_resolution_tree` seam shed its `#[allow(dead_code)]` (now live). **Inert-flag guard satisfied** — three binary tests on emitted bytes (`tests/start_explain.rs`): the unmodified-pack tree (`overrides applied: none` + every step's source layer, mints nothing), the post-`replace-step` tree (`overrides applied: 1` + the `← replaces step:implement at position 2` annotation), and the `--format json` structured projection (parseable, same provenance). No new fork: within the settled `--explain` contract + flow 3a + the pinned task-independence elaboration.

## 2026-06-03 — M4 (override application) shipped: milestone-completion audit triage

M4 built end-to-end via the `milestone-build` harness (48-agent run, no build halts — the risk-first ordering front-loaded the determinism-trap wiring and the from-scratch phase-4 structural work, both of which held). The milestone-completion audit (independent code-review + real-binary e2e, driving the built binary in throwaway repos) surfaced five findings; all were human-triaged for fix and remediated + re-verified through the binary:

- **(HIGH — FIXED `7a1d07f`) `slot-fill-orphan` bricked the front door.** The resolution-time orphan check keyed on the *currently-composed workflow's* step bodies, but slot-fill deltas carry no workflow scope — so once any project recorded a slot-fill on the shipped `step:implement#extra-guidance` point, bare `jigc start` (composing the default `router`, which omits `implement`) and every non-`single-task` workflow falsely exit 1. Re-keyed on the **target step's** resolved body (matching the write-time check); a slot-fill whose target step the composed workflow omits is inert, not an orphan. The acceptance test missed it because `scenario_7` only ever composed `--workflow single-task`.
- **(MEDIUM — FIXED `789eaeb`) `{{include:}}` hoisted to the step end.** A step body mixing prose + `{{include: step:X}}` emitted the included body at the end instead of in place, inverting worked-examples-3a order. The builders' replace-step test had *explicitly declined* to assert intra-step order ("pre-order flattening property"). Fixed to expand in place; the no-override determinism golden stayed byte-identical (shipped pack step bodies contain no includes).
- **(LOW — FIXED `e02ad08`) inline nested `{{fill:}}` leaked.** The whole fill machinery was line-based; a mid-line `{{fill:}}` evaded the write-time guard, phase-5 substitution, and the survivor check, leaking unresolved into output. Made token-based (recognizes `{{fill:}}` anywhere in a line); lone-line behavior unchanged, determinism golden held.
- **(Advisory — FIXED `50e452f`)** removed two `#[cfg(test)]`-only cascade accessors (`scalar_provenance`/`scalar_override_count`); tests rewritten over the live `scalar_overrides()`.
- **(Advisory — FIXED `eeee1f6`)** `--explain` JSON `overrides_applied` was structural-only while the text header showed structural + scalar; made `overrides_applied` the total so the two emitted surfaces agree (structural-only count recoverable; scalar breakdown in `scalar_overrides`).

Lesson reinforced (M3's too): the independent audit earns its keep — both real defects were masked by builder in-loop tests that asserted too narrowly (single composed workflow; declined order assertion). M4 (override application) ships: a project individualizes the pack via recorded deltas across all four kinds, the cascade applies them live, and the determinism boundary holds. The recorded deltas (now carrying pinned blake3 base-hashes on tracked-forks) are M5's reconciliation input.

## 2026-06-03 — Fold M4-audit lessons back into the planning/build harness

The M4 milestone-completion audit caught two real defects (a front-door-bricking `slot-fill-orphan` scoped to the composed workflow instead of the target step; `{{include:}}` hoisting instead of in-place expansion) that had cleared Plan → Execute → independent Validate — the same shape as M3's masking-test miss: the builder's in-loop tests asserted too narrowly, so the independent audit carried weight the earlier gates should have. Pushed the audit's adversarial mindset one stage earlier (process-only; no product code):

- **Validation hardening #5 — context-scoped features must be exercised against a context that OMITS the target** ([increment-workflow.md](implementation/increment-workflow.md)), now a mandatory check in [increment-validator](.claude/agents/increment-validator.md) and a Red-phase rule in [build-executor](.claude/agents/build-executor.md). A green happy-path pass over a single composing context is not coverage — it would have caught the front-door brick at increment 4.
- **Settle must exercise its own design claims** ([milestone-planning-workflow.md](implementation/milestone-planning-workflow.md) Settle + Why-this-shape; [milestone-plan command](.claude/commands/milestone-plan.md)): (a) pin the *scope* of every check the design introduces (an unscoped check is a fork the build resolves wrong — M4's orphan check), (b) spike every acceptance-flow's engine-behaviour assumption against the real binary before locking it (M4's flow-3a assumed in-place include expansion the engine lacked). The [design-reviewer](.claude/agents/design-reviewer.md) now adversarially checks both (it has `Bash` to spike).

Root cause addressed: the independent audit is a safety net, but a net catching a *severe* bug means the upstream nets leak. The fix isn't more audit — it's the adversarial mindset (omit-the-target coverage; exercise-don't-infer on the design's own claims) one phase earlier.

## 2026-06-03 — Split the upgrade milestone: M5 (reconcile) + M6 (severity); spine renumbered

The M5-planning baseline audit (four `capability-auditor` subagents) found the combined "upgrade reconciliation" milestone bundled two separable subsystems: the **reconciliation** itself (override-default probe + `jigc upgrade` + clean/conflict/orphaned) and the **severity-tuning subsystem** the probe's *tunable* checks rest on — which the audit measured as "the bulk of the work" (~0% built: every probe hardcodes severity; the M4 severity knobs are per-probe and read by nothing; no demotion-lock; no non-task probe seam). The severity subsystem is orthogonal to reconciliation and serves tunability nothing in the product yet uses. Human decision: split, and ship reconciliation first.

- **M5 · upgrade reconciliation** — the override-default probe + guarded `jigc upgrade` reporting clean/conflict/orphaned, `override-default`'s checks **blocking-by-default**. On `conflict`, **block with a review route** (keep / re-target / drop) — *not* a 3-way merge.
- **M6 · severity tuning & demotion-lock** (new) — cascade-assigned severities at every tunable probe site, per-check knob granularity, the intrinsic/tunable demotion-lock floor, and a non-task probe entry point.

Two design calls settled in the same pass (Scope-phase forks, human-gated):
- **Conflict path = detect + surface, defer 3-way merge.** The stateless design keeps only base-*hash*, but a true 3-way merge needs base-*content* (the v1 ancestor) — a real tension. M5 detects/surfaces the conflict and routes to human review (mirroring `file_state`'s own deferred-merge precedent); the diff3/merge-authoring surface (and any base-content retention) is separable and deferred past M5+M6. The load-bearing value — *every divergence surfaces at a known moment* — needs only detection.
- **`replace`/`remove` base-hash is an M5 substrate fix.** M4 recorded `base-hash` only for `tracked-fork`; the conflict table makes `replace`/`remove` content-dependent too, and stateless = no v1 to reconstruct, so M5's first increment extends those verbs to record the target's base-hash at authoring time.

Old **M6 (milestone execution / fan-out) → M7**, old **M7 (project setup) → M8**; cross-refs updated (overrides.md demotion-lock M5→M6 + the deferred-merge note; doctype-map idea-development pointer M7→M8). This commit carries only the spine restructure + the design-text reconciliation; M5's increment decomposition follows from the in-progress `/milestone-plan M5` run.

## 2026-06-03 — M5 (upgrade reconciliation) planning: scope + design settled

Four `capability-auditor` subagents established a clean post-M4 baseline ("no claim-vs-reality landmines" — the M4 docs scrupulously marked M5 work `(M5)` and the code matches); four `gap-detector` subagents (each spiking the exact shape against the real binary, per the hardened harness) surfaced the forks below. Human-gated settle:

- **The override-default probe** classifies each recorded delta against the *current* pack: target still exists? no → `orphaned`. content-bearing (`replace`/`remove`/`tracked-fork`) AND `current-hash ≠ recorded base-hash`? → `conflict`. else → `clean`. Stateless (no old pack kept). Runs via a **bespoke upgrade-time path** (not `validate_task`, which is task-working-area-shaped); the generic probe seam stays M6.
