Reuses the `Finding`/`ValidationReport` envelope, **identifying each delta by its target string** (a delta has no stable id — its target *is* its identity). Checks ship **blocking-by-default**.
- **`replace`/`remove` base-hash (the substrate fix)** — basis = the **blake3 of the displaced/removed pack unit's resolved native bytes** (the pack step the override sits on; reuses `resolve_fork_bytes`+`hash_bytes`, symmetric with `tracked-fork`). Parsed **Optional** for these kinds (old M4 manifests lack it); a content-bearing delta with **no** recorded base-hash classifies as **`needs-rebasing`** (re-record route) — **never** silently re-derived from the current pack (which would mask every conflict).
- **Version / trigger** — the stateless compare needs no version diff, so `jigc upgrade` is a **manual command that re-checks every delta against the current pack unconditionally**; **no per-project "last-reconciled version" record** is introduced. The pack version is **narrative/provenance only** (recorded `base-version` = `CARGO_PKG_VERSION` at record time, shown in the conflict route, never compared). The `0.0.0` freeze is cosmetic — `CARGO_PKG_VERSION` bumps naturally at release; M5 builds no version machinery.
- **`jigc upgrade` semantics = report-and-route only** — classify → emit findings+routes → block on non-clean → done. **No manifest-mutation transaction.** The human resolves by re-running `jigc config` verbs (which re-pin the base-hash to current — a natural re-baseline on human action, no silent drift). Sidesteps a net-new guarded-write/rollback substrate (finalize's git-rollback doesn't transfer — the target is the manifest).
- **Conflict route = free `String` prose** (mirroring `file_state`'s deferred-merge conflict-block), **not** the `run-command`/`reconcile` tagged union `validation.md` names — that promotion would break the pinned `finding` JSON golden and ripple to every route producer; a separate cross-cutting decision.
- **The v1→v2 acceptance-test seam** — build a **`FilesystemPack`** (a named-but-unbuilt post-MVP `PackSource` seam) gated by a **`JIGC_PACK_DIR`** env var, via a single pack-source factory replacing the ~16 hardcoded `EmbeddedPack::new()` sites. The e2e records deltas against a v1 pack dir, then runs `jigc upgrade` against a v2 dir — a **genuine** two-pack upgrade through the real binary (chosen over a stale-hash simulation: honest acceptance + the seam is independently reusable for project-local packs).

**No new managed doctype** (doctype-map untouched). Docs elaborated this milestone: overrides.md (per-kind base-hash basis, version/trigger model, the report-only `jigc upgrade` verb contract, the FilesystemPack seam — `jigc upgrade` lives here, with the cascade/override story, not in write-commands.md), worked-examples.md (a new upgrade-reconciliation flow, spiked via the FakePack/FilesystemPack seam), validation.md (override-default full-logic-in-M5, blocking-by-default, bespoke run path, String route).

## 2026-06-03 — M5 (upgrade reconciliation) decomposed into 4 risk-first increments

Cut into four ordered increments in [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → "Milestone 5 — upgrade reconciliation: decomposition": (1) the base-hash substrate fix for `replace`/`remove` (pack-direct basis, in a separate in-memory record off the compose-facing `StructuralDelta` so compose stays byte-identical — design-review B2); (2) the `override-default` classifier (clean/conflict/orphaned/needs-rebasing, the **pack-direct re-read that bypasses a tracked-fork's own shadow** — design-review B3, the silent-defeat risk — proven in-process over a two-version `FakePack`); (3) the `FilesystemPack` + `JIGC_PACK_DIR` factory seam (byte-identical when unset); (4) the `jigc upgrade` command (report-and-route only) + the genuine two-pack e2e = worked-examples flow 7. Risk-first: the recording substrate, then the classifier (the #1 logic risk, incl. the shadow-bypass), then the test seam, then the command + acceptance. The design survived the independent `design-reviewer` pass (now hardened to spike acceptance-flow assumptions + flag under-specified check scopes) whose eight findings were **all accepted and baked** before the cut — three blocking: the FilesystemPack version source (read from `defaults.yaml`), the replace/remove on-disk-vs-in-memory representation, and the fork shadow-bypass. Ready for `milestone-build` (base = the planning commit).

## 2026-06-03 — M5 Increment 1 planning: decomposition

Cut [Increment 1 — base-hash substrate for `replace` / `remove`](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-1--base-hash-substrate-for-replace--remove) into three ordered, single-concern tasks. The increment extends the M4 base-hash recording (today only `tracked-fork` writes `base-version`+`base-hash`) to the other two **content-bearing** delta kinds — `replace-step` and `remove-step` — so all three carry a re-comparable basis for the (M5 increment 2) `override-default` classifier, recorded the **same pack-direct way** (blake3 of the *displaced pack unit's* resolved native bytes). *Why this order:* the basis must reach the classifier **without** touching the compose-facing `StructuralDelta` (the byte-identical-compose invariant the basis must never perturb — design-review B2), so the cut is: first the loader/engine surface that carries the basis in a **separate record keyed by target** and parses the keys **Optional** for replace/remove (so a present-but-no-basis old manifest still loads), then the two verbs that *write* the keys reusing the fork's resolve-then-hash path, then the explicit compose byte-identical golden that proves `StructuralDelta` (hence composed output) is unchanged whether or not the basis is present. *Grounded facts the cut rests on (verified against HEAD `5cac501`, not assumed):*

- **The pack-direct resolve+hash path already exists and is reused verbatim** — `resolve_fork_bytes(pack: &dyn PackSource, step_id) -> Result<Vec<u8>>` (`config.rs:482`) reads the **pack-default** step bytes via `pack.read(Steps, step_id)` (the exact "pack-direct, bypassing any project shadow" basis the design mandates), and `engine::file_state::hash_bytes(&[u8]) -> String` (`file_state.rs:41`, `pub`, blake3 lowercase-hex, golden-tested) is the chosen hash. `run_fork` (`config.rs:432`/`445`) already pairs them (`hash_bytes(&resolve_fork_bytes(...))`); `replace`/`remove` reuse the **same** two functions — no new resolve or hash path. *(Note: `resolve_fork_bytes` currently has a `run_fork`-only caller; the verb tasks add the replace/remove callers, and the design names the displaced **pack** unit — the basis is `pack.read`, deliberately not the cascade-resolved owner.)*
- **`replace`/`remove` write nothing today beyond `kind`/`target`/`with`** — `delta_to_yaml` (`config.rs:863`) for `Replace`/`Remove` emits only those keys; `run_replace_step`/`run_remove_step` (`config.rs:300`/`343`) build a `StructuralDelta::Replace`/`Remove` and call `append_delta` → `delta_to_yaml` → `append_delta_entry`. The base keys are **net-new** on these entries — there is no existing recording to preserve.
- **The compose-facing `StructuralDelta` enum (engine `cascade.rs:246`) carries NO base fields and must stay that way** — compose reads `Vec<StructuralDelta>` for phase 4; the design pin (B2) is that the basis rides a **separate in-memory record keyed by target**, never new `StructuralDelta` fields, so phase-4 compose + its byte-identical goldens are untouched. `StructuralTarget` (`cascade.rs:84`) is `Clone + Debug + PartialEq + Eq` (carries `workflow_id` + `Anchor::At(step-id)`), so it keys the record uniquely for a replace/remove displaced unit.
- **The loader is the only round-trip surface that must learn the basis** — `parse_one_delta` (`start.rs:1286`) builds `StructuralDelta::Replace`/`Remove` from `kind`/`target`/`with`; `tracked-fork` already reads `base-version`/`base-hash` via `required_field` (`start.rs:1382`, a clear located error, never a panic). For replace/remove the keys must be **Optional** (old manifests lack them) — a missing basis is **not** an error here (it becomes increment-2's `needs-rebasing`), distinct from `tracked-fork`'s required basis. `parse_deltas` (`start.rs:1247`) returns `(Vec<StructuralDelta>, Vec<SlotFillDelta>, Vec<TrackedForkDelta>)` and `load_project_layer` (`start.rs:1100`) the 4-tuple `ProjectLayer`; the separate base record threads through both as an added returned surface.
- **The append/preserve path is shared and reused** — `append_delta_entry` (`config.rs:699`) is the one manifest-preserving round-trip over `deltas:` that `append_delta`/`append_slot_fill`/`append_fork` all share; the replace/remove writers add the two keys to the entry mapping `delta_to_yaml` builds (or write through the same preserve path), so the existing `scalar:`/prior-`deltas:` preservation is inherited, not re-implemented.

- **T1** — **Loader + engine surface for the replace/remove basis** (engine `cascade.rs` + cli `start.rs`): a separate base-record type **keyed by `StructuralTarget`** (e.g. a `StructuralBasis { target, base_version, base_hash }` or a `Vec`/map off the loader return) — **not** new fields on `StructuralDelta`; `parse_one_delta` learns to read **Optional** `base-version:`/`base-hash:` off `replace-step`/`remove-step` entries (both present → a basis record for that target; absent → none, never an error), while `tracked-fork`'s stay **required** (unchanged); `parse_deltas`/`load_project_layer` thread the new record through as an added returned surface (callers that don't need it bind `_`). *Done:* a `start.rs` unit test seeds a manifest with (a) a `replace-step` entry **with** `base-version`/`base-hash` and (b) a `remove-step` entry **without** them, and asserts `load_project_layer` returns the structural deltas unchanged **plus** a basis record for (a) only — (b) yields no basis (loads clean, not an error); the malformed case (one base key present, the other missing on a replace/remove) is a clear located error, not a panic; the `tracked-fork` required-basis path is unaffected. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Per-kind base-hash basis, On-disk vs in-memory representation; [storage.md](design/storage.md) → Config layout (one `deltas:` list, every kind).
- **T2** — **`replace-step`/`remove-step` record the displaced pack unit's basis** (cli `config.rs`): `run_replace_step` and `run_remove_step` resolve the **displaced pack unit's** bytes pack-direct (`resolve_fork_bytes(&pack, step_id)` — the pack step the override sits on, the same fn `run_fork` uses) and `hash_bytes` them, writing `base-version` (= `pack.pack_version()`) + `base-hash` (= the blake3) onto the appended manifest entry alongside `kind`/`target`/`with`; the existing write-time checks (`check_basename_collision`/`check_anchor_present`) and the preserve-discipline append path are unchanged. *Done:* a `config.rs` unit/binary test runs `config replace-step workflow:single-task#implement <file>` (and `remove-step workflow:single-task#implement`) in a set-up repo and asserts the appended manifest entry carries `base-version` = the pack version and `base-hash` **equal to an independently computed `hash_bytes(pack.read(Steps, "implement"))`** (the recorded basis equals an independent pack-direct hash — the increment's headline claim), and the entry round-trips back through T1's loader to a basis record for that target. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Per-kind base-hash basis (the basis = blake3 of the pack-default unit's resolved native bytes; record-time reads `pack.read` pack-direct), Authoring deltas (the `replace-step`/`remove-step` rows).
- **T3** — **Compose byte-identical golden: the basis never perturbs compose** (engine `compose.rs` and/or cli `start.rs`): an explicit test proving the compose-facing `StructuralDelta` (hence composed output) is **unchanged** whether or not the basis is recorded — a manifest entry carrying `base-version`/`base-hash` parses to the **same** `StructuralDelta::Replace` (and feeds compose the identical include-list mutation) as one without them, so the composed bytes are identical. *Done:* a test composes a workflow under a `replace-step` delta authored **with** the basis and one authored **without**, and asserts the composed output (and the `Vec<StructuralDelta>` compose receives) is **byte-for-byte identical** between the two — the basis rides only the separate record, never `StructuralDelta`, so phase-4 compose and its goldens are untouched (B2). *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → On-disk vs in-memory representation ("the phase-4 compose path and its byte-identical goldens are untouched"); [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-1--base-hash-substrate-for-replace--remove) → Increment 1 (the no-override compose **byte-identical**; `StructuralDelta` unchanged).

Each task ends in one conventional commit; serial over the shared tree. No new fork surfaced — every bullet maps to settled M5 decisions (2026-06-03, the planning entry above incl. design-review B2's separate-record representation pin + Optional-for-replace/remove parse) and the locked [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Per-kind base-hash basis / On-disk vs in-memory representation, plus the increment-1 roadmap scope. The basis is **recorded only** here (`replace`/`remove` join `tracked-fork`); the `override-default` classifier that *reads* it (clean/conflict/orphaned/**needs-rebasing**, with the pack-direct re-read + fork shadow-bypass), the `FilesystemPack` seam, and `jigc upgrade` all stay in increments 2–4.

**T1 landed** — `StructuralBasis{target, base_version, base_hash}` (engine `cascade`) rides a separate `Vec` keyed by target on `load_project_layer`'s tuple, never on the compose-facing `StructuralDelta`. The `base-version`/`base-hash` pair on `replace-step`/`remove-step` is **all-or-nothing-optional**: both → a basis record, neither → none (M4 back-compat, never an error), exactly one → a clear located error (a half-basis can't round-trip). `tracked-fork`'s required-field path is untouched.

## 2026-06-03 — M5 Increment 2 planning: decomposition

Cut [Increment 2 — the `override-default` classifier (the core logic)](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-2--the-override-default-classifier-the-core-logic) into three ordered, single-concern tasks. The increment builds the **engine** `override-default` classifier — a pure `(deltas, &dyn PackSource) → Vec<Finding>` that maps each recorded delta against a given pack to **clean / conflict / orphaned / needs-rebasing**, proven in-process over a two-version `FakePack`, before any command or CLI surface (increments 3–4). M5's #1 logic risk: the **tracked-fork shadow-bypass** (design-review B3) — the classifier must re-read the pack-default unit via `PackSource::read`, *never* the cascade-resolved owner, or a fork compares to its own copy and falsely says `clean`. *Why this order:* the probe asks up to three deterministic questions per delta in a fixed order — **(1) target exists? (2) content-bearing with no recorded basis? (3) content changed?** ([overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Upgrade reconciliation) — so each task is a complete vertical for one question, each builds on the last's classifier surface, and no task ships a function that mishandles a coherent input class: first the function + the existence question (orphaned vs not, every kind), then the content compare for the content-bearing kinds *with* the pack-direct re-read + shadow-bypass (clean vs conflict — the headline risk), then the needs-rebasing branch (basis-less legacy delta, distinct from clean) + the omitting-context hardening. *Grounded facts the cut rests on (verified against HEAD `60ca90f` — Increment 1 landed, tree clean — not assumed):*

- **The classifier's input is the delta surfaces Increment 1 finalized** — `load_project_layer` (`start.rs:1106`) returns the 5-tuple `(OverrideLayer, Vec<StructuralDelta>, Vec<SlotFillDelta>, Vec<TrackedForkDelta>, Vec<StructuralBasis>)` (`start.rs:1064`); the content-bearing kinds the conflict column is real for are `replace`/`remove` (`StructuralDelta`, paired with a `StructuralBasis` keyed by `StructuralTarget` when a basis was recorded) and `tracked-fork` (`TrackedForkDelta`, basis on the delta itself). The classifier is a **new engine function** (a `pub mod` in `engine`, e.g. an `override_default`/`reconcile` module — no existing classifier stub: grep finds none) taking these surfaces + a `&dyn PackSource`.
- **The pack-direct re-read + the hash are the exact functions M4/Inc-1 use at record time** — `PackSource::read(kind, &ResourceId)` (`packsource.rs:75`) reads a unit's bytes by id from *that* pack (deliberately not the cascade-resolved owner — the shadow-bypass), and `engine::file_state::hash_bytes(&[u8]) -> String` (`file_state.rs:41`, blake3 lowercase-hex, golden-tested) is the chosen hash; the record-time path already pairs them pack-direct (`run_fork` → `resolve_fork_bytes(&pack, step_id)` = `pack.read(Steps, id)` → `hash_bytes`, `config.rs:453/466/503`). The probe re-computes `hash_bytes(pack.read(Steps, <#step-id>))` against the *given* pack and compares to the recorded `base_hash` — a pure stateless compare, no old pack kept ([overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Per-kind base-hash basis, The probe re-reads the pack-default unit).
- **The unit a content-bearing delta names is the `#<step-id>` step** — `StructuralTarget` (`cascade.rs:84`) carries `workflow_id` + `Anchor::At(step-id)` for replace/remove/fork; the unit to re-read is `Steps`/`<step-id>` (the cli derives this id via `at_step`, `config.rs:955`). "Target still exists" for these = `pack.read(Steps, <step-id>)` is `Ok`; absent → `orphaned`.
- **The finding envelope + the `String` route already exist** — `Finding::block(code, message, route)` (`finding.rs:139`) is precisely the **blocking finding carrying a human-readable `String` route** the design mandates (not the aspirational `run-command`/`reconcile` tagged union — [validation.md](design/validation.md) → Findings, `override-default` emits a `String` route); `Severity::Blocking` is the blocking-by-default default. The classifier returns `Vec<Finding>` (the caller in Inc-4 wraps it in `ValidationReport::new`, `result.rs`). **Each delta is identified in its finding by its target string** (`workflow:<id>#<step-id>`) — a delta has no stable id, its target *is* its identity; the engine has no `Display` for `StructuralTarget` today (the `workflow:{id}#{step}` spelling lives in cli `config.rs:537`), so an engine-side target→string rendering is a **doc-elaboration the executor pins** (T1 owns it), not a fork.
- **A `FakePack` two-version test double is straightforward** — `packsource.rs` tests already carry a trivial in-memory `FakePack` (`version` + a `HashMap<(kind,id), bytes>`, `packsource.rs:85`); the classifier's tests build a `v1`/`v2` pair (same id, changed/absent bytes) the same way (in the classifier module's test mod — the existing one is `#[cfg(test)]`-private to `packsource`).

- **T1** — **Classifier function + the existence question (orphaned)** (engine, new `override_default` module): the `pub fn` signature `(deltas: …, pack: &dyn PackSource) → Vec<Finding>` taking the recorded delta surfaces (the `StructuralDelta`/`TrackedForkDelta`/`StructuralBasis` records, plus the scalar/slot-fill surfaces needed to walk every kind) + a `&dyn PackSource`, and the **first question only**: per delta, does its target unit still exist in `pack`? For content-bearing kinds (`replace`/`remove`/`tracked-fork`) the target is `pack.read(Steps, <#step-id>)`; absent → an `orphaned` blocking `Finding::block` identified by the delta's **target string** (the engine-side `StructuralTarget`→`workflow:<id>#<step-id>` rendering pinned here). A present target yields **no finding yet** (content compare is T2). The other kinds (`scalar-set` existence, `slot-fill`/`insert` anchor existence) walk through returning their clean/orphaned-by-existence shape per the conflict table. *Done:* an engine unit test over a `FakePack` v1→v2 asserts a `replace` (and a `tracked-fork`) delta whose `#<step-id>` the **v2 pack omits** classifies `orphaned` (a blocking finding, target string in the message/identity, with a remove-or-re-target route), while a delta whose target v2 still carries yields no orphaned finding; a present-but-not-yet-compared content-bearing delta produces nothing (no spurious conflict). *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Upgrade reconciliation (question 1, the per-kind outcome table), [validation.md](design/validation.md) → `override-default` (target: override, `String` route, blocking-by-default).
- **T2** — **Content compare for content-bearing kinds: clean vs conflict + the fork shadow-bypass** (engine, same module): for a content-bearing delta whose target still exists **and** carries a recorded basis (`tracked-fork`'s `base_hash`, or a `replace`/`remove` with a matching `StructuralBasis` record), compute `hash_bytes(pack.read(Steps, <#step-id>))` against the given pack and compare to the recorded `base_hash` — equal → `clean` (no finding), differs → a `conflict` blocking `Finding::block` (target string + a keep/re-target/drop route). The pack-direct re-read **bypasses any project shadow** by construction (it reads `pack`, never a cascade-resolved owner). *Done:* an engine unit test over a `FakePack` v1→v2 asserts (a) an **unchanged** unit → `clean` (no finding) for each content-bearing kind, (b) a **changed** `replace` and a **changed** `remove` → `conflict`, and — the headline — (c) a **`tracked-fork`** whose recorded basis is v1's bytes but whose v2 pack bytes differ classifies **`conflict`**, proving the probe compared against the *pack* unit and not the fork's own shadowed copy (a shadow-aware read would have returned the fork's copy = the recorded hash and falsely said `clean` — the silent-defeat B3 risk, now a live test). *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → The probe re-reads the pack-default unit (shadow-bypass), Per-kind base-hash basis; [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-2--the-override-default-classifier-the-core-logic) (the changed-fork→conflict case).
- **T3** — **The needs-rebasing branch (basis-less legacy) + the omitting-context hardening** (engine, same module): a content-bearing `replace`/`remove` delta whose target **exists** but for which **no `StructuralBasis` was recorded** (an M4-era manifest) classifies **`needs-rebasing`** — a blocking `Finding::block` with a re-record route — **distinct from `clean`** (a present-and-equal basis), and **never** silently re-derived from the current pack (which would compare equal and mask every conflict). Question order holds: exists (T1) → no-basis → needs-rebasing (T3), before the changed? compare (T2). *Done:* an engine unit test asserts (a) a `replace`/`remove` with a **present-and-equal** basis → `clean` and one with **no basis record** → `needs-rebasing` (the two are not confused), and (b) the [Validation hardening #5](implementation/increment-workflow.md) omitting-context case — the classifier run over a delta set against a pack that **omits** the target behaves correctly (`orphaned`, not a panic or a false `clean`), exercising the absent-target path as its own check rather than only the including pack. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Backward-compat (`needs-rebasing` distinct from `clean`, never re-derived); [increment-workflow.md](implementation/increment-workflow.md) → Validation hardening #5.

Each task ends in one conventional commit; serial over the shared tree. No new fork surfaced — every bullet maps to settled M5 decisions (2026-06-03, the planning entry above: the three-question classifier, the pack-direct re-read + fork shadow-bypass B3, `needs-rebasing` distinct from `clean`, the `String` route, blocking-by-default) and the locked [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Upgrade reconciliation / The probe re-reads the pack-default unit / Per-kind base-hash basis and [validation.md](design/validation.md) → `override-default`, plus the increment-2 roadmap scope. This increment is the **engine classifier only**, proven in-process over a `FakePack` — the `jigc upgrade` command, the findings+routes rendering at a CLI surface, the `FilesystemPack` + `JIGC_PACK_DIR` seam, and the genuine two-pack e2e all stay out (increments 3–4). The one doc-elaboration the executor pins (not a fork): the engine-side `StructuralTarget`→`workflow:<id>#<step-id>` target-string rendering the findings identify each delta by (the cli already spells this form; the engine gains its own).

## 2026-06-03 — M5 Increment 2 T1 shipped: `override-default` classifier + the existence question

New engine `override_default` module: `classify(deltas: RecordedDeltas<'_>, pack: &dyn PackSource) → Vec<Finding>`, where `RecordedDeltas` borrows the M5-relevant `load_project_layer` 5-tuple surfaces (`structural`/`forks`/`bases`). Question 1 only: a content-bearing `replace`/`remove`/`tracked-fork` delta whose target step the **pack omits** (`pack.read(Steps, <#step-id>)` is `NotFound`) → a blocking `orphaned` `Finding::block` (`override-default.target-exists`) carrying the delta's target string `workflow:<id>#<step-id>` + a remove/re-target route; a target the pack still carries → no finding (no content compare yet, so **no** spurious `conflict`). The read is **pack-direct** (the shadow-bypass T2's fork case depends on). The engine-side `StructuralTarget`→`workflow:<id>#<step-id>` rendering is pinned here (cli already spells it; engine gains its own). FakePack `v1→v2` unit-proven.

## 2026-06-03 — M5 Increment 3 planning: decomposition

Cut [Increment 3 — `FilesystemPack` + the pack-source factory seam](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-3--filesystempack--the-pack-source-factory-seam) into three ordered, single-concern tasks. The increment adds the genuine-`v1→v2` testability seam M5's acceptance (increment 4) needs: a `FilesystemPack` `PackSource` reading a pack tree from a directory, selected by a **`JIGC_PACK_DIR`** env var via **one pack-source factory** that replaces *every* production `EmbeddedPack::new()` site — with the env unset, all output **byte-identical** to today. *Why this order:* risk-first — build and unit-prove the new impl in isolation first (no behavior change yet), then the factory + the routing obligation (the load-bearing requirement: every production construction goes through one factory so the recording and upgrade paths read the **same** env-selected pack — design-review N1, the obligation not a site count), then the integration determinism guard that exercises the built binary with the env unset (byte-identical), set (directory pack loads), and across two pack dirs reporting distinct `version`s. *Grounded facts the cut rests on (verified against HEAD `50cea9d` — M5 increments 1–2 landed, tree clean — not assumed):*

- **The `PackSource` trait is the exact seam to implement** — `engine::packsource::PackSource` (`packsource.rs:67`) is `pack_version(&self) -> String` + `list(&self, kind: PackResourceKind) -> Vec<ResourceId>` + `read(&self, kind, &ResourceId) -> Result<Vec<u8>, PackError>`; `EmbeddedPack` (`cli pack.rs:43`) already impls it over `include_dir!`, mapping each `PackResourceKind` to a sub-dir (`schemas`/`workflows`/`steps`/`config` via `kind_dir`, `pack.rs:34`) and a `ResourceId` to a file **stem** (`pack.rs:54`, sorted; `read` matches by stem, `pack.rs:66`). `FilesystemPack` mirrors that contract over a real directory (`std::fs::read_dir` of `<dir>/<kind-subdir>/`, stem = `ResourceId`), `NotFound` on an absent id — the same `PackError::NotFound { kind, id }` shape. The trait is object-safe (`packsource.rs:187` proves `&dyn` use), so `FilesystemPack` slots in behind `&dyn PackSource` like `EmbeddedPack`.
- **`pack_version` reads the dir's `config/defaults.yaml` `version` key, `fs-local` sentinel if absent** — the pack tree already carries `config/defaults.yaml` (`pack-id: dev` + `default-workflow: router`, no `version:` today); `EmbeddedPack::pack_version` returns `CARGO_PKG_VERSION` (`pack.rs:44`), but a `FilesystemPack` has no compiled version, so it parses `version:` out of its own `config/defaults.yaml` (the file `pack_id_from_config` already reads via `serde_yaml_ng`, `start.rs:1042`/`orient.rs:81`), falling back to the fixed `fs-local` sentinel when the key is absent ([overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → the FilesystemPack seam). Narrative-only `base-version` makes the sentinel harmless; a flow-7 route-text test sets `version:` in each dir.
- **Every production construction is `let pack = EmbeddedPack::new();` (or a struct field) flowing into `&dyn PackSource`** — the production sites are `start.rs:188/223/495/692`, `config.rs:185/264/316/360/401/443`, `orient.rs:42`, plus the two struct fields `TaskArea.pack` (`task.rs:242/261`) and `ActiveTask.pack` (`doc.rs:169/195`); every downstream consumer takes `&dyn PackSource` (`resolve_cascade`, `compose_drained`, `orient_with`, `PackStepSource.pack: &'a dyn PackSource` `start.rs:894`, `selectable_workflows`, `all_schemas`, …) or derefs a struct field (`self.pack.list(...)`, `task.rs:278`/`doc.rs:214`). So a factory returning a boxed `dyn PackSource` (`Box<dyn PackSource>`) drops in at each call site (`&*pack` / `&pack` both coerce) and the two struct fields become `Box<dyn PackSource>` — `self.pack.list(...)` is unchanged because `Box<dyn>` derefs. No downstream signature churns; this owner-type elaboration is within the settled seam, an executor doc-elaboration, **not** a fork.
- **The test doubles (`FakePack`/`FixturePack`) are NOT production sites and stay** — the `EmbeddedPack::new()` calls under `#[cfg(test)]` (`pack.rs` test mod; `start.rs:2273`+; `config.rs:1234`) and the in-test `FakePack`/`FixturePack` impls (`orient.rs:133`, `start.rs:1673`, `config.rs:1099`, `packsource.rs:85`, `override_default.rs:253`) are *test* constructions — the routing obligation is over **production** sites only (the factory replaces the non-test `EmbeddedPack::new()` callers).
- **A binary-driving determinism guard is the existing test idiom** — `crates/cli/tests/` already drives the real `jigc` binary via `assert_cmd` (`start_compose.rs`, `start_orientation.rs`), and snapshot/byte goldens are the established no-override proof. The guard runs `jigc start`/`jigc start --explain` (or the orientation path) **with no env** asserting byte-identical to the committed golden, **with `JIGC_PACK_DIR=<copied-pack-dir>`** asserting the directory pack loads (same composed output for an identical copy), and over **two pack dirs** asserting distinct `pack_version()`s surface (the provenance `Pack: <id>/<version>` label `start.rs:505`/`orient.rs:62` renders the version, so a dir with `version: 0.4.0` shows it).
- **The factory's home is `cli` (alongside `EmbeddedPack`), not `engine`** — `module-layout.md` keeps the engine empty of pack content (it knows only the trait) and the `EmbeddedPack`/`FilesystemPack` impls live in `cli`; the factory (env read → `EmbeddedPack` default vs `FilesystemPack`) is a cli concern. `FilesystemPack` lands in `cli pack.rs` next to `EmbeddedPack`.

- **T1** — **`FilesystemPack` impl** (cli `pack.rs`): a `FilesystemPack { root: PathBuf }` impl of `engine::packsource::PackSource` — `list(kind)` reads `<root>/<kind-subdir>/` (the same `kind_dir` mapping `EmbeddedPack` uses), yielding each file's **stem** as a `ResourceId`, **sorted** (matching `EmbeddedPack`'s order); `read(kind, id)` returns the bytes of the file whose stem == `id` under that sub-dir, else `PackError::NotFound { kind, id }`; `pack_version()` parses the `version:` key from `<root>/config/defaults.yaml` (reusing the `serde_yaml_ng` read shape `pack_id_from_config` uses), returning the fixed **`fs-local`** sentinel when the file or key is absent. *Done:* a `pack.rs` unit test over a temp dir seeded with a minimal pack tree asserts (a) `list(Workflows)` returns the seeded stems **sorted**, (b) `read` round-trips the seeded bytes and an absent id → `NotFound`, (c) `pack_version()` = the seeded `defaults.yaml` `version:` value, and (d) a dir whose `defaults.yaml` omits `version:` (or is absent) → `fs-local`. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → the FilesystemPack seam; [module-layout.md](implementation/module-layout.md) → The dev pack's home (`PackSource` impls live in `cli`); [packsource.rs](crates/engine/src/packsource.rs) (the trait contract `EmbeddedPack` mirrors).
- **T2** — **The `JIGC_PACK_DIR` pack-source factory routed through every production site** (cli `pack.rs` + `start.rs`/`config.rs`/`orient.rs`/`task.rs`/`doc.rs`): one factory fn (e.g. `make_pack() -> Box<dyn PackSource>`) returning a `FilesystemPack::new(<dir>)` when `JIGC_PACK_DIR` is set (to a readable dir), else `EmbeddedPack::new()`; replace **every** production `EmbeddedPack::new()` site (the `start.rs`/`config.rs`/`orient.rs` locals + the `TaskArea`/`ActiveTask` struct fields, which become `Box<dyn PackSource>`) with a factory call — the recording verbs (`jigc config …`) and the (increment-4) upgrade path thereby read the **same** env-selected pack (design-review N1). Test-only `EmbeddedPack::new()` calls and the `FakePack`/`FixturePack` doubles are untouched. *Done:* a unit/integration test asserts (a) `make_pack()` with `JIGC_PACK_DIR` **unset** yields an `EmbeddedPack`-backed source whose `list`/`read`/`pack_version` equal `EmbeddedPack`'s, (b) with `JIGC_PACK_DIR=<dir>` yields a `FilesystemPack` reading that dir, and (c) a grep-guard / explicit assertion that no **production** path constructs `EmbeddedPack::new()` directly (every prod site routes through the factory); `cargo build` + `clippy -D warnings` clean (the struct-field type change compiles). *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → the FilesystemPack seam (one factory; every production construction goes through it; `EmbeddedPack` default when unset); [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-3--filesystempack--the-pack-source-factory-seam) (route **every** production pack-source construction through it — the obligation, not a site count).
- **T3** — **Determinism guard: no-env byte-identical + env-loaded + distinct versions** (cli `tests/`): a binary-driving integration test (`assert_cmd`, the existing idiom) asserting (a) **no-env** `jigc start "<intent>"` (and/or `jigc start --explain` / orientation) composed output is **byte-identical** to the committed golden (the env-unset path is byte-for-byte today's behavior — the determinism boundary), (b) `JIGC_PACK_DIR=<dir>` where `<dir>` is a faithful copy of the embedded pack loads the **directory** pack and composes identically (same bytes, proving the dir path is wired through the factory end-to-end), and (c) two pack dirs whose `config/defaults.yaml` carry distinct `version:` values report **distinct** `pack_version()`s through the binary (observable in the `Pack: <id>/<version>` provenance label). *Done:* the integration test passes; the no-env assertion is byte-identical to the pre-increment golden, the `JIGC_PACK_DIR` assertion loads the dir pack, and the two-dir assertion shows distinct versions. *Refs:* [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-3--filesystempack--the-pack-source-factory-seam) → Determinism guard (no-env golden byte-identical; `JIGC_PACK_DIR=<dir>` loads the directory pack; the two pack dirs report distinct `version`s); [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → the FilesystemPack seam (a real two-pack reconciliation through the built binary; `EmbeddedPack` stays the default when unset).

Each task ends in one conventional commit; serial over the shared tree. No new fork surfaced — every bullet maps to settled M5 decisions (2026-06-03 planning entry: the `FilesystemPack` reads `version` from `config/defaults.yaml` with the `fs-local` sentinel, one `JIGC_PACK_DIR` factory replacing **every** production `EmbeddedPack::new()` site, byte-identical when unset) and the locked [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → the FilesystemPack seam, plus the increment-3 roadmap scope. This increment is the **seam only** — the `jigc upgrade` command and the genuine two-pack reconciliation e2e (flow 7) stay in increment 4; the seam here only *enables* them. The one doc-elaboration the executor pins (not a fork): the factory's owner type — production pack values become `Box<dyn PackSource>` so the two struct fields (`TaskArea.pack`/`ActiveTask.pack`) and the locals carry the env-selected impl behind one type; every downstream consumer already takes `&dyn PackSource`, so no signature churns.

## 2026-06-03 — M5 Increment 3 T2 shipped: `make_pack()` factory routed through every production site

`make_pack() -> Box<dyn PackSource>` (cli `pack.rs`) is the single production construction point; every production `EmbeddedPack::new()` (the `start.rs`/`config.rs`/`orient.rs` locals + `TaskArea.pack`/`ActiveTask.pack`, now `Box<dyn PackSource>`) routes through it. Three minor executor refinements within the settled seam (not forks): (1) selection logic lives in a private `make_pack_from(Option<OsString>)` that `make_pack()` feeds `std::env::var_os("JIGC_PACK_DIR")` — so the unit tests drive the seam without mutating process env (parallel-test-safe, the repo has no serial-test/`temp-env` dep). (2) An **empty** `JIGC_PACK_DIR=` is treated as unset (falls through to `EmbeddedPack`), so an unset-equivalent value can't select an empty-path `FilesystemPack`. (3) The few `&dyn PackSource`-taking consumers receive `pack.as_ref()` (a one-line `let pack = pack.as_ref();` rebind per function): `&Box<dyn T>` does **not** unsized-coerce to `&dyn T`, so the planning note's "no signature churns" holds (signatures unchanged) but the call sites drop the `&` against the rebound `&dyn`.

## 2026-06-03 — M5 Increment 4 planning: decomposition

Cut [Increment 4 — `jigc upgrade` + the genuine v1→v2 acceptance](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-4--jigc-upgrade--the-genuine-v1v2-acceptance) into four ordered, single-concern tasks. The increment lands M5's headline command — `Command::Upgrade` (clap + dispatch), **report-and-route only** (load the cascade deltas, run the `override-default` classifier against the env-selected pack, render via the existing findings+routes renderer, exit non-zero on blocking, **mutate nothing**) — and proves it **genuinely** through the binary: a two-pack e2e (`JIGC_PACK_DIR=<v1>` record → `JIGC_PACK_DIR=<v2>` upgrade) classifying every outcome (clean / conflict incl. the **fork** conflict / orphaned / needs-rebasing) per [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow 7, then re-pin via `jigc config` verbs → re-run all-clean. *Grounded facts the cut rests on (verified against HEAD `37f146a` — M5 increments 1–3 landed, tree clean — not assumed):*

- **The classifier as built omits the `slot-fill` existence question that flow 7's `orphaned` outcome requires** — `engine::override_default::classify(deltas: RecordedDeltas<'_>, pack)` (`override_default.rs:76`) carries **only** `structural` (filtered to `replace`/`remove`) + `forks` + `bases` in `RecordedDeltas` (`override_default.rs:42`); it does **not** carry a `slot-fill` surface and asks no slot-fill question (`insert` is explicitly `continue`d). But the Increment-4 deliverable mandates the v2 e2e "removes a `{{fill:}}` point" and asserts **orphaned**, and flow 7's orphaned finding *is* `slot-fill step:locate#hints` ([worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → flow 7; [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) conflict table: `slot-fill | the slot's existence | clean / orphaned`). So the slot-fill orphan classification is a **buildable prerequisite this increment must add before its acceptance can pass** — NOT a new fork: its behavior is fully locked (conflict table + flow 7), and the machinery exists (`engine::compose::fill_ids_in(&body)` recognizes a step body's declared `{{fill:}}` points — `config.rs:621`'s write-time `check_fill_point_present` already uses it; `SlotFillDelta.target` carries `step_id` + `fill_id`, `cascade.rs:404`/`290`). The pack-direct slot-fill read (`pack.read(Steps, step_id)` → parse → `fill_ids_in`) is the same shadow-bypass discipline the content kinds already use. M5 Increment 2's own plan anticipated "the scalar/slot-fill surfaces needed to walk every kind"; the built classifier deferred them, so this increment completes the surface the flow-7 acceptance needs.
- **The delta surfaces the command loads already exist** — `load_project_layer(project_config)` (`start.rs:1110`) returns the 5-tuple `(OverrideLayer, Vec<StructuralDelta>, Vec<SlotFillDelta>, Vec<TrackedForkDelta>, Vec<StructuralBasis>)`; `RecordedDeltas` borrows positions 2/4/5 today, and T1 adds the `slot_fills` (position 3) borrow. The command's "re-apply — load the resolved cascade and walk every recorded delta" ([overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → The `jigc upgrade` command, step 1) is exactly wrapping those surfaces into `RecordedDeltas` and calling `classify` — a new cli assembly seam (T2 owns it), not an existing entry point.
- **The env-selected pack is one factory call** — `make_pack() -> Box<dyn PackSource>` (`pack.rs:97`, Increment 3) honors `JIGC_PACK_DIR`; the upgrade path calls it so the recording verbs and the upgrade read the **same** env-selected pack ([overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → the FilesystemPack seam — the load-bearing requirement). `classify` takes `&dyn PackSource`, so `&*make_pack()` drops in.
- **The renderer + the exit-code shape already exist** — `render::validation(format, &ValidationReport)` (`render.rs:200`, `pub`) is "the existing findings+routes renderer" the scope names: one `severity · code — message` line per finding + an indented `route:` line + the routing footer (the same envelope `task validate` uses); `ValidationReport::new(findings)` (`result.rs:158`) + `has_blocking()` (`result.rs:168`) give the report and the non-zero gate. `classify` already returns blocking `Finding`s with `String` routes (the `keep / re-target / drop`, `remove / re-target`, `re-record` prose), so the command wraps `classify`'s `Vec<Finding>` in a `ValidationReport`, renders it, and exits `FAILURE` iff `has_blocking()`.
- **The project-layer locator is the established `.jigc/config` resolution** — every `jigc config` verb resolves it via `require_project_layer` (`config.rs:634`, repo-root + `.jigc/config` + the "run `jigc setup`" routed error); the upgrade path needs the same locator (reuse it — promoting it to a shared helper, or replicating the small resolution, is an executor reuse call, not a fork).
- **The genuine two-pack e2e idiom exists** — `tests/pack_source_determinism.rs` (Increment 3) already drives the built binary with `JIGC_PACK_DIR` over copied pack-dir trees (`copy_tree` + `dir_pack_with_version` set a dir's `config/defaults.yaml` `version:`; a self-cleaning `TempDir`; a real git repo via `init_repo`); flow 7's e2e mirrors it — record deltas with `JIGC_PACK_DIR=<v1>` (the `jigc config` verbs), then `JIGC_PACK_DIR=<v2>` (a copy whose `implement`/`finalize` bytes changed, whose `locate` step drops its `{{fill:}}` point, `locate` body otherwise untouched) `jigc upgrade`.

- **T1** — **Extend the classifier with the `slot-fill` existence question** (engine `override_default.rs`): add a `slot_fills: &[SlotFillDelta]` field to `RecordedDeltas` and a per-`slot-fill` question — read the target step **pack-direct** (`pack.read(Steps, &ResourceId::from(target.step_id))`), parse it to a step body, and check whether `target.fill_id` is a declared point (`engine::compose::fill_ids_in(&body)`); the step absent **or** the `fill_id` no longer a declared point → a blocking `orphaned` `Finding::block` identified by the slot-fill's target string (`step:<step-id>#<fill-id>`) with a drop/re-target route; a still-declared point → no finding (clean). The read is pack-direct (shadow-bypass, same discipline as the content kinds). *Done:* an engine unit test over a `FakePack` v1→v2 asserts (a) a `slot-fill` whose v2 step body **drops** its `{{fill:<id>}}` point classifies `orphaned` (blocking, target string `step:<step-id>#<fill-id>` in message + route, drop/re-target route), (b) a `slot-fill` whose v2 step **still declares** the point → no finding, and (c) the [Validation hardening #5](implementation/increment-workflow.md) omitting-context case — a `slot-fill` over a step the v2 pack **omits entirely** classifies `orphaned` (the absent-step path), never a panic or a false `clean`. *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Upgrade reconciliation (conflict table: `slot-fill | the slot's existence | clean / orphaned`), The probe re-reads the pack-default unit (shadow-bypass); [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → flow 7 (the `slot-fill step:locate#hints` orphaned outcome); [validation.md](design/validation.md) → `override-default`.
- **T2** — **The upgrade assembly seam: load deltas + classify → a `ValidationReport`** (cli, new `upgrade.rs`): a function `upgrade_in_repo(cwd) -> Result<ValidationReport>` (or equivalent) that locates the `.jigc/config` project layer (reusing the `require_project_layer` locator), calls `load_project_layer` for the recorded delta surfaces, constructs `make_pack()` (the env-selected pack — same pack the recording verbs read), borrows the surfaces into `RecordedDeltas` (`structural`/`forks`/`bases`/`slot_fills`), runs `engine::override_default::classify`, and wraps the `Vec<Finding>` in `ValidationReport::new` — **reading nothing, writing nothing** (report-and-route only; no manifest mutation, no transaction). *Done:* a cli integration/unit test seeds a throwaway repo + `.jigc/config` with a hand-authored manifest carrying a delta whose target the env-selected pack omits, calls `upgrade_in_repo` against a `JIGC_PACK_DIR` pack, and asserts the returned `ValidationReport` carries the expected blocking `orphaned` finding (and that a no-delta / clean manifest returns an empty report, `has_blocking() == false`); asserts the manifest file is **unchanged** on disk after the call (mutates nothing). *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → The `jigc upgrade` command (steps 1–2: re-apply, classify; report-and-route only, never mutates the manifest), the FilesystemPack seam (recording + upgrade read the same env-selected pack); [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-4--jigc-upgrade--the-genuine-v1v2-acceptance).
- **T3** — **`Command::Upgrade` (clap + dispatch) → render + gate** (cli `cli.rs` + `main.rs`): a new top-level `Upgrade` command (no args; honoring the global `--format`); its dispatch arm calls `upgrade_in_repo(cwd)`, renders the `ValidationReport` through `render::validation(format, &report)` (the existing findings+routes renderer + footer), prints it, and maps the exit code to `report.has_blocking()` — **non-zero on any blocking finding**, zero on an all-clean report. A locator error (not inside a repo / no `.jigc/config`) surfaces on stderr with its routed message and exits non-zero. *Done:* a clap-parse unit test asserts `jigc upgrade` parses to `Command::Upgrade` (and `jigc upgrade --format json` selects `Json`); a cli integration test drives the built binary in a seeded repo and asserts (a) a manifest with a blocking-classifying delta exits **non-zero** and the rendered output carries the finding's `severity · code — message` + `route:` line, and (b) an all-clean / no-delta manifest exits **zero** with the `no findings`-style positive line — the agent-facing contract validated through the **emitted bytes** ([increment-workflow.md](implementation/increment-workflow.md) hardening #4). *Refs:* [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → The `jigc upgrade` command (step 3: report through the standard findings+routes renderer, blocking-by-default; report-and-route only); [validation.md](design/validation.md) → Findings; [render.rs](crates/cli/src/render.rs) (`validation`).
- **T4** — **The genuine v1→v2 e2e acceptance + the re-pin→clean loop** (cli `tests/`): a binary-driving integration test (mirroring `tests/pack_source_determinism.rs`'s `JIGC_PACK_DIR` + copied-pack-dir + real-git-repo idiom) that (1) under `JIGC_PACK_DIR=<v1>` records the flow-7 delta spread via the real `jigc config` verbs — a `scalar-set`, a `replace-step` on `implement`, a `tracked-fork` on `finalize` and on `locate`, a `slot-fill` on `locate`'s `{{fill:}}` point, plus one M4-era basis-less `replace` (hand-authored into the manifest, no `base-hash`); (2) builds `<v2>` as a copy whose `implement` + `finalize` bytes change, whose `locate` step **drops** its `{{fill:}}` point (body otherwise untouched), other targets unchanged; (3) runs `JIGC_PACK_DIR=<v2> jigc upgrade` and asserts the emitted report shows **clean** (the scalar + the unchanged `locate` fork — silent), **conflict** (the changed `replace #implement` AND the changed `tracked-fork #finalize` — the **fork conflict** is the load-bearing shadow-bypass case), **orphaned** (the dropped `slot-fill` point), **needs-rebasing** (the basis-less `replace`), exit **non-zero**; (4) resolves via the `jigc config` verbs (re-record the conflicted/needs-rebasing deltas — re-pinning their basis to v2 — drop the orphaned slot-fill by the sanctioned manifest delta-form edit) and re-runs `jigc upgrade` against `<v2>` asserting **all-clean**, exit **zero**. *Done:* the integration test passes end-to-end through the built binary, asserting each of the four outcomes by its emitted finding line + the all-clean re-run. *Refs:* [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → flow 7 (the full arc: the four outcomes, the fork conflict, resolve → re-run all-clean); [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → The `jigc upgrade` command (step 4: resolve by re-running the `jigc config` verbs; a clean re-run is the verification); [increment-workflow.md](implementation/increment-workflow.md) → Validation hardening (#4 emitted bytes, #5 a context that omits the target — the dropped `{{fill:}}` point).

Each task ends in one conventional commit; serial over the shared tree. **No new fork surfaced.** The one gap planning grounding turned up — the built classifier omits the `slot-fill` existence question that flow 7's `orphaned` outcome demands — is **not** a fork but a **buildable prerequisite** folded in as T1: its behavior is locked by the [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) conflict table + [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow 7, and the recognizer (`fill_ids_in`) + the `SlotFillDelta.target` surface + the pack-direct read discipline all exist; M5 Increment 2's own plan anticipated the slot-fill surface and merely deferred it. Everything else maps to settled M5 decisions (2026-06-03 planning entry: `jigc upgrade` report-and-route only, blocking-by-default, the `String` route, the FilesystemPack seam, the genuine two-pack e2e = flow 7) and the locked [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Upgrade reconciliation / The `jigc upgrade` command and [validation.md](design/validation.md) → `override-default`. This is M5's **final** increment — the recorded deltas, now with complete bases, become M6's input.

## 2026-06-03 — M5 Increment 4 T1 shipped: slot-fill existence question in the classifier

`RecordedDeltas` gains a `slot_fills` surface; `classify` orphans a `slot-fill` (code `override-default.slot-fill-orphaned`, target string `step:<id>#<fill-id>`, drop/re-target route) when its target step's pack-direct v2 body no longer declares the `{{fill:<id>}}` point — covering both the point-dropped and the step-omitted-entirely paths — and stays silent when the point still resolves. The read is pack-direct (`pack.read(Steps, …)` → `load_step_def` → `fill_ids_in`), the same shadow-bypass the content questions use. Prerequisite for flow 7's `step:locate#hints` orphaned outcome (T4).

## 2026-06-03 — M5 (upgrade reconciliation) shipped: milestone-completion audit triage

M5 built end-to-end via the `milestone-build` harness — a 24-agent run (~half M4's footprint), **no build halts**, all four increments validated clean with **0 fix rounds**. The first run on the M4-hardened harness, and the hardening showed: the B3 fork-shadow-bypass trap (a `tracked-fork` shadows the same step id, so a shadow-aware re-read would compare a fork to itself → every fork falsely `clean`) was caught in **design** by the hardened design-reviewer and never reached the build; the e2e audit then *independently proved* the bypass is real (an unchanged fork stays clean, a changed one conflicts).

The milestone-completion audit returned **e2e overall-pass with no findings** and two LOW code-review notes:
- **(FIXED `ba7f86f`)** `jigc upgrade` deferred `scalar-set`/`insert` existence classification below the approved increment-2 scope (only the 3 content kinds + slot-fill were reconciled). Completed: a removed knob key → `override-default.scalar-set-orphaned`; a dropped insert anchor → `override-default.target-exists` orphaned (existence-only — neither ever conflicts). Now the conflict table is whole and `jigc upgrade` is the complete one-stop divergence report. Proven through the binary via the `JIGC_PACK_DIR` seam against a v2 that omits the target (Validation hardening #5).
- **(ACCEPTED as deferred)** content-target existence is checked against step-*file* presence (`pack.read(Steps, id)`), not workflow include-list membership — MVP-correct for the single-workflow pack (step ids map 1:1 to includes, no reachable incorrect behavior); a future multi-workflow pack revisits it.

M5 (upgrade reconciliation) ships: a project individualizes the pack via recorded deltas (M4) and, on a pack upgrade, `jigc upgrade` re-classifies every delta clean/conflict/orphaned/needs-rebasing — no upstream change silently lost, no override silently broken. The recorded deltas (now with complete bases across all content kinds) are M6's input.

## 2026-06-03 — Fold the M5-audit lesson back into the harness — and generalize

The M5 milestone-completion audit caught one LOW finding the per-increment gates had passed over: `jigc upgrade` shipped `scalar-set`/`insert` orphan-classification *below* increment 2's approved scope ("per-kind existence + content compare"). The mechanism is the lesson: the work was quietly **punted from increment 2 to "a later task,"** increment 4 built only slot-fill, **neither built it**, and each per-increment validator treated it as the other increment's job — an **unowned cross-increment deferral** that cleared every gate and surfaced only at the audit. The build had *documented* the reduction (module comment + a pin), and the validators read "documented" as "acceptable."

This is the **third variant of one root** (M3: a masking test; M4: a single-context exercise; M5: an unowned deferral) — completeness slipping past the per-increment gate, caught only by the milestone audit. Per the M4 review's own note ("if a third variant shows up, generalize rather than enumerate a fourth special case"), the fold-back **states the general principle** and adds the M5 instance as a worked example under it:

- **[increment-workflow.md](implementation/increment-workflow.md) → Validation hardening:** new check **#6** (completeness against the *approved* scope — *documented ≠ approved*; an undelivered approved-scope bullet is **blocking**, and a cross-increment deferral must be followed to its named landing and confirmed delivered), plus the **unifying principle**: the six checks instantiate one thing — *the per-increment gate owns completeness against the approved plan, not just the internal honesty of what got built* — so a future variant is a new face of the principle, not a new category to enumerate.
- **[build-planner](.claude/agents/build-planner.md):** must cover the *whole* approved scope — every *Grouped scope* bullet maps to a task; silently narrowing it (a "rides along, lands in a later task" punt) is forbidden — either build it in this increment or **halt** for the human to approve the reduction; a within-increment stage names the specific task that delivers it.
- **[increment-validator](.claude/agents/increment-validator.md):** its scope-honesty check now verifies every approved-scope bullet was delivered (documented-deferral-as-blocking) and follows cross-increment punts to their landing.

Root addressed at both control points — the planner that punts and the validator that should own the punt's completion — so the deferral has nowhere to fall. Process docs/agents only; no product code.

## 2026-06-04 — M6 (severity tuning & demotion-lock) planning: scope + design settled

Three `capability-auditor` subagents established the baseline (severity-into-probes is ~0% built, as the handover predicted: severity 100% hardcoded across ~17 sites; `Probe` trait a 6-line stub; `validate_task` monolithic + task-scoped; M4's two per-probe severity knobs declared but **read by nothing**; no demotion-lock; `override-default` runs bespoke). Four `gap-detector`s + this settle shaped the design. Done-picture: prove *"severity is a cascade setting, never a code change"* end-to-end — (1) cascade-assigned severity, (2) per-check knob granularity, (3) intrinsic/tunable two-tier + demotion-lock floor, (4) a non-task `Probe` seam with M5's `override-default` retrofitted onto it. Home of record: [validation.md](design/validation.md) → Severity assignment / The non-task `Probe` seam; [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Locked keys; acceptance = [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow 8.

Settled (human-gated forks):
- **Finding identity → structured `(probe, check)` fields.** The pre-M6 `Finding` had only a dotted `code`, and the built codes did **not** all parse to a clean `<probe>.<check>` (`override-default.content-changed` vs inventory `target-unchanged`; `file-state` emitting `reconciliation.*`). So `Finding` grows explicit `probe`/`check` fields (re-pins the JSON golden — accepted cost); `code` retained for render only. M6 reconciles emitted codes to the inventory ids (the inventory is the single source of truth — **18 checks / 11 intrinsic**; earlier "16/10" and "17/11" tallies were stale as the inventory grew — `knobs.yaml` ships exactly these 18 keys).
- **Demotion-lock floor → a `floor:` field on `KnobDecl`** (pack-declared in `knobs.yaml`). *Intrinsic-ness = "the knob carries `floor: blocking`"* — no separate engine intrinsic-set. Accepted tradeoff: a pack omitting a floor un-locks the check; guarded by a pack-content test, not the engine.
- **Declare all ~17 per-check keys** (intrinsic ones floored) — so a demotion attempt gets a clean "check is locked" rejection, not a confusing undeclared-key abort.
- **`warning` becomes a live third tier** — surfaced, non-blocking (the gate keys on `blocking`), rendered distinctly. Pre-M6 it was declared but never produced; M6 adds the produce/render/exit coverage.

Settled (design-faithful, surfaced not silently picked):
- **Severity = one engine-owned post-pass over aggregated findings** (validation.md:28-31's "engine assigns, probe suggests"), not threading `Resolved` into ~17 sites. Handles synthetic categories + the non-task probe through one mechanism.
- **Byte-identity via override-only-on-explicit-delta** — the post-pass overrides a finding's severity *only* when the cascade carries a `scalar-set` for its key; absent a delta the hardcoded default stands, so the no-override path is byte-identical automatically (the milestone's #1 risk). Per-check → per-probe → declared-default is a deliberate three-step lookup, not exact-key resolution.
- **Soft-rejection channel at resolution** — a declared-key-below-floor demotion is logged-not-applied and resolution **continues** (a new rejected-deltas surface on the resolved cascade, rendered by `--explain`); an *undeclared* key stays a hard `UndeclaredScalar` abort. The two are deliberately distinct.
- **Minimal `Probe` seam** — fit to `override-default`'s `(deltas, pack)` shape; `validate_task`'s proven inline probes stay wired and tune via the post-pass (no rewrite of the byte-stable path). Don't force one trait over both ctx shapes (the M3 over-generalization trap). `upgrade_in_repo` is the only `classify` caller — one seam to touch.
- **Backward-compat additive** — per-probe severity keys retained as defaults (never renamed), so an M4-authored manifest still resolves; an M4-authored demotion of an all-intrinsic probe is now correctly floor-rejected.
- **`route` stays `Option<String>`** — the tagged-union promotion is *not* bundled into M6 even though the golden re-pins for `(probe, check)` (scope discipline).
- **`inverse-cardinality`** scope-dependent default (advisory at task / blocking at store) — the knob default is the task-scope value; store-scope promotion stays engine logic.
- **Acceptance = flow 8 with `override-default` as the headline** (the seam's only real consumer this milestone): a conflict demoted `blocking → warning` so `jigc upgrade` warns instead of blocks; tunable-demote + intrinsic-floor-rejection + byte-identity as supporting assertions.
- **Doc drift fixed** — the false present-tense "engine assigns severity via cascade" in `finding.rs` (M4-style overstatement) is corrected when increment 1 restructures `Finding`; validation.md now marks the assignment as M6-wired.

Independent `design-reviewer` (read-only, spiked the baseline) — headline: **the byte-identity guard is sound in principle but had one leak + one understated cost**. All findings baked before decompose:
- **B1 (gate ordering)** — severity assignment runs at the single `ValidationReport` construction point, *before* `has_blocking()`; pinned by test. (validation.md → post-pass.)
- **B2 (the real byte-identity risk)** — `task validate`/`finalize` and `jigc upgrade` build **no `Resolved` today**; the post-pass forces resolving the cascade at those entry points for the first time, so the **no-delta golden must cover the validate + upgrade paths**, not just `start_compose`. Enumerated as scope.
- **B3 (scope correction)** — the per-check + `floor` knob surface (~17 keys, 11 floored, a new `KnobDecl.floor` field + loader + re-pinned `loaded_knobs_seed…` golden) is *first-class* milestone work, not an additive footnote. Reflected in the decomposition.
- **B4 (code→check mapping)** — the 5 built `override-default` codes map many-to-one onto the 3 canonical checks (3 orphan emissions collapse to one `target-exists` knob — intended minimality; `content-changed`→`target-unchanged`, `needs-rebasing`→`basis-recorded` renames). Explicit table baked into validation.md.
- **S1** — the `--explain` rejected surface needs new fields on `Resolved` + `ResolutionTree` (a `SCHEMA_VERSION`/projection bump) — enumerated as scope.
- **S2** — severity total order **`blocking > warning > advisory`** defined; soft-rejection reworded "delta dropped, value resolves from remaining layers" (not "floored literal stands").
- **S3** — inventory note distinguishes *informational-advisory* (`baseline-adopt`, `absorb`) from *blocking-but-untunable* (`reconciliation.{rename,conformance-block,conflict-block}`) — demoting `hash-matches` never silently disables a conflict-block.
- **S4** — the post-pass matches on inventory `(probe,check)` **membership, never code-prefix**, so a per-probe key can't catch an unkeyed determinism-boundary sibling (`schema-conformance.unknown-type`, parser `conformance.*`).
- **M1 (the one human-gated review fork)** — **hybrid floor guard**: floors stay pack-declared, but the engine **asserts at pack-load that its known 11 intrinsic check-ids are each floored** (fails loudly vs silently un-locking). Assertion-only — never assigns severity, never ships pack content, so engine-empty holds. (Reversed the initial "no engine-side list" call.)
- **M2/M3** — bare `jigc start --explain` must resolve `default-workflow` (build-time spike); the `warning` render path is real build scope.
- **No invariant conflict found** with CLAUDE.md/VISION.md.

## 2026-06-04 — M6 (severity tuning & demotion-lock) decomposed into 3 risk-first increments

Cut into 3 linear increments ([roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md#milestone-6--severity-tuning--demotion-lock-decomposition)): **(1)** the `Finding` `(probe, check)` restructure + the engine-owned severity post-pass + `Resolved` threaded into the validate/finalize path + the no-override byte-identity goldens (the determinism spine — the #1 risk); **(2)** the per-check `knobs.yaml` surface + `KnobDecl.floor` + the load-time intrinsic-floored assertion + the soft-rejection channel in `resolve` + the `--explain` rejected line; **(3)** the minimal non-task `Probe` seam + the `override-default` retrofit (severity threaded into `classify`, upgrade gains `Resolved`) + the live `warning` tier + flow 8 acceptance (an `override-default` conflict demoted to `warning` so `jigc upgrade` warns not blocks). Risk-first: the determinism trap in isolation, then the demotion-lock, then the seam + genuine acceptance. Ready for the `milestone-build` harness.

## 2026-06-04 — M6 Increment 1 planning: decomposition

Increment 1 — `Finding` (probe, check) + the engine-owned severity post-pass (the determinism spine) — cut into 4 ordered, single-concern tasks ([roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-1--finding-probe-check--the-engine-owned-severity-post-pass-the-determinism-spine); design [validation.md](design/validation.md) → Severity assignment — the M6 post-pass / Severity inventory / Code-id reconciliation). Serial spine: the `(probe, check)` handle first (every emit site populates it), then the post-pass that reads it, then the `Resolved` real cascade feeding the post-pass at the two entry points that resolve none today, then the determinism goldens that close the byte-identity guard. *Why this order:* the post-pass (T2) cannot match on `(probe, check)` membership until the fields exist (T1); the no-override byte-identity guard (T4) can only assert byte-identity once a real `Resolved` is threaded (T3), since the post-pass over an empty cascade is already inert.

- **T1 — `Finding` grows `probe`/`check` fields + reconcile every emitted code to its inventory `(probe, check)`.** Add `probe`/`check` to `Finding` (keep `code` for render); every production emit site sets them. The override-default 5-code→3-check mapping is explicit (`target-exists`/`scalar-set-orphaned`/`slot-fill-orphaned` → `target-exists`; `content-changed` → `target-unchanged`; `needs-rebasing` → `basis-recorded`); for all other probes `check` is the code suffix and `probe` the prefix. Codes with no inventory row (`schema-conformance.unknown-type`, the parser `conformance.*`, the non-inventory `workflow-refs.*`/`file-state.*`/`reconciliation.*`) still carry a `(probe, check)` but are exempt from the post-pass — *the inventory's keyed surface is the membership set, not the emitted-code set.* **Done:** the re-pinned inline finding JSON golden in `finding.rs` shows the `probe`/`check` keys, and a unit test asserts the 5→3 override-default mapping and that ≥1 named exempt code (`schema-conformance.unknown-type`) carries its `(probe, check)`; gate green. Files: `crates/engine/src/finding.rs`, `crates/engine/src/validate.rs`, `crates/engine/src/override_default.rs`, `crates/engine/src/file_state.rs`, `crates/engine/src/parse.rs`, `crates/engine/src/index.rs`.
- **T2 — the engine-owned severity post-pass in `ValidationReport::new(findings, &resolved)`.** Severity assigned in one pass at report construction, before any read: for each finding whose `(probe, check)` is an inventory row, three-step lookup (`validation.<probe>.<check>.severity` → `validation.<probe>.severity` → the engine's declared default), overriding **only** when the resolved cascade carries a `scalar-set` for the key (else the emitted default stands → byte-identical no-override); exempt findings pass through untouched. Runs before `has_blocking()`. The inventory membership set + per-check defaults are an engine constant (the validation.md table — legitimately engine knowledge, no `knobs.yaml` dependency this increment). All construction sites updated to pass a `Resolved` (`render.rs`, `upgrade.rs`, `task.rs`, `doc.rs`, `finalize.rs` + tests). **Done:** a post-pass unit test fed a synthetic resolved-override map proves a per-check `scalar-set` flips a blocking finding to advisory (and an advisory one to blocking) and `has_blocking()` reflects the assigned severity; a no-delta `Resolved` leaves every severity at its emitted default; gate green. Files: `crates/engine/src/result.rs`, `crates/engine/src/validate.rs`, `crates/cli/src/render.rs`, `crates/cli/src/upgrade.rs`, `crates/cli/src/task.rs`, `crates/cli/src/doc.rs`, `crates/engine/src/finalize.rs`.
- **T3 — thread a real `Resolved` into `task.rs::validate`/finalize and `upgrade`.** The validate/finalize path and `jigc upgrade` resolve **no cascade today** (review B2); each builds one via the existing `resolve_cascade` pattern (`make_pack` + `<repo>/.jigc/config`) and feeds it to the post-pass — an absent project layer resolves to base scalars (post-pass inert), so the no-override path is unperturbed. **Done:** `task validate`/`finalize` and `jigc upgrade` construct a `Resolved` and pass it through `ValidationReport::new`; an existing-behavior test (a recorded `validation.<probe>.<check>.severity` scalar-set demotes a real blocking finding observed through `task validate`'s exit code / rendered output) passes; gate green. Files: `crates/cli/src/task.rs`, `crates/cli/src/upgrade.rs`, `crates/cli/src/start.rs` (shared `resolve_cascade`).
- **T4 — determinism goldens: no-delta byte-identical over compose + the validate/finalize + upgrade paths.** Extend the byte-identity guard beyond `start_compose` (review B2): with no recorded delta, the emitted bytes of `task validate`/`finalize` and `jigc upgrade` are byte-identical to a captured pre-M6 baseline (the existing `start_compose` no-override goldens remain green unchanged). **Done:** a no-delta golden over the `task validate`/`finalize` rendered output and over `jigc upgrade`'s rendered output asserts byte-identity; gate green. Files: `crates/cli/tests/task_lifecycle.rs`, `crates/cli/tests/finalize_to_git.rs`, `crates/cli/tests/upgrade.rs` (or `upgrade_v1_v2.rs`).

No new fork surfaced: the increment is fully covered by the milestone's settled decisions (post-pass = override-only-on-explicit-delta, matched by inventory membership; `Resolved` threaded at the validate/upgrade entry points per B2) and the locked [validation.md](design/validation.md). All four *Grouped scope* bullets map to a task (bullet 1 → T1; bullet 2 → T2; bullet 3 → T3; bullet 4 → T4); the `warning` render tier and the `knobs.yaml` per-check key surface stay out — they are increments 3 and 2 respectively, not narrowed here.

## 2026-06-04 — M6 inc-1 T1 — `Finding` `(probe, check)` shipped: derived-at-construction + the `result_large_err` allow

`Finding` grew the two structured-handle fields `probe`/`check` (serialized after `severity`, before `code`; `#[serde(default)]` so older/hand-built JSON round-trips). Population is **centralized at construction, not re-parsed on read** (honoring [validation.md](design/validation.md) → Severity assignment): a private `split_code` splits the dotted `code` on its first `.`, invoked by `Finding::blocking` / `Finding::block` and a new general `Finding::graded(severity, code, message, location, route)` constructor. The `graded` constructor exists so no `Finding { … }` struct-literal anywhere in the workspace has to spell the new fields — every emit site that was a struct literal (in `state.rs`, `store.rs`, `finalize.rs`, `compose.rs`, `validate.rs`, `file_state.rs`, `index.rs`, plus `render.rs`/`result.rs`/`finalize.rs` tests) routes through a constructor instead, so the field additions stayed mechanical. `parse.rs` needed **no** edit — it already emits only via `Finding::blocking`, which now derives the handle.

- **`override-default` is the one rename surface.** Its five emitters set the canonical inventory `check` via a new `Finding::with_check` adjuster (the descriptive `code` is retained for render): the three orphan emissions → `target-exists`, `content-changed` → `target-unchanged`, `needs-rebasing` → `basis-recorded`. A unit test (`emitted_codes_reconcile_to_three_canonical_checks`) pins the 5→3 mapping; the `finding.rs` `exempt_code_still_carries_probe_check` test pins that a non-inventory code (`schema-conformance.unknown-type`) nonetheless carries a derivable handle.
- **`clippy::result_large_err` allowed workspace-wide.** The two added `String` fields pushed `Finding` (the engine's by-value error type, returned through ~38 `Result<_, Finding>` signatures — the "one finding shape" contract) past clippy's 128-byte threshold. Boxing every propagation site is cross-cutting churn that fights the by-value envelope design and is out of T1's named-file scope; the proportionate fix is a documented `[workspace.lints.clippy] result_large_err = "allow"` (both crates opt in via `[lints] workspace = true`). No new design fork — a lint-policy elaboration. The 8 finding-JSON `.snap` goldens were re-pinned to show the new keys (all diffs were exactly the two added keys, confirming the prefix/suffix derivation).

## 2026-06-04 — M6 inc-1 T2 — the engine-owned severity post-pass in `ValidationReport::new(findings, &resolved)`

Severity assignment is one engine pass at the single report-construction point (`ValidationReport::new` now takes `(mut findings, &resolved)` and runs `assign_severity` before stamping), so `has_blocking()` and every consumer read post-pass severities ([validation.md](design/validation.md) → Assigned once, at report construction — before the gate). Two doc-elaboration calls within the settled M6 design, no new fork:

- **Override-only-on-explicit-delta keys on the cascade's *override provenance*, not `scalar()`.** `Resolved::scalar(key)` returns the pack-default **base** value for any declared key, so it cannot distinguish "a `scalar-set` was applied" from "the key has a base value" — using it would re-grade on the base value and break byte-identity once knobs.yaml seeds these keys (inc-2). Added `Resolved::overridden_scalar(key) -> Option<&str>` (returns `Some` only when `scalar_provenance` records a winning override layer for the key); the post-pass's three-step lookup (per-check key → per-probe key → keep emitted) reads *that*. This is exactly what makes the no-override path byte-identical without the knob defaults mirroring the code's emitted literals.
- **The inventory constant is the `(probe, check)` membership set only — no declared-default column.** Matching is by membership, never code-prefix ([validation.md](design/validation.md)): an exempt sibling under a keyed probe (e.g. `schema-conformance.unknown-type`) keeps its emitted severity. The declared per-check default severity + `floor` are *not* read this increment (override-only-on-explicit-delta keeps the emitted severity), so carrying them now would be dead data; they land in `knobs.yaml` in inc-2 where `check_value`/`--explain` consume them.
- **Signature ripple.** `validate_task` gained a `&Resolved` param (it constructs the report). The five not-yet-cascade-resolved production sites (`task.rs` validate + finalize-error branch, `doc.rs` write-block, `upgrade.rs`) pass a no-delta `Resolved` via a new `cli::cascade_util::no_delta_resolved()` **T2 placeholder** — the post-pass is correctly inert over it; T3 threads the real project cascade there. Unit tests in `result.rs` fed a synthetic override map prove a per-check `scalar-set` flips blocking→advisory *and* advisory→blocking with `has_blocking()` reflecting it, the no-delta case is byte-identical, and a non-inventory check under a keyed per-probe override stays exempt.

## 2026-06-04 — M6 inc-1 T3 — thread a real `Resolved` into `task validate`/`finalize` + `jigc upgrade`

`task validate`, `finalize`, and `jigc upgrade` now resolve the **project cascade** (not the no-delta placeholder) before feeding the severity post-pass, so a recorded `validation.<probe>.<check>.severity` scalar-set actually tunes their findings ([validation.md](design/validation.md) → Every finding-emitting entry point must resolve the cascade). The resolution prefix (knobs → `PackDefaultLayer` → `load_project_layer` → `cascade::resolve`) was factored in `start.rs` into `resolve_layers`, shared by the new `resolve_severity_cascade` and the existing `resolve_cascade`/`resolve_step_body`. Two elaborations within the settled design, no new fork:

- **`jigc upgrade` needs a *resilient* resolve.** Upgrade compares a layer recorded against an *older* pack; an orphaned `scalar-set` (a knob the current pack dropped) is exactly what `override-default` reports — but feeding it to `cascade::resolve` trips the closed-surface `UndeclaredScalar` and would abort the upgrade that exists to report it. So `resolve_severity_cascade_resilient` filters the project scalar-sets to keys the current pack still declares before resolving (new `OverrideLayer::scalar_set_pairs()` companion to `scalar_set_keys()`). `task validate`/`finalize` stay strict — their manifests are valid by the `config set` write-time gate.
- **`no_delta_resolved` stays for the exempt path only.** `doc.rs`'s write-time block findings are never inventory checks, so the post-pass is a guaranteed no-op over them; that one site keeps `no_delta_resolved()` (the honest input), and its module doc was retargeted accordingly. The T3 done-criterion is proven through the binary in `task_lifecycle.rs`: a seeded `file-state.hash-matches` drift blocks `task validate` (exit non-zero), and a recorded `validation.file-state.severity: warning` demotes it to a still-surfaced warning so validate exits zero.

## 2026-06-04 — M6 Increment 2 planning: decomposition

Increment 2 — the per-check knob surface + `KnobDecl.floor` + the demotion-lock (soft-rejection) — cut into 5 ordered, single-concern tasks ([roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-2--the-per-check-knob-surface--knobdeclfloor--the-demotion-lock-soft-rejection); design [validation.md](design/validation.md) → Severity inventory (the SoT, **18 checks / 11 intrinsic**) / The two-tier rule, [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) → Locked keys / Soft-rejection-not-abort, [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → `--explain` output contract layer 1). Serial spine: the pack knob surface first (the keys everything downstream reads), then `KnobDecl.floor` + the load-time intrinsic-floored assertion (so floors exist as data), then the soft-rejection channel in `resolve` (reading those floors), then the `--explain` rejected line (rendering the channel's new surface), then the end-to-end proof through the binary. *Why this order:* the floor loader (T2) needs the declared keys to read a `floor:` off (T1); `resolve`'s soft-rejection (T3) needs floors threaded as data (T2); the `--explain` rejected line (T4) renders the rejected-deltas surface T3 adds to `Resolved`; the e2e (T5) drives all of it through the real binary.

- **T1 — `knobs.yaml`: the full per-check severity surface (3 → 17 keys, 11 floored) + re-pin the `loaded_knobs_seed…` golden.** Replace the two M4 per-probe `validation.<probe>.severity` keys (`workflow-refs`, `file-state`) **retaining them as additive defaults — never a rename** — and add every per-check `validation.<probe>.<check>.severity` key the [Severity inventory](design/validation.md#mvp-check-inventory) lists (the inventory is the single source of truth): the 7 `workflow-refs.*`, 4 `schema-conformance.*`, `file-state.hash-matches`, `schema-completeness.inverse-cardinality`, 3 `override-default.*` (`target-exists`/`target-unchanged`/`basis-recorded`), 2 `commit-rendering.*`. Each declares `type: enum`, `of: [blocking, warning, advisory]`, and the inventory's `default`.
