The 11 intrinsic keys (`workflow-refs.*` ×7 + `schema-conformance.*` ×4) — and only those — also carry no special key shape here beyond their default; their `floor:` is added in T2's loader-aware form (T1 may write `floor: blocking` on them as data since the loader still ignores an unknown body key only after T2; *to avoid a `deny_unknown_fields` break, T1 ships the keys+defaults and T2 adds `floor:` to both the model and the 11 lines in one task* — see note). `default-workflow` is unchanged. **Done:** the embedded `knobs.yaml` declares all 17 `validation.*.severity` keys + the 2 retained per-probe keys + `default-workflow`; the re-pinned `loaded_knobs_seed_the_pack_default_scalar_surface` test (`knobs.rs`) asserts the resolved closed key set is exactly the new surface and each key resolves to its inventory default; `pack-id` is still absent; gate green. Files: `crates/cli/pack/config/knobs.yaml`, `crates/engine/src/knobs.rs`.
- **T2 — `KnobDecl.floor` field + the loader carrying floors into `KnobSet` + the load-time intrinsic-floored assertion.** Add `#[serde(default)] floor: Option<String>` to the private `KnobDecl` (`knobs.rs`) and write `floor: blocking` on the 11 intrinsic keys in `knobs.yaml`; `load_knobs` collects a `floors: BTreeMap<String,String>` (key → floor value) onto `KnobSet` with an accessor, alongside the existing base scalars. The engine then **asserts at load** that each of its **known intrinsic check-id set** (the 11: `workflow-refs.{placeholder-resolves,include-resolves,command-ref-resolves,include-cycle-absent,at-marker-on-non-scalar,run-marker-not-shadowed,body-include-only}` + `schema-conformance.{ref-resolves,required-slot-present,required-field-present,field-value-conformant}`, each as `validation.<probe>.<check>.severity`) carries `floor: blocking` — an unfloored intrinsic check is a typed `KnobError` (assertion-only; never assigns severity, never ships pack content, so engine-empty holds — review M1 hybrid). The intrinsic-id set is an engine constant (legitimately the engine's own load-bearing invariants per [validation.md](design/validation.md) → What "intrinsic" means mechanically). **Done:** `load_knobs(embedded)` succeeds and exposes the 11 floors; a unit test feeds a `knobs.yaml` missing a `floor` on one intrinsic key and asserts the typed unfloored-intrinsic error; a test asserts a tunable key (e.g. `file-state.hash-matches`) carries no floor; gate green. Files: `crates/engine/src/knobs.rs`, `crates/cli/pack/config/knobs.yaml`.
- **T3 — the soft-rejection channel in `cascade::resolve` + the rejected-deltas surface on `Resolved`.** Define the severity total order `blocking > warning > advisory` (a small engine ranking over the three string values — `Severity` derives no `Ord` today). Thread each declared key's floor into `resolve` (additive: a `PackDefaultLayer::with_floors(BTreeMap)` builder defaulting to empty, fed by the production resolve sites that hold a `KnobSet` — `start.rs` `resolve_layers`/`resolve_severity_cascade_resilient`; the other 16 `PackDefaultLayer::new` call sites keep the existing signature with no floors). In the phase-3 fold, a `scalar-set` whose key has a floor **and** whose value ranks below that floor is **dropped — not applied — and resolution continues** (the value resolves from the remaining layers / the base, never a synthetic floor literal); the dropped delta is recorded on a new `rejected` surface on `Resolved` (attempted value · floor · source `LayerKind`, keyed by knob key), with an accessor mirroring `scalar_overrides()`. An *undeclared* key stays the hard `CascadeError::UndeclaredScalar` (the two outcomes are deliberately distinct). **Done:** a `cascade.rs` unit test proves a project `scalar-set` of an intrinsic key to `advisory` is dropped (the key resolves to its base `blocking`), is recorded on the rejected surface with `(attempted=advisory, floor=blocking, layer=project)`, and resolution returns `Ok`; a below-floor `warning` on a `blocking`-floor key is also dropped; a *tunable* key demotion still applies; an undeclared key still hard-errors; gate green. Files: `crates/engine/src/cascade.rs`, `crates/engine/src/finding.rs` (severity ranking).
- **T4 — `--explain` rejected-demotion line + the `ResolutionTree` field + `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump.** Add a `rejected_demotions: Vec<RejectedDemotion>` field (each: key · attempted value · floor · source `LayerKind`) to `ResolutionTree`, fed from `Resolved`'s rejected surface by the caller (mirroring how `scalar_overrides` flows through `start::build_resolution_tree` → `compose::build_resolution_tree`); bump `result::SCHEMA_VERSION` 1 → 2 (the projection changes — re-pin the affected `--explain`/result `.snap` goldens). Render the line in `render::explain_agent_text` distinctly from applied overrides (showing attempted value · floor · source layer, per [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → `--explain` output contract layer 1). **Done:** a `render.rs`/`result.rs` test pins the new field projects under the bumped `schema_version`; the agent-text `--explain` body shows a rejected-demotion line distinct from the applied-override lines when a below-floor scalar-set is present, and shows none when absent; gate green. Files: `crates/engine/src/result.rs`, `crates/cli/src/start.rs`, `crates/engine/src/compose.rs`, `crates/cli/src/render.rs`.
- **T5 — e2e: a real-key tunable demotion stops a check blocking; an intrinsic demotion is floor-rejected, logged, shown in `--explain`, not applied.** Through the real binary: (a) `jigc config set validation.file-state.hash-matches.severity advisory` then drive a `file-state` drift through `task validate` and assert the check no longer blocks (exit 0, finding surfaced as advisory) — the tunable path end-to-end; (b) `jigc config set validation.workflow-refs.placeholder-resolves.severity advisory` (an intrinsic key) is **floor-rejected** — assert the delta is not applied (the check still blocks where it fires) and that `jigc start --explain` shows the rejected-demotion line (attempted `advisory` · floor `blocking` · `project`). **Done:** an e2e test (`crates/cli/tests/`) drives both paths verbatim through the emitted binary surface (the `--explain` assertion reads the emitted bytes, not a reconstruction) and passes; gate green. Files: `crates/cli/tests/severity_tuning.rs` (new), reusing the existing repo-fixture / `jigc config` idioms.

### M6 inc-2 T5 — e2e both tiers through the binary; closed the floors-not-wired gap

- **The demotion-lock floors were never attached to the production resolve path — T5's e2e is what caught it.** T3's done-criterion named the wiring (`start.rs` `resolve_layers`/`resolve_severity_cascade_resilient` feed `PackDefaultLayer::with_floors`), but the landed T3 shipped the engine channel + builder and left both production sites calling `PackDefaultLayer::new(..)` floor-less — so a below-floor intrinsic `scalar-set` *applied* end-to-end (the engine soft-rejection was unreached: a `with_floors`-only-from-tests gap). The fix is one line at each site: `.with_floors(knobs.floors().clone())` on `resolve_layers` (shared by `start --explain` and the validate/finalize severity cascade via `resolve_severity_cascade`) and `resolve_severity_cascade_resilient` (the upgrade path). This closes T3's approved scope rather than narrowing it (the hardening #2/#6 cross-task-deferral lesson: a feature built but unwired is a blocking gap, not the next task's job).
- **The tunable-drift e2e seeds a stale `file-state.json` baseline.** `task validate` loads but never persists the record, so a working-area `hash-matches` drift can only arise from a pre-existing recorded baseline; the test writes the plain-JSON record directly (mirroring `engine::validate`'s `record.record(..)` unit setup), then drives the demotion through `jigc config set` + `task validate`. The `--explain` rejected-demotion assertion reads the emitted bytes verbatim (hardening #4).

### M6 inc-2 T4 — `--explain` rejected-demotion line + `ResolutionTree.rejected_demotions` + `SCHEMA_VERSION` 1 → 2

- **A rejected demotion is logged, not applied — so it is never folded into `overrides applied: N`.** `rejected_demotions` rides `ResolutionTree` layer 1 as its own field/render line (`rejected demotion: <key> = <attempted> below floor <floor>  (<layer>)`), distinct from the applied `scalar_overrides`; `overrides_applied` counts only applied deltas (a soft-rejected delta did not become an override). The field serializes with `skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty"`, so the no-rejection JSON is unchanged but for the version bump. `SCHEMA_VERSION` is a single shared const, so the 1 → 2 bump also stamps `Orientation`/`OrientationView`/`ValidationReport` (their projection-shape tests re-pinned to 2) — intentional, the marker is per-contract, not per-type.

### M6 inc-2 T2 — `KnobDecl.floor` + the load-time intrinsic-floored assertion

- **The intrinsic-floored assertion fires on *declared-but-unfloored*, not on *absent*.** `load_knobs` rejects an intrinsic key that a pack declares without `floor: blocking` (typed `KnobError::UnflooredIntrinsic`), but a pack that omits an intrinsic key entirely passes — an absent key is not a demotion risk (a `scalar-set` on an undeclared key already hard-aborts `UndeclaredScalar`), and minimal test packs legitimately ship only `default-workflow`. This is the safe reading of "each floored": the threat is a demotable declared key, not a missing declaration. The intrinsic-id set is an engine constant (`knobs::INTRINSIC_CHECK_KEYS`).

No new fork surfaced: the increment is fully covered by the milestone's settled decisions (2026-06-04 planning entry — the per-check + `floor` knob surface as first-class scope per review B3; the hybrid load-time intrinsic-floored assertion per review M1; soft-rejection = *drop the delta, value resolves from remaining layers* per review S2 / overrides.md → Soft-rejection-not-abort; severity total order `blocking > warning > advisory` per review S2; the rejected surface on `Resolved` + the `--explain` line + the `ResolutionTree`/`SCHEMA_VERSION` bump per review S1; per-probe keys retained as additive defaults, never a rename) and the locked [validation.md](design/validation.md) / [overrides.md](design/overrides.md) / [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md). All five *Grouped scope* bullets map to a task (bullet 1 → T1+T2's `knobs.yaml`+golden; bullet 2 → T2; bullet 3 → T3; bullet 4 → T4; bullet 5 → T5). The non-task `Probe` seam, the `override-default` retrofit, the live `warning` exit-coverage tier, and the flow-8 acceptance stay out — they are increment 3, not narrowed here. Doc-elaboration calls the executor pins (not forks): (i) `knobs.yaml` ships keys+defaults and `KnobDecl.floor` is added with the 11 `floor:` lines together so `deny_unknown_fields` never sees an unmodeled `floor` (T1+T2 are sequenced so the surface and its loader land coherently); (ii) floors reach `resolve` via an additive `PackDefaultLayer::with_floors` builder so the 16 floor-less `PackDefaultLayer::new` sites (mostly tests) are untouched; (iii) the engine's intrinsic-id constant is the 11 `(probe, check)` ids the inventory floors — the same legitimately-engine-owned set the post-pass exempts against in inc-1.

## 2026-06-04 — M6 Increment 3 planning: decomposition

Increment 3 — the non-task `Probe` seam + `override-default` retrofit + flow 8 acceptance (the headline) — cut into 3 ordered, single-concern tasks ([roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-3--the-non-task-probe-seam--override-default-retrofit--flow-8-acceptance-the-headline); design [validation.md](design/validation.md) → The non-task `Probe` seam / Severity assignment — the M6 post-pass, [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → flow 8). Serial spine: stand up the seam + route `classify` through it (the structural deliverable), then close the `warning` live-tier exit-code coverage, then the flow-8 acceptance through the binary on top of both. *Why this order:* the flow-8 headline (T3) demotes an `override-default` finding to `warning` and asserts exit 0 — it can only be a genuine end-to-end proof once `override-default` runs through the seam (T1) and the `warning` produce→render→exit path is proven non-blocking (T2).

**Ground-truth that reshaped the cut (verified against the code, not assumed):** the inc-1 post-pass already does the severity *assignment*, and inc-1 T3/T4 already wired it into the upgrade path — `upgrade_with_pack` (`upgrade.rs`) **already** builds a `Resolved` via `resolve_severity_cascade_resilient` and routes `classify`'s findings through `ValidationReport::new`, so a recorded `validation.override-default.<check>.severity` scalar-set **already tunes** `override-default` end-to-end. The roadmap bullet-1 sub-items "thread the resolved severity lookup into `classify`" and "`upgrade_in_repo` builds `Resolved`" are therefore **delivered by the post-pass already in place** — the literal phrasing must not be taken as "add severity-assignment inside `classify`" (that would duplicate the post-pass and break override-only-on-explicit-delta). Likewise the bullet-1 "no-delta byte-identical golden for the upgrade path (review B2)" **already exists** (`upgrade.rs::no_delta_upgrade_render_is_byte_identical_to_the_baseline`, landed inc-1 T4) — not re-added here. So bullet 1's *remaining* genuine work is **the seam structure itself** (`probe.rs` is a 6-line doc-only stub) and routing `classify` through it without regressing the already-wired tuning. Similarly the `warning` render *line* (distinct from advisory) and the `has_blocking()`-excludes-warning gate already exist (`render.rs::finding_line`, `result.rs::has_blocking`); bullet 2's remaining work is the **exit-code coverage proving a genuine warning-only report exits 0** end-to-end + the M2 `--explain` confirm. These are recorded as task red-step assumptions to *prove*, not optimistic reuse pins.

- **T1 — the minimal non-task `Probe` seam in `probe.rs`; route `override-default`'s `classify` through it.** Replace the doc-only `probe.rs` stub with a minimal non-task `Probe` abstraction shaped to `override-default`'s `(deltas, pack)` ctx — admitting the non-task shape only, **not** a trait unified over `validate_task`'s task-scoped shape (the M3 over-generalization trap; [validation.md](design/validation.md) → Two ctx shapes, not one trait over both). `override-default` is the seam's first and only real consumer: `classify` runs through the seam, and `upgrade_in_repo`/`upgrade_with_pack` invoke it via the seam rather than the bespoke direct call. The severity tuning is the inc-1 post-pass (already wired through `ValidationReport::new` in `upgrade_with_pack`) and stays there — the seam routes findings into aggregation, it does not re-implement assignment. **Done:** `probe.rs` exposes the non-task seam, `override-default` is invoked through it in `upgrade.rs`, and an existing-behavior test proves an `override-default` finding **still tunes via the post-pass after the seam routing** — a recorded `validation.override-default.target-unchanged.severity warning` demotes a conflict finding to `Warning` through the seam path (asserted in-process over the two-version `FilesystemPack`/`FakePack` idiom, the omitting-target context per hardening #5); the upgrade no-delta byte-identical golden (`upgrade.rs`) stays green unchanged; gate green. Files: `crates/engine/src/probe.rs`, `crates/engine/src/override_default.rs`, `crates/engine/src/lib.rs`, `crates/cli/src/upgrade.rs`.
- **T2 — the `warning` live-tier exit-code coverage (produce → render → exit, non-blocking) + the M2 `--explain` confirm.** Prove the third tier is live end-to-end: a `ValidationReport` whose only non-clean finding is `Severity::Warning` renders the warning line **distinct from advisory** (the `render.rs::finding_line` arm) and exits **0** through the upgrade gate (`run_upgrade` keys on `has_blocking()`, which excludes `Warning`) — closing the produce/render/exit coverage the roadmap names (review M3). Also confirm (review M2) that **bare `jigc start --explain` resolves `default-workflow`** (no spurious failure when no intent/workflow is given), since the post-pass entry points now resolve a cascade there. **Done:** a test drives a warning-only report through the render + exit path and asserts a non-blocking exit (exit 0) with the `warning` line present and textually distinct from `advisory`; a test asserts bare `jigc start --explain` resolves `default-workflow` and exits success; gate green. Files: `crates/cli/src/render.rs`, `crates/cli/src/cli.rs`, `crates/cli/tests/start_explain.rs` (or a render unit test), `crates/cli/tests/upgrade.rs`.
- **T3 — flow 8 acceptance through the real binary: an `override-default` conflict demoted `blocking → warning` warns instead of blocks (exit 0).** The mandated acceptance path ([worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → flow 8): in a throwaway repo over the `JIGC_PACK_DIR` v1→v2 seam (reusing the `upgrade_v1_v2.rs` / `pack_source_determinism.rs` idiom), record a `replace #implement` against v1, `jigc config set validation.override-default.target-unchanged.severity warning`, then `JIGC_PACK_DIR=<v2> jigc upgrade` against a v2 whose `implement` changed — assert the conflict is **surfaced as a warning**, **0 blocking findings**, and the process **exits 0** (the headline; pre-M6 this was hardcoded blocking and unsettable). The supporting trio — a tunable demote stops a check blocking, an intrinsic demote is floor-rejected + shown in `--explain`, and the no-override path is byte-identical — is already covered by `severity_tuning.rs` (file-state tunable + intrinsic-floor) and `upgrade.rs` (byte-identical) from inc-1/inc-2; T3 adds the **`override-default`-specific warning-on-upgrade** assertion that is not yet covered (existing `upgrade_v1_v2.rs` asserts the conflict *blocks*) and cross-references the existing supporting coverage in the test module so the flow is tracked complete. The `--explain` / emitted-bytes assertions read the binary's emitted output verbatim (hardening #4). **Done:** a new/extended e2e test (`crates/cli/tests/`) drives the demote-to-warning upgrade verbatim through the binary and asserts exit 0 with the override-default conflict rendered as a warning and zero blocking findings; gate green. Files: `crates/cli/tests/severity_tuning.rs` or a new `crates/cli/tests/flow8_override_default_warning.rs` (reusing the v1→v2 + `jigc config` idioms).

No new fork surfaced: the increment is fully covered by the milestone's settled decisions (2026-06-04 planning entry — minimal `Probe` seam fit to `override-default`'s `(deltas, pack)` shape, *not* one trait over both ctx shapes; `override-default` the seam's only real consumer; `warning` a live third tier surfaced-but-non-blocking; acceptance = flow 8 with `override-default` as the headline conflict→warning demote) and the locked [validation.md](design/validation.md) / [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md). All three *Grouped scope* bullets map to a task (bullet 1 → T1, with its already-delivered sub-items — `Resolved`-in-upgrade and the no-delta byte-identical golden — recorded above as verified-done, not re-built; bullet 2 → T2; bullet 3 → T3). The verified-done items are the load-bearing correction this plan makes: the executor must **prove the seam preserves the already-wired post-pass tuning** (T1 red step) and **not re-implement severity assignment inside `classify`** nor re-add the upgrade byte-identical golden — both already exist from inc-1.

## 2026-06-04 — M6 inc-3 T1 — the non-task `Probe` seam shipped; `override-default` routed through it

`probe.rs` (a 6-line doc-only stub) now exposes the minimal **non-task `Probe` seam**: a `Probe` trait `check(OverrideCtx) -> Vec<Finding>` over `OverrideCtx { deltas, pack }` (the `(recorded deltas, pack)` shape), plus `OverrideDefaultProbe` delegating to the M5 `classify`. **Two ctx shapes, not one trait over both** — the seam admits only the non-task shape; `validate_task`'s task-scoped probes are untouched (a future store-scoped probe extends the seam when a real consumer exists). `upgrade_with_pack` (`upgrade.rs`) now invokes `OverrideDefaultProbe.check(OverrideCtx { … })` in place of the bespoke direct `classify(…)` call. **Severity stays at the inc-1 post-pass** (`ValidationReport::new`); the seam carries no `Resolved` and re-implements no assignment, so the already-wired tuning is unchanged — proven by the new `upgrade.rs` test: a recorded `validation.override-default.target-unchanged.severity warning` demotes a fork conflict to `Severity::Warning` *through the seam path*, while the omitting-target sibling (no severity override) keeps it blocking (hardening #5). The no-delta byte-identical golden stays green unchanged.

## 2026-06-04 — M6 (severity tuning & demotion-lock) shipped: milestone-completion audit triage

M6 built end-to-end via the `milestone-build` harness — a **21-agent run, no build halts, 0 fix rounds** across all three increments (the cleanest run yet; M4 had several fix rounds + 1 HIGH, M5 0 rounds + 2 LOW). Severity is now a cascade setting end-to-end: an engine-owned post-pass assigns severity over aggregated findings (override-only-on-explicit-delta → no-override byte-identical), the full 18-key per-check knob surface ships with the 11 intrinsic checks floored, a below-floor `scalar-set` is soft-rejected at resolution (logged on `--explain`, never aborts), and the M5 `override-default` probe is cascade-tuned through the new non-task `Probe` seam (flow 8: a conflict demoted `blocking → warning` makes `jigc upgrade` warn instead of block, exit 0).

**The harness bet held — and fired visibly.** Hardening #5/#6 caught a real completeness gap *inside* increment 2: T5's e2e found the demotion-lock floors built and unit-tested but **unwired to the two production resolve sites** (`resolve_layers` / `resolve_severity_cascade_resilient` built `PackDefaultLayer` floor-less), so intrinsic demotions applied end-to-end — the exact M5-class "planned-but-unwired" shape, but caught at the per-increment gate (the emitted-bytes e2e), fixed before the increment validated. So the milestone-completion audit found **zero completeness shortfalls** — only 2 LOW code-review notes + 1 advisory, all triaged and remediated:
- **(FIXED `8f58708`)** deduped `result::parse_severity` into the public `Severity::from_token` (drift hazard if a 4th tier is added).
- **(FIXED `771e161`, human chose "harden")** the demotion-lock rested on a two-guard composition (declared⇒floored + undeclared⇒hard-abort); now every engine-known intrinsic check must also be *declared* (`KnobError::UndeclaredIntrinsic`), so the floor itself locks the boundary rather than the closed-surface rule. Consequence surfaced mid-fix and re-confirmed with the human: every pack (incl. test fixtures) must now declare the full 11-intrinsic surface — minimal fixtures gain it via a DRY helper generated from `INTRINSIC_CHECK_KEYS`.
- **(FIXED `39a91bb`)** corrected the stale inventory tally (prose said 17; the table + `knobs.yaml` ship 18 / 11 intrinsic).

The design-review byte-identity hardening (B1/B2) held: the build delivered no-delta goldens over compose/validate/finalize/upgrade and hit no severity-regression the design didn't pre-empt. M6 ships: *severity is a cascade setting, never a code change* — proven through the real binary. The recorded-delta + validation substrate (M4 apply · M5 reconcile · M6 tune) is complete; next is M7 (milestone execution / fan-out-join).

## 2026-06-04 — Parked: self-hosting (distill the harness into a jigc pack, dogfood on a fresh project)

After the M6 retro, parked the project's designed terminus as an idea: once the roadmap is built, **distill the accumulated harness** (decisions + the hand-run planning/increment/dev/completion workflows + their folded-in learnings) into a **jigc methodology pack**, **bootstrap a new project with jigc driving it**, measure against the hand-run build-health baseline (M1–M6: halts · fix-rounds · audit-findings), and improve. The workflow docs already frame the hand-run loops as "the dogfood for the product workflow we will eventually compose," so this names the finish line. Key framing: encoding-into-jigc is a *forcing function* that sorts each harness lesson into **mechanizable** (becomes a step/probe — now enforced) vs **irreducible judgment** (stays agent-authored — the determinism boundary), and the dogfood audits which of our habits are real structure vs tribal. Honest caveat carried in the idea: all our hardening to date is **reactive** (every check is a post-mortem), so the distill pass must include a *forward-looking* adversarial review of the process itself. Exploratory, not locked; presupposes M7/M8 + workflow-as-artifact machinery. Full shape in [ideas/self-hosting.md](ideas/self-hosting.md); indexed from [VISION.md](VISION.md#open-questions).

## 2026-06-04 — M7 (milestone execution) planning: split into core + control-plane, settled design

Planned via [`/milestone-plan`](.claude/commands/milestone-plan.md). Three `capability-auditor`s established the baseline (the substrate is solid; the fan-out/join machinery is **entirely greenfield** — no step-kind concept, no `milestone` root, no multi-area merge; edge-index "site 4" is the only unbuilt site; the spawn binding is absent on **both** seam sides). Four `gap-detector`s + an independent process-review converged on one keystone gap and a clean split.

- **The keystone gap — the fan-out list-source has no entity behind it.** Every design example uses `{{milestone.tasks}}`, but the binary *rejects* `milestone` as an undeclared root, `milestone` is not a doctype, and nothing feeds a task collection. The fork (mint a milestone work-unit / fan out over a spec's `criteria` / human-supplied list) gates the whole milestone.
- **Settled — mint a `milestone` work-unit** (the design-faithful path: `jigc milestone create` is already the named future mint site, `milestone.tasks` the named live-state root, in [structural-grammar.md](design/structural-grammar.md#work-units-and-runtime-identity)). Engine-native state, **not** a new doctype. Hierarchy stays **`milestone > task`** for M7 — `increment` minting stays deferred (the increment-vs-task fan-out boundary remains a post-MVP question). Task list populated **both** ways: `jigc milestone add-task <intent>` (incremental) **and** a spec-seed path (one task per repeatable `criterion`, reusing M3 bind/read) — the human wanted both.
- **Settled — SPLIT M7 along the determinism boundary** (CLAUDE.md: "coordination is a control plane; the CLI is the data plane"). The combined milestone was ~2× a normal one (work-unit minting *and* the concurrency primitive). Split:
  - **M7 · the deterministic join core (data plane)** — milestone work-unit (`milestone > task`) + its task-list verbs; **isolated sub-task area dirs** (structural) + a **join-time isolation check**; parallel mint + `-2`/`-3` collision-suffix + intra-doc self-ref rewrite; the by-task-id join merge (edge-index site 4); cross-area ref rejection; disjoint-union merge with same-doc clash = blocking. Proven by **permutation tests over real multi-area fixtures** (feed sub-areas in scrambled orders → byte-identical committed output) — *not* a real agent spawn. This dodges the hollow-spawn trap and proves determinism more rigorously than a flaky launch would.
  - **M8 (new) · spawn binding + acceptance (control plane)** — fan-out/join *step kinds* + the spawn-instruction emit class; the **`{{milestone.tasks}}` data-value root** (the read-path placeholder the fan-out step consumes); the adapter spawn binding + the new `jigc workflow W --task <id>` sub-agent re-entry + the **write-time `--task`-scoped barrier**; the `milestone-execution` workflow; the genuine real-Task-tool end-to-end (flow 9 driven by real spawn). Old **M8 project setup → M9** (follows the M4/M5 split precedent).

- **Pre-decompose design review (independent, code-grounded) — bake-back.** The reviewer caught two blocking flaws + refinements, all accepted:
  - **(B1) The clash discriminator was undecidable from recorded state** — the engine couldn't tell "two sub-tasks edited the *same existing* doc" (block) from "two each *created* a new doc that slugs alike" (suffix). Fix: `jigc milestone create` pins **one shared base** all sub-tasks inherit, and each staged doc records **`created` vs `edited-from-base`** provenance. Rule: **block** iff two sub-areas stage `edited-from-base` writes to the same committed-at-base slug; **suffix** iff two stage `created` writes to the same slug; the **mixed case** (one created, one edited, same slug) is itself **blocking**.
  - **(B2) The write barrier had no M7 mechanism** ("which sub-task am I" comes from the M8 `--task` re-entry; today's write path rejects when >1 task area exists). Fix: M7's isolation = **separate area dirs + a join-time check** that each staged doc belongs to its own sub-area; the **write-time `--task`-scoped barrier moves to M8** with the real sub-agent write path.
  - **(I2) `{{milestone.tasks}}` had no M7 consumer** (the fan-out *step* that reads it is M8; M7's join reads the task list as **engine state** directly). Fix: the **data-value root moves to M8**; M7 builds the work-unit + verbs + join only.
  - Minor bakes: the cross-area check runs **per sub-area** (`committed ∪ that one area`, reusing `ref_resolves` once per area); `add-from-spec` enumerates criteria via the **parse-items** path (not the M3 bind/read slice) and blocks on a zero-criteria spec; `finalize` always consumes a **post-join, suffix-resolved** overlay; #7's HashMap rule targets **unsorted** iteration reaching output, not the mere presence of a hash container.

## 2026-06-04 — M7 decomposition: 4 risk-first increments

Cut M7 (the deterministic join core) into four ordered, linear increments ([roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → Milestone 7 decomposition): **(1)** the `milestone` work-unit + `create`/`add-task` (the substrate spine — N isolated areas under one shared base, deterministically-ordered task list); **(2)** stage-time `created`/`edited-from-base` provenance + `add-from-spec` (the join's input substrate — the B1 discriminator + the spec populator, before the join consumes them); **(3)** the by-task-id join merge (the **#1 risk** in isolation — disjoint union, provenance-clash, collision-suffix + self-ref rewrite, per-sub-area cross-area rejection, join-time isolation check, **proven order-invariant by permutation** per hardening #7); **(4)** milestone finalize + the flow-9 genuine permutation acceptance through the binary. Risk-first: substrate → discriminator substrate → the join risk → acceptance. The hardening-#7 obligations (≥3 divergent orders incl. reverse; forced overlap; `read_dir`-order ≠ id-order fixtures; no unsorted hash iteration to output) are baked into increments 1/3/4's acceptance. **Ready for the [milestone-build](implementation/increment-workflow.md) harness.**
- **Settled — join semantics = disjoint union, block on clash.** Partitions are disjoint by construction (storage.md), so the join is a clean by-task-id union; a same-doc/same-id collision at the join is a **blocking integrity finding**, not a section-merge. Rejected section-level union as solving a problem the disjoint-partition model treats as an error — keeps the merge order-invariant and cheap to prove.
- **Settled — cross-area ref rejection is a *narrowing*, not a clone.** There is no built "cross-task rule" to mirror (it was incidental dangling-detection). At the join, each sub-task's outgoing refs resolve against `committed ∪ that sub-task's OWN area`; a ref resolving *only* in a sibling area is **blocking**. Critical: today's `ref_resolves` keys on filesystem-path existence, so a naïve multi-area union would silently resolve cross-area refs (the bug to prevent) — surface-b must be scoped per-`from` to the authoring area. Intrinsic + floored (M6 machinery).
- **Settled — the `jigc workflow` re-entry command** (human chose to add it over reusing `jigc start --task`) lands in **M8**, not M7 — it is control-plane (sub-agent launch). The design docs that already name `jigc workflow … --task` are correct-but-unbuilt-until-M8, not wrong.
- **Deferred (logged, not forgotten):** fan-out progress/resumption (a partial fan-out restarts from scratch in M7/M8; no mid-fan-out resume policy); the squash-vs-per-sub-task-commit cascade knob (declare when the commit boundary is built); `increment` work-unit minting.
- **Process hedge:** ran the forward-looking adversarial review of the harness itself ([ideas/self-hosting.md](ideas/self-hosting.md) graduation-pass part 4), scoped to "what concurrency-class defect could our green gates still pass?" — its findings + the surfaced traps (non-deterministic join masked by an id-ordered test; isolation leak with no overlapping state; HashMap nondeterminism invisible to a 1-element golden; order-dependent finding aggregation; self-ref-rewrite masked by refs-to-others) feed the increments' acceptance criteria + the increment-validator checklist.

## 2026-06-04 — M7 Increment 1 planning: decomposition

Increment 1 — the `milestone` work-unit + `create` / `add-task` (the substrate spine) — cut into 4 ordered, single-concern tasks ([roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-1--the-milestone-work-unit--create--add-task-the-substrate-spine); design [structural-grammar.md](design/structural-grammar.md#work-units-and-runtime-identity) → Work-units and runtime identity / Minting sites, [storage.md](design/storage.md#the-by-task-id-join-m7) → The by-task-id join / The per-task working area, [write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md) → Minting a milestone). The substrate is **entirely greenfield** in code — `milestone` has no engine state, no module, no command (the only `milestone` mentions are tests asserting it is *rejected* as a data-value root, which stays true: the `{{milestone.tasks}}` read-path root is M8). The cut mirrors the proven task-minting substrate in `engine::state` (`mint_task` → slug-from-source + `BasePin` + serial-collision reject; intent/workflow/base persisted as per-task working-area files; sort-after-`read_dir` already the house discipline at `task.rs::staged_docs`). Serial spine: the milestone container + its shared-base pin first, then the sub-task mint that inherits that base + appends, then the deterministic sorted-by-id enumeration the join will consume, then the CLI surface that drives all three through the binary. *Why this order:* `add_task` (T2) cannot pin a sub-task to "the milestone's base" until `create` (T1) records it; the ordering property (T3) is a property *of* the list `add_task` writes; the CLI (T4) wires HEAD + the gitignore + dispatch over the engine primitives T1–T3 prove.

- **T1 — the `milestone` work-unit: engine state, mint site, shared base pinned at create (no sub-task yet).** A new `engine::milestone` module: `mint_milestone(jigc_root, title, base) -> Result<MintedMilestone, Finding>` slugs the title into a frozen id (empty → a `milestone` type-name fallback, the same `mint_id` discipline as `state::mint_task`), opens the milestone's gitignored area under `.jigc/milestones/<id>/`, writes the **single shared `BasePin`** (= HEAD, supplied by the caller — engine does no git I/O) and an **empty task list** as on-disk state, and **rejects** a serial collision (an active milestone dir of that id already exists) with a route-bearing blocking `milestone.serial-collision` finding, creating nothing new. Address is `milestone:<slug>` (no fragment, per structural-grammar Work-units). The on-disk state shape (e.g. `base.json` + a `tasks` list file/field) is a doc-elaboration pin the executor makes, golden-locked, mirroring `base.json`'s frozen byte form. **Done:** an engine unit test (temp root) asserts `jigc milestone create "Cache rework"` shape mints `milestone:cache-rework` with a `BasePin` round-tripping the supplied SHA and a task list reading back **empty**, and a second mint of the same slug returns a blocking route-bearing `milestone.serial-collision` finding creating no second dir (golden over the frozen on-disk bytes); gate green. Files: `crates/engine/src/milestone.rs`, `crates/engine/src/lib.rs`.
- **T2 — `add_task`: mint a sub-task pinned to the milestone base, isolated `tasks/<sub>/` area, append to the task list; serial collision within the milestone rejects.** `engine::milestone::add_task(jigc_root, milestone_id, intent) -> Result<…, Finding>`: load the milestone's shared `BasePin`, mint a sub-task work-unit (id = slug from intent, the `mint_task` fallback discipline) **pinned to the milestone's base, not HEAD** — reusing `state::mint_task` so the sub-task opens its isolated `.jigc/tasks/<sub>/` area with the inherited base/intent/workflow files — and **append** the sub-task id to the milestone's task list. A serial slug collision **within this milestone** (the intent slugs to an id already in this milestone's list) **rejects** with a route-bearing blocking finding (no suffix — the deterministic `-2` suffix runs only at the join, never at incremental add, per write-commands → Minting a milestone). A nonexistent milestone id rejects. **Done:** an engine unit test adds two distinct sub-tasks to a created milestone and asserts each opens an isolated `tasks/<sub>/` area whose `base.json` equals the **milestone's** base (not a fresh HEAD) and that both ids appear in the milestone's task list; a third `add_task` whose intent slugs to an already-listed id returns a blocking route-bearing collision finding and appends nothing; an `add_task` against an unknown milestone id rejects; gate green. Files: `crates/engine/src/milestone.rs`.
- **T3 — the task list as deterministically sorted-by-sub-task-id engine state (Validation hardening #7).** The milestone's task-list **enumeration** (the read the join will consume) returns sub-task ids **sorted by id**, never `read_dir`/insertion/completion order. Concretely: enumeration sorts by the stable sub-task id at the boundary (a sorted `Vec`/`BTreeSet` keyed by id), so neither on-disk write order nor filesystem `read_dir` order can leak into the enumerated output. **This is a distinct concern from T2's append** — T2 proves the list *records* both ids; T3 proves the list *enumerates* them in a canonical order regardless of how they were added. **Done (hardening #7 — the `read_dir`-order ≠ id-order assertion):** an engine unit test adds sub-tasks whose **insertion order differs from their id-sort order** (e.g. add intent "Zebra fix" before "Alpha fix" so insertion order is `[zebra-fix, alpha-fix]` but id order is `[alpha-fix, zebra-fix]`) and asserts the enumeration returns the **id-sorted** order, not insertion order; if the area is enumerated from `read_dir`, the fixture is constructed so the filesystem order also differs from id order, and the test asserts id order regardless; gate green. Files: `crates/engine/src/milestone.rs`.
- **T4 — `jigc milestone create` + `jigc milestone add-task` (clap + dispatch) — through the binary.** Add a `Command::Milestone { verb }` with a `MilestoneCommand` subcommand enum (`Create { title }`, `AddTask { milestone_id, intent }`) to `cli.rs`, dispatched (the `TaskCommand`/`ConfigCommand` pattern) to a new `crate::milestone` CLI module: discover the repo root + read HEAD as the `BasePin` (reuse the `start.rs` `read_head`/`discover_repo_root` idiom), then call the engine `mint_milestone` / `add_task`; a blocking engine finding surfaces on stderr with its route and exits non-zero, success prints a summary and exits 0. Ensure `.jigc/.gitignore` carries `milestones/` (the milestone area is gitignored runtime state like `tasks/` — extend `ensure_jigc_gitignore`'s set, a doc-elaboration pin). **Done (through the real binary):** a CLI integration test in a throwaway git repo runs `jigc milestone create "Cache rework"` (asserts exit 0 + the minted `milestone:cache-rework` in the output), then `jigc milestone add-task cache-rework "Zebra fix"` and `… "Alpha fix"`, then asserts (by re-running an enumeration surface or inspecting the on-disk list) the task list is the **id-sorted** `[alpha-fix, zebra-fix]`, that each sub-task area is pinned to the milestone's base, that a duplicate `add-task` intent rejects with a non-zero exit + routed finding, and that `.jigc/milestones/` is gitignored; clap parse tests cover the two verbs + arg shapes; gate green. Files: `crates/cli/src/cli.rs`, `crates/cli/src/milestone.rs`, `crates/cli/src/main.rs`, `crates/cli/src/task.rs` (gitignore set), `crates/cli/tests/milestone.rs`.

No new fork surfaced: the increment is fully covered by the milestone's settled decisions (2026-06-04 M7 planning entry — `milestone > task` for M7 with `increment` minting deferred; **one shared base pinned at `create`**, every sub-task inheriting it; serial slug collision within a milestone **rejects**, the `-2` suffix reserved for the join; the task list as deterministically-ordered engine state) and the locked [structural-grammar.md](design/structural-grammar.md) / [storage.md](design/storage.md) / [write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md). All three *Grouped scope* bullets map to a task (bullet 1 *the milestone work-unit / mint site / shared base* → T1, with its CLI surface in T4; bullet 2 *the two verbs + add-task semantics + within-milestone collision-reject* → T2 for the engine semantics + T4 for the clap/dispatch surface; bullet 3 *the deterministically-ordered task list + the `read_dir`≠id-order test* → T3, exercised end-to-end in T4). The join merge, stage-time provenance, `add-from-spec`, and milestone finalize stay out — they are increments 2–4, not narrowed here. Doc-elaboration pins the executor makes (not forks): (i) the on-disk milestone-state shape under `.jigc/milestones/<id>/` (a frozen `base.json` + a task-list representation), golden-locked, mirroring the per-task `base.json` byte form; (ii) sub-task areas live in the **shared `.jigc/tasks/<sub>/`** namespace (storage.md → "their own `tasks/<sub-task-id>/` area"), reusing `state::mint_task`, so a slug colliding with a *non-milestone* task is the existing `task.serial-collision` reject — within-milestone collision detection keys on the milestone's own task list; (iii) `milestones/` added to the `.jigc/.gitignore` set as gitignored runtime state.

T1 landed (2026-06-04): new `engine::milestone` module — `mint_milestone(jigc_root, title, base) -> Result<MintedMilestone, Finding>` mirroring `state::mint_task`. On-disk shape pin: `.jigc/milestones/<id>/base.json` (the single shared `BasePin`, frozen byte form identical to a task's `base.json`) + `tasks.json` (a `TaskList { tasks: Vec<String> }`, written **empty** at mint; pretty-JSON + trailing newline, golden-locked). Serial collision = blocking route-bearing `milestone.serial-collision`, nothing created; I/O failure = blocking `milestone.area-io`. `read_task_list` / `read_base_pin` are the resume-path readers. T2 will append to `tasks.json` + reuse `mint_task` for sub-tasks; T3 owns the *enumeration*-is-id-sorted property (T1's empty list makes ordering trivial).

T3 landed (2026-06-04): `TaskList::enumerate(&self) -> Vec<String>` returns the sub-task ids **sorted by id** (the order the by-task-id join consumes); `TaskList::tasks` keeps insertion order as the recorded audit trail (enumeration is sorted at the boundary, so neither insertion nor `read_dir` order leaks into output — hardening #7). Test enumerates the same two ids under **divergent insertion orders incl. the reverse** and asserts byte-identical id-sorted output, with a fixture-shape check that the on-disk sub-task areas' raw `read_dir` order diverges from id order in at least one run.

T4 landed (2026-06-04): the `jigc milestone <verb>` front door — `Command::Milestone { verb: MilestoneCommand { Create { title }, AddTask { milestone_id, intent } } }` (clap + dispatch in new `crate::milestone`, the TaskCommand/ConfigCommand pattern). `create` reads HEAD as the shared `BasePin` (the `start.rs` `read_head` idiom) and calls `mint_milestone`; `add-task` calls the T2 `add_task` primitive. Blocking findings (serial collision, unknown milestone, within-milestone collision) → stderr with route + non-zero exit via `finding_to_err`; success → a summary line + `render::milestone`. CLI-integration test through the built binary covers the full done-criterion (create → two adds in non-id order → id-sorted enumeration → duplicate-add reject → `git check-ignore` on the milestone area). Doc-elaboration pins: (i) `milestones/` added to the `.jigc/.gitignore` set, ensured at `create` (idempotent, amends an adapter-written `.gitignore` predating `milestones/`); (ii) a milestone sub-task is minted from the **`single-task`** work-workflow — the cascade `default-workflow` is the `creates-task: false` router (not a work-workflow), and the per-sub-task execution-workflow choice is an M8 control-plane concern, so T4 records the canonical work-workflow at mint.

## 2026-06-04 — M7 Increment 2 planning: decomposition

Increment 2 — stage-time provenance + `add-from-spec` (the join's input substrate) — cut into 3 ordered, single-concern tasks ([roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-2--stage-time-provenance--add-from-spec-the-joins-input-substrate); design [storage.md](design/storage.md#the-by-task-id-join-m7) → The by-task-id join / classification by provenance, [write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md) → Minting a milestone, [structural-grammar.md](design/structural-grammar.md#ids-provenance-and-minting) → IDs: provenance and minting). Grounded in the code: staged docs are today **bare `.md` files** under a task's `docs/` with **no provenance representation**; the only two staging entry points are `state::provision_doc` (called by `create_gated` + `provision_commit_doc`) and `state::copy_in` (verified to have **zero production callers** today — only tested — so the `edited-from-base` path is recorded at the primitive but not yet reached by any live edit command, which is correct: M8 wires the real sub-agent edit path, and Increment 3's join consumes the bit over fixtures). `add-from-spec` reuses the proven parse-items read path (`store::canonical_path` → `parse::parse_sections` → the `criteria` `ParsedSection.items` → each `ParsedItem.title`, the `id-from: title` criterion text) and the proven `milestone::add_task` sub-task mint — no new minting discipline. Serial spine: record the provenance bit at the two staging primitives first (an isolated engine concern the join reads), then the engine spec-seed primitive that mints one sub-task per criterion, then the CLI verb that drives it through the binary. *Why this order:* T1 (provenance) is independent of seeding — it touches the staging path the join reads, provable in isolation; T3's binary verb cannot exist before T2's engine primitive it dispatches to; the zero-criteria block is provable at the engine layer (T2) and re-exercised through the binary (T3).

- **T1 — record `created` vs `edited-from-base` provenance at the two staging primitives, in the staged-doc on-disk representation the join reads.** Add the provenance discriminator (`provision_doc` → **`created`**, `copy_in` → **`edited-from-base`**, measured against the milestone's shared base) to the staged-doc on-disk representation under a task's `docs/` ([storage.md](design/storage.md#the-by-task-id-join-m7) → classification by provenance). The on-disk form of the bit is a doc-elaboration pin the executor makes (e.g. a per-doc provenance sidecar or a manifest under `docs/`), golden-locked, written atomically by the two staging fns alongside the `.md` body, with a reader the Increment-3 join will consume — chosen so the bare `.md` body bytes the writer/round-trip own stay byte-for-byte unchanged (the bit rides beside the doc, not in it). Both staging primitives carry the bit; the live `create_gated`/`provision_commit_doc` callers thread `created` through unchanged. **Done:** an engine unit test stages one `created` doc (via `provision_doc`) and one `edited-from-base` doc (via `copy_in`) into a single task `docs/` area and asserts the recorded representation reports **distinct** provenance for the two slugs (golden over the frozen on-disk bytes of the provenance record), and that the doc `.md` bodies are byte-identical to the pre-change staging output (the writer is untouched); gate green. Files: `crates/engine/src/state.rs`.
- **T2 — `milestone::add_from_spec`: enumerate a committed spec's `criteria` via parse-items, mint one sub-task per criterion, zero-criteria blocks.** A new engine primitive `add_from_spec(jigc_root, repo_root, milestone_id, spec_addr, schemas, workflow_id) -> Result<…, Finding>`: resolve the spec address to its committed path (`store::canonical_path`), read + `parse::parse_sections` against the spec schema, take the `criteria` repeatable section's `items`, and for each item mint a sub-task (reusing `add_task`) with the **criterion text** (`ParsedItem.title`) as the intent — appended in physical item order to the milestone's task list (enumeration stays id-sorted per Increment 1). A spec with **zero `criteria` items** is a **blocking** route-bearing `milestone.no-criteria` ("nothing to seed from") finding, minting nothing; an unknown milestone / unknown spec / unparseable spec each reject with a routed blocking finding (reusing the existing milestone + store finding shapes). The parse-items path is the read path explicitly mandated over the `jigc task bind` slice ([write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md) → Minting a milestone). **Done:** an engine unit test over a **3-criteria** committed-spec fixture asserts `add_from_spec` mints **exactly 3** sub-tasks, each pinned to the milestone's shared base in its own isolated `tasks/<sub>/` area, with the 3 sub-task ids in the milestone's task list; a separate **zero-criteria** spec fixture returns the blocking `milestone.no-criteria` finding (route present) and mints nothing; gate green. Files: `crates/engine/src/milestone.rs`.
- **T3 — `jigc milestone add-from-spec <milestone-id> <spec-addr>` (clap + dispatch) — through the binary.** Add an `AddFromSpec { milestone_id, spec_addr }` variant to the existing `MilestoneCommand` enum + a `run_add_from_spec` dispatch arm that discovers the repo root, loads the shipped schemas (the `TaskArea::schemas` idiom), parses the spec address, and calls the T2 `add_from_spec`; a blocking engine finding surfaces on stderr with its route + non-zero exit (the `finding_to_err` envelope), success prints a summary naming the N seeded sub-tasks and exits 0. **Done (through the real binary):** a CLI integration test in a throwaway git repo that commits a 3-criteria spec, runs `jigc milestone create` then `jigc milestone add-from-spec <id> spec:<slug>`, asserts exit 0 + a summary naming 3 seeded sub-tasks, and `jigc milestone list-tasks <id>` reports the 3 id-sorted sub-task ids; a second run over a committed zero-criteria spec asserts a **non-zero exit** + the routed `milestone.no-criteria` message and an unchanged (empty) task list; a clap parse test covers the new verb's arg shape; gate green. Files: `crates/cli/src/milestone.rs`, `crates/cli/tests/milestone.rs`.

No new fork surfaced: the increment is fully covered by the milestone's settled decisions (2026-06-04 M7 planning entry + B1 bake-back — stage-time `created` vs `edited-from-base` provenance is the join's clash discriminator, recorded *before* the join depends on it; `add-from-spec` enumerates criteria via the **parse-items** path, *not* the M3 bind/read slice, and **blocks on a zero-criteria spec**; one shared base every sub-task inherits) and the locked [storage.md](design/storage.md) / [write-commands.md](design/write-commands.md) / [structural-grammar.md](design/structural-grammar.md). All three *Grouped scope* bullets map to a task: bullet 1 *the provenance bit at `provision_doc`/`copy_in` in the staged-doc representation* → T1; bullet 2 *`add-from-spec` enumerate-criteria + zero-criteria block* → T2 (engine semantics) + T3 (the clap/dispatch surface through the binary); bullet 3 *tests* → the done-criteria of T1 (distinct-provenance), T2 (3-criteria→3 sub-tasks + zero-criteria block at the engine), and T3 (the same through the binary). The by-task-id join merge itself — which *consumes* the provenance bit — stays out: it is Increment 3, not narrowed here. Doc-elaboration pins the executor makes (not forks): (i) the on-disk form of the provenance bit (a per-doc sidecar or a `docs/` manifest), golden-locked, written so the `.md` body bytes the round-trip writer owns stay byte-for-byte unchanged; (ii) `add_from_spec` reuses `store::canonical_path` + `parse::parse_sections` + `add_task` unchanged, so it introduces no second spec-read or minting discipline; (iii) the criterion *text* seeded as a sub-task intent is the item's `title` (the `id-from: title` id-source), the same value the sub-task slug is minted from.

T1 landed (2026-06-04): the stage-time provenance bit lands as a per-`docs/`-area **manifest** (chosen over a per-doc sidecar — one record naturally golden-locks "distinct provenance for the two slugs" and keeps `docs/` to one extra file). New `engine::state` surface: a kebab-case `Provenance { Created, EditedFromBase }` enum + a `ProvenanceRecord { docs: BTreeMap<String, Provenance> }` (address-sorted → deterministic bytes, the `RolesRecord`/`base.json` convention) with `path_in`/`record`/`get`/`to_bytes`/`load` (absent-is-empty, never an error) — the reader Increment 3's join consumes. On-disk shape pin: `.jigc/tasks/<id>/docs/provenance.json`, pretty-JSON + trailing newline, golden `engine__state__tests__provenance_two_staged_docs.snap`. `provision_doc` records `created`, `copy_in` records `edited-from-base`, each via a shared `record_provenance` helper that loads-merges-writes the manifest **atomically after** the `.md` body lands (`write_atomic`), so the body bytes the round-trip writer owns stay byte-for-byte unchanged (asserted in the done-criterion test). Signatures unchanged — the kind is fixed per primitive (the discriminator mapping), not a parameter, so the live `create_gated`/`create`/`provision_commit_doc` callers thread `created` through with no edit (`provision_commit_doc` stages via `state::persist`, not `provision_doc`, so it is untouched by this task — its `created` provenance, when the milestone edit path needs it, is M8's concern). No new fork: within the planning pin's doc-elaboration latitude.

T2 landed (2026-06-04): `milestone::add_from_spec(jigc_root, repo_root, schemas, milestone_id, spec_addr, workflow_id) -> Result<Vec<AddedTask>, Finding>` seeds the task list from a committed spec. Order: unknown-milestone reject (reuses `add_task`'s `milestone.unknown` block) **before** any spec read or mint → read the spec via the **parse-items** path (`Address::parse` → schema lookup → `store::canonical_path` → `parse::parse_sections` → the `criteria` `ParsedSection.items` → each `ParsedItem.title`) → zero items ⇒ blocking route-bearing `milestone.no-criteria` ("nothing to seed from"), minting nothing → else one `add_task` per criterion in **physical item order** (recorded audit trail; enumeration stays id-sorted per Increment 1), the criterion **title** as the sub-task intent (the `id-from: title` value the slug mints from). Spec-read failures reuse the **store finding shapes** (`store.unknown-type`/`store.transient-type`/`store.not-found`/`store.unparseable`/`store.no-such-section`) via a local `store_block` helper rather than `store::read_slice` — `read_slice` requires a `#fragment` and *renders* the items (the bind slice path the design explicitly excludes), so add-from-spec reads `ParsedItem.title`s directly. Doc-elaboration pins (not forks): (i) arg order is `(jigc_root, repo_root, schemas, …)` — `schemas` ahead of the ids, vs the planning pin's `(…, spec_addr, schemas, …)`, a cosmetic ordering choice; (ii) the criteria section is located by the fixed id `criteria` (the shipped `spec.yaml` section id, matching `store.rs`'s `#criteria`), so a spec without a `criteria` section is a routed `store.no-such-section` block. No new fork: within the planning pin's doc-elaboration latitude.

## 2026-06-04 — M7 Increment 3 planning: decomposition

Increment 3 — the by-task-id join merge (the **#1 risk**, in isolation) — cut into 5 ordered, single-concern tasks ([roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-3--the-by-task-id-join-merge-the-1-risk-in-isolation); design [storage.md](design/storage.md#the-by-task-id-join-m7) → The by-task-id join (the 5-step algorithm), [structural-grammar.md](design/structural-grammar.md#ids-provenance-and-minting) → IDs: provenance and minting (the collision suffix), [validation.md](design/validation.md) → Fan-out cross-area refs (the per-sub-area narrowing), acceptance = [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow 9). Grounded in the code: the substrate Increments 1–2 built is in hand — `milestone::read_task_list` + `TaskList::enumerate` give the **id-sorted** sub-task ids (the order to fold by; never `read_dir`/completion), each sub-task's isolated `tasks/<sub>/` area is a normal task working area, and `state::ProvenanceRecord::load(task_dir)` reads the `created` vs `edited-from-base` bit at `docs/provenance.json`. The per-area basis to **reuse unchanged** is `index::overlay_working(committed, task_dir, schemas)` (parses an area's `docs/*.md` → its forward edges + `from` identities) and `index::ref_resolves(overlay, repo_root, task_dir, schemas)` (the single-area forward-ref walk over `committed ∪ that one task_dir`). **Verified-fact corrections to the roadmap's loose phrasing the executor must NOT trust blindly** (each turned into a task red step): (a) the **cross-area check needs NO new check id** — validation.md → Fan-out cross-area refs (the authoritative spec) says the join "invokes the existing single-area forward-ref walk **once per sub-area**… stays authoritative and unchanged," so a cross-area ref emits the existing **`schema-conformance.ref-resolves`** finding (already an inventory row + `INTRINSIC_CHECK_KEYS` member + `knobs.yaml` floor — no edits to any of those three); (b) the join's two **net-new** emissions (`join.same-doc-clash`, the isolation finding) are **blocking-by-construction, NOT `knobs.yaml`/inventory rows** — the verified `reconciliation.*` precedent (`reconciliation.conflict-block`/`reconciliation.rename` emit `Severity::Blocking` directly with **no** `knobs.yaml` key, exempt from the M6 post-pass, validation.md → "Blocking-but-untunable outcomes… No row, stay blocking"); the roadmap's "intrinsic, floored" is loose phrasing satisfied by construction here, **not** an instruction to extend `INTRINSIC_CHECK_KEYS` (which would force a `knobs.yaml` declaration the inventory — the single source of truth — has no row for, the exact M3-class unsatisfiable-pin trap). **This increment is engine-genuine and is NOT wired to finalize/commit** (Increment 4); the `jigc milestone join` verb reports the join outcome + findings and commits nothing. Serial spine: the deterministic enumerate+disjoint-union fold first (the merge skeleton, no contention), then the provenance clash/suffix/self-ref discrimination over it, then the per-sub-area cross-area + isolation validation, then the CLI surface through the binary, then the permutation determinism acceptance (hardening #7). *Why this order:* T2's clash/suffix logic operates on the merged accumulator T1 builds; T3's per-area validation is a distinct concern from the merge itself; the CLI verb (T4) wires HEAD/discovery/render over the engine join T1–T3 prove; the permutation acceptance (T5) is the milestone's #1-risk **Proves** and must run over the *full* overlapping fixture all prior tasks established, so it lands last.

- **T1 — the join skeleton: enumerate sub-areas by sorted task id + disjoint-union fold of staged docs into the merged overlay, classified by provenance (no contention yet).** A new engine entry `milestone::join(jigc_root, repo_root, milestone_id, schemas, …) -> Result<JoinOutcome, Finding>` (the result shape is a doc-elaboration pin): load the milestone, **enumerate its sub-task ids via `TaskList::enumerate` (id-sorted)**, and for each sub-area in that order parse its staged docs (reuse `index::overlay_working` as the per-area basis) + read its `ProvenanceRecord`, folding the staged docs into a **merged parent overlay** whose every accumulator (staged-doc set, edge overlay, finding list) is an **order-keyed container (`BTreeMap`/sorted `Vec` keyed by the stable id), never an unsorted `HashMap` whose iteration could reach output** (hardening #7, storage.md algorithm step 1). With **disjoint** areas (no slug overlap) the fold is a clean union. Clash/suffix (T2) and cross-area/isolation (T3) are stubbed out / absent here — T1 proves only the deterministic enumerate-and-fold. **Done:** an engine unit test folds **≥2 disjoint sub-areas** (distinct slugs, each with a staged doc carrying a known provenance) and asserts the merged overlay contains both docs with their provenance, the merge accumulators are id-keyed (a fold under a reversed enumeration yields the identical merged overlay), and `read_dir` order is shown to diverge from id order in the fixture so a green result is not an accident of filesystem order; gate green. Files: `crates/engine/src/milestone.rs`.
- **T2 — the provenance clash rule (block) vs collision-suffix (distinct `created`) + the self-ref rewrite of the suffixed instance.** Over T1's merged fold, apply storage.md algorithm steps 3–4: (i) **same-doc clash → blocking `join.same-doc-clash`** when two sub-areas stage **`edited-from-base`** writes to the **same committed-at-base slug**, **plus** the **mixed case** (one `created`, one `edited-from-base`, same slug) — both blocking, `Severity::Blocking` emitted directly, route-bearing, no `knobs.yaml` row (the verified `reconciliation.*` precedent); (ii) **colliding `created` instances → deterministic numeric suffix** — two sub-areas each staging a `created` instance whose minted id slugs the same are *distinct docs*: the **lower-task-id** instance keeps the bare slug, each higher takes `-2`/`-3`… **in task-id order** ([structural-grammar.md](design/structural-grammar.md#ids-provenance-and-minting); the numeric-suffix helper is greenfield — none exists today); (iii) the join then **rewrites the suffixed instance's intra-document self-references** (a `ref` field/edge whose `from`==`to`==the doc's own old slug) to the suffixed id in lockstep, so the renamed doc never dangles or points at its sibling. **Done:** an engine unit test over a fixture forcing genuine overlap asserts: two `created` `adr:cache-strategy` instances suffix to `adr:cache-strategy` (lower id) + `adr:cache-strategy-2` (higher id, task-id order), the suffixed instance's **own** self-reference is rewritten to `adr:cache-strategy-2` (the fixture's colliding doc references its *own* slug on purpose, so a broken self-rewrite fails the test — not a refs-to-siblings masking case), and two `edited-from-base` writes to one committed-at-base slug (and the mixed case) each emit a blocking route-bearing `join.same-doc-clash`; gate green. Files: `crates/engine/src/milestone.rs`.
- **T3 — per-sub-area cross-area ref rejection (reusing `ref_resolves` unchanged, once per area) + the join-time isolation check.** (i) **Cross-area refs:** run the **existing single-area `index::ref_resolves` once per sub-area**, passing **that area's own `task_dir`**, so each area's outgoing edges resolve against `committed ∪ that one area` only — **never** a merged multi-area overlay (validation.md → Fan-out cross-area refs: the per-`from` narrowing; the danger is that a naïve union of all sub-areas' `docs/` would resolve a cross-area ref *clean* by mere path-existence). A ref resolving only in a sibling area emits the existing **`schema-conformance.ref-resolves`** finding (no new check id, no inventory/`knobs.yaml`/`INTRINSIC_CHECK_KEYS` edit — verified-fact (a) above). (ii) **Join-time isolation check:** a staged doc not attributable to its own sub-area is a **blocking** finding (the M7 isolation mechanism, the write-time `--task` barrier being M8) — `Severity::Blocking` emitted directly, no `knobs.yaml` row (the `reconciliation.*` precedent). **Done:** an engine unit test asserts (a) a sub-area B authoring `supersedes: adr:lru-eviction` where that slug byte-exists **only** in sibling area A's `docs/` is **blocked** with `schema-conformance.ref-resolves` — and a control assertion shows the same ref would resolve *clean* against a naïve all-areas union (proving the per-area scoping is load-bearing, not incidental); (b) the isolation check fires blocking on a doc not attributable to its own sub-area; gate green. Files: `crates/engine/src/milestone.rs`.
- **T4 — `jigc milestone join <milestone-id>` (clap + dispatch) — through the binary, reporting the join outcome, NOT wired to finalize.** Add a `Join { milestone_id }` variant to the existing `MilestoneCommand` enum + a `run_join` dispatch arm that discovers the repo root, loads the shipped schemas (the `TaskArea::schemas`/`run_add_from_spec` idiom), reads HEAD/committed edge index as the join needs, and calls the T1–T3 engine `join`; the merged outcome (suffix decisions + any blocking findings) renders via `render::milestone`, a blocking finding surfaces on stderr with its route + non-zero exit (`finding_to_err`), a clean join prints a summary and exits 0. **The verb commits nothing and does not call finalize** (Increment 4 wires the post-join overlay into finalize). **Done (through the real binary):** a CLI integration test in a throwaway git repo creates a milestone, populates ≥2 sub-areas as fixtures (a `created` collision with a self-ref + a clean disjoint doc), runs `jigc milestone join <id>`, and asserts exit 0 + a summary naming the `-2` suffix decision (and the self-ref rewrite); a second fixture with a same-doc clash (two `edited-from-base`) asserts a **non-zero exit** + the routed `join.same-doc-clash` message and that **nothing was committed** (no new commit, working tree unchanged); a clap parse test covers the new verb's arg shape; gate green. Files: `crates/cli/src/cli.rs`, `crates/cli/src/milestone.rs`, `crates/cli/src/render.rs`, `crates/cli/tests/milestone.rs`.
- **T5 — the permutation determinism acceptance (Validation hardening #7 — the #1-risk Proves).** A test feeds the **same populated area set** to the engine `join` under **≥3 divergent enumeration/feed orders** — **id order, reverse id order, and a seed-shuffled order** — and asserts the merged outcome (the suffix-resolved overlay + the ordered finding list) is **byte-identical** across all three. The fixture **forces genuine overlap**: two same-slug `created` instances (one **self-referential**), a cross-area ref, and an `edited-from-base` clash; and a **`read_dir`-order ≠ id-order** sub-area layout so a result that passed only because the filesystem handed back id order is caught. Reverse order is mandatory (an id-ordered fixture makes a completion-ordered/broken merge trivially equal id order, hiding the bug). The test additionally asserts no path produces output that varies with hash-container iteration (the accumulators are the order-keyed containers T1 established). **Done:** the ≥3-order byte-identical assertion passes over the forced-overlap, `read_dir`≠id-order fixture; the reverse and shuffled orders each produce output byte-identical to id order; gate green. Files: `crates/engine/src/milestone.rs` (or a dedicated engine test module).

No new fork surfaced: the increment is fully covered by the milestone's settled decisions (2026-06-04 M7 planning entry + bake-backs — the by-task-id join = disjoint union, **blocking** on a same-doc clash, **suffix** on colliding distinct `created` instances with **self-ref rewrite**, cross-area refs rejected **per sub-area** against `committed ∪ that one area` reusing `ref_resolves` unchanged, the join-time isolation check, proven **order-invariant by permutation** per hardening #7; the `created`/`edited-from-base` provenance the B1 fix recorded in Increment 2 is the clash discriminator) and the locked [storage.md](design/storage.md) / [structural-grammar.md](design/structural-grammar.md) / [validation.md](design/validation.md) / [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow 9. **All four *Grouped scope* bullets map to a task:** bullet 1 *edge-index site 4 — enumerate by sorted task id, fold staged docs into the parent overlay, reuse the single-area overlay derivation* → **T1**; bullet 2 *the provenance clash (block) vs collision-suffix + self-ref rewrite* → **T2**; bullet 3 *cross-area ref rejection per sub-area + the join-time isolation check, both intrinsic/floored* → **T3** (delivered as blocking-by-construction per the verified `reconciliation.*` precedent — cross-area via the already-floored `schema-conformance.ref-resolves`, the two net-new emissions blocking-by-construction; see the verified-fact note above); bullet 4 *determinism acceptance — ≥3 divergent orders byte-identical, forced overlap, `read_dir`≠id-order, no unsorted hash iteration to output* → **T5**, with the through-the-binary surface in **T4**. Milestone finalize + the genuine flow-9 e2e through the binary stay out — they are Increment 4, not narrowed here. Doc-elaboration pins the executor makes (not forks): (i) the `join` entry's result shape (a `JoinOutcome` carrying the suffix-resolved merged overlay + the ordered finding list), golden-locked where bytes matter; (ii) the numeric-suffix helper is greenfield (`slug.rs` names it as future but ships none) — added applying `-2`/`-3`… in task-id order; (iii) the two net-new join findings (`join.same-doc-clash`, the isolation finding) emit `Severity::Blocking` directly with a route and **no** `knobs.yaml`/inventory/`INTRINSIC_CHECK_KEYS` row (the verified `reconciliation.*` precedent — see the verified-fact note); (iv) the cross-area check is the existing `index::ref_resolves` called once per sub-area with that area's own `task_dir`, unchanged, emitting `schema-conformance.ref-resolves` (no new check id).

## 2026-06-04 — M7 Increment 3 T1 landed: the by-task-id join skeleton

`engine::milestone::join` enumerates a milestone's sub-task areas by sorted task id (`TaskList::enumerate`, never `read_dir`/completion order) and folds each area's staged docs into the parent working overlay, classified by provenance (`ProvenanceRecord::load`), reusing the single-area `index::overlay_working` derivation as the per-area basis (its `task_froms` are the staged addresses, its `task_edges` the per-`from` forward edges). **Result-shape pin:** `JoinOutcome { overlay: BTreeMap<String, MergedDoc>, findings: Vec<Finding> }`; `MergedDoc { provenance, source_task, edges }`. The overlay is an **address-keyed `BTreeMap`** so iteration is id-sorted and no enumeration/`read_dir`/completion order can leak into output (hardening #7); `findings` is empty in the skeleton (the clash/suffix rule T2 and cross-area/isolation checks T3 populate it). The fold is extracted into `fold_areas(sub_ids, …)` taking an explicit order so order-invariance is testable directly — the unit test folds two disjoint sub-areas (one `created` via `provision_doc`, one `edited-from-base` via `copy_in`) and asserts byte-identical overlay across id order, reverse, and raw `read_dir` order, with the recorded backing list reverse-id (the guaranteed divergence). A staged doc with no recorded provenance is a blocking `join.missing-provenance` (never defaulted). No new fork. NOT wired to finalize.

## 2026-06-04 — M7 Increment 3 T2 landed: the provenance clash rule vs collision-suffix + self-ref rewrite

`fold_areas` now gathers staged docs per minted `<type>:<slug>` address (a `BTreeMap<address, Vec<StagedDoc>>`), then resolves each group **strictly by task id** (`sort_by(source_task)` — so the suffix order and clash listing never depend on the fed `sub_ids` order; hardening #7 holds for the suffix case, not just the disjoint one). A lone instance is a disjoint insert (unchanged). A colliding group with **any** `EditedFromBase` is a partition violation → a blocking route-bearing **`join.same-doc-clash`** naming the contending sub-tasks (covers both two-edited and the **mixed** `created`+`edited` case), and the clashing slug is **kept out** of the overlay (never blind-merged). A colliding group of **all `created`** is distinct docs: the lowest-task-id keeps the bare slug, each higher takes `slug::suffixed(slug, nth)` (`-2`, `-3`, … — the greenfield helper `slug.rs` named as future, now shipped); the suffixed instance's **own self-references** (a ref edge whose `to == its old address`) are rewritten to the suffixed address via `write::set_field` on the staged body, and its edges are **re-derived from the rewritten bytes** (new `index::edges_from_source(schema, source, from)`) so the overlay edges are the bytes' truth, never hand-patched into divergence (`from` re-keyed to the suffix in all cases). `join.same-doc-clash` (+ defensive `join.unknown-type`/`join.self-ref-rewrite`) emit `Severity::Blocking` directly, route-bearing, **no** knobs/inventory row (verified `reconciliation.*`/`file_state.rs` intrinsic-block precedent). No new fork — within the planning pin's doc-elaboration latitude. NOT wired to finalize.

## 2026-06-04 — M7 Increment 3 T3 landed: per-sub-area cross-area ref rejection + the join-time isolation check

`join`/`fold_areas` gain a `repo_root` param (threaded ahead of `milestone_id`; the only callers are tests + the not-yet-built T4 CLI surface). **Cross-area refs** are rejected by invoking the existing single-area `index::ref_resolves(area, repo_root, sub_dir, schemas)` **once per sub-area** with *that area's own* `sub_dir` — so each outgoing edge resolves against `committed ∪ this one area` only, never a flattened multi-area union; a ref resolving only inside a sibling area is unreachable here and surfaces as the existing **intrinsic, already-floored `schema-conformance.ref-resolves`** (no new check id, no `INTRINSIC_CHECK_KEYS`/inventory/`knobs.yaml` edit — the verified fact). The unit test's **control assertion** feeds `ref_resolves` a naïve all-areas union (B's edges over the union of every area's `task_froms`, A's body copied into the read path) and asserts it resolves *clean* — proving the per-area scoping is load-bearing, not incidental. **Join-time isolation:** a sub-area's `ProvenanceRecord` that **attributes an address to itself** with no body in that area's `docs/` (not in `task_froms`) emits a new blocking route-bearing **`join.area-isolation`** finding directly — no `knobs.yaml` row (the `reconciliation.*` blocking-but-untunable precedent; the write-time `--task` barrier is M8). All gather-loop findings (cross-area + isolation, gathered in input order) are **sorted by `(code, message)`** before return, so the finding list stays a pure function of the sub-area *set* (hardening #7). No new fork — within the planning pin's doc-elaboration latitude (pins iii/iv). NOT wired to finalize.

## 2026-06-04 — M7 Increment 3 T4 landed: the `jigc milestone join <id>` verb (clap + dispatch through the binary)

A `MilestoneCommand::Join { milestone_id }` variant + dispatch surface the by-task-id join through the binary. `run_join` does the CLI I/O the engine refuses (no git in engine): discover the repo root + `.jigc/` home, read the milestone's **shared base** pin for the stamp `index::load_committed` keys the committed `EdgeIndex` to (an unknown milestone has no pin → empty stamp, leaving the engine's routed `milestone.unknown` block to fire), load the shipped schemas (the existing `shipped_schemas` idiom), then call `milestone::join`. `Join` has its **own dispatch arm** (`dispatch_join`) because it reports a `JoinOutcome` (overlay + findings), not the one-line summary the other verbs return, **and** because a same-doc clash is a *blocking finding inside an `Ok` outcome* (the merge ran, then routed the contention): the merged-overlay summary always prints on stdout (the agent reads the suffix/rewrite decisions even on a clean join), and any `Severity::Blocking` finding in the outcome additionally routes to stderr + exits non-zero. New `render::milestone_join` derives the **`← suffixed -N on collision` / `; self-ref rewritten`** annotations as a **pure function of the merged overlay** (no engine change): an entry is a collision suffix iff its address ends `-<N≥2>` and the de-suffixed base is also an overlay key (the bare instance the lower task id kept), and the self-ref rewrite is named iff that suffixed entry carries an edge back to its own address. **Engine touch (minimal, in-scope for the JSON `--format` arm every render fn supports):** `MergedDoc`/`JoinOutcome` gain `Serialize, Deserialize` (their fields — `Provenance`, `Vec<Edge>`, `Finding` — already derive both). The verb **commits nothing** (Increment 4 wires the suffix-resolved overlay into finalize); the integration test asserts `git rev-parse HEAD` + `git status --porcelain` are unchanged across both the suffix and clash cases. No new fork — within the planning pin's doc-elaboration latitude (pin i: the `JoinOutcome` result shape, now surfaced).

## 2026-06-04 — M7 build halt (inc-4 Plan): the milestone commit-message source

The build cleared increments 1–3 (the join validated clean with **0 fix rounds** — proven byte-identical across id/reverse/shuffled feed orders + 3 process runs; the proactively-added hardening **#7** held against the concurrency core) then **halted at inc-4 Plan** on a genuine fork under-specified at Settle: milestone `finalize` needs a single commit message, but a milestone has **no commit doc** (the existing finalize renders one from a staged `commit:<slug>.md`; milestone minting never provisions one — `provision_commit_doc` lives only on the `jigc start` path), and `finalize.md` ("one commit per sub-task + parent's") conflicted with the roadmap's "one logical commit boundary." The planner correctly refused to guess.

**Settled — synthesized deterministic message (the human's call).** M7 milestone `finalize` ships **one** commit, its message **synthesized by the CLI** as a *structural projection* of the milestone id + its **id-ordered sub-task list** — no authored prose, so it stays on the CLI's side of the determinism boundary (structure, like git's auto-generated merge message), and it is byte-identical across feed orders (which flow-9 requires). Rejected: (a) a milestone commit doc the agent authors — its authoring belongs to the M8 `milestone-execution` workflow, machinery ahead of need in M7; (c) the per-sub-task N+1-commit model — contradicts the roadmap's one-boundary and needs commit docs the M7 fixtures don't carry. The per-sub-task-commit variant + the `finalize.fan-out.squash` knob are **deferred** to M8+ (when a workflow earns per-sub-task commit authoring). [finalize.md](design/finalize.md) → `fan-out` finalize reconciled. *(Confirmed in-spec, not a fork: inc-4 materializes the join's suffix-rewritten doc **bodies** into the parent area — the join keeps only edges today.)*

## 2026-06-04 — M7 Increment 4 planning: decomposition

Increment 4 — milestone finalize + the flow-9 acceptance (the headline) — cut into 4 ordered, single-concern tasks ([roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md#increment-4--milestone-finalize--the-flow-9-acceptance-the-headline); design [finalize.md](design/finalize.md) → `fan-out` finalize, [storage.md](design/storage.md#the-by-task-id-join-m7) → The by-task-id join (algorithm step + "finalize then commits the overlay … one logical commit boundary"), acceptance = [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow 9).
