- **T4 (cli, e2e acceptance — binary no-clobber + idempotency):** lift the irreversibility proof to the binary. *Done:* a new `setup.rs`-style integration test (real `CARGO_BIN_EXE_jigc` over a throwaway temp repo) **seeds** a non-trivial pre-existing `CLAUDE.md` (house rules) + a `.claude/settings.json` carrying a pre-existing non-jigc hook, runs `jigc setup` **twice**, and asserts (a) the seeded human content in both files survives **verbatim** (structure-aware merge — the house-rules prose + the pre-existing hook still present, never clobbered), (b) the `@.jigc/AGENT.md` import + `jigc *` allowlist + `SessionStart` hook were added, and (c) the **second run is a byte-identical no-op** on `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/settings.json` + `.jigc/AGENT.md` — gate green. ([worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → flow 11 / flow 12 assertion 3.)
- **T5 (cli, e2e acceptance — flow 11):** the new-project arc end-to-end lands one `docs(prd)` commit. *Done:* a new `finalize_spec_only.rs`-style integration test drives the whole `project-setup` loop through the real binary: `jigc setup` (fresh temp git repo) → `jigc start --workflow project-setup "<idea>"` (mint, bound to `task.brief`) → `jigc doc create prd --title "<…>"` (create-gate mint of `prd:<slug>`) → `set-slot` the three slots `prd:<slug>#vision`/`#requirements`/`#context` → `set-field commit:<task>#type docs` (+ scope/summary/body) → **no code change** → `jigc task finalize <id>`; asserts (a) finalize exits zero, (b) **exactly one** new commit on HEAD, (c) `prds/<slug>.md` is committed at its canonical path (promotion), (d) the rendered commit subject carries `docs`, (e) the working area `.jigc/tasks/<id>/` is gone — gate green.

- *Elaboration pin (T2 — `develop-idea` step + `task.brief` is create-gate-only):* `develop-idea` is a pure-prose reasoning step (no `cli.*` ref) shaping vision/requirements/context from `{{task.intent}}`; `author-prd` mirrors `author-spec`'s create-gate idiom (`{{cli.create-prd}}` Run line then `<<author:>>` slot lines). The `as: brief` binding **only gates the create** (admits `prd`, binds to `task.brief`); **no M9 step reads `task.brief`** (confirmed [project-setup.md](design/project-setup.md) → Flow 1), so `author-prd`'s `<<author: {{task.brief#…}}>>` lines address the bound role for the write path but no context-slice consumes it — a doc-elaboration pin within the locked workflow-dialect, not a fork.
- *Elaboration pin (T2 — `create-prd` command-ref):* the `config/commands.yaml` entry mirrors `create-spec` exactly (`command: jigc`, `args: [doc, create, prd, --title, {agent: title, hint: …}]`); the create-gate enforcement is `project-setup`'s `allows-create`, not the catalog entry. No engine/CLI change — `commands.yaml` is loaded by the built catalog parser.

## 2026-06-06 — M9 Increment 2 planning: decomposition

Cut [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → M9 Increment 2 ("the ingestion engine spine — the #1 net-new risk, in isolation") into three ordered, single-concern tasks, each with an observable red step over **fixtures** (a temp repo with the four candidate shapes — no CLI command/workflow this increment; that is inc 3). The whole increment is **net-new engine** in `crates/engine/` (a new `ingest` module), built on two confirmed-public substrate functions. *Grounding (read the code at HEAD `3891df0`, exercised against the M9 ingest shape):* (a) **discovery is today location-scoped** — verified, not assumed: both `index::rebuild_committed` (index.rs:165-172) and `file_state::reconcile_committed_store` (file_state.rs:272-285) loop `for (ty, schema) in schemas` and `read_dir(repo_root.join(location))`, so they glob **only** declared `location:` dirs (`decisions/`/`specs/`/`prds/`) and never root / `docs/` / `README.md`; repo-wide candidate enumeration is genuinely net-new, and both existing sweeps already `slugs.sort()` (the sort discipline the new walk must match for deterministic verdict order). (b) **The classifier substrate is exactly two public engine fns, signatures confirmed**: `engine::parse::parse_sections(schema, source) -> Result<Document, Vec<Finding>>` (parse.rs:168 — **binary parse-or-not**: a non-empty finding set means *no* `Document` is returned, so there is no fuzzy middle) and `engine::validate::schema_conformance(schema, source, &doc) -> Vec<Finding>` (validate.rs:229 — empty `Vec` ⇔ conformant); `Schema` carries `ty: String` (schema.rs:33) + `location: Option<String>` (schema.rs:39), so "under this schema's `location:`" is a `path.starts_with(location)` test, the same shape `file_state::persisted_committed_path` (file_state.rs:337-345) already uses. (c) **The baseline-adopt hole is real and the adopt action must be distinct**: `file_state::file_state` on `UNKNOWN` (file_state.rs:118-140) records the hash with **no schema check and no index population** — so the net-new adopt is `parse → schema_conformance-gate → index::EdgeIndex::absorb_doc(schema, from, doc) (index.rs:118) → FileStateRecord::record(path, hash_bytes(bytes))`, register-only, never touching the file on disk. *Three claims the executor must PROVE in a red step, not trust (per increment-workflow.md "reuse is a claim, not a fact"):* (i) **discovery order is deterministic regardless of `read_dir` order** — T1 builds a fixture whose natural `read_dir` order differs from sorted order and asserts the candidate list is byte-identical sorted (hardening #7: a report sorted only because the fixture was created in id-order is the trap); (ii) **the location discriminator actually decides the conformant-wrong-location case** — T2 exercises a **conformant `adr` sitting at `docs/x.md`** (claims the type, wrong home) and asserts `needs-reconcile`, *not* `adoptable` and *not* `unmanaged` (the design-review B2 silent-drop risk — exercised, never assumed); (iii) **adopt indexes the `supersedes` edge** — T3 adopts a conformant `adr` carrying `supersedes: adr:<x>` and asserts the `(adr:<slug>, supersedes, adr:<x>)` forward edge is present in the index (the `adr` schema declares `supersedes` as a `ref` field at `decisions/` — adr.yaml:7,16 — so `absorb_doc` populates it), the `file-state` baseline carries the file's hash, **and the file's bytes on disk are unchanged** (register-only). Risk-first, linear: discovery (T1) feeds the classifier (T2) which gates adopt (T3); each adds one concern over the last on the shared four-shape fixture. *Why this cut:* the smallest set delivering all three engine *Grouped scope* bullets (repo-wide sorted discovery, the location-aware N-candidate classifier, the net-new schema-gated adopt action). The "Tests (fixtures)" bullet is **not** a fourth task — it enumerates the per-task fixture assertions; its two cross-cutting clauses are folded into T2's done-criterion (the four-verdict assertion over one shared fixture) and T3's (adopt indexes + baselines + moves no file; a non-conformant doc is never adopted — the hole closed). No creep into inc 3's `jigc ingest` command / `ingest-existing` workflow / triage-report surface.

- **T1 (engine — repo-wide sorted discovery):** enumerate candidate `.md` beyond the location dirs. *Done:* a new `engine::ingest` module exposes a discovery fn that, given `repo_root` + the schema set, returns the **sorted, deduped** list of candidate `.md` paths across **root + `docs/` + every schema `location:` dir** (the bounded slice per [project-setup.md](design/project-setup.md) → Flow 2), excluding `.jigc/` internals; an engine unit test over a fixture repo (a `.md` in root, one in `docs/`, one in `decisions/`, one freeform) asserts the exact sorted candidate set, **and** a second fixture whose natural `read_dir` order differs from sorted order yields a **byte-identical** list (the determinism red step, hardening #7) — gate green.
- **T2 (engine — location-aware N-candidate classifier):** classify each candidate to a verdict. *Done:* `engine::ingest` exposes a classifier fn that, per discovered file, runs each persisted schema through `parse_sections` + `schema_conformance` and reduces to a verdict enum `Adoptable { ty }` / `NeedsReconcile { … }` / `Unmanaged` with **location as discriminator** — `adoptable` ⇔ conformant against exactly one schema **and** under that schema's `location:`; `needs-reconcile` ⇔ under some schema's `location:` but fails parse/conformance **or** conformant but at the wrong location; `unmanaged` ⇔ outside every `location:` dir and conformant against nothing ([project-setup.md](design/project-setup.md) → Flow 2, [validation.md](design/validation.md), [parsing.md](implementation/parsing.md)); an engine test over the **four-shape fixture** (a conformant `adr` at `decisions/`, a non-conformant near-miss in `decisions/`, a conformant `adr` at `docs/`, a freeform doc) asserts the **four verdicts** — including the conformant-wrong-location → `needs-reconcile` red step (claim iii above), proving the location discriminator decides it rather than `adoptable`/`unmanaged` — gate green.
- **T3 (engine — net-new schema-gated adopt action):** adopt an `adoptable` doc, register-only. *Done:* `engine::ingest` exposes an adopt fn that takes an `adoptable` candidate (its bytes + matched schema) and does **parse → `schema_conformance` re-gate → `index::EdgeIndex::absorb_doc(schema, from, doc)` → `FileStateRecord::record(path, hash_bytes(bytes))`** — **explicitly distinct from** `file_state`'s silent `UNKNOWN → baseline-adopt` (which schema-checks nothing and indexes nothing — [reconciliation.md](design/reconciliation.md)); engine tests assert (a) adopting a conformant `adr` carrying `supersedes: adr:<x>` populates the `(adr:<slug>, supersedes, adr:<x>)` forward edge in the index + records the `file-state` baseline hash, (b) the file's **bytes on disk are unchanged** (register-only, no move/rewrite), and (c) a **non-conformant doc is never adopted** — the adopt fn refuses it (the `baseline-adopt` safety hole closed) — gate green.

## 2026-06-06 — M9 Increment 3 planning: decomposition

Cut [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → M9 Increment 3 ("the existing-project ingestion flow — command + workflow + acceptance") into four ordered, single-concern tasks, each with an observable red step **through the real binary** (this is the flow-12 acceptance increment, not a fixtures increment). Inc 3 *wraps* inc-2's proven `engine::ingest` engine in a CLI command + a pure-pack-data workflow + the flow-12 e2e. *Grounding (read the code at HEAD `a828940`, exercised against the M9 inc-3 shape):* (a) **inc-2's engine surface is the exact substrate, signatures confirmed public**: `engine::ingest::discover_candidates(repo_root, &[Schema]) -> Vec<String>` (sorted/deduped), `engine::ingest::classify(rel_path, source, &[Schema]) -> Verdict` (ingest.rs:139 — the `Verdict` enum is `Adoptable{ty}` / `NeedsReconcile` / `Unmanaged`, ingest.rs:108-122), and `engine::ingest::adopt(&mut FileStateRecord, &mut EdgeIndex, &Schema, rel_path, bytes) -> Result<(), Vec<Finding>>` (ingest.rs:216 — register-only, persists nothing itself: "the caller persists the advanced record + index"). (b) **The top-level-command + dispatch pattern is `Setup`/`Upgrade`**: `Command::Setup` (cli.rs:148) / `Command::Upgrade` (cli.rs:156) are bare unit variants dispatched by `run_setup`/`run_upgrade` (cli.rs:233, 219-220) that `locate`/`discover_repo_root` the cwd, run the action, render through the global `Format`, and map success/finding to the exit code — `jigc ingest` mirrors this as `Command::Ingest` + a new `cli/src/ingest.rs` (sibling of `setup.rs`). (c) **Persistence substrate is confirmed**: the CLI loads `index::load_committed(repo_root, jigc_root, &schemas, &head)` (index.rs:213; the same call `start.rs:1107` + `milestone.rs:409` use) + `FileStateRecord::load(jigc_root)` (file_state.rs:101), runs discover→classify→adopt-the-`adoptable` over them, then `EdgeIndex::save(jigc_root)` (index.rs:101) + `FileStateRecord::save(jigc_root)` (file_state.rs:90) — register-only, no file moved/rewritten. (d) **The D1 orientation copy already names `ingest-existing`** (render.rs:398/485, shipped in inc-1 T3), so no orientation-copy work rides in inc 3. (e) **The `Finding` carries `severity`/`code`/`message`/`location`/`route`** (finding.rs:140-166) and renders through the existing finding renderers — the triage report's `needs-reconcile` rows reuse this envelope.

***The catalog-phrasing fork, resolved from the locked docs (not halted).*** The roadmap bullet says `ingest-existing` "auto-appears in the router catalog (pure pack data)" — but the **built, M8-audit-hardened** selectable-catalog gate `selectable_workflows` is `creates_task && selectable && non-empty when` (start.rs:856-869), and its doc-comment is explicit: "A `creates-task: false` workflow (the router itself) is never a selectable entry." `ingest-existing` is `creates-task: false`, so forcing it into the **selectable router catalog** would re-introduce the exact M8 leak the hardening warns against (a non-committing workflow leaking into the catalog — increment-workflow.md → #4's snapshot-masking face). The locked design resolves the phrasing: worked-examples flow 12 (line 715) reaches `ingest-existing` via **`jigc start --workflow ingest-existing` (Form D)** — which `compose_named_in_repo` (start.rs:256) already serves for a `creates-task: false` workflow (composes, mints nothing) — **not** via the selectable catalog; project-setup.md:96 frames it "`creates-task: false` orient/route shape — like `router`," and `router` itself is `creates-task: false` and never lists itself. So "auto-appears in the router catalog" is **loose phrasing** for "auto-discovered as pure pack data (no engine registration via `pack.list`) and reachable to the agent" — satisfied by Form D + the already-shipped orientation copy naming it. **Pin for the executor: do NOT relax the `creates_task`-gated selectable catalog to admit `ingest-existing`** — that is the M8 leak; the workflow is reached via Form D, and T3's red step asserts both (it composes via Form D *and* stays absent from the selectable catalog). This is resolvable from the locked docs (flow 12 invocation + the M8 hardening invariant), so it is a pin, not a halt.

*One claim the executor must PROVE in a red step, not trust (per increment-workflow.md "reuse is a claim"):* the **`needs-reconcile` triage rows must carry a routed [finding](design/validation.md)** (scope bullet 1; flow-12 row shape, worked-examples.md:724-729) — but inc-2's `classify` returns a **bare `Verdict::NeedsReconcile` that discards the underlying findings** (ingest.rs:139,158,165 — the `parse_sections`/`schema_conformance` findings are computed then dropped). So the routed finding is **net-new command-layer report-assembly work** (re-derive the located finding for a `needs-reconcile` file from the home schema's `parse_sections`/`schema_conformance` output, or — if cleaner — surface it from the engine), NOT a free reuse: T1 asserts a `needs-reconcile` row renders a finding with `severity: blocking`, a located message, and a route. (This is command-layer triage assembly, inc-3 scope — not an inc-2 re-litigation.)

Risk-first, linear: the read-only command spine — discover→classify→triage report (T1) — before adopt is layered on (T2); the pack workflow that orients the agent to run the command (T3); the flow-12 e2e wrapping all three (T4). *Why this cut:* the smallest set delivering all three *Grouped scope* bullets — the `jigc ingest` command + triage report (T1+T2), the `ingest-existing` workflow + steps (T3), the flow-12 acceptance (T4) — no creep (auto-migration stays deferred; no prose rewritten; the catalog gate untouched). The command bullet is split T1/T2 because the read-only triage report (a verdict surface, no side effects) and the adopt action (which mutates the index + file-state baseline) are two distinct, separately-red-step-able concerns — and T1's report is the natural substrate T2's adopt-confirmation extends.

- **T1 (cli — `jigc ingest` command spine: discover → classify → triage report, read-only):** the command surface + the verdict report. *Done:* `Command::Ingest` (a bare top-level variant mirroring `Setup`/`Upgrade`) + a new `crates/cli/src/ingest.rs` module (sibling of `setup.rs`) whose `run` locates the repo root, loads the schema set + `index::load_committed` + `FileStateRecord::load`, runs `engine::ingest::discover_candidates` then `classify` per candidate, and renders the **triage report** (`file × best-match type × verdict`, rows in the sorted candidate order) through the global `Format`; `needs-reconcile` rows carry a **routed finding** (`severity: blocking` + a located message + a route — re-derived at the command layer from the home schema's `parse_sections`/`schema_conformance` output, the claim PROVEN above). **No adopt yet** (read-only this task — the report mutates nothing). A `tests/ingest.rs`-style integration test (real `CARGO_BIN_EXE_jigc` over a throwaway repo seeded with the four flow-12 candidate shapes after `jigc setup`) asserts the report lists all four candidates in sorted order with verdicts `adoptable`/`needs-reconcile`/`needs-reconcile`/`unmanaged`, and that **at least one `needs-reconcile` row renders a finding with a blocking severity + a located message + a route** — gate green. ([project-setup.md](design/project-setup.md) → Flow 2 bullets 1/2/4.)
- **T2 (cli — adopt-conformant wired through the command, register-only + persisted):** layer the schema-gated adopt onto the command. *Done:* `ingest::run` now, after classifying, calls `engine::ingest::adopt(&mut record, &mut index, schema, rel_path, &bytes)` for **every `adoptable` candidate**, then persists with `EdgeIndex::save(jigc_root)` + `FileStateRecord::save(jigc_root)`; the report's `adoptable` rows render an **adopt-confirmation** (the pinned adopt-confirmation shape — an `adopted` marker per row). The integration test (extending T1's, post-`jigc setup`) asserts (a) running `jigc ingest` over the four-candidate repo adopts **only** the conformant-at-location `decisions/rate-limit.md` — the index gains its `supersedes` forward edge (if present) and the file-state baseline gains its hash; (b) the two `needs-reconcile` docs and the `unmanaged` doc are **NOT** adopted (absent from the index + baseline); (c) **no candidate file's bytes on disk change** and **no file is moved** (register-only — assert the four files' contents + paths are byte-identical pre/post `jigc ingest`); (d) **nothing is adopted without a schema check** (the `baseline-adopt` hole closed end-to-end — the non-conformant `decisions/auth-choice.md` under a `location:` dir is never adopted) — gate green. ([project-setup.md](design/project-setup.md) → Flow 2 bullet 3; [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → flow 12 assertion 2.)
- **T3 (pack — `ingest-existing` workflow + steps, pure pack data):** ship the orient/route workflow that drives the agent through `jigc ingest`. *Done:* `crates/cli/pack/workflows/ingest-existing.yaml` (`creates-task: false`, a non-overlapping `when:` hint, body = the step includes) mirroring the `router` orient/route shape; its steps (e.g. `steps/run-scan.yaml` emitting `Run: jigc ingest`, `steps/review-verdicts.yaml` instructing review-and-route — pure prose, the run-scan → review-verdicts → adopt/route walk per the scope bullet); a `start_compose`/`compose_named`-style test asserts `jigc start --workflow ingest-existing "<intent>"` **composes** (Form D, `creates-task: false` ⇒ mints nothing) and the four-class emit carries the `Run: jigc ingest` line; **and** a `selectable_workflows`/catalog-style test asserts `ingest-existing` is **absent** from the selectable router catalog (the M8-leak pin proven — a `creates-task: false` workflow never enters the catalog) — gate green. ([project-setup.md](design/project-setup.md) → Flow 2; [workflow-dialect.md](design/workflow-dialect.md) → On-disk definition format; the catalog-phrasing pin above.)
- **T4 (cli, e2e acceptance — flow 12):** the four-candidate repo classified + routed end-to-end through the binary. *Done:* a `tests/`-level integration test drives the whole brownfield arc through the real binary on a throwaway repo: seed `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/settings.json` + the four flow-12 candidates (a conformant `adr` at `decisions/rate-limit.md`, a non-conformant near-miss `decisions/auth-choice.md`, a conformant-wrong-location `docs/old-adr.md`, a freeform `docs/notes.md`) → `jigc setup` → `jigc start --workflow ingest-existing "<intent>"` (asserts it composes + emits `Run: jigc ingest`) → `jigc ingest`; asserts (a) the **conformant-at-location** `rate-limit.md` **adopts** (indexed + baselined, **no file moved**), (b) the **non-conformant** and **wrong-location** docs **route** (`needs-reconcile`, not adopted), (c) the **freeform** doc is **left untouched** (`unmanaged`, not adopted, bytes unchanged), (d) **nothing is adopted without a schema check** (the hole closed end-to-end), and (e) **no prose is rewritten** (every candidate file byte-identical pre/post) — gate green. ([worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) → flow 12.)

- *Elaboration pin (T3 — `ingest-existing` steps are pure prose, no new `cli.*` ref):* the steps emit the already-built `jigc ingest` verb as a `Run:` line via plain prose (like `present-catalog`/`route-to-workflow` emit their guidance); no `config/commands.yaml` entry is needed because `jigc ingest` is a fixed top-level command, not an agent-substituted command-ref. A doc-elaboration within the locked workflow-dialect, not a fork.
- *Elaboration pin (T1/T2 — adopt-confirmation + triage-report shape):* the exact triage-report layout (column order, the `adopted`/routed markers) and the `needs-reconcile` routed-finding rendering are **pinned by the executor at build** against the built renderer + flow-12's illustrative shape ([worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md):721-731) — illustrative-and-net-new in the design (project-setup.md:96, the M8 "spike the acceptance-flow commands" discipline), so the concrete bytes are a doc-elaboration pin the executor makes, asserted verbatim in T1/T2's tests (the emitted-bytes contract, increment-workflow.md → #4).

- **T1 pinned at build — `jigc ingest` verb surface + triage-report bytes.** `Command::Ingest` is a bare unit variant (mirrors `Setup`/`Upgrade`); `cli/src/ingest.rs::run` is read-only — it locates the repo + project layer, loads schemas + `index::load_committed` + `FileStateRecord::load` (the substrate T2 adopts onto; T1 consults neither for the verdict, which reduces on location+conformance), then `discover_candidates` → `classify` per candidate in sorted order. **Report row shape:** `<verdict> <file> → <type>` (or `→ (parses against no schema — left untouched)` for `unmanaged`), one row per candidate, a `needs-reconcile` row carrying its routed finding indented two spaces beneath (the existing `finding_line` envelope). **The net-new routed finding re-derives the two `needs-reconcile` shapes at the command layer** (the engine `classify` discards them): a *near-miss* (under a `location:` dir, non-conformant) surfaces the **home schema's** first `parse_sections`/`schema_conformance` finding retargeted with a `reconcile <path> against the <ty> schema` route; a *wrong-location* (conformant off its `location:`) synthesizes a blocking `ingest.wrong-location` finding routing the human to relocate it (jigc never auto-moves). Both carry `severity: blocking` + a located message + a route. README.md and similar repo-root prose classify `unmanaged` (parses against no schema), which is correct, not noise.
- **T2 pinned at build — adopt wired through `jigc ingest` + the adopt-confirmation bytes.** `ingest::run` is no longer read-only: it loads `index::load_committed` + `FileStateRecord::load` **mutably**, and for **every `adoptable` candidate** calls `engine::ingest::adopt(&mut record, &mut index, schema, rel_path, &bytes)` (the schema looked up by the verdict's `ty`). Adopt re-gates over the same substrate, so a row is marked `adopted` only when the re-gate *also* passes (a refusal leaves the row un-adopted and never errors the scan — defensive, though the at-location verdict already conformed). After the scan, `run` persists the advanced surfaces with `EdgeIndex::save` + `FileStateRecord::save` **iff anything adopted** (no adopt ⇒ no write; adopt is register-only — it touches no candidate file). `TriageRow` gains a serialized `adopted: bool`; **the adopt-confirmation marker (pinned bytes)** is the agent-text suffix `  (adopted — indexed + baselined, no file moved)` appended only to an adopted row (the flow-12 illustrative `(conformant, at location)` made concrete as the register-only confirmation). Asserted verbatim through the binary in `tests/ingest.rs`: only `decisions/rate-limit.md` adopts (index gains its `supersedes` forward edge, baseline gains its hash), the two `needs-reconcile` docs + the `unmanaged` doc are absent from both, every candidate's bytes are byte-identical pre/post (no move), and the non-conformant near-miss under a `location:` dir is never adopted (the `baseline-adopt` hole closed end-to-end).

- **T3 pinned at build — `ingest-existing` workflow + steps; the `Run:` marker forces a command-ref (revising the planner's plain-prose elaboration pin).** `pack/workflows/ingest-existing.yaml` is `creates-task: false` (orient/route like `router`) with a non-overlapping `when:` (`bring an existing repo's docs under jigc management by scanning and routing`) and a two-step body (`run-scan` → `review-verdicts`). The planner's elaboration pin said the steps emit `jigc ingest` as a **plain-prose `Run:` line, no `commands.yaml` entry** — but the built compose-conformance guard `find_run_shadow` (compose.rs:1935) **rejects any step-prose line whose trim starts with `Run: `**: that left-margin marker is reserved exclusively for a resolved `{{cli.<id>}}` command-ref (the M7 four-class emitter). So the only conformant way to emit the flow-12 `Run: jigc ingest` artifact is a command-ref. Added a fixed (no `{agent:…}` args) `run-ingest` catalog entry to `config/commands.yaml` (`args: ["ingest"]`) — well-precedented by `recompose-task`/`finalize-task`, which are equally fixed-shape — and `steps/run-scan.yaml` emits `{{ cli.run-ingest }}`, rendering the contract bytes `` Run: `jigc ingest` ``. `ingest-existing` is reached via Form D (`compose_named_in_repo`) + the shipped D1 orientation copy, and stays **absent from the selectable router catalog** (`creates_task && selectable && non-empty when` — the M8-leak pin); both proven in `start.rs` tests over the real embedded pack.

## 2026-06-06 — M9 (project setup — new & existing) shipped

M9 is built end-to-end and **the milestone spine is complete** (M1–M9 all shipped + audited). Project setup works for new **and** existing projects, layered on the already-built `jigc setup` install command. Shipped: the `prd` doctype (fixed single-word prose slots, pure pack data); the `project-setup` work-workflow (`creates-task: true`, plan-shaped — mint → author the prd via the create-gate → code-less `docs(prd):` finalize promoting `prds/<slug>.md`); the D1 orientation-copy fix; the binary-level no-clobber/idempotency acceptance for `jigc setup`; the net-new `engine::ingest` module (repo-wide **sorted** discovery + a **location-aware** N-candidate classifier + a **schema-gated register-only adopt** — parse → conformance-gate → `index.absorb_doc` → file-state record — that **closes the silent `baseline-adopt` hole**); the `jigc ingest` command (discover → classify → adopt-conformant + triage-route the rest); and the `ingest-existing` orient/route workflow. The research-grade **auto-migrate** core is consciously deferred with a trigger, as are the `prd` repeatable-requirements shape + the multi-word-heading parser fix.

Built via the [milestone-build](.claude/workflows/milestone-build.js) harness across three risk-tiered increments with **0 build halts** and **0 in-increment fix rounds** — the strongest convergence signal yet, and the M9 win condition met: the gap-spike + scope-bounding at Settle (bounded detect-and-route; auto-migrate deferred) converted into clean builds rather than mid-build flailing on "what does ingest even mean."

**[milestone-completion audit](implementation/milestone-completion-workflow.md): e2e overall-pass + code-review "M9 genuinely holds"; 2 distinct findings, both human-triaged to fix + remediated + re-verified.**
- (FIXED `5497c6f`) **MEDIUM** the canonical writer produced **non-reparseable** output when a **non-terminal** prose slot was filled with prose lacking a trailing newline — a pre-existing byte-stability round-trip defect (`parse::trim_span` anchored an empty slot's zero-width span at its *trailing* edge, fusing prose onto the next heading; validate-after-write correctly rejected it, so no committed corruption, but valid input was wrongly refused). The `prd` 3-slot flow was the most exposed, and a builder **flow-11 test had masked it** by always passing newline-terminated prose (the M8 masking-vector pattern, caught only by the independent audit). Fix: the empty-slot span now anchors at the *leading* edge (populated slots + every byte-stability golden untouched, no churn); the flow-11 test is **de-masked** (now fills a non-terminal slot with no trailing newline).
- (FIXED `243752c`) **LOW** `jigc ingest --help` + doc-comments advertised "read-only — adopts nothing" while the command adopts-and-persists (`.jigc/index` + `.jigc/state`) — an agent-facing honesty defect (an agent reads `--help` to decide whether a command writes). Corrected to honestly describe the **register-only** adopt (records into the edge index + file-state baseline, never moves/rewrites a candidate file). The adopt behavior itself was correct; only the labeling lied.

**Harness:** this cycle re-exercised the M8 masking-vector discipline — the MEDIUM round-trip defect had been hidden by a builder acceptance test that declined to exercise the failure path (non-newline-terminated prose), and was caught only by the independent e2e audit, then de-masked in the fix. No new numbered principle needed. **The spine is done** — next is the human's call on the post-spine cluster (self-hosting the designed terminus, `describe`, the `doc↔code` pack-probe family, multi-pack), each of which needs a milestone *created* ([decisions-pending.md](implementation/decisions-pending.md) → After the spine completes).

## 2026-06-06 — The post-spine arc (M10–M14) created

With the foundational spine (M1–M9) complete, the post-spine cluster — previously tracked in [decisions-pending.md](implementation/decisions-pending.md) as "real, locked-direction items with no home milestone" — was **created and ordered** as the post-spine arc, shaped collaboratively suggestion-by-suggestion. Still strictly linear and milestone-level only ([roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → The post-spine arc); each is decomposed by [milestone-planning](implementation/milestone-planning-workflow.md) when picked up.

- **M10 · doc↔code validation** — the last deferred *differentiator* (VISION #6 "validate against reality"), now unblocked (real doc corpora exist post-M3/M9). The weight is two **firsts**: pack-provided probes (the subprocess + determinism contracts, already designed in [validation.md](design/validation.md)) and the `code-anchor` field type (first pack-provided field type). Bounded to: mechanism + symbol/file-existence (floor) + criterion→test-mapping (headline) + `code-anchor`; staleness a stretch; **OS-level sandboxing deferred** (trusted-pack model). Targets shipped `adr`/`spec`; `arch-doc↔code` rides the same mechanism at M13.
- **M11 · describe / introspection** — the prose-projection self-description surface; design **locked** 2026-05-30 ([ideas/describe.md](ideas/describe.md)), so its planning is mostly the open thread (which definitions carry `description:`/`usage:` — workflows+doctypes the floor — required-vs-optional, cascade merge). Placed before M12 so the methodology-pack dogfood can `describe` itself.
- **M12 · self-hosting** *(exploratory)* — the designed terminus ([ideas/self-hosting.md](ideas/self-hosting.md)): distill → encode-as-pack → dogfood+measure vs the hand-run baseline, the forcing function that sorts the harness into the **three-way determinism cut** (mechanizable step · irreducible judgment · owner-assigned artifact). Kept honestly **exploratory** because the real prerequisite — workflow-as-managed-artifact dialect surface for multi-phase/human-gated/fan-out workflows — **can only be designed by attempting the encode**; the attempt may reveal a dedicated dialect-extension milestone (named then, not guessed now). Mints the second pack.
- **M13 · doctype & surface completion** — *added this session at the human's suggestion*, sitting between self-hosting and multi-pack: the self-hosting **fold-back**. `arch-doc` finally earns its driver (named in VISION, driverless since), and the methodology's own docs (roadmap / deferral-ledger / decisions-log) graduate to managed doctypes — each earning its schema from its driving workflow. **Candidate, not locked** — confirmed after M12 surfaces what the dogfood needs. Note the distinction settled here: the *doctype* gaps are M13 (fold-back); the *dialect-surface* prerequisite is M12-internal (can't be a fold-back of the thing it enables).
- **M14 · multi-pack composition** — the generalization: compose **our two packs only** (dev + methodology) in the cascade with deterministic collision resolution — explicitly **not** a public pack-authoring API (VISION's "not a second domain / not a public extension API" non-goal holds). Blocked until M12 mints the second pack, so last.

**The one hard dependency that fixed part of the order:** multi-pack (M14) needs a second pack, which self-hosting (M12) mints — so M14 sits last. doc↔code/describe float (independent); placed M10/M11 by value (doc↔code is the biggest product gap) and the describe→self-hosting synergy. **Still genuinely unscheduled** (deferred beyond the arc): **legacy auto-migration** (the research-grade existing-project rewrite M9 bounded away). Source directions: [ideas/describe.md](ideas/describe.md), [ideas/self-hosting.md](ideas/self-hosting.md), [VISION.md](VISION.md) → Open questions; the arc graduates the [decisions-pending.md](implementation/decisions-pending.md) "needs a milestone created" cluster.

**Independent design-review (post-creation, spiked):** the skeleton was verified sound — the ordering is right (M14-last is the only true hard dependency, correctly identified; M10/M11 float and are value-placed), and the M10-sandboxing and M14-publication bounds are honest. 3 blocking + 5 advisory, all on the M12/M13 seam, **baked back**: (B1) M12's "Prereqs ✅" line silently dropped the dialect-surface prerequisite two source docs call hard → re-cast explicitly as substrate-prereqs-✅ + dialect-surface-discovered-by-encoding; (B2, the sharpest) the M12-internal-vs-M13-foldback split was contradicted by M12's dogfood needing the methodology docs → M12 now states it dogfoods with those docs as **plain markdown** (only workflows encoded; doctype graduation is M13's fold-back), with the honest consequence that M12 measures *workflow-composition fidelity*, not self-managed docs; (B3) M11-before-M12 → added the dialect-shape-agnostic caveat (describe projects usage/intent prose, surviving M12's extensions); (A1) M14's bound clarified as the *publication* boundary not the *generality* one (the collision mechanism is inherently general, kept internal); (A3) keyed **workflow progress/resumption** to M12 in the deferral-ledger (the encode of multi-phase/human-gated workflows forces it — closing a no-trigger gap); (A5) fixed the stale `arch-doc` "no firm trigger" → M13; (A6) M13 "surface completion" clarified as the named graph nodes *and* edges (triage `spec —decided-by→ adr` at M13 planning). One finding (A4, a dropped difficulty-guard) was a **false positive** — M12 already carries "comparable-difficulty milestone." The "second pack" count was verified correct (one pack today).

**Cross-model review (Codex / gpt-5.5, before M10 pickup):** caught **two substantive items the in-house review missed** + three tightenings, all baked. (1) **M10 sandboxing** — the in-house review called "OS-sandboxing deferred" *honest*, but Codex spotted it blurred two things [validation.md](design/validation.md) keeps distinct: the **determinism contract** (six rules + four meta-findings) is *binding* ("cannot ship without satisfying the contract"), while only the **OS-level sandboxing implementation** (seccomp/Landlock) is deferrable. M10 reworded: the contract is **satisfied, not waived** (rules binding + timeout/crash/malformed-output meta-findings enforced); only OS-level sandboxing + the sandbox-violation meta-finding it backs defer under the trusted-pack model. (2) **M12↔M14 circularity** — a faithful self-hosting dogfood runs the methodology *with* the dev pack's surfaces (`commit`/`adr`/dev loop), which **is** the dev+methodology multi-pack composition M14 enables → M12 would need M14. Resolved by bounding M12 to a **single composed pack** (the methodology pack vendors/subsumes the dev surfaces it needs); true co-composition is M14's distinct proof — breaking the circularity (M14 needs M12's pack; M12 must not need M14). Whether the methodology pack subsumes-vs-stays-thin is an M12-planning fork. Tightenings: M10 "first pack-provided *probe* + *field type*" (not "first extension" — the dev pack already shipped); M12 plain-markdown docs named as **direct/unmanaged writes** (the place-every-write proof waits for M13); M13 "VISION's *starting set* complete + methodology candidates folded back *if M12 earns them*". The cross-model pass earned its keep — the two blocking catches were exactly the M10/M12 bounds the same-model review had waved through.

## 2026-06-06 — M9 retro + post-spine-arc-session learnings folded back

M9 planning + build ran **0 halts / 0 in-increment fix-rounds** — the spike-the-bound-at-Settle + acceptance-flow-command-spiking disciplines *working*, not a new lesson (build-health trend M7=1 → M8=1 → M9=0). Three learnings from the M9 audit + the post-spine-arc session fold back as **widened gate faces, not new numbered principles**:

- **Cross-model review caught blocking items the same-model review shared with the author → [milestone-planning](implementation/milestone-planning-workflow.md) Review + the [`design-reviewer`](.claude/agents/design-reviewer.md) prompt.** Reviewing the post-spine arc, the in-house `design-reviewer` (same model family) explicitly *blessed* M10's sandboxing bound as "honest" and explicitly *denied* the M12↔M14 dependency; Codex (different vendor) flagged both as blocking. Textbook same-model shared-priors miss. **The fix is two-pronged, cost-aware:** (1) cross-model review (`/review-codex`) stays a **human-approved *suggestion*** for high-stakes/milestone-creation/scope-bound calls — *not* auto-run, because it costs and isn't universally available, so the human decides; (2) the lever we own and that's free — the **same-model `design-reviewer` prompt** — is hardened to hunt the two exact blind-spot classes it shared: **bound-vs-deferral conflation** (a "X deferred" that silently waives a *binding* requirement the source locks — the M10 contract case) and **hidden circular/co-dependency** (N produces what M needs *and* uses it in a way that needs M first — the M12↔M14 case).
- **Input-coverage masking — "the agent owns the prose" makes the writer's round-trip need a hostile-input pass too → [increment-workflow](implementation/increment-workflow.md) face #3 + the validator/code-reviewer agents.** M9's MEDIUM canonical-writer round-trip bug (non-reparseable output when a non-terminal slot is filled with prose lacking a trailing newline) was **masked** by a flow-11 acceptance that always passed newline-terminated prose — caught only by the independent e2e audit. Distinct from the M8 golden-*widening* face (an output problem): this is an **input** problem. Since the determinism boundary makes the agent the author of slot prose, the writer must round-trip *any agent-plausible* input; a red/acceptance test that only feeds CLI-canonical input is a masking test for input-conditional defects.
- **A pre-existing defect that the milestone's flow exercises *and* a builder test masked is a fix-now candidate, not a defer → [milestone-completion](implementation/milestone-completion-workflow.md) triage.** "It predates this milestone" is not by itself grounds to track-as-known: the milestone surfaced it and a green gate hid it (the M9 writer bug — pre-existing across `spec`/`adr`, but the `prd` flow hit it). Recorded as a triage rule so it's a rule, not a one-off judgment.

No new numbered principle — the M7 "widen + name the new face" discipline held again. The cross-model lesson is the session's sharpest: the same-model reviewer has a demonstrated blind spot on scope-bounds and dependencies, addressed by hardening its prompt (always-on) + suggesting a cross-model pass for high-stakes calls (human-gated).

## 2026-06-06 — M10 (doc↔code validation) planning: scope settled

Planned via [milestone-planning](implementation/milestone-planning-workflow.md) (scope → 3 `capability-auditor`s → 4 `gap-detector`s → settle → review → decompose). **The baseline audit overturned the roadmap's reuse framing** — three independent auditors + four gap-detectors converged: the roadmap said *"the Probe trait + in-process impls shipped; M10 adds the subprocess impl"* and *"the `code-anchor` field type (+ adjudicator)"*, but the verified code (HEAD f4415d4) shows **both reuse claims are false**, and the milestone is materially larger and more architectural than framed:

- **The `Probe` trait is the wrong shape.** Built: `check(&self, ctx: OverrideCtx) -> Vec<Finding>` — **no `target` arg**; `ctx` holds a live `&dyn PackSource`, **not serializable** (`probe.rs:40-55`). [validation.md](design/validation.md):97 + [module-layout.md](implementation/module-layout.md):59-61 both claimed `check(target, ctx)` with a *serializable* ctx so "subprocess admits with zero engine change" — **untrue at HEAD**. There is **no probe scheduler/registry**; probes are hardcoded inline in `validate_task`.
- **`code-anchor` is a closed-enum engine variant, not pack-provided.** `FieldType` is a closed Rust enum incl. `CodeAnchor`, adjudicated as a bare string (`schema.rs:211`, `write.rs:2337`). A pack **cannot** supply a field type or adjudicator today — no extension hook.
- **The in-process shortcut cannot satisfy the locked contract.** The contract's meta-findings (`timeout`/`crash`/`malformed-output`) are *defined over a subprocess wire*; an in-process Rust fn has no exit code, no JSON to malform, no clean timeout — an "in-process prototype" would **waive** exactly the meta-findings the [post-spine cross-model review](#2026-06-06--the-post-spine-arc-m10m14-created) said must be *satisfied, not waived*. Compounded by the **engine's shell-free invariant** (`finalize.rs:11-13`) — code-reading / subprocess invocation lives in the **CLI**, not the engine.

**Four forks put to the human, settled:**

- **Architecture → subprocess-from-the-start.** The only path that genuinely satisfies "satisfied, not waived" (the in-process branch structurally can't). **The engine owns** the reshaped `Probe` seam (`target` + a *serializable* effective-state ctx/snapshot), Finding ingestion, the report merge, and meta-finding synthesis; **the CLI owns** the subprocess invoker (`std::process::Command` + a wait-timeout), so the shell-free engine invariant holds and the probe executable is a separate program. The wire contract is concretized this milestone (no longer "illustrative").
- **`code-anchor` → a genuine pack-field-type extension axis** (the human chose the larger path over the engine-native-variant bound). The meta-schema gains a way for a pack to *declare* a field type and *bind* its adjudicator to a probe; `code-anchor` moves out of the closed engine enum to become the **first pack-declared field type**, adjudicated by the dev pack's `doc-code` probe at finalize. Makes "first pack-provided field type" literally true.
- **Criterion authoring → ride the editable channel** (defer `add-item`). The headline criterion + its `maps-to-test` anchor are authored by raw git edit (the M3 e2e precedent), then `doc-code` validates at finalize. The probe **is** the adjudicator (finalize-time, mirroring `ref-resolves`), so write-time repeatable-item adjudication isn't needed — `add-item` + per-leaf write adjudication stay deferred ([decisions-pending.md](implementation/decisions-pending.md)).
- **Targets → `adr` header anchor (floor) + `spec` criterion anchor (headline).** `adr` gains an optional `cites-code` code-anchor in its `status` header section (the **floor**: symbol/file existence); `spec`'s `criteria` block gains an optional `maps-to-test` code-anchor (the **headline**: criterion→test). Both firsts proven on the cheapest path for each. *(Review fix B1/B2:* a `code-anchor` is a probe-checked field, **not an edge**, so the blast-radius walk can't reach a citing doc from changed code — `doc-code`'s task-scope targets are the **task's effective-state docs carrying anchors**: created/edited working-deltas ∪ bound read-roles. So the floor `adr` is **created in-task** via the create-gate (a working delta, in scope), and the headline `spec` is **bound** (read-role, in scope). `set-field` cannot today insert an absent optional header field — wiring the existing `insert_front_matter_field` engine primitive to the CLI `set-field` verb is the small in-CLI floor-authoring piece (the headline criterion still rides the editable channel — `add-item` deferred).)*

**Two design-detail forks, settled:**

- **Anchor grammar + resolver → `<repo-relative-path>#<symbol>`, tree-sitter, multi-language.** The `path#symbol` shape matches the codebase's own fixtures (`maps-to-test: test/rate_limit_spec.rb#burst`); a bare `path` is a file-existence check. Resolution is **static parsing via tree-sitter** (grammar picked by file extension) — `cargo test --list` is forbidden (it compiles → wall-clock/network/shell-out, violating contract rules 1/3/5/6). Multi-language symbol-existence is the floor; the "is-a-test" predicate ships **Rust-first** (`#[test]`) over a general mechanism. (Human chose tree-sitter/multi-language over Rust-only `syn`.)
- **Blocking trigger → optional field, dangling-anchor blocks.** `maps-to-test`/`cites-code` are optional; the probe **blocks** when an anchor is *present but unresolvable* (points at a symbol/test that doesn't exist) — the exact mirror of the proven `ref-resolves` "blocks when it dangles" pattern (M1 `supersedes`, M3 `implements`). A criterion with no anchor is at most an advisory finding, **not** the headline blocker. The acceptance proof is the **blocking** case (a deleted test → finalize blocks), never the happy path.

**Check inventory (pinned, [validation.md](design/validation.md) → MVP check inventory):** `doc-code.symbol-exists` + `doc-code.criterion-maps-to-test` (blocking default, **tunable** — they tune through the M6 post-pass for free once their `(probe,check)` rows land); `pack-probe-integrity.timeout` / `.crash` / `.malformed-output` (**intrinsic blocking** — a probe that misbehaved can't be trusted). `pack-probe-integrity.sandbox-violation` stays deferred with OS-level sandboxing.

**Favorable findings (verified, not assumed):** finalize runs `validate()` over the **working tree** *then* `git add --all` — no mutation step between, so the "code-anchor resolved at compose but code changed before finalize" risk is **closed by construction** (the probe reads working-tree *code* + committed-store *doc*). Severity tunes for free via M6. `doctype-map` needs no structural-edge change (doc↔code is a probe, not a relation).

**Deferred (tracked, not gaps):** OS-level sandboxing + `sandbox-violation` (trusted-pack model); **staleness** (a stretch — and when built, via a *content-hash* like `file-state`'s blake3, never mtime, which rule 3 forbids); **store/doc-scope validate** (task-scope + blast-radius covers the in-task "deletion vanishes the cited symbol" case — a pure "audit a committed adr with no task open" demo would need a non-task entry, out of slice); **`add-item` + repeatable-item write adjudication**; **findings caching** (took the recompute-on-demand default). Design homes elaborated this session: [validation.md](design/validation.md) (the `doc-code` probe + concretized wire contract + meta-findings), [document-type-schema.md](design/document-type-schema.md) (the pack-field-type extension axis), [module-layout.md](implementation/module-layout.md) (the subprocess architecture + corrected trait shape), [worked-examples.md](design/worked-examples.md) flow 13 (the acceptance walk).

**Independent pre-decompose review (`design-reviewer`, spiked against HEAD):** verified the architecture sound — the bound-vs-deferral line holds in every doc (contract *satisfied, not waived*; the Codex catch stays closed), the inventory tallies (25/16/9), the baseline corrections are fully absorbed, and there is no hidden circularity (editable-channel authoring genuinely doesn't need `add-item`). **Two BLOCKING findings, both baked back before decompose:** (B1) a `code-anchor` is a probe-checked *field, not an edge*, so the blast-radius walk can't reach a citing doc from changed code — and flow 13's floor `adr` was an *unrelated pre-committed* doc never in the task's effective state, so the acceptance would have passed with **zero `doc-code` checks run** (the masking trap). Fixed: `doc-code`'s task-scope targets are **the task's effective-state docs carrying anchors** (created/edited ∪ bound), enumerated directly; the floor `adr` is now **created in-task** (a working delta, in scope), the headline `spec` **bound** (read-role, in scope); the acceptance bar now asserts the checks **ran** (a zero-`doc-code`-finding report fails as hard as a missed block). Store-wide anchor audit stays the deferred store-scope check. (B2) the floor's "reuses `set-field` authoring" was false — `set-field` can't insert an absent optional header field; fixed by scoping the small `insert_front_matter_field`→verb wiring into M10 (distinct from the deferred `add-item`). Advisories folded: the load-time intrinsic-floor assertion tracks only *live-keyed* checks (the deferred `sandbox-violation` row isn't asserted); the multi-language claim downgraded to "tree-sitter so a 2nd language is a fixture-add; **Rust proven** in M10"; the `doc-code` snapshot contents itemized (`(target-address, anchor-value, check-id)` pairs + working-tree root); two stale `module-layout.md` "zero-engine-change / post-MVP" phrases corrected.

## 2026-06-06 — M10 Increment 1 planning: decomposition

Cut [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → M10 Increment 1 ("the pack-declared field-type extension axis + the two schema anchors — the first 'first'") into four ordered, single-concern tasks, each with an observable red step. The increment delivers the **first pack-declared field type** — `code-anchor` moves out of the closed engine enum into the dev pack, bound to the (later-built) `doc-code` adjudicator — plus the two shipped doctypes' anchors and the absent-optional-header authoring path. No probe runs this increment ([document-type-schema.md](design/document-type-schema.md) → Pack-declared field types). *Grounding (read the code at HEAD `f4415d4`, exercised against the M10 inc-1 shape):*

- **(a) `FieldType` is a closed Rust enum with `CodeAnchor` hardcoded as a variant** (`crates/engine/src/schema.rs:197-212`); `Field.ty: FieldType` deserializes **directly from the YAML string** via serde, so today `type: code-anchor` resolves to the hardcoded `CodeAnchor` variant with **no pack involvement**. `load_schema(bytes)` takes only bytes — **there is no declared-type-set threading**, and **no `SchemaError::UnknownFieldType` variant exists** despite the `schema.rs:192` doc-comment naming one (the comment describes an aspirational state; the real undeclared-type path today is the generic `Malformed` serde error against the closed enum). Opening the enum to *engine-native variants + a pack-declared variant carrying (name, adjudicator)* and rejecting an undeclared name with a typed error is the architectural heart (T1).
- **(b) `check_value`/`check_scalar` collapse `String | Ref | CodeAnchor` into one shared arm** (`crates/engine/src/write.rs:2337`) — non-empty + control-char-rejecting, but **no `code-anchor`-specific shape check** (the design wants additionally: single-line, parses as `path#symbol`). Note control-char rejection (incl. newline/tab) is **already** in the shared arm, so the "control chars rejected" test clause holds pre-split — T2's red step must prove the *added* shape constraints (a `path#symbol` with `#`/`/` passes; a multi-line value is **newly** rejected as not-single-line) and that splitting the arm leaves `String`/`Ref` byte-identical. `check_value_types_each_native_kind` (`write.rs:2783`) is the existing per-type test that T2 extends.
- **(c) The third scope bullet ("wire `insert_front_matter_field` to `set-field` so an absent optional header field is settable") is *already largely built* — verified, not assumed.** `set_field_validated` (`crates/engine/src/write.rs:2435-2495`) **already** routes `locate_field_value == None` (an absent optional field) to `insert_front_matter_field` for a header section / `insert_field` for a body group, proven by the engine test `absent_front_matter_ref_materializes_in_schema_order` (`write.rs:2977`) for `commit.implements`. The CLI `run_set_field` (`crates/cli/src/doc.rs:141-167`) **already** calls `set_field_validated`. So the engine+CLI plumbing exists; the design-review's B2 ("`set-field` can't insert an absent optional header field") was **refuted at the source level for the `commit` shape**. The genuine remaining work is therefore (i) the pack field on a *real* shipped doctype (`adr.cites-code`, whose header section id is **`status`**, not `commit`'s `header`) and (ii) a **binary-level proof** that the absent-insert path works for `adr` through `jigc doc set-field` end-to-end — the executor must PROVE this in a red step, never assert it pre-done; if the `adr`/`status` path diverges from the proven `commit`/`header` path, T4 adds the gap-closing wiring (T4).
- **(d) Pack discovery is generic** — `cli/src/doc.rs::schemas()` enumerates **all** `schemas/*.yaml` via the embedded pack tree + `load_schema`, so adding fields to `adr.yaml`/`spec.yaml` needs no registration; the `schema_adr_golden`/`schema_commit_golden` inline-snapshot goldens (`schema.rs:257-272`) pin the parsed projection and **must be re-pinned** when `adr.yaml` gains `cites-code` (a justified output-*adding* snapshot diff, hardening #4 — recorded here as intended). The design's worked declaration places `maps-to-test` as a `code-anchor` leaf in the `criteria` block (`document-type-schema.md:179`) and `cites-code` in `adr`'s `status` header (`document-type-schema.md:141`, `validation.md:141`).

*No genuine new fork surfaced.* The one item the locked design leaves illustrative — the **on-disk format by which the pack declares `(name, adjudicator-probe)`** (a new pack config file, or a `field-types:` block) and the **`load_schema` signature change** to thread the pack-declared-type set into deserialization-time validation — is a **doc-elaboration pin** (the design settles the *semantics*: meta-schema admits a pack-declared type + adjudicator binding, rejects an undeclared one with a typed error, engine ships none), **made and logged by the executor** per [increment-workflow.md](implementation/increment-workflow.md) → "doc-elaboration pins are made by the executor," not a halt. Risk-first, linear: the engine extension axis (T1) before the dispatch that consumes a pack-declared type (T2); the pack declaration + two doctype anchors (T3) ride on T1+T2; the binary authoring proof (T4) rides on T3's real `adr.cites-code` field. *Why this cut:* the smallest set delivering all five *Grouped scope* bullets — the open-enum + pack-declared axis (bullet 1 → T1), `check_value` pack-adjudicator dispatch + the `code-anchor` shape check (bullet 2 → T2), the pack `code-anchor` declaration + `adr.cites-code` + `spec.maps-to-test` (bullet 4 → T3), the `set-field` absent-insert wiring/proof (bullet 3 → T4). The fifth "Tests (fixtures + binary)" bullet is **not** a fifth task — its five clauses are folded into the per-task done-criteria (pack-declared field parses/round-trips → T3; undeclared pack type fails loudly at load → T1; `set-field` populates absent `adr.cites-code` → T4; `#`/`/` pass the shape check, control chars rejected → T2). No creep into inc 2 (the `Probe` seam reshape) / inc 3 (subprocess + meta-findings) / inc 4 (the `doc-code` executable) / inc 5 (the task-gate wiring + flow 13).

- **T1 (engine — open the `FieldType` enum to a pack-declared extension axis):** make the field-type vocabulary *engine-native variants + a pack-declared `(name, adjudicator-probe)` variant*, with `load_schema` admitting a pack-declared type and rejecting an undeclared one with a typed error; the engine ships **no** pack field type (engine-empty invariant holds). *Done:* `crates/engine/src/schema.rs` — `FieldType` (or its replacement) no longer hardcodes `CodeAnchor` as an engine variant; a pack supplies its declared `(name, adjudicator-probe)` set (the declaration on-disk form + the `load_schema` signature threading the declared-type set are executor elaboration pins — log them); `load_schema` of a schema whose `Field.ty` names an **undeclared** type (neither engine-native nor pack-declared) returns a typed `SchemaError::UnknownFieldType` (the variant the `schema.rs:192` doc-comment already names, made real), **never** a panic; an engine unit test asserts (i) a schema field typed `code-anchor` loads **only when** `code-anchor` is in the supplied pack-declared set and carries its `doc-code` adjudicator binding through the model, and (ii) a field typed `not-a-real-type` (undeclared) **fails loudly** with `UnknownFieldType` — gate green.
- **T2 (engine — `check_value` pack-adjudicator dispatch + the `code-anchor` write-time shape check):** split today's shared `String | Ref | CodeAnchor` arm so a pack-declared type runs its optional write-time *shape* check, leaving the engine-native arms byte-identical. *Done:* `crates/engine/src/write.rs` — `check_scalar` dispatches a pack-declared field type to its optional write-time shape check (its real adjudication is the bound finalize-time probe, built later); the `code-anchor` shape check is **non-empty + single-line + parses as `path#symbol`** (a bare `path` with no `#` is the file-existence form and also passes); `String`/`Ref` keep their existing non-empty + control-char-rejection semantics unchanged; the existing `check_value_types_each_native_kind` (`write.rs:2783`) test is extended (or a sibling added) asserting a `code-anchor` value with `#` and `/` (e.g. `crates/engine/src/validate.rs#validate_task`) **passes**, a bare-path `code-anchor` passes, a **multi-line** value is rejected (the newly-added single-line constraint), an empty value is rejected, and a value with an interior control char is rejected — gate green.
- **T3 (pack — declare `code-anchor` + add `adr.cites-code` + `spec.criteria/maps-to-test`):** the dev pack declares the `code-anchor` field type (bound to `doc-code`) and both shipped doctypes carry their optional anchor. *Done:* the dev pack declares `code-anchor` → `doc-code` in the pack-declared-type form T1 introduced (under `crates/cli/pack/`); `crates/cli/pack/schemas/adr.yaml` gains an **optional** `cites-code` field (`type: code-anchor`) in the `status` header section; `crates/cli/pack/schemas/spec.yaml`'s `criteria` repeatable block gains an **optional** `maps-to-test` leaf (`type: code-anchor`); the `schema_adr_golden`/`schema_spec`-style inline-snapshot golden(s) (`crates/engine/src/schema.rs` tests) are re-pinned to admit the added `cites-code` field (the justified, intended output-adding snapshot diff — grounding (d)); an engine/CLI test asserts `adr.yaml` and `spec.yaml` **load** with the pack-declared `code-anchor` set supplied (and would fail to load with it absent — closing the loop with T1), and a hand-authored `adr` instance carrying a `cites-code: <path>#<symbol>` header field **canonical-writes then re-parses idempotently** (the round-trip clause) — gate green.
- **T4 (cli + binary — `set-field` populates an absent optional `adr.cites-code` in-place):** prove (and wire iff the red step shows a gap) that `jigc doc set-field` populates an **absent** optional header field on a real `adr`. *Done:* a binary-level integration test (real `CARGO_BIN_EXE_jigc` over a throwaway repo) creates an `adr` with **no** `cites-code` line present, runs `jigc doc set-field <adr-addr>#status/cites-code --value <path>#<symbol> --task <id>`, and asserts (a) the command exits zero, (b) the staged `adr` now carries a single `cites-code:` line inserted **in schema order** inside the `status` front-matter (after `status`/`date`/`supersedes` per `adr.yaml`), (c) the buffer **re-parses** against the `adr` schema, and (d) a malformed value (a multi-line / empty / control-char `code-anchor`) is **blocked** with a `write.malformed-value` finding and nothing persisted; **if** the absent-insert path that works for `commit#header/implements` does **not** work for `adr#status/cites-code** (grounding (c) — the executor must confirm by running it, not assume), close the gap with the minimal wiring in `crates/cli/src/doc.rs` / `crates/engine/src/write.rs` (the `insert_front_matter_field` primitive already exists) — gate green.

- *Elaboration pin (T1 — pack-declared-type on-disk form + loader signature):* the design settles the *semantics* (engine-native ∪ pack-declared, undeclared → typed error, engine ships none) but leaves the *on-disk declaration form* and the *`load_schema` signature* illustrative. The executor chooses and logs the concrete form (e.g. a `field-types:` declaration the pack supplies, threaded into `load_schema` as the declared `(name, probe)` set); the binding is *type → probe* (a `code-anchor` leaf *means* `doc-code` applies, mirroring `ref` ⇒ `ref-resolves`), declared once per pack — [document-type-schema.md](design/document-type-schema.md) → Pack-declared field types. A doc-elaboration pin within the locked schema model, not a fork.
- *Grounding pin (T4 — B2 partially refuted at source):* the design-review's B2 framed `set-field` absent-header insert as wholly unbuilt; the engine path (`set_field_validated` → `insert_front_matter_field`) is in fact **built and tested for `commit`** (grounding (c)). T4 is therefore a **verification-first** task (prove for `adr`/`status` through the binary; wire only the residual gap, if any), never a from-scratch build — and the validator should expect the bulk of this bullet to be the binary-level proof + the pack field, not new engine machinery.

## 2026-06-06 — M10 Increment 1 / T1 shipped: `FieldType` opened to a pack-declared extension axis

`crates/engine/src/schema.rs` — `FieldType` is no longer a closed `Copy` enum with `CodeAnchor` hardcoded; it is **engine-native unit variants + `Pack(PackFieldType)`**, where `PackFieldType { name, adjudicator: Option<String> }` carries the pack's `(spelling, probe)` binding. The engine ships **no** pack type (engine-empty invariant holds) — `code-anchor` lands in the pack at T3. *Elaboration-pin resolution (the on-disk form + loader signature the planner left to the executor):*

- **Engine representation + loader signature (locked here):** `FieldType` hand-rolls `Serialize`/`Deserialize` as a **bare string** (round-trips the spelling). A non-native `type:` string deserializes to an *unresolved* `Pack { name, adjudicator: None }`; `load_schema_with_types(bytes, &[PackTypeDecl])` then **resolves** each unresolved `Pack` against the supplied set — a declared name is promoted to carry its `adjudicator: Some(probe)`, an **undeclared** name is `SchemaError::UnknownFieldType { field, ty }` (the variant the schema.rs doc-comment named aspirationally, now real; a typed error, never a panic, never a generic serde `Malformed`). `load_schema(bytes)` is preserved as the **native-only** wrapper (`= load_schema_with_types(bytes, &[])`), so the ~60 existing callers compile unchanged and a pack type loads **only** when its declaration is threaded in.
- **The pack's on-disk `field-types:` declaration form is deferred to T3** (the task that introduces a pack file naming `code-anchor → doc-code`). T1 fixes only the engine-side `(name, adjudicator)` carrier (`PackTypeDecl`) the loader threads; how a pack *spells* the declaration on disk is T3's to settle and wire into pack discovery. Within-spec elaboration, not a fork.
- **Knob blast-radius:** `knobs.rs` reuses the `FieldType` vocabulary and deserializes it directly; before this, a typo'd knob `type` was a closed-enum serde error. To preserve that strictness once the vocabulary opened, `load_knobs` now rejects a `Pack`-typed knob with a typed `KnobError::NonNativeType` (a pack field type is never a knob). Equivalent behaviour (non-native knob type still rejected), via a typed error instead of serde.

## 2026-06-06 — M10 Increment 1 / T2 shipped: `check_value` pack-adjudicator dispatch + the `code-anchor` write-time shape check

`crates/engine/src/write.rs` — `check_scalar`'s shared `String | Ref | CodeAnchor` arm is split. `String | Ref` keep their exact prior semantics (non-empty + single-line/no-control-char), now via a `check_opaque_scalar` helper. `FieldType::Pack(pack)` **dispatches on the pack type's spelling**: `code-anchor` runs `check_code_anchor` (the optional **write-time shape check**), any other pack-declared type falls back to the opaque floor. The match moved from `field.ty` to `&field.ty` (the enum is no longer `Copy` post-T1).

- **`code-anchor` write-time shape = non-empty + single-line + parses as `path#symbol`** — a bare `path` (no `#`) is the file-existence form and passes; a value with a `#` requires both a non-empty path and a non-empty symbol, and rejects a second `#`. This is the **cheap local gate only**; the *real* adjudicator is the bound `doc-code` probe at finalize (built in a later increment), so out-of-band-authored anchors are still caught by the probe, not this gate (`design/document-type-schema.md` → Pack-declared field types: Adjudication splits write-time vs finalize-time). `String`/`Ref` are explicitly **not** subject to the shape check (a `ref` carrying `#`/`/` stays accepted) — proven byte-identical by the extended `check_value_types_each_native_kind`.

## 2026-06-06 — M10 Increment 1 / T3 shipped: `code-anchor` declared in the dev pack + both shipped doctypes carry their optional anchor

`code-anchor` is now the **first pack-declared field type** — out of the engine, into the dev pack, bound to `doc-code`. The dev pack ships `crates/cli/pack/config/field-types.yaml` (the on-disk form T1 deferred to here); `adr.yaml`'s `status` header gains an optional `cites-code` (`code-anchor`) field and `spec.yaml`'s `criteria` block gains an optional `maps-to-test` (`code-anchor`) leaf. *Elaboration pins (made by the executor, within the locked schema model):*

- **On-disk declaration form (locked here):** a pack declares its field types in `config/field-types.yaml` — a YAML **sequence** of `{ name, adjudicator }` (`PackTypeDecl`, which gained `Serialize/Deserialize` + `deny_unknown_fields`). The CLI reads it via `pack::pack_field_types(pack)` (an **absent file = empty set**, never an error — a pack need not declare any) and threads it through the new `pack::load_pack_schema(pack, bytes)` wrapper, which is the **single CLI entry point** that replaces the bare engine `load_schema` at every *shipped-pack-schema* load site (`doc`/`task`/`milestone`/`ingest`/`start` schema loaders). The engine stays domain-empty: it ships no `code-anchor`, so the bare `load_schema` still rejects it (`UnknownFieldType`) — proven over the *shipped* bytes by `schema::tests::shipped_adr_and_spec_load_with_the_pack_set_and_fail_without_it` (the loop-closer with T1).
- **A pack-declared field type is author-OPTIONAL (genuine semantics elaboration):** `validate::is_author_required` now exempts any `FieldType::Pack(_)` (alongside the existing optional-`ref` exemption), because a pack type's *adjudication* — not its *presence* — is the finalize-time probe's job, the exact mirror of optional-`ref` ⇒ `ref-resolves`. Without this, the shipped `adr` (whose `cites-code` is always absent at create) would blocking-fail `required-field-present` on **every** task — surfaced by the `ingest`/`finalize` sweeps when the anchors landed, fixed test-first (`validate::…::an_omitted_pack_anchor_field_is_not_author_required`). This realizes the scope's "Both anchor fields are OPTIONAL" and DECISIONS 2026-06-06 (M10 inc-1: anchors optional, the probe blocks only on a *present-but-unresolvable* anchor).
- **Goldens re-pinned (justified, intended output-adding diff — hardening #4):** `schema_adr`/`schema_spec` snapshots gained the `cites-code`/`maps-to-test` rows; every engine test loading the shipped `adr`/`spec` bytes now threads `dev_pack_field_types()` (the bare `load_schema` would now reject them). A hand-authored `adr` carrying `cites-code: <path>#<symbol>` round-trips byte-identical (`write::canonical::adr_with_a_cites_code_anchor_round_trips_byte_identical`).

## 2026-06-06 — M10 Increment 1 / T4 shipped: `set-field` populates an absent optional `adr.cites-code` header field, proven through the binary

The verification-first proof (binary-level integration test `crates/cli/tests/set_field_absent.rs`) ran `jigc doc set-field adr:<slug>#status/cites-code` against a freshly-`create`d ADR and **refuted** the planning assumption that the `commit.implements` machinery already covered the `adr`/`status` shape unchanged. *Genuine gap found + fixed (engine, in T4 scope):*

- **Empty front-matter fence pair was un-insertable.** A `create`d ADR renders its front matter as an **empty** `---\n---` fence (defaults/`on-create` derivers are not materialized at create), and pulldown-cmark emits **no** `Block::Metadata` for a contentless fence — so `insert_front_matter_field` failed with `write.wrong-shape` ("no front-matter block to insert into"). `commit#header/implements` never hit this because the commit doc renders `type:`/`scope:` lines, giving a non-empty fence. Fix (engine/`write.rs`): `front_matter_content` now falls back to `empty_front_matter_content`, which detects a leading empty `---\n---` fence pair directly and returns the **zero-width** inner-content range just past the opening fence, so the first header field slots in between the fences in schema order. Pinned by `write::set_field_generate::absent_field_materializes_into_empty_front_matter_fence` (engine) + the binary test (CLI).
- **No CLI change needed:** `run_set_field` → `set_field_validated` → `insert_front_matter_field` was already wired; the address form `#status/cites-code` resolves through the existing `field_target` two-hop. The fix was purely the engine's empty-fence detection.

## 2026-06-06 — M10 Increment 2 planning: decomposition

Cut [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → M10 Increment 2 ("the reshaped `Probe` seam + serializable effective-state snapshot + target enumeration — the engine plumbing") into three ordered, single-concern tasks, each with an observable red step. The increment retires the **reshape risk** — admitting the doc-code shape (a `target` + a *serializable* effective-state ctx) onto the seam without breaking the lone existing impl (`override-default`), and giving the engine the means to **name which anchors a task must check** — in isolation, before the subprocess (inc 3) and the real `doc-code` resolver (inc 4) ride on it. **No probe is invoked over the snapshot this increment** beyond an **in-process test probe**; the subprocess invoker + meta-findings are inc 3. *Grounding (read the code at HEAD `f4415d4`, exercised against the M10 inc-2 shape):*

- **(a) The `Probe` seam is the M6 non-task shape — `target`-less and non-serializable.** `crate::probe::Probe::check(&self, ctx: OverrideCtx<'_>) -> Vec<Finding>` (`crates/engine/src/probe.rs:51-55`); `OverrideCtx<'a> { deltas: RecordedDeltas<'a>, pack: &'a dyn PackSource }` is a **live, borrowed, non-serializable** handle (a trait-object pack ref + borrowed delta slices) and carries **no `target`**. The roadmap's reshape is `check(target, ctx)` with a *serializable* effective-state ctx. **The shape this lands on is a verified design point, not assumed:** `override-default`'s ctx genuinely is `(deltas, pack)` — target-less and non-serializable — so the reshaped seam must admit **both** a serializable effective-state ctx (the doc-code shape) **and** `override-default`'s existing non-task shape **with no behaviour change**. T1's red step must *prove* the migrated `override-default` is byte-identical (its findings + their order + severity post-pass unchanged), never assert it. The concrete Rust form of the reshaped trait (e.g. a `target` parameter + a ctx that is either an enum over the two shapes, an associated type, or a generic) and the snapshot struct + its serde form are **doc-elaboration pins** within the locked seam semantics ([module-layout.md](implementation/module-layout.md) → Probe boundary; [validation.md](design/validation.md) → The wire contract) — made and logged by the executor, not a halt.
- **(b) The lone live caller + the tests that lock byte-identity.** `OverrideDefaultProbe.check(OverrideCtx{…})` is called in exactly one production site (`crates/cli/src/upgrade.rs:68`); the seam's one engine unit test is `probe::tests::override_default_probe_delegates_to_classify`. The flow-8 / upgrade acceptance that must stay green: `crates/cli/tests/flow8_override_default_warning.rs`, `crates/cli/tests/upgrade.rs`, `crates/cli/tests/upgrade_v1_v2.rs`, `crates/cli/tests/severity_tuning.rs`. `OverrideDefaultProbe::check` delegates to `override_default::classify(deltas, pack)` — the reshape changes the *call shape*, never the reconciliation.
- **(c) Effective-state docs are two surfaces, both already reachable.** *Created/edited* (working deltas) are staged under `<task_dir>/docs/<type>:<slug>.md` and enumerated in path-sorted order by `validate::staged_instances` (`crates/engine/src/validate.rs:164`). *Bound read-roles* live as `<type>:<slug>` addresses in the task's `roles.json` (`engine::state::RolesRecord`), resolved to committed `<location>/<slug>.md` bytes the way `start.rs`'s committed-store / overlay path already resolves a bound `{{@task.spec#criteria}}` slice (the M3 proven path). The enumeration walks **both**, and must **skip committed docs not in effective state** (a committed `adr`/`spec` neither edited nor bound contributes nothing) — the design's masking-trap guard ([validation.md](design/validation.md) → Target surface).
- **(d) A `code-anchor` leaf is a typed field, enumerated directly — never the edge index.** Post-inc-1, a `code-anchor` field is `schema::FieldType::Pack(PackFieldType { name: "code-anchor", adjudicator: Some("doc-code") })` (`crates/engine/src/schema.rs`). After parsing a doc against its schema (`parse::parse_sections` → `Document`), the anchor *values* live as opaque text in `parse::ParsedSection.fields` (the `adr.cites-code` **header** field) and `parse::ParsedItem.fields` (the `spec` `criteria/<id>/maps-to-test` **repeatable-item** leaf), each a `field_block::Field { key, value }`. The enumeration matches a field against its schema declaration to find the `code-anchor`-typed leaves and emits `(target-address, anchor-value, check-id)` — **direct field enumeration**, *not* the edge-index blast-radius walk (a `code-anchor` is a field, not an edge; [doctype-map.md](implementation/doctype-map.md), [validation.md](design/validation.md) → Target surface). The check-id is `symbol-exists` for `adr.cites-code` and `criterion-maps-to-test` for `spec` `maps-to-test`, so the probe applies the test predicate only to the latter (the design-itemized snapshot contents, [validation.md](design/validation.md):103).
- **(e) The snapshot is read-only + recompute-on-demand.** The wire contract carries the snapshot **by path-ref** (`effective_state: { snapshot_path }`, [validation.md](design/validation.md):106) — the engine *materializes* it; the actual subprocess hand-off is inc 3. M10 planning resolved findings to **recompute-on-demand** (no cache layer — a subprocess probe is read-only + deterministic). This increment proves only that the engine can **build + serialize** the snapshot (the `(target-address, anchor-value, check-id)` pairs + the working-tree root) and that an **in-process test probe** can consume it over the serialized form.

*No genuine new fork surfaced.* Every shape below is settled: the reshaped-seam target+serializable-ctx in [validation.md](design/validation.md) → The wire contract ("M10 reshapes the seam") + [module-layout.md](implementation/module-layout.md) → Probe boundary; the direct-enumeration target surface (created/edited ∪ bound, skip committed-not-in-effective-state) in [validation.md](design/validation.md) → Target surface (the design-review B1 fix); the snapshot contents itemized in [validation.md](design/validation.md):103. The only illustrative items — the concrete reshaped-trait Rust signature, the ctx/target carrier shape, and the snapshot struct + serde form — are **doc-elaboration pins** made + logged by the executor. Risk-first, linear: the seam reshape with `override-default` byte-identical first (T1 — retire "don't break the lone impl" before anything rides the new shape), the direct anchor enumeration over effective state next (T2 — the engine names *which* anchors), the serializable snapshot the wire carries by path-ref last (T3 — proven end-to-end with an in-process test probe). *Why this cut:* the smallest set delivering all four *Grouped scope* bullets — the seam reshape + `override-default` migration (bullet 1 → T1), the target-surface enumeration (bullet 2 → T2), the materialized serializable snapshot (bullet 3 → T3). The fourth "Tests (fixtures)" bullet is **not** a fourth task — its four clauses fold into the per-task done-criteria (in-process test probe over a serialized snapshot → T3; enumeration finds anchors in created **and** bound docs + skips committed-not-in-effective-state → T2; `override-default` byte-identical through the reshaped seam → T1). No creep into inc 3 (subprocess invoker + meta-findings) / inc 4 (the `doc-code` executable) / inc 5 (the task-gate wiring + flow 13) — **no real probe is invoked over the snapshot here**, only an in-process test double.
