- **T1 (engine — reshape the `Probe` seam to `check(target, ctx)` with a serializable effective-state ctx; migrate `override-default` with no behaviour change):** the seam admits a `target` + a *serializable* effective-state ctx (the doc-code shape) **and** `override-default`'s existing non-task `(deltas, pack)` shape, with `override-default` byte-identical. *Done:* `crates/engine/src/probe.rs` — the `Probe` seam carries a `target` and the ctx surface is reshaped so a serializable effective-state ctx is representable (the concrete carrier — a `target` param + an enum/associated-type/generic over the two ctx shapes — is an executor elaboration pin; log it); `OverrideDefaultProbe` is migrated onto the reshaped seam still delegating to `override_default::classify`; the one live caller (`crates/cli/src/upgrade.rs:68`) compiles against the new shape; an engine unit test proves `OverrideDefaultProbe` over the reshaped seam returns **exactly** what `classify(deltas, pack)` returns over the same `(deltas, pack)` (the migrated-`probe.rs` test), and the flow-8 / upgrade acceptance suite (`flow8_override_default_warning.rs`, `upgrade.rs`, `upgrade_v1_v2.rs`, `severity_tuning.rs`) is **green with goldens untouched** (no behaviour change) — gate green.
- **T2 (engine — target-surface enumeration over the task's effective-state docs):** the engine enumerates every `code-anchor` leaf as a `(target-address, anchor-value, check-id)` pair over the task's effective state — the docs **created/edited** (staged `<dir>/docs/*.md`) ∪ the docs **bound** into read-roles (`roles.json` → committed `<location>/<slug>.md`) — by **direct field enumeration**, never the edge index. *Done:* a new engine enumeration (an `engine` module, e.g. extending `validate`/`state` or a new `target_surface` module) walks the staged instances and the bound committed instances, parses each against its schema, and collects every field whose `FieldType::Pack` names `code-anchor` (header field + repeatable-item leaf) into a deterministically-ordered (stable address-sorted) list of `(target-address, anchor-value, check-id)` pairs, with check-id = `symbol-exists` for `adr.cites-code` / `criterion-maps-to-test` for `spec` `maps-to-test`; a fixture-task test proves (i) anchors in a **created** `adr` **and** a **bound** `spec` are both found, (ii) a committed `adr`/`spec` that is **neither edited nor bound** contributes **zero** pairs (the masking-trap guard — hardening #5: exercised against effective state that **omits** an unrelated committed doc), and (iii) a doc with no `code-anchor` value contributes zero pairs — gate green.
- **T3 (engine — materialize the serializable effective-state snapshot; prove with an in-process test probe):** the engine materializes the read-only snapshot the wire contract carries by path-ref — the enumerated `(target-address, anchor-value, check-id)` pairs (T2) + the working-tree root — as a *serializable* value, and an in-process test probe consumes it over the serialized form. *Done:* a serializable snapshot type (the executor pins its struct + serde form — itemized: the pairs + the working-tree root, [validation.md](design/validation.md):103, recompute-on-demand) is built from T2's enumeration; an engine unit test round-trips the snapshot through its serialization (serialize → deserialize → equal, **serialization unit-proven**); and an **in-process test probe** (an `impl Probe` test double, not a subprocess — that is inc 3) is handed a `(target, ctx)` carrying the **serialized** snapshot and returns findings derived from the pairs it reads (proving the reshaped seam admits a probe consuming the serialized effective-state ctx end-to-end) — gate green.

- *Elaboration pin (T1 — reshaped-trait signature + ctx/target carrier):* the design settles the *semantics* (the seam carries a `target` + a serializable effective-state ctx, and `override-default`'s non-task `(deltas, pack)` shape survives byte-identical) but leaves the concrete Rust carrier illustrative. The executor chooses + logs the form (a `target` parameter plus either an enum over the two ctx shapes, an associated type per probe, or a generic ctx); the binding is that an *in-process* and a *subprocess* probe consume the **same logical input** (a live graph vs. its serialized read-only snapshot + path-ref), [validation.md](design/validation.md) → The wire contract. A doc-elaboration pin within the locked seam model, not a fork.
- *Elaboration pin (T3 — snapshot struct + serde form):* the design itemizes the snapshot *contents* (`(target-address, anchor-value, check-id)` pairs + the working-tree root; the code is read from the repo directly, **not** copied into the snapshot — [validation.md](design/validation.md):103) and that the wire carries it **by path-ref**, but leaves the concrete struct + serde encoding illustrative. The executor pins it (a serializable Rust type with a stable field order); the actual subprocess path-ref hand-off is inc 3. Within-spec elaboration, not a fork.
- *Grounding pin (T2/T3 — in-process only this increment):* the roadmap's "in-process test probe over a serialized snapshot" is deliberate — **no subprocess is invoked** in inc 2 (the `std::process::Command` invoker + the `pack-probe-integrity.*` meta-findings are inc 3, and the real tree-sitter `doc-code` executable is inc 4). The validator should expect this increment to land **no** CLI subprocess machinery and **no** real probe scheduling in `validate_task` — only the reshaped engine seam, the engine-side enumeration, the serializable snapshot, and an in-process test double.

## 2026-06-06 — M10 Increment 2 / T1 shipped: `Probe` seam reshaped to `check(target, ctx)`; `override-default` migrated byte-identical

- **Carrier pin resolved — a `target` parameter + two associated types (`Target`, `Ctx<'a>`).** `trait Probe { type Target; type Ctx<'a>; fn check<'a>(&self, target: &Self::Target, ctx: Self::Ctx<'a>) -> Vec<Finding>; }` (`crates/engine/src/probe.rs`). Chosen over the enum-over-both-ctx-shapes and single-generic-ctx alternatives the pin (DECISIONS 2026-06-06, M10 inc-2 decomposition) left open, because each probe must declare *exactly* its own target + ctx with **no coercion**: an enum variant would force `override-default`'s live `&dyn PackSource` handle to share a type with the serializable doc-code snapshot, and a single generic ctx loses per-probe specificity. Per-probe associated types let `OverrideDefaultProbe` keep `Target = ()` (target-less — its ctx names the override surface it reconciles) + `Ctx<'a> = OverrideCtx<'a>` (the M6 live, non-serializable handle, **unchanged**), while the doc-code probe (inc 3+) will declare `Target = Address` + a serializable snapshot `Ctx`. `Ctx<'a>` is a **GAT** (stable since 1.65; edition-2024 / Rust 1.95 here) so a borrowing ctx (`OverrideCtx<'a>`) and a borrowed-snapshot ctx are both expressible — the binding ("in-process and subprocess probes consume the *same logical input*", validation.md → The wire contract) holds: the serializable ctx is *representable* without the live ctx being forced serializable.
- **`override-default` byte-identical (verified red, not asserted).** The migration changes only the *call shape* (`check(&(), ctx)` — a `target` it ignores), never `override_default::classify`. Red was genuine: the reshaped `check<'a>(target, ctx)` signature does not compile against the M6 `check(&self, ctx)` call sites, so the migrated `probe::tests::override_default_probe_delegates_to_classify` (now driving `check(&(), OverrideCtx{…})`) and the lone live caller (`crates/cli/src/upgrade.rs:68`) had to move onto the new shape before the suite went green. The flow-8/upgrade acceptance suite (`flow8_override_default_warning.rs`, `upgrade.rs`, `upgrade_v1_v2.rs`, `severity_tuning.rs`) passes with **goldens untouched** — no behaviour change. Files: `crates/engine/src/probe.rs`, `crates/cli/src/upgrade.rs`.

## 2026-06-06 — M10 Increment 2 / T2 shipped: target-surface enumeration over the task's effective-state docs

- **Direct enumeration, never the edge index (B1 fix realized in code).** New `engine::target_surface::enumerate_target_surface(task_dir, repo_root, schemas) -> Vec<TargetAnchor>` walks the task's effective-state docs and collects every `FieldType::Pack("code-anchor")` leaf (a *field*, not an edge — `doctype-map.md`) into address-sorted `(address, anchor_value, check_id)` triples. Effective state = **created/edited** (staged `<task_dir>/docs/*.md`, the `staged_instances` surface) ∪ **bound** (`roles.json` addresses resolved to committed `<location>/<slug>.md` via `store::canonical_path`, the M3 `{{@task.spec}}` path). A committed doc neither edited nor bound contributes **zero** pairs — exercised red (the masking-trap guard, hardening #5: the test stages an unrelated committed ADR with its own anchor and asserts it never appears; dropping the bound surface fails the test, proving both surfaces load-bearing).
- **`check_id` discriminator is POSITION, not field name (elaboration pin).** A `code-anchor` in a header/simple section → `symbol-exists` (the `adr.cites-code` floor); a `code-anchor` in a repeatable-item block → `criterion-maps-to-test` (the `spec` `criteria/<id>/maps-to-test` headline). Position is the structural discriminator the design draws (`validation.md` → What it checks: the criterion's anchor is the only one carrying the test predicate), and it matches both shipped anchors exactly without hard-coding `cites-code`/`maps-to-test` by name — a new header anchor gets `symbol-exists`, a new criterion anchor gets the test predicate, no code change. New module `crates/engine/src/target_surface.rs`; `state::DOCS_DIR` already `pub(crate)`. Output address-sorted + deduped so it is reproducible regardless of directory-read order. Feeds T3's serializable snapshot.

## 2026-06-06 — M10 Increment 2 / T3 shipped: serializable effective-state snapshot + in-process test probe over its serialized form

- **Snapshot struct + serde form pinned (the T3 elaboration pin the planner left open).** `engine::probe::EffectiveStateSnapshot { anchors: Vec<TargetAnchor>, working_tree_root: PathBuf }` — built from T2's enumeration via `EffectiveStateSnapshot::new(anchors, working_tree_root)`. **Field order is `anchors` then `working_tree_root`**, locked by an inline `insta` golden over the pretty JSON (a reorder/serde-slip breaks it — verified red: reordering the two fields fails `snapshot_round_trips_through_serde`). The snapshot carries **no code** — per determinism-contract rule 4 the probe reads the cited bytes directly from `working_tree_root` (`validation.md`:103: "the code is read directly from the repo, not copied into the snapshot"); the snapshot carries only the `(target-address, anchor-value, check-id)` pairs + the root. The wire carries it **by path-ref** out-of-process; the actual subprocess path-ref hand-off is inc 3.
- **The reshaped seam admits a serialized-ctx probe end-to-end (in-process double, NO subprocess).** A test `InProcessDocCodeProbe` (`impl Probe`) declares `Target = Address` + `Ctx<'a> = &'a EffectiveStateSnapshot` and emits one finding per snapshot anchor whose file the working tree lacks — handed a `(target, ctx)` over a snapshot that crossed `serialize -> deserialize` (the exact bytes the path-ref will carry), proving a probe consumes the **serialized** effective-state ctx, not a live handle. No `std::process::Command` invoker, no `pack-probe-integrity.*` meta-findings, no `validate_task` scheduling lands this increment (inc 3); the real tree-sitter resolve is inc 4. File: `crates/engine/src/probe.rs`.

## 2026-06-06 — M10 Increment 3 planning: decomposition

Cut [roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md) → M10 Increment 3 ("the subprocess invoker + wire contract + the meta-findings — the contract, *satisfied not waived*, the #1 watch item") into four ordered, single-concern tasks, each with an observable red step. The increment retires **the milestone's #1 risk** — proving the determinism contract is *genuinely enforced, not a trust-the-pack stub* — by standing up the subprocess **invoker** (CLI, `std::process::Command` + a wait-timeout, engine stays shell-free), the **concrete wire contract** (engine request/response types over the inc-2 snapshot), and the **three enforced meta-findings** (`pack-probe-integrity.{timeout,crash,malformed-output}`, intrinsic blocking, floor-locked), all proven against **adversarial test stubs** (well-behaved / sleeps-past-budget / crashes / emits non-JSON) **before the real `doc-code` program exists** (inc 4). Homes of record: [validation.md](design/validation.md) → The wire contract + Failure semantics + What "intrinsic" means mechanically; [module-layout.md](implementation/module-layout.md) → Probe boundary. *Grounding (read the code at HEAD `9d3ae3b`, exercised against the M10 inc-3 shape):*

- **(a) The reshaped seam + serializable snapshot are built and proven (inc 2), but nothing invokes a subprocess yet.** `engine::probe::{Probe, EffectiveStateSnapshot}` (`crates/engine/src/probe.rs`) carry the `target` + serializable ctx; `EffectiveStateSnapshot { anchors, working_tree_root }` serde-round-trips under a pinned golden. The only doc-code consumer today is the **`#[cfg(test)]` `InProcessDocCodeProbe`** (probe.rs:204) — a test double, NOT a production probe. So the invoker, the wire request/response envelope, and meta-finding synthesis are **all net-new** this increment; none is a wiring of an existing production probe.
- **(b) No subprocess machinery exists.** Repo-wide, the only `std::process::Command` use is `crates/cli/src/start.rs:42` (git); there is **no** wait-timeout dependency in either `Cargo.toml` and **no** thread+channel timeout pattern anywhere. The wait-timeout *mechanism* is an **executor elaboration pin** (the design settles the *requirement* — "a bounded wall-clock budget the CLI invoker enforces", `validation.md`:129 — leaving the mechanism illustrative); minimal-deps reflex (20 deps, std `Command` already present) favors a std thread + `recv_timeout` around `child.wait()` over a new crate, executor's call.
- **(c) The meta-finding `(probe, check)` handle is a code-id reconciliation, exactly like `override-default`.** `validation.md`:120-122 emits the *descriptive* shape `probe-failure { probe, reason: timeout }`, but the **inventory check ids** (`validation.md`:205-208) and the cascade keys are `pack-probe-integrity.{timeout,crash,malformed-output}` / `validation.pack-probe-integrity.<reason>.severity`. So a synthesized meta-finding must carry `probe = "pack-probe-integrity"`, `check = "<reason>"` (the canonical handle the post-pass + floor key on), with the descriptive text in `code`/`message` — the same five-codes-onto-three-checks pattern `Finding::with_check` (`finding.rs:207`) already supports. The post-pass routes a finding **only if its `(probe, check)` is a `CHECK_INVENTORY` row** (`result.rs:197`, matched by membership never code-prefix), so the three meta-findings **must** be added to `CHECK_INVENTORY` or their floor is never even consulted.
- **(d) The floor-lock machinery is built; inc 3 adds the three keys to it.** `KnobSet::floors` + the load-time assertion over `INTRINSIC_CHECK_KEYS` (`knobs.rs:50-64,205-212`) already soft-reject a below-floor `scalar-set` and fail-loud an unfloored/undeclared intrinsic. Today `INTRINSIC_CHECK_KEYS` lists the 13 `workflow-refs.*`/`schema-conformance.*` checks; `knobs.yaml` declares them floored. Inc 3 **adds the three `validation.pack-probe-integrity.*.severity` keys** to `INTRINSIC_CHECK_KEYS` + `knobs.yaml` (`floor: blocking`, `default: blocking`) + `CHECK_INVENTORY`; the `loaded_knobs_seed_the_pack_default_scalar_surface` (`knobs.rs:241`) closed-surface golden and `embedded_knobs_floor_the_intrinsic_checks` (`knobs.rs:466`) intrinsic-set assertion **must be re-pinned** to admit exactly these three (a justified, intended additive diff — hardening #4, recorded here as intended).

**Scope-seam resolved (tracked, not a fork) — the `pack-probe-integrity.*` inventory rows land HERE (inc 3), the `doc-code.*` rows land at inc 5.** The roadmap's inc-5 bullet ([roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md):639) says *both* the `doc-code.{symbol-exists,criterion-maps-to-test}` (tunable) **and** `pack-probe-integrity.{timeout,crash,malformed-output}` (intrinsic) rows "land in `CHECK_INVENTORY` + `knobs.yaml`" at inc 5 — but inc-3's own scope (bullet 4 / *Proves*) requires proving each meta-finding "is blocking and **cannot be demoted below `blocking`** (floor-rejected at resolution)", which **mechanically requires** the `pack-probe-integrity.*` keys to exist in `knobs.yaml` (`floor: blocking`), in `INTRINSIC_CHECK_KEYS` (the load-time floor-assertion set), and in `CHECK_INVENTORY` (or the post-pass never routes them) **this increment** — a stubbed proof would defeat the whole point of the #1 watch item. The two readings reconcile with no narrowing: **inc 3 lands the three intrinsic `pack-probe-integrity.*` rows** (required for its floor-rejection proof — T4 below); **inc 5 lands the two tunable `doc-code.*` rows** (which need the real probe + the severity-demotion acceptance) and reconciles the count to the [validation.md](design/validation.md):210 inventory (25 checks / 16 intrinsic). This is a *staged-across-increments* delivery of one roadmap clause, named to its landings per [increment-workflow.md](implementation/increment-workflow.md) → Validation hardening #6 — the inc-5 planner must add the `doc-code.*` rows and is responsible for the final count reconciliation; the inc-3 validator confirms the three `pack-probe-integrity.*` rows are present + floor-locked, never the `doc-code.*` two.

*No genuine new fork surfaced.* Every shape is settled: the request/response envelope (`{probe_id, target, effective_state:{snapshot_path}, config, schema_version}` → `{findings, schema_version}`) in [validation.md](design/validation.md):106-112; the snapshot carried **by path-ref** (the engine materializes it, the invoker hands the path) in `validation.md`:101; the three enforced meta-findings + the deferred `sandbox-violation` (no `knobs.yaml` key, not in the floor-assertion set) in `validation.md`:127-129,236; the intrinsic-blocking floor mechanics in `validation.md`:176,230-236. The illustrative items — the concrete Rust request/response struct shapes + serde form, the wait-timeout mechanism, and the invoker's raw-outcome carrier type (stdout bytes + exit/timed-out status the engine ingests) — are **doc-elaboration pins** made + logged by the executor. Risk-first, linear: the wire-contract types first (T1 — the envelope both sides bind to, before anything sends it), the CLI invoker over real adversarial stub processes next (T2 — the wall-clock budget + read-only invocation proven against a sleeper/crasher/garbage-emitter), the engine ingestion + meta-finding synthesis over the invoker's outcome (T3 — well-behaved findings ingest; misbehavior synthesizes the right meta-finding with the canonical `(probe, check)`), the floor-lock that makes the meta-findings undemotable (T4 — knobs + intrinsic-set + inventory, proven by a soft-rejected below-floor `scalar-set`). *Why this cut:* the smallest set delivering all four *Grouped scope* bullets — the wire contract (bullet 2 → T1), the CLI subprocess invoker keeping the engine shell-free (bullet 1 → T2), the meta-finding synthesis + ingestion (bullets 1+3 → T3), the three intrinsic floor-locked meta-findings (bullet 3's "intrinsic blocking" + bullet 4's "cannot be demoted below `blocking`" → T4). The fourth "Tests (fixtures)" bullet is **not** a fifth task — its clauses fold into the per-task done-criteria (well-behaved stub ingests → T3; sleeps-past-budget → timeout → T2 raises the timed-out outcome, T3 synthesizes the meta-finding; non-zero/no-JSON → crash and non-JSON → malformed-output → T3; each meta-finding blocking + floor-rejected at resolution → T4). **No real `doc-code` resolver, no tree-sitter, no `validate_task` scheduling, no flow-13 acceptance** lands this increment — those are inc 4 (the executable) and inc 5 (the gate wiring + acceptance); inc 3's probe stubs are **throwaway adversarial test fixtures**, never the shipped probe.

- **T1 (engine — the concrete wire request/response contract):** the serde types the invoker sends and the engine parses, concretizing `validation.md`'s illustrative envelope; the snapshot is carried **by path-ref** (`effective_state: { snapshot_path }`), never inlined. *Done:* `crates/engine/src/probe.rs` (or a sibling) defines a serializable **request** type projecting to `{ probe_id, target, effective_state: { snapshot_path }, config, schema_version }` and a **response** type projecting to `{ findings: [<Finding>], schema_version }`, both with explicit pinned `serde` attributes and the `schema_version` field present on both sides (the contract-evolution marker; the *policy* stays deferred); the response's `findings` deserialize into the engine's one [`Finding`] shape (`finding.rs`); an engine unit test pins the request/response JSON projection via an inline `insta` golden (a field rename / reorder / serde-slip breaks it — the wire is a stable contract a separate-program probe is built against), and asserts a response round-trips serialize → deserialize → equal — gate green. **No invoker, no process spawn, no meta-findings this task.**
- **T2 (cli — the subprocess invoker over real adversarial stub processes):** `std::process::Command` + a wait-timeout, so the engine never shells out; the invoker writes the JSON request to the child's stdin, enforces a **bounded wall-clock budget**, and returns a raw outcome the engine ingests (the stdout bytes + an exit/timed-out status — the invoker classifies *nothing*; classification is T3's engine job). *Done:* `crates/cli/src/` gains the invoker (`fn` taking the probe program path + the T1 request, returning the raw outcome carrier — its concrete shape an elaboration pin); a CLI integration test builds **real stub probe executables** (a test-fixture binary, or a script the test writes + marks executable) and drives the invoker against four: a **well-behaved** stub (echoes a valid JSON response) → the invoker returns its stdout + zero exit; a stub that **sleeps past the budget** → the invoker returns a **timed-out** outcome (and the child is killed, not leaked — assert it does not hang the test); a stub that **exits non-zero with no JSON** → a non-zero-exit outcome; a stub that **emits non-JSON on stdout** → the raw (unparseable) bytes + zero exit. The wall-clock budget is a real elapsed-time bound (the sleeper exceeds it; the well-behaved one is well under). The engine crate has **no** `std::process` use added (shell-free invariant — assert by inspection / a grep guard if cheap) — gate green.
- **T3 (engine — response ingestion + the three meta-finding synthesis):** given the invoker's raw outcome, the engine ingests a well-behaved probe's findings into the report or synthesizes the right `pack-probe-integrity.*` meta-finding; the engine never crashes on a misbehaving probe. *Done:* `crates/engine/src/probe.rs` (or sibling) gains the ingestion/synthesis fn — on a **well-behaved** outcome it parses the T1 response and returns its `findings` (ingested verbatim, severity left for the post-pass); on a **timed-out** outcome it synthesizes one finding with `probe = "pack-probe-integrity"`, `check = "timeout"` (canonical handle, grounding (c)), `severity = Blocking`; on a **non-zero-exit / no-JSON** outcome → `check = "crash"`; on a **zero-exit-but-unparseable-JSON** outcome → `check = "malformed-output"`; each synthesized via `Finding::graded(..).with_check(..)` (or equivalent) so `(probe, check)` is the inventory handle while `code`/`message` carry the descriptive detail; an engine unit test feeds each of the four outcome shapes and asserts (well-behaved) the probe's findings appear ingested + no meta-finding; (each misbehavior) **exactly one** `pack-probe-integrity.{timeout,crash,malformed-output}` blocking finding with the asserted `(probe, check)` and **no panic** — gate green. **No `validate_task` wiring** (that is inc 5); this is the pure synthesis fn proven in isolation.
- **T4 (engine + pack — the three meta-findings are intrinsic + floor-locked):** the `pack-probe-integrity.*` checks join the inventory + the intrinsic floor-assertion set + the knob surface, so a synthesized meta-finding is post-pass-routed, defaults blocking, and a `scalar-set` below `blocking` is **soft-rejected at resolution**. *Done:* `crates/cli/pack/config/knobs.yaml` gains the three `validation.pack-probe-integrity.{timeout,crash,malformed-output}.severity` keys (`type: enum`, `of: [blocking, warning, advisory]`, `default: blocking`, `floor: blocking`); `crates/engine/src/knobs.rs` `INTRINSIC_CHECK_KEYS` gains the three (so the load-time assertion floors them and a stripped/unfloored one fails loud — extend `unfloored_intrinsic`/`undeclared_intrinsic` coverage or add a sibling); `crates/engine/src/result.rs` `CHECK_INVENTORY` gains `("pack-probe-integrity","timeout"|"crash"|"malformed-output")` (so the post-pass routes them, grounding (c)); the closed-surface golden `loaded_knobs_seed_the_pack_default_scalar_surface` (`knobs.rs:241`) and the intrinsic-set assertion `embedded_knobs_floor_the_intrinsic_checks` (`knobs.rs:466`) are **re-pinned** to admit exactly these three (the justified additive diff — grounding (d)); a test proves a project `scalar-set validation.pack-probe-integrity.timeout.severity advisory` is **soft-rejected** at cascade resolution (the meta-finding stays `blocking`, the rejection surfaces as a `RejectedDemotion`, mirroring the existing demotion-lock tests) — i.e. the meta-finding **cannot** be demoted below blocking — gate green. **Only the three `pack-probe-integrity.*` rows; the `doc-code.*` tunable rows are inc 5** (scope-seam above).

- *Elaboration pin (T1/T2 — wire struct shapes + invoker outcome carrier + wait-timeout mechanism):* the design settles the *envelope* (`validation.md`:106) and the *requirement* (bounded wall-clock, read-only, by-path-ref snapshot) but leaves the concrete Rust types, the invoker's raw-outcome carrier (stdout bytes + exit/timed-out status), and the timeout mechanism (a std thread + `recv_timeout` around `child.wait()` vs. a `wait-timeout` crate) illustrative. The executor chooses + logs the concrete forms; the binding is *the engine classifies the outcome, the CLI only invokes + times out* (so the shell-free invariant + the engine-owned-severity invariant both hold). Doc-elaboration pins within the locked wire contract, not forks.
- *Cross-increment punt (tracked to its landing):* the two **`doc-code.{symbol-exists,criterion-maps-to-test}`** tunable inventory rows ([roadmap.md](implementation/roadmap.md):639) are deliberately **not** delivered in inc 3 — they ride to **inc 5** (the gate-wiring increment), which needs the real probe + the severity-demotion acceptance to exercise them and which reconciles the final 25/16/9 count. The inc-5 planner owns adding them; the inc-3 validator confirms only the three `pack-probe-integrity.*` rows landed. Named here so the punt is tracked to a verified landing (hardening #6), never left to "a later task" in the abstract.

## 2026-06-06 — M10 Increment 3 / T1 shipped: the concrete wire request/response contract

- **Elaboration pin resolved — the concrete Rust wire types (within the locked envelope).** `engine::probe::{ProbeRequest, ProbeEffectiveState, ProbeResponse}` (`crates/engine/src/probe.rs`) concretize `validation.md`:106-112's illustrative `{probe_id, target, effective_state:{snapshot_path}, config, schema_version}` → `{findings, schema_version}`. Three pins inside the locked shape: (1) **`target` is the address grammar's string form** (`String`), not the internal [`Address`] enum — the wire carries the flat URI a separate-program probe reads (matching `TargetAnchor.address`, already a `String`); the engine parses it where it needs the typed form. (2) **`config` is `serde_json::Map<String, Value>`** (an opaque object) — always present, projecting `{}` when the cascade resolves none; this keeps the envelope honest without prematurely typing a config schema the design defers. (3) **`schema_version` reuses `result::SCHEMA_VERSION` (=2)** — the wire shares the engine's one contract-evolution marker rather than minting a second; the *versioning policy* stays deferred (`validation.md`:112). Field order is **pinned** on both structs (`probe_id, target, effective_state, config, schema_version` / `findings, schema_version`) and golden-locked — a reorder/rename/serde-slip breaks `probe::tests::request_json_projection_is_the_pinned_envelope` (verified red by a deliberate field swap). The response's `findings` deserialize into the engine's one [`Finding`] shape from **probe-authored** JSON (a probe may omit the `(probe, check)` handle — `#[serde(default)]` — and the engine derives it downstream), and a response round-trips serialize → deserialize → equal. **No invoker, no process spawn, no meta-findings this task** (T2/T3/T4).

## 2026-06-06 — M10 Increment 3 / T2 shipped: the CLI subprocess invoker over real adversarial stub processes

- **Elaboration pins resolved — the wait-timeout mechanism + the raw-outcome carrier shape.** `cli::invoke::invoke_probe` (`crates/cli/src/invoke.rs`) is the subprocess invoker: it writes the T1 request to the child's stdin, enforces a wall-clock `budget`, and returns a **raw, classification-free** outcome. (1) **Mechanism = std-only, no `wait-timeout` crate** (minimal-deps reflex; `std::process::Command` already used across the CLI): the child's stdout is drained on a dedicated reader thread (so a chatty probe can't deadlock on a full pipe), and the main thread keeps the `Child` handle and polls `try_wait` against the deadline at a 10ms interval. The handle is retained *precisely* so a budget overrun can `kill()` + `wait()` (reap) the child — the `wait_with_output`-in-a-thread + `recv_timeout` shape the planner floated was abandoned because it *moves* the child into the waiter, leaving no kill handle on timeout (a leaked process). (2) **Carrier shape:** `ProbeOutcome { stdout: Vec<u8>, status: ProbeStatus }` with `ProbeStatus::{Exited { code: Option<i32> }, TimedOut }` — the invoker classifies **nothing** (a zero/non-zero exit, valid JSON, garbage, or empty stdout are all just *reported*); T3's engine turns these into the `pack-probe-integrity.*` meta-findings. A timed-out outcome carries **empty** stdout (a timed-out probe is untrusted, partial output discarded). (3) **Crate structure:** the `cli` crate gains a `[lib]` target (`src/lib.rs`, `pub mod invoke`) so the invoker is integration-testable against real stub processes independent of the binary's command dispatch; `main.rs`'s module tree is untouched (T3 wires the invoker into the validate path). Proven by `tests/probe_invoker.rs` — four stubs **compiled with `rustc` into a temp dir** (real processes, not mocks): well-behaved (verbatim stdout + zero exit), sleeper (real elapsed bound → `TimedOut`, child killed not leaked — the test asserts it doesn't hang), crasher (non-zero exit reported raw), garbage (raw unparseable bytes + zero exit). **The engine adds NO `std::process` use** (shell-free invariant holds).
